## Top Picks

**Member-facing**
1. Roll browser notifications out past their flag (`browser_notifications`); in-app is already on for everyone.
2. Chat drafts: syncing with Slack's drafts is blocked on Slack (see Messaging).
3. Save Find a Prickle's answers as a check-in.

**Admin-facing (operations, incl. churn reduction)**
0. **High priority, data loss: member merge drops most of a member's data.** About 45 tables that point at `members` are not moved by a merge and cascade away with the duplicate (writing, books, awards, course progress, notes, settings...). Until fixed, don't merge members who have any of it. See "Member merge drops most of a member's data" under Bug Fixes; the list is `NOT_MOVED` in `src/lib/member-merge-coverage.ts`.
1. Member merge log with exact undo.
2. Prickle check-in patterns page (reach-out prompt).
3. Host-facing: let a prickle's host report unmatched Zoom attendees (member-facing, scoped write path needed).

**User experience**
1. Return-to after session expiry mid-form, and origin-aware back links.
2. Basic date format localization (non-US hedgies).
3. Show on Settings when a channel can't reach the member.

**Developer experience** (speed and tooling: CI time, agents coordinating)
1. Re-introduce test parallelism.
2. Coverage floors for `src/app/(admin)` and `src/app/(member)`, plus a ratchet.
3. Test Slack at the HTTP level with MSW.

**Tech debt and code quality**
1. Move the remaining hand-rolled sends onto `createNotifier` (staff-channel posts as admin notification kinds).
2. Turn the React Compiler lint rules back on from `warn`.
3. Finish the pre-prickle-nudge rename; move the ~32 direct-import route tests onto `apiFetch`.

**Infrastructure and reliability**
1. Serialize or coalesce concurrent members/attendance reprocessing (advisory lock); a prerequisite for change propagation.
2. Make member merge atomic (one Postgres function); it runs about 20 writes with no transaction.
3. Downstream change propagation in the processing DAG.
Also: re-audit the public repo for private data (see Security).

**Costs**
1. Replace the per-event Slack activity rebuild (cost grows with the day's traffic).
2. Mux spending alerts have no API; keep a manual checklist as the course launches.

## Member Status Refinements

### Course-only members have no status _(Needs Scheduling)_
Nothing today tells a course-only buyer (a course without a membership) from a member. The only
course at launch includes membership, so none exist yet, but a course sold on its own (the
Self-Editing Academy had no membership before and may return) would need it. Add a tier concept
(member vs course-only) that the member layout and navigation read, so a course-only person sees
courses, profile and settings only. Start in `src/app/(member)/layout.tsx`, `src/components/MemberNavigation.tsx`
and the course access rules in `src/lib/courses/`.

### Access sync: ban and sign out when a member stops being active _(Needs Scheduling)_
`scripts/precreate-auth-users.ts` (`src/lib/auth-provisioning.ts`) creates auth users banned and
releases the active ones by hand at launch. Afterwards nothing keeps auth in step with status.
Automatically ban the auth user and delete their `auth.sessions` rows when a member stops being
active (on hiatus counts as not active), and unban when they become active again. Run it from
`/api/process/members` and `recomputeMemberStatus`, plus a periodic sweep as the safety net.
Never ban staff or admins.

### What may leads, former members and members on hiatus do? _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** no written rule for what each non-active status can see or do. Today the working assumption is that anyone who is not active cannot sign in or see anything (see the access sync entry above, where hiatus counts as not active). Questions to settle per status (lead, on hiatus, cancelled/former, course-only): sign in at all; read-only access to their own writing, profile and certificates; whether their public writer profile and Finisher entries stay up after they leave (and whether they can still edit them); whether they can still be listed on the Bookshelf; export of their own data.
- **Why deferred:** needs the owner's decision, and the answer changes the sign-in ban (access sync), the public writer profile (see "Writer profiles") and the retention work for former members.
- **Where to start:** `src/app/(member)/layout.tsx`, `src/lib/auth-provisioning.ts`, `MemberStatusRepository`; write the decision table in `docs/` and link it from the entries above. The public page's side of the answer goes in one function, `isProfilePubliclyServable` (`src/lib/writer-profile.ts`, `docs/product/WRITER_PROFILES.md`), which already receives the member's status; until then a page is served unless it was removed.

### Rename `members` to `contacts` _(Needs Scheduling)_
The `members` table holds everyone (leads, active, on hiatus, cancelled), so "member" is
the wrong word for most of its rows. Its ids are being converted to short prefixed text ids
(`cnt_...`, "contact", the name HubSpot, Intercom, Mailchimp and Kajabi use for the same
unified entity), so the prefix already says what the table should be called. Rename the
table, `member_id` columns, API routes, types and docs to match. Do it as its own change:
a rename touches the same ~50 foreign keys, views and policies as the id conversion, so
keep it separate from that one. "Member" can stay as the UI word for `status = 'active'`
people.

### "Former BFF"-Style Labels
Single-product former-member labels need the `member_products` table (see Kajabi Data Expansion), not a `status` value.


### Guest Attendance Tracking (Deferred)
Some people who attend prickles have no Kajabi footprint at all (not a member, trial, or lead) — e.g. a guest brought by a member. Tracking them would mean creating member-like records for people who never appear in any Bronze source.

**Why deferred:** Different problem from member status/matching — the whole pipeline assumes attendees resolve to a `members` row derived from Kajabi. Guests break that assumption and need their own identity/record model rather than a fix to existing matching logic.

### Program Cohorts: Still Open
- **Self-Editing Academy's level-within-cohort progression.** The cohort moves together through 3 levels, plus a flexible "stay in the previous level" option; rules are TBD, needs a conversation with Ania before modeling. Today's schema tracks one shared cohort window, not levels.
- **Broader direction (not scoped):** first-class local data for `courses`, `events` and other Kajabi "products", plus a sync to keep them current: part of an eventual move away from Kajabi as the source of truth.
- **Ad-hoc Kajabi Payments subscriptions aren't reconciled.** Some members pay through a custom product in Kajabi's Payments/Billing area (subscriptions/invoices, not the Offers catalog the sync was built around), so reconciliation shows them as `cancelled` despite an active paid subscription. Stopgap: a `gift` override per member. Real fix: sync Kajabi's Payments API.
- Matching rule worth remembering: the date that decides whether a purchase falls in a cohort window is `created_at_kajabi`, falling back to `effective_start_at` only when null, never both (`src/lib/kajabi-cohort-matching.ts`).

### Support several Stripe customers per member _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** `members.stripe_customer_id` holds one customer. A retreat or course checkout can create a second Stripe customer for someone who already has one, so a member can have several. The members processing now links the one with a live subscription (`src/lib/stripe-customer-link.ts`), but the others are not linked to anyone.
- **Why deferred:** the single column is read in many places; a link table is a larger change than fixing which customer is picked.
- **Until then:** the member page's Transactions tab finds a member's other customers by matching Stripe customer emails against the member's primary and alias emails (`MemberDetailPage.tsx`); switch it to the link table when it exists.
- **Where to start:** a link table (member, customer) behind a repository, `src/lib/data/members.ts`, `src/app/api/process/members/route.ts`, and the hygiene pages under `src/app/(admin)/admin/hygiene/`.

### Hygiene: live membership subscriptions whose customer is not linked to a member _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** a check listing live Stripe subscriptions whose customer is not any member's `stripe_customer_id`. Deferred because it needs the multi-customer decision above first. Start from `src/app/(admin)/admin/hygiene/missing-member-data`.

### Hygiene: linked customer has no live subscription but another customer on the same email does _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** a check for members whose linked customer holds no live subscription while another customer with the same email has one. The members processing prevents new cases (`src/lib/stripe-customer-link.ts`); the check would catch any that slip through, such as an email that differs. Start from `src/app/(admin)/admin/hygiene/` and `BillingRepository`.

### Hygiene: paused subscriptions on cancelled members _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** a check for Stripe subscriptions in `paused` status whose member is cancelled, which may be a forgotten pause that should be ended. Start from `src/app/(admin)/admin/hygiene/` and `BillingRepository.stripeSubscriptionsWithStatus`.

### Store payment plan totals and payments remaining as structured data _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** for a Kajabi payment plan (`payment_type` `multipay`), the number of payments made is in the imported purchase (`data.attributes.multipay_payments_made`), but the total is not: Kajabi documents `payment_plan_total_payments` on a purchase, yet the list endpoint the import calls (`/v1/purchases`, `src/lib/kajabi/client.ts`) does not return it, and the import keeps each record whole in `kajabi_purchases.data`, so nothing was dropped on our side. The only place the total appears is the offer's `price_description` text ("6 monthly payments of $115.00 USD"), which is fragile to parse. So "payments left" and "when does this plan end" cannot be asked of the database cleanly, and a program's last-payment date has to be guessed.
- **Also:** the import sets `kajabi_purchases.status` to `active` whenever `deactivated_at` is empty (`src/app/api/import/kajabi/route.ts`), so a plan that has finished all its payments still reads `active`. Anything that treats `active` as "still paying" overcounts.
- **Why deferred:** it needs a check that the single-purchase endpoint (`/v1/purchases/{id}`) returns the total, and a decision on where to keep it (a column on `kajabi_purchases` filled by a second fetch for `multipay` purchases only, or a derived view). Not verified against Kajabi's live API yet.
- **Where to start:** fetch one multipay purchase through the detail endpoint and look at its attributes; if the total is there, add `payment_plan_total_payments` and a derived `payments_remaining` to the purchase record through `BillingRepository.upsertKajabiPurchases`, with an idempotency test, and show it where a member's program plan is listed. Needed for a "payment plan ends before or after the cohort ends" view.

### Hygiene: a Stripe membership pause that does not match what the Hub expects _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** a check that compares each paused membership subscription's resume date (`stripe_subscriptions.pause_collection.resumes_at`) with the date the Hub says it should resume, and lists the differences with the number of days. The Hub already knows the expected date in most cases:
  - **A member in a program that includes membership:** the end of the last such cohort they are in. A pause that outlasts it is membership given away; one that ends before it bills too early. In production a pause outlasting its cohorts by more than a year was found on one active member, and about ten others ran 1 to 8 weeks past a cohort end.
  - **A member on hiatus:** the hiatus end date. Most match exactly; a few have an end date in the Hub but no resume date in Stripe, so nothing resumes them by itself, and one hiatus has no end date at all.
  - **A member the Hub shows as cancelled:** there is no expected date, and any resume date is a risk, because Stripe will start billing them when the pause lifts. Production had several cancelled members with a future resume date and several with an old pause and no date. These need the subscription cancelled in Stripe, not a date.
  - **An active member with no program and no hiatus on record:** no expected date can be derived, so list them for a person to explain, or record the reason (a hiatus row or an enrollment) so the check can apply next time.
- **Why deferred:** needs the Stripe pause (`pause_collection`, including `behavior` and `resumes_at`) exposed through `BillingRepository`, and a decision on who sets or corrects the resume date (a person in Stripe today). Ending a hiatus automatically (resume the subscription from the Hub at the hiatus end date) would remove the mismatch at its source and is the longer-term fix.
- **Where to start:** `src/app/(admin)/admin/hygiene/`, `BillingRepository.stripeSubscriptionsWithStatus`, `ProgramRepository.allEnrollmentCohorts` (cohort ends per member), `MemberStatusRepository` (hiatus windows).

### Check whether a hiatus that ends on the billing day skips an extra month _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** one member's recorded hiatus ended on the 7th, her billing day. Her payment history in Stripe shows no charge for the two hiatus months and also none on the end date itself, with billing resuming the following month, so she appears to have had one more unpaid month than the hiatus. It is one confirmed example, not a finding. If it is systematic (how Stripe treats a pause whose resume time falls on the billing day, or how the pause is lifted by hand), every hiatus ending on the billing day gives away a month. In production, about three quarters of the hiatus ends in the last two years fall on the member's billing day (the usual schedule is whole months from the billing day), roughly 20 of them in the last year, so the exposure is the member count times one month of membership, and it recurs with every hiatus.
- **Resolved (2026-11-25, checked in production with the imported invoices):** not systematic. Of 49 hiatuses that ended on the member's billing day in the last two years, 37 were charged on the end day itself, 1 three days later, 4 had their next membership payment 30 or 31 days later (a possible skipped month), and 7 had none after it (cancelled, paused or not yet due). So the end day is normally charged and the theory is not confirmed.
- **Still open:** the 4 cases with a 30 to 31 day gap were not looked at one by one; the member's billing schedule (Transactions tab) now shows them as a missed day, so check those by hand in Stripe. If a pattern shows (the same way the pause was lifted), add it to the pause hygiene entry above.
- **Original note, kept for history:** the database held no Stripe invoices or charges, so the claim could not be confirmed from data. It needs the payment history of a handful of members whose hiatus ended on their billing day, read in Stripe.
- **Where to start:** check three to five such members by hand in Stripe (list them from `member_hiatus_history` end dates against the day of `billing_cycle_anchor` in `bronze.stripe_subscriptions`). If confirmed, either set the pause's `resumes_at` a day or more before the billing day, or have the Hub resume the subscription itself at the hiatus end date (see the pause hygiene entry above), and add the case to that hygiene check.

### Import Stripe invoices and charges as Bronze data _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What was missing:** the Stripe import kept customers, products and subscriptions, but not invoices or charges, so the database could not say who was billed what and when. That blocks paid months per member (the basis for any price or annual-plan analysis), the hiatus billing-day check above, whether a paused member was charged, and failed or incomplete payments.
- **Resolved:** `bronze.stripe_invoices` and `bronze.stripe_charges` (service role only; amounts in cents; no raw record, card details, customer email or name, receipt links or free-text failure message, only the failure code), read and written through `BillingRepository` (`stripeInvoices`, `stripeInvoicesByCustomers`, `stripeCharges`, `upsertStripeInvoices`, `upsertStripeCharges`). A pull import (`src/lib/stripe/billing-import.ts`) runs in the manual Stripe import (`POST /api/import/stripe`, `?full=1` for the whole history) and nightly at 04:30 UTC (`/api/reconcile/stripe-billing`): the first run reads everything, later runs read since the newest stored row minus 45 days and ask again about every stored open or draft invoice. A key that may not read invoices or charges is reported as such in the import result.
- **Still open:** (1) done for the membership product: the billing schedule per member (`docs/architecture/BILLING_RECONCILIATION.md`) is the derived paid-months view; what is left is the same for programs, courses and events (see "Billing reconciliation: the other purchase types"); (2) a decision on retention for payment data (nothing is removed today); (3) the webhook latency path (`invoice.*`, `charge.*`) is not built, the pull import is the only path; (4) an invoice that Stripe voids or deletes after the 45-day window and was not open or draft when last seen is not refreshed except by `?full=1`; (5) the nightly job has no Checkly heartbeat (`unmonitored` in `src/lib/cron-heartbeats.ts`, plan limit), so a failing run, for example a key without invoice read access, shows only in the Vercel logs.
- **Where to start:** `BillingRepository.stripeInvoicesByCustomers`.

### Billing reconciliation: the other purchase types, acknowledgement and checks on a schedule _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **Resolved:** the membership schedule per member (expected billing days against Stripe, statuses paid, refunded, missed, failed, open, charged when not expected, unexpected, not expected), on the member's Transactions tab and as an overview of members with a row that needs a look (`/admin/hygiene/billing-reconciliation`, permission `billing.reconciliation.read`). Docs: `docs/architecture/BILLING_RECONCILIATION.md`.
- **Still open:** (1) **Programs, courses and events** have no schedule: `PurchaseType` names them, but a payment plan's expected dates and total are not stored (see the payment plan totals entry), so nothing is guessed; the retreat payments PR fills the event type in. (2) **"Reviewed / explained"**: a table keyed by the row key (member, purchase, month) with a reason and who and when, so a known exception stops showing as needing a look; deferred to keep the first version read-only (new table, grants, RLS, repository, pgTAP). (3) **One Stripe customer per member** in the overview (the linked one); the member's own tab also finds customers under their other emails (see "Support several Stripe customers per member"). (4) **Past pauses are unknown**: only the current `pause_collection` is stored, so a currently paused subscription shows every unmatched billing day as not expected, including months before the pause began, which hides a real miss; the pause hygiene entry above needs the pause history. (5) No scheduled run or alert: the overview is read when opened.
- **Where to start:** `src/lib/billing-reconciliation.ts` (`buildSchedules`), `src/lib/billing-reconciliation-loader.ts`.

### Match Gmail addresses the way Gmail does _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is missing:** email matching is a lowercase plus the alias map (`resolveEmail`, `src/lib/email-aliases.ts`). Gmail ignores dots in the local part and everything after a `+`, and `googlemail.com` is the same mailbox as `gmail.com`, so `a.b@gmail.com`, `ab@gmail.com` and `ab+x@googlemail.com` are one inbox but today count as three people. A member who types a different spelling in Stripe, Kajabi or Zoom than in the Hub matches nobody until someone adds an alias by hand. This is only about spelling: two different names (an alias for a nickname, a work address) still need an alias.
- **Why deferred:** it touches every email match (members processing, Kajabi, Stripe, Slack, Zoom, sign-in), `members.email` uniqueness and `canonical_email`, so it needs a decision on whether to store the normalized form or only compare on it, and a pass over existing members to find addresses that would now collide. It was left out of the Stripe customer link fix on purpose.
- **Where to start:** one `normalizeEmail` helper used by `resolveEmail` and the alias lookups (Gmail and `googlemail.com` only; leave other providers alone, since dots and `+` can matter there), then a read-only query for current members and aliases that collapse to the same normalized address.

## Landing pages (page builder)

Slice 1 and the opt-in form block are built (`docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`). What it leaves out, with why and where to start.

### Landing page builder: the rest of the layout options _(Needs Scheduling)_
The Section has hide on desktop and mobile, space inside per size (now the spatial four-side control), width, text alignment, where narrow content sits, fill the page and, new, vertical position (`verticalAlign`, with Fill the page). Left out, each because it needs a schema decision or touches every block: (1) **Hide a single block** on desktop or mobile, and per-block **space inside / outside** and **flush** (no gap to its neighbours): every block would take common props, which needs a wrapper decision in `config.tsx` (the `span` box already wraps each block in a Section; start there, and reuse `FourSidesField`). (2) **Equal-height blocks** and **a block on its own row**: the Section grid is 12 columns with `span`; equal height is `items-stretch` on the grid plus a stretch option on `Columns` (`verticalAlign` there is top or middle only). (3) **Mobile "image on top"** for Split, and per-size text alignment: Split has `imageSide` for both sizes. (4) **Make the section full height** is Fill the page only on a short page; a "screen tall" minimum height is not there. **Where to start:** `src/components/landing/blocks.tsx` (Section, Columns, Split), `src/components/landing/editor-config.tsx`, `src/lib/landing/json-transfer.ts` (`EXTRA_PROPS`), `src/lib/landing/kajabi-import.ts` (which already reads some of these from Kajabi).

### Landing pages: what the conversion specialist can't do yet _(Needs Scheduling)_
The conversion specialist edits and publishes landing pages (`pages.read`, `pages.edit`, `pages.publish`; migration `20270930000000` gives them the page, version and site template policies) and browses the media library (`media.read`). Left out, with why: (1) **Uploading images** (`media.manage`, `POST /api/media`) stays admin-only: `media_assets` insert and update policies and the storage upload path need their own policies for the role. (2) **Preview of an unpublished image**: `/api/media/<id>/<preset>` serves a non-public asset only to an admin, so a draft image picked from the library may not show in the editor for them until the page is published. (3) **Deleting a page** (`pages.delete`) is withheld on purpose (a destructive action nobody asked for). (4) Not exercised through the repositories with a real specialist session: the policies are proved in `supabase/tests/database/conversion_specialist_role.test.sql`, the pages and actions in unit tests with fakes; a signed-in run against a local stack would catch a table the editor reads that the policies miss. **Where to start:** `src/app/api/media/**`, `supabase/migrations/20270225100000_create_media_assets.sql` for the policies to mirror, and a db test next to `tests/api/data/`.


### Landing pages: finish and publish the new homepage _(Needs Scheduling)_

The "Homepage" (a published landing page template, `home-2`; the in-code starter it came from is retired) is the reviewed design, built from the Homepage blocks (`src/components/landing/homepage-blocks.tsx`, `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Homepage blocks"): an unpublished draft the owner edits under Admin, Pages. **Resolved:** the blocks and the template, and **live data** for four of them (`docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Live data"): Outcome numbers items can be bound to a registry figure (hours written together, prickles with writers, writers this week, words logged in the Hub), the Schedule panel can list the next upcoming prickles (all of them, whatever the type; no joiner counts; "N writing now" only at 2 or more), and the Announcement strip can say a prickle is on or starts soon, and Coming up can list the next events announced publicly with real dates and places left (events with a capacity and a linked Kajabi offer; the link is the offer's checkout page, never the sign-in-only event page); each falls back to its typed values. **Still open:** (1) **host names and fun facts**: decided, see "Landing pages: host fun facts on the homepage schedule"; until built only "An experienced host" (100 or more past prickles) exists; the option is `hostStyle` on the Schedule panel (`src/lib/landing/live-schedule.ts`), where a "first name" style can be added once decided, with the host's consent in mind; also the public schedule and strip list **all** upcoming prickles (the owner's call), so a future "open to drop-ins" flag should narrow them (see "Prickles: an open to drop-ins flag per slot"): the owner wants it **per slot** (per recurring schedule slot in `prickle_schedules`, with an optional default per host or per prickle type), not per prickle type, and it is not the same as `announced_publicly`, which means event-worthy; until it exists a private or members-only session of any type would show (check the types first); the Coming up block now lists `announced_publicly` events, but the Homepage's Coming up is still typed (switch it to live in the editor; today most announced upcoming events are everyday sessions, so tick the kinds, for example Retreat and Virtual retreat); FinisherCards stays typed; (2) **THE END is not recorded anywhere yet** (checked: no project completion, goal or badge means it), so "100s of Finishers" and any Finisher wall stay hand-typed until a Finisher record is built. It needs: a place where members or staff record that someone finished, consent for a public name and photo (opt-in, revocable, per Finisher), and then a public-safe count and list (no member rows, one query behind a repository). (3) a Finisher page template (the "Read her story" and "See every Finisher" links, a page per Finisher, and what a Finisher consents to publish); (4) a Compare page for "Compare all programs" (the level-of-support table lives there, not on the homepage); (5) to publish: the buttons' links, the sign-up form's Kajabi form id (Kajabi, Marketing, Forms), the placeholders in [brackets] (Finisher names and lines, retreat names, the Self-Editing Academy line), pictures from the media library and the search title, description and social image in Details; (6) `/` still sends visitors to the sign-in page: serving the published `home` page at `/` for signed-out visitors, and pointing `quillandcup.com` at the Hub, wait for the domain move off Kajabi (see "Landing pages: import the existing Kajabi pages"); (7) the Homepage blocks are not offered to the AI builder (`AI_BLOCKS` in `src/lib/landing/ai.ts`) on purpose. **Start with:** the Homepage template under Admin, Pages, Templates, then set the Homepage blocks to "Live from the Hub" in the editor.

### Landing pages: site-wide button width and per-button text colour _(Idea)_
The style guide now has a button style and a button text colour (`src/lib/landing/style.ts`, `src/lib/landing/button-contrast.ts`), but two parts of Kajabi's button settings are not built. **Width (full or auto)** was left out because it does not fit cleanly: buttons sit inline in a Buttons row, a hero, a banner and the header, where "full width" means something different (a stretched row, two buttons that no longer fit side by side), so it wants a decision on which blocks it applies to; the Button block, Link list and sign-up form already have their own full-width choice. **Text colour on a button's own colour:** the text colour applies only to solid buttons in the primary colour; a light or brown button, and an outline button, keep theirs. Also not checked: a button that chooses its own look is outside the publish check, and a site on outline buttons does not check a button's solid choice. Start in `src/app/globals.css` (`lp-btn-*`) and `buttonClasses` in `src/components/landing/blocks.tsx`.

### Landing pages: host fun facts on the homepage schedule _(Needs Scheduling)_

The public schedule shows only "An experienced host" (100 or more past prickles). **Design decided 2026-11-21:**
- **Name:** a host's first name appears only if they have a writer profile and consent to showing their name there (the writer profile's own name consent); no writer profile, no name, only the generic line. A title like "Bestie" is the host's display name, so nothing new is recorded.
- **Consent per fact:** reuse the visibility toggles that awards and published books already have; a fact kind without a toggle (hosted N prickles, member since, working on a project) needs its own toggle. A fact with no consent is never shown.
- **Levels, not a flat list:** facts are ranked in levels of interest (for example: a published book or award, then hosted 100+ prickles, then a project in progress, then member since 2+ years). For each host, pick randomly within the first level that has a usable fact, and never fall to a less interesting level. A published author might show "author of <title>", "authored 3 books in the Hedgie House" or "hosted 100 prickles", but never "member since 2023". Across the hosts shown together, prefer a mix of fact kinds so the list reads as varied.
**Where to start:** `src/lib/landing/live-schedule.ts` (`hostStyle`), the writer profile visibility settings, `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md` ("Live data"). **Deferred:** needs the toggles above and the level list agreed (kinds and thresholds).

### Landing pages: a public "all events" page _(Needs Scheduling)_

The homepage's Coming up list (style "list") ends with a typed "See all events" link, but no public page lists every event: the member Events pages (`/events`, `/events/<slug>`) need a sign-in, so the link has no good public target and the Homepage leaves its address empty (the link shows only when it has one). The owner posts the same events on social media, so until a page exists the link stays empty or points at the social posts. **Decided:** we want the page (the owner asked for it to be tracked). **Still open for the build:** what each card shows beyond the public title, date and places left (the Coming up block is the model), and the URL. **Why deferred:** not part of the homepage work. **Where to start:** `src/lib/landing/live-events.ts` (`loadComingUp` is the public-safe loader to reuse), `docs/product/CALENDAR_EVENTS.md` ("announced publicly"), the `/events` page for the cards a public version would mirror.

**Events that were announced before covers went public:** an event's cover shows on the homepage only when its media asset is public. Saving a publicly announced event in the admin now marks its cover public (`publishEventCover`), but an event announced and given a cover earlier keeps a private asset until it is saved again in the admin or the picture is marked public in the media library; until then its tile shows the neutral look. Check `/admin/media` (the "Public" tag) before the first publish.

### Prickles: an "open to drop-ins" flag per slot _(Needs a Decision)_

**Decided 2026-11-21: nothing changes on the homepage for now.** The Schedule panel may list the weekly coaching session (it is a real program); the public page simply does not offer a way to drop in on it. The flag is its own piece of work, outside the homepage.

The public homepage schedule and "next prickle" strip list **every** upcoming prickle (the owner's call: part of the brand is that someone is almost always writing at the table to join). That means a members-only or paid session of any type shows its time and title to the public. In production on 2026-11-19, every upcoming type but one was an everyday writing session; the exception was a weekly coaching session. **Idea:** a flag "open to drop-ins" that decides what the homepage (and other public surfaces) may show. **Decided so far:** it is **per slot** (per recurring schedule slot in `prickle_schedules`), with an optional default per host or per prickle type that a slot can override, not a flag on the prickle type alone; it is **not** the same as `announced_publicly`, which means event-worthy (can appear in a "Coming up" list) and says nothing about who may drop in; and it is not specific to the homepage, so the member-facing prickle list could use it too. **Open:** whether we want it at all (to be discussed with the program lead before building), what the default is when unset (open, to keep the homepage full, or closed), how a slot's flag reaches the prickle it creates on each calendar sync (`src/lib/prickle-schedules.ts`, `src/lib/calendar-events.ts`), where staff set it (the schedule page and the host's slot), and whether it also gates the Join link shown to non-members. **Where to start:** `src/lib/landing/live-schedule.ts` (the loader that lists upcoming prickles for the homepage; add the filter there), `prickle_schedules`, and the audience chain notes in `docs/product/CALENDAR_EVENTS.md`. 

### Landing pages: header logo reserves its width _(Idea)_

The header logo is a fixed 48 px high with an automatic width, so the links beside it shift sideways once the picture loads (no vertical jump). **Why deferred:** the page render knows only the asset id, not its size. **Where to start:** carry the asset's width and height into the site template when the logo is chosen (`src/lib/landing/site-template.ts`, the Design tab's picker) and set `width` and `height` on the `img` in `src/components/landing/site-frame.tsx`.

### Landing pages: a contact form block, and a richer site header and footer _(Needs Scheduling)_

Kajabi's Contact page is a built-in page with a name, email and message form that cannot be exported, so the Hub's Contact page is a short page with the team's email address as a link (published by hand from a JSON file). **Missing:** a contact-form block (name, email, message, a send button, a thank-you message) that delivers the message to the team's inbox. **Why deferred:** the cutover only needed the page to exist; a form needs a decision on where messages land (the shared inbox by email through the existing mail sender, or a table staff read in the admin, or both), spam protection (a honeypot and a rate limit at least; no third-party captcha without a look at `/privacy`), and the same rule as the opt-in form that the Hub stores no more than it must. **Where to start:** the opt-in block and `OptInDestination` (`src/lib/landing/opt-in.ts`) for the shape of a form block that posts through a relay route, and the notifier and mail code for the delivery; then rebuild the Contact page with it (a two-column page with a picture beside the form, like the Kajabi original).

Also on the site template (Pages, Design), compared with the Kajabi header and footer: the footer has no logo, and the header has no announcement bar above it ("Want to see if the community is right for you? Join our newsletter") and no per-page choice to hide it. Add them only if the owner wants them back (`src/lib/landing/site-template.ts`, `src/components/landing/site-frame.tsx`).

### Placeholders: more entries, other fields, a real-browser check _(Idea)_

The placeholder registry (`src/lib/landing/placeholders.ts`, `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Placeholders") has one entry, the year, offered in the two footer text fields. **Why deferred:** staff asked for more later (a member's name first) but nothing needs them yet, and each one raises a question the year does not. A member's name needs a per-viewer render, and public pages are anonymous and rendered the same for everyone, so it fits only where a member is signed in (a notification or an email, not a landing page) or needs a small script that fills it in after load; decide that before adding an entry. The context passed to `resolvePlaceholders` is where the viewer would go. Other places that could opt in with `placeholders`: a button label, a heading. **Not checked:** the field was tested in jsdom only (typing, pills, Backspace and Delete, paste, the edit box), not in a real browser, so click through the footer text in Design and in a landing page's own footer: the pill's × on hover, arrowing onto a pill and Delete, the popovers inside Puck's side panel (they sit in the panel's scroll area and could be clipped near its bottom edge), and a long footer text wrapping. **Also open:** the tag box (`TagInput`) shares the `Pill` look and remove button but not the keyboard behavior, which is the host's in both places (Backspace in the empty box removes the last tag; Backspace beside a pill removes it); sharing it would mean one component that owns a list of pills and the text between them, which the tag box does not need.

### Landing pages: JSON import limits _(Idea)_

The JSON import (`docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Export and import") keeps only the props a block has at its top level; props inside a list or card item (`items`, `stages`, ...) are not pruned, they only go through the sanitizer and link check. **Resolved (Sentry HUB-23):** a server action body was capped at Next's default 1 MB, below the 2 MB cap the file checks, so a page between the two failed in the transport with a generic error (save, publish, import and create-from-JSON had the same gap). `next.config.ts` now sets `serverActions.bodySizeLimit` to `SERVER_ACTION_BODY_LIMIT_BYTES` (`src/lib/server-action-limits.ts`, 4 MB, under Vercel's 4.5 MB request limit), and `tests/lib/server-action-limits.test.ts` fails if it drifts below twice `MAX_LANDING_BYTES`. **Still open:** pruning inside items. **Why deferred:** no known harm (renderers read only the props they know) and no page is near 1 MB. **Where to start:** `pruneToKnownBlocks` in `src/lib/landing/json-transfer.ts` (item shapes can come from `BLOCK_DEFAULTS`).

### Landing page editor: preview another Journey map stage in the canvas _(Idea)_

The Journey map block lets a visitor choose a stage to see its programs, but in the editor canvas Puck's selection overlay is expected to take the click, so the canvas always shows the stage marked current and the other stages' offers are edited in the side panel only (not checked in a browser when the block was built). **Idea:** have the canvas follow the stage row being edited (Puck's `resolveData` or a small editor-only context), or let the owner click a stage in the canvas while the block is selected. **Why deferred:** the side panel works, and it needs a look at how Puck routes pointer events in this editor first. **Where to start:** `src/components/landing/JourneyTabs.tsx` and `src/components/landing/editor-config.tsx`.

### Landing pages: richer host lines from facts a host chose to share _(Idea)_

The live Schedule panel shows "An experienced host" (100 or more past prickles hosted) under a prickle, and no names (`hostStyle` on the block, `src/lib/landing/live-schedule.ts`). **Idea (owner):** make the line warmer and specific, from facts the host opted in to show publicly: a title or the most recent award ("Bestie, winner of <award>"), "hosted N prickles" or, for newer hosts, "hosted N guests", "author of <book>" or "author of N books", "working on <project>". The line is the first fact the host shared, in a fixed priority, and falls back to "An experienced host" or nothing. **Why deferred:** these facts are member data on a public page, so each kind needs its own opt-in ("show on public pages", revocable, separate from `show_on_profile`, which only means visible to other members), and the owner has not yet decided whether a host's first name may show. **Where to start:** the facts exist as `member_awards`, `member_badges`, `member_books` and `writing_projects` (check where a title like "Bestie" is recorded); add per-fact public-consent settings under Settings, Profile; add a public-safe read behind a repository (counts and chosen titles only, no ids, nothing for a host who shared nothing); extend `hostStyle` with a "chosen facts" style next to "experience"; test that an opt-out or a revoked consent removes the line within the cache window (60 s).

### Landing pages: move opt-in sign-ups from Kajabi to the Hub, one piece at a time _(Idea)_

The opt-in form block exists and submits to Kajabi through the `OptInDestination` interface (`src/lib/landing/opt-in.ts`, `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Opt-in forms"); the Hub stores nothing yet. Order of the move, each step its own PR: a Hub leads table (service role only, a repository with a contract suite, soft delete, a consent record) written alongside Kajabi by a dual-write destination; a nightly reconcile against Kajabi's contacts and form submissions (`/v1/form_submissions`; a webhook alone is never enough, see CLAUDE.md "Nothing through webhooks alone"); mapping a Kajabi form's tags to Hub segments (and the block's form setting to a Hub list); the newsletter send and automations (Resend, `src/lib/email.ts`, with a per-list unsubscribe and double opt-in, which Kajabi does for us today); last, stop writing to Kajabi. **Resolved:** a real, owner-run test showed the Instagram handle is `custom_1` in the owner's account (set it per page; the block leaves it out by default), and that Kajabi runs the form's tags and email sequences on a submission. **Still open:** what status Kajabi answers for a refused address (only 401 and 403 are documented).

### Landing pages: what is left of templates _(Idea)_

Templates are pages of kind `template` with a draft, a published copy and History (`docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Templates"). Deferred, with why: (1) **Resolved:** the Homepage is a published template and the built-in code starter is retired. **Names:** the leftover "starter" names are renamed and the old column and kind are removed (`landing_pages.replaced_at`, version kind `before_replace`, `keepDraftBeforeReplace` / `replaceDraft`); the Activity Log still reads `starter_applied_at` for old rows; (2) **a template's pictures stay private**: publishing a template does not make them public (it isn't on the public site), and a page made from it marks them public when that page is published; so a picture used only by a template shows as unused in the media library's usage list (`docs/product/MEDIA_LIBRARY.md`) and a sweep could delete it, and a picture deleted since the template was saved shows broken on a page made from it (the JSON import clears missing pictures; applying a template does not yet); start in `src/lib/landing/template-data.ts`; (3) **the Templates tab doesn't show "changes not published yet"** (the list has no draft documents; it would need a flag on the summary); (4) **a visual preview of a version** in History; (5) **templates for other roles** (a community manager has no access to landing pages at all: opt-in rule for new roles).

### Landing pages: saved sections and thank-you page templates _(Idea)_

Many existing pages are clones with small text changes (thank-yous, challenge variants, waitlists), so duplicate page is in, but a reusable section (save a Section with its blocks, insert it anywhere, update it once) and a thank-you template (heading, text, countdown or video, a button to another page) would save more. **Resolved:** the site-wide header and footer exist for website pages (the site template, `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Site template"). **Still open:** a landing page that opts into the site header instead of its own root fields. Start at `src/components/landing/config.tsx` (`PageFrame`) and Puck's `Data` shape.

### Landing pages: popups, A/B tests, custom code and tracking pixels _(Idea)_

**Resolved:** the **Exit popup** block (see "Exit popup: next slices" below for what is left of popups). **Still open:** A/B pages, custom code and pixels. Kajabi has A/B pages and custom code. The page editor's Settings tab leaves custom code out for this reason: it is where a head or body script would go, and it runs third-party code on a public page, so it needs the consent decision below before it exists. Deferred: each needs its own consent and privacy decision (custom code and pixels are third-party scripts, so they must follow the consent banner in `docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`), and A/B needs anonymous visitor measurement the Hub does not have. Scheduled publish (publish at a time, and unpublish at a time) is also not built; it needs a nightly or minute job that calls the same `publish_landing_page` function.

### Exit popup: next slices _(Needs Scheduling)_

The Exit popup block shipped as slice 1 (`docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Exit popup"; design in `docs/architecture/PAGE_BLOCKS.md`, "A block with a page-level job"): per page, a time and exit-intent trigger, a frequency cap, never to signed-in people or sign-ups, editor and publish check. Deferred, in this order, each its own PR:

- **Owner decisions first (the conservative choice is built).** (1) *Storage and consent:* the popup's "closed on" record is kept in the visitor's browser only after Accept where the consent banner asks (EU, EEA, UK, unknown country), and always elsewhere. Decide whether that record may be treated as needed for the page to behave (then it is kept everywhere, and the popup stops showing on every page load for EU visitors who have not answered), and what a notice for **public visitors** (not members: `/privacy` is for members) says about it; until then do not change `mayRemember` (`src/lib/landing/exit-popup.ts`). (2) *Phones:* there is no exit intent on a phone, so a popup set to "when they go to leave" alone never shows there; decide whether a scroll-up or back-button heuristic is wanted at all (they misfire and can trap visitors, so none was built), or whether "after a time" on phones is the answer. (3) *Staff on the live page:* signed-in staff never see the popup on `/p/<page>` (use Preview or a private window); say so if a "show it to me" switch is wanted.
- **A site-wide popup with a per-page override.** Kajabi's is per page, so slice 1 is too. A default in the site template (`landing_site_template`, its own draft and publish cycle, "Use site settings" like the style and favicon) would give every page the same offer without copying the block; a page's own popup, or "no popup on this page", wins. Start at `src/lib/landing/site-template.ts`, `resolveStyle` for the pattern, and the Design tab. It touches the site template's normalizer, publish check and versions, and a pgTAP/repository contract if a column is added; a block stored in the template's JSON needs none.
- **More in the popup, and a second kind.** Allow Video, Countdown and Columns inside the popup, and give its blocks the layout settings Kajabi has (width and mobile layout per block; a Section's `span` fields only show inside a Section). Kajabi also has a **two-step popup** (opens when a visitor clicks a button or link): a trigger "on click of this button" and an anchor link to open it. Start at `POPUP_BLOCKS` in `src/components/landing/editor-config.tsx` and `ExitPopup.tsx`.
- **Import from Kajabi.** The export may carry the theme's exit and two-step popups (`kajabi-import.ts` lists them as left out). Check what the zip holds for a popup, then map it to `ExitPopup` (trigger, image, text, form) with a test per setting. Start at `src/lib/landing/kajabi-import.ts`.
- **Popup measurement.** Shown, closed and signed-up counts need the anonymous visit analytics decision ("Landing pages: anonymous visit analytics and a funnel"); until then the popup records nothing about visitors. When decided, declare the events in `src/lib/analytics/events.ts`.
- **Editor panel groups.** Kajabi's collapsible "Exit Popup Settings" and "Layout Settings": Puck 0.24 renders fields as one list; a custom grouped field or the Outline's "Settings" level could split them (`src/app/(admin)/admin/pages/[slug]/OutlinePanel.tsx`).
- **Page-level placement.** The popup is a block, so it appears among the page's blocks and the Outline; a Page-level row in the Outline ("Exit popup: on") that selects it would match Kajabi's per-page section better.

### Landing pages: anonymous visit analytics and a funnel _(Needs Scoping)_

`landing_page_viewed` and a funnel (visit, click, sign-up) are not built: the usage events are for signed-in members and carry a `user_id` (`docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`), and there is no anonymous id or cookie by design. Visitors to a public page are anonymous. Decide first what may be recorded without a sign-in and under which consent (a consent-gated, aggregate-only counter with no id, or a provider's), then declare the events in `src/lib/analytics/events.ts`. Until then the builder records only what admins do (`landing_page_published`, ...) through `withAction`.

### Landing page editor: a calmer layout, device sizes, spacing and width controls _(Needs Scheduling)_

Owner feedback after comparing the Puck editor (`src/app/(admin)/admin/pages/[slug]/LandingEditor.tsx`, `src/components/landing/editor-config.tsx`) with Kajabi's editor. Deferred as design work, each piece its own PR. **Resolved:** the canvas keeps the app's light or dark theme when it changes (`useMirrorHostTheme`; Puck copies the host `<html>` attributes into its iframe only once); (1) **device sizes**: after Tablet or Phone is chosen, a dropdown of popular models sets the canvas width (`devices.ts`; the tablet and phone widths are portrait CSS widths, the Android ones approximate; no landscape toggle beyond the existing Tablet landscape button); **Resolved later:** each model now has its portrait CSS height, the canvas is set to the exact width and height, Tablet landscape swaps the chosen model's size, and a dashed fold line marks the first screen (`FoldMarker.tsx`). Not done: phone landscape, browser-toolbar-aware heights, the fold line was not checked in a real browser. **Still open:** (2) **Left panel (Resolved)**: the rail opens on Outline, which is now a Kajabi-style drill-down in one panel (page, then a section's blocks, then a block's fields, with Back; `OutlinePanel.tsx`, `outline-tree.ts`), and Add is a searchable list grouped by category. Fields live in the left panel; the right panel stays closed unless pinned from the header. Reorder is @dnd-kit with a Move to position menu (Puck's outline drag primitives are not exported). **Still open from it:** the drill-down was checked in component tests only, not in a real browser (the local database lacked the landing pages migration), so click through it with a real page; items can't be dragged between lists (a block into another section) from the outline, only on the canvas or by adding a new one; there is no delete or duplicate in the outline rows (the canvas action bar has them); Add from the outline is click to add at the end, dragging from the rail's Add tab still drops anywhere; no shortcut to open Add. (3) **More direct manipulation on the canvas (Resolved for fifteen blocks)**: Steps, Stats, Finisher cards, Timeline, Week strip, Finish-line cards, Plan phases, Buttons, List, Testimonials, FAQ, Card grid, Link list, Schedule panel and Outcome numbers hold their items as child blocks in a slot, which drag on the canvas (`docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Blocks with items"); checked by a real pointer drag in a throwaway Puck harness for the first seven, and by the same `move` action the drag dispatches in the real Puck editor for the rest (`tests/components/landing-puck-editor.test.tsx`), not by a pointer in the admin editor. The Schedule panel draws the live rows itself and its slot holds the typed fallback; Outcome numbers keeps its live figures as the child blocks' own `source` and `stat`, with the server writing `live` into each (`src/lib/landing/live-data.ts`). **Still open from it:** **Coming up** events and the **Journey map**'s stages and offers are still array fields. Why: an item cannot see its block, and both need that. Coming up draws every event one of three ways (cards, feature tiles, a dated list) by the block's `style`, and an item in a slot has no way to read it (no React context in the server render, no props from the parent), so the item would have to carry all three drawings and be hidden by classes on the parent, or take a `style` of its own; the live cards would also have to be drawn by the block apart from the slot, as the Schedule panel does. The Journey map's stage is a tab whose name sits in the stepper while its panel (with the stage's own list of offers, a nested slot) sits under it, and `JourneyTabs.tsx` is one client component handed all stages at once, so the parent cannot see the stage names to draw the stepper from child blocks. **Where to start:** Coming up first: give the event item a `style` of its own (set in the block's fields as "how to show them" and copied to each item by a `resolveData` on the block) or draw all three layouts in the item behind parent classes (`@container` plus group variants), then move the live cards to the block's own draw like `SchedulePanel` does, and add `ComingUpEvent` to `SLOT_ALLOW`. For the Journey map, make the stage block draw its own tab button and its panel, and let the radio-and-label pattern (no state, CSS only) select between them in a parent grid; nest an `offers` slot in the stage (`SLOT_ALLOW` takes one list per slot name already). Also still open: a keyboard-only check of moving an item (Puck's own alternative) in the real editor; outline names show the item's type, not its content; a Link list's style and colour are now set per link, not once for the list (a "set all" would need a field on the parent that rewrites its children). (4) **Consistent spacing controls (Resolved)**: a Section has padding top, bottom, left and right per size and hide on desktop or mobile, and (5) **block width (Resolved)**: a block inside a Section takes 1 to 12 columns with a mobile override (`src/lib/landing/layout.ts`, `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Spacing and width"). **Still open from them:** Hero and Outcome numbers keep their Small/Medium/Large "Space above and below" (not four numbers per size); the width applies only inside a Section, not inside Columns slots; the Kajabi importer does not yet turn a block's twelfths width into `span`; and the controls were checked in a browser only through a throwaway harness page (Puck editor, plus the public render with the container resized across the 48rem switch), not the real admin editor. (6) **Site-wide header and footer (Resolved for website pages, with version history for the site template (History, Bring back, Reset to published):** `landing_pages.page_type`, one Pages sheet with tabs, and the site template edited in the Design tab, with Logo, Menu, Call to action, Social links and Sign in blocks and a narrow-page layout; the canvas shows them as fixed regions. **Still open:** a signed-in user menu, which needs a per-visitor render and public pages are anonymous and cached, so it needs its own design (a small script that asks the app who is signed in, or a link only); logo glyphs for the social links; the editor does not refresh the site regions until reloaded) as their own editable regions with desktop and mobile layout and header-only blocks (a sign-in or account menu); today they are root fields (see also "saved sections, site-wide header and footer defaults"). (7) **A Settings area (Resolved:** a Settings tab in the editor's rail with the favicon, the style guide, the design tools and a keyboard shortcuts button; the site-wide values are edited in the Design tab and a landing page can override each group, `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Settings". **Still open:** custom code, which runs third-party scripts and needs the consent decision first, see "popups, A/B tests, custom code and tracking pixels"; **Resolved later:** a site-wide button style (solid or outline) and button text colour, with a publish check for readable buttons (`docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Buttons"); see "Landing pages: site-wide button width and per-button text colour" for what is left of the button style; the editor reads the site design when it opens, so reload it after changing Design.) **Start with:** (3) or (6).

### Landing pages: import the existing Kajabi pages _(In progress)_

**Resolved:** Pages has Import from Kajabi (see `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Importing from Kajabi"): an uploaded page export becomes a draft, mapped deterministically (text, headings, dividers, buttons, the sign-up form, header logo, footer copyright, section colours and background pictures), idempotent on a key derived from the export, with the old draft kept in history when it is replaced. Piloted on two pages (a simple one and the link-in-bio page with many buttons and a form).

**Still open:**
- **Buttons inside text blocks.** **Resolved:** they are Button blocks after their text (`use_btn` and the `btn_*` settings), and pictures or embeds in a text block are reported. **Still open:** a text block's `new_tab` and `btn_size` are not carried over (the Button block has neither; all the owner's exports had new-tab off).
- **Blocks the exports use that the mapper leaves out.** **Resolved** (see `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Importing from Kajabi"): feature, card, pricing, accordion, countdown, video, link list, social icons (in a page section), plain-HTML embedded code and the blog (left out, said). **Still open:** (1) **Embedded Shopify product buttons** (the "Books" page: dozens of script embeds) have no counterpart: they need a book or product card block that links out (a title, a picture, a price text and a Buy link), filled from a list, not from the export. (2) **Kajabi-hosted video** is not in the export (only an id), and the live page has no embed address either (Kajabi plays it from its own player and shows a poster picture; a section background video seems to be a Wistia-hosted file, which the Video block does not play): the video is set by hand in the editor. It belongs with the course migration, which moves videos to Mux: once a page's video is on Mux, the importer could take the playback id from a lookup keyed by the Kajabi video id. **Resolved:** the countdown's date (read from the live page when the admin gives its path, `kajabi-lookups.ts`), the sign-up form's id and the `/resource_redirect/` links. (3) **Offer blocks** carry only an offer id; a lookup from Kajabi's offers (the Hub already imports them) could fill the title, price and checkout link (a button's link to an offer already uses that lookup). (4) **Footer link lists and the theme's popups** (exit and two-step) have no home on a page; **header and footer social icons** are resolved for website pages (the site template's social links are icon links). (5) **Icons for social links**: **resolved**. (6) Event rows with a price and sold-out state, a quiz (a Kajabi assessment is a link today), and carousel sections. (7) **Rich text inside cards, Accordion text and FAQ answers** is plain lines (the Card grid, Accordion and FAQ blocks hold plain text); keeping bold and links needs rich text on those items (an FAQ answer sent to search engines would then need its HTML limited to what `FAQPage` allows). The settings of each block type are in the export's `snippets/block_*.liquid`.
- **Per-button colours.** We offer plum, light and brown; a black button is shown brown. A free colour is deliberately not offered (contrast), so this wants a decision, not code.
- **The picture at the top of a page is cropped to 16:9** by the existing picture presets; see "pictures at the right shape and size".
- **Form names and fields.** The export carries only a form's id and not its name or fields, so the form is chosen in the editor and the Instagram question turned on by hand. If a page's forms are all known, the id from the export could be matched to a form chosen once. **Update:** the sign-up form now takes the export's form id as the form to send to (it is the id the live page posts to), so only the name and fields stay unknown.
- **Imported links point at Kajabi addresses, not at the Hub's imported pages.** A button that went to another Kajabi page (`/resource_redirect/landing_pages/<id>`) now links to the path Kajabi sends it to (`/waitlist`). After the cutover the root serves a Hub page at that path only if the path is named in `src/lib/root-routing.ts`; an imported page is published at `/p/<address>`, so until a page is given its top-level path the button opens the Kajabi page on the courses site instead. **Why deferred:** which Hub page answers at which path is the owner's decision per page. **Where to start:** after importing a set of pages, link a button to the Hub page that was made from the target export (the importer could remember the Kajabi path for the page it creates, `landing_pages.import_key` is the layout, not the path, so this needs a new column or the page's address chosen at import), and add the top-level paths the pages should answer at. A countdown's date is copied from the live page only when the admin gives that page's path in the dialog, because the export doesn't say which page it came from; a lookup by section ids over the site's sitemap could find it without asking.
- **Picture descriptions: not yet done.** The suggestions use only headings (the owner decided no picture goes to a model), so a picture whose headings say little gets a generic description the admin should improve. A picture-aware suggestion would need a decision on where members' faces may go (no vendor does today). A Hero's side picture and a Finisher card's photo are not offered a description in the dialog because the importer does not build them. The model is not called for logos with a brand name. Nothing in CI calls the real API, and a live call has not been tried (needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; see "Build with AI"). Start at `src/lib/landing/kajabi-alt-text.ts`.
- **A page's identity is its layout** (resolved: see `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Importing from Kajabi"; the export has no page id). **Still open:** (1) a re-export after blocks were added or removed is a new layout, so it is not offered as a match; the admin picks the page with "Replace the draft of another imported page". A looser match (for example how many block ids two layouts share) would offer it, but needs the section and block ids stored per page (today only their hash is). (2) Pages imported under the first key format keep that key until they are replaced by choice; once none is left, a migration can null them (expand, then contract). (3) If Kajabi's export ever carries a page id or title, use it for the key and the title. (4) "Import all": each export in a multi-file import is chosen and imported one by one; there is no one-click import for several.
- **Checked locally only through the public render:** the two pilot drafts were viewed as published pages on a local stack (the admin dropdown and dialog are covered by component tests, not driven in a browser), and each page still needs a look against the original in the editor, and its picture descriptions (the export has none), before it is published. The public render of a Section was broken (its slot was not declared in the render config); fixed with this change.
- **Pages with no export** cannot be imported (no scraping, by decision): export them from Kajabi one at a time.

### Landing pages: a general form block, not only the mailing-list sign-up _(Idea)_

The Sign-up form block has fixed fields (name, email, an optional Instagram handle) and submits to a Kajabi form (`src/components/landing/OptInForm.tsx`, `src/lib/landing/opt-in.ts`). An audit of the owner's 13 Kajabi forms found every one is name and email, usually with the Instagram handle, except a contact form that also asks for a message (no page uses it today). Wanted: a configurable field list in the editor (text, long text, dropdown, checkbox, each with a label, a required flag and a placeholder), the block renamed "Form", and each field mapped to a destination field. Kajabi's submit call takes only name, email and `custom_1` to `custom_3` (in the owner's account: Instagram Handle, Message, Timezone), so a Kajabi-backed form can collect at most those until more custom fields are added there; a survey or feedback form that is not a contact needs the Hub to store answers (a table, a repository, a consent record), which is a separate decision from the mailing-list move above. Also open: redirecting to a thank-you page after submit (Kajabi forms do this on their own side today; the block has its own success message or redirect), registering a contact for an event on submit (one Kajabi form does), and a publish check for a form block with no form chosen (one draft page has one). Start at the `OptInDestination` interface, which is already separate from the block.

### Landing pages: a quiz block _(Idea)_

One audited page, the Writers Block Quiz, is a Kajabi assessment (a quiz with a Start button), not a form, and the builder has no block for it. Decide whether the Hub runs quizzes itself (questions, scoring, results that map to a tag or a segment, as the owner's "QUIZ" and "RESULT" tags do in Kajabi) or links to Kajabi's. Until then a link button to the Kajabi quiz works. Related and also not built: a generic iframe embed with a strict address allowlist and a sandbox.

### Landing pages: pictures at the right shape and size _(Needs Scheduling)_

Blocks ask the media route for the existing presets (`event_hero` 1600x900, `event_card` 800x450), which crop, so a logo or a tall picture is cropped to 16:9 unless the asset's own fit is set to keep the whole picture. Add presets that keep the picture's shape (a logo at a bounded height, a page-width image) in `src/lib/media/presets.ts`, and ask for them in `src/components/landing/blocks.tsx`. Also: an asset marked public when a page is published stays public after the page is unpublished (it may be used elsewhere); a sweep that lists public assets no live page or other feature shows would close that.

### Landing pages: AI help building a page, without Puck Cloud _(Partly done)_

**Resolved:** an AI tab in the editor (Add blocks, Change the page) and "Build with AI" on New page: a description becomes an unpublished draft through our own call to the Claude API (`src/lib/landing/ai.ts`, `docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md` "Build with AI"). Output is reduced to the blocks and props the builder has and sanitized; only the admin's description is sent.

**Still open:** (1) `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` must be set in Vercel (`pnpm env:sync --only=ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`) and a live call tried; none has been made. (2) The AI tab (Add and Change) is built; Change replaces the whole page in one undo step, so a long page can't be changed in one go (about 60 KB of page text) and there is no "change only the selected block". (3) Pictures: the model cannot pick from the media library; it could be given asset titles and descriptions to choose from. (4) Offering Testimonials, Countdown, Video and the sign-up form once the admin can supply the real quotes, dates, links and form id in the prompt. (5) Cost and abuse limits: admin-only today, no per-admin rate limit. (6) A standing line on `/privacy` about AI is the owner's call; the changelog entry says only what was checked (only the description is sent).

### Landing pages: an Audit tab in the editor's left rail _(Idea)_

Puck's own demo has "AI" and "Audit" tabs beside Blocks and Outline; ours has only those two. The AI tab is Puck Cloud's and is covered by the AI help entry above (it would be our own panel there too). The accessibility checks (`checkLandingPage`, `src/lib/landing/a11y.ts`) run only when an admin opens Publish, so errors surface late. Deferred because it is a convenience, not a gap in what publishing enforces. Where to start: register a Puck plugin (`plugins` prop on `<Puck>` in `src/app/(admin)/admin/pages/[slug]/LandingEditor.tsx`) that renders the issues from `checkLandingPage` for the current data, updating as the page changes, with a click on an issue selecting its block; reuse `IssueList` so the Publish dialog and the panel read the same. Component test with Puck mocked, as `tests/components/LandingEditor.test.tsx` does.

### Landing pages: two admins editing the same page _(Idea)_

Autosave writes `draft_data` and the last write wins, so two admins on one page silently overwrite each other (Puck Cloud's multiplayer is announced but not shipped, and we don't use Cloud). Start small: store the draft's `updated_at` when the editor loads, send it with `saveLandingDraft`, and make `saveDraft` in `src/lib/data/landing-pages.ts` (a plain update today) refuse a save whose base is older than the row (add `.eq("updated_at", base)` and treat no row as a conflict), then show a calm "someone else changed this page" notice with a reload. Live co-editing (Yjs or similar) is a much larger step and not worth it for a handful of staff. Deferred because it is a rare case with a handful of staff. Where to start: `src/app/(admin)/admin/pages/actions.ts` (`saveLandingDraft`), `LandingEditor.tsx`, the repository contract in `tests/helpers/landing-page-contract.ts`. This is the "autosave conflict warning" item below, split out.

The options, from smallest to largest. Puck keeps a page as one JSON object and takes it through the controlled `data` and `onChange` props (which `LandingEditor.tsx` already uses), so any of these can be built around it without Puck Cloud. This list comes from a web search summary, not from Puck's docs or a trial, so check each before committing to it:
- **Pessimistic locking** (document-level): one admin holds the page while editing and the others see it as taken. Done in our own database, so it fits Supabase and RLS as is. The simplest way to stop two people overwriting the same JSON, and enough when simultaneous editing isn't needed.
- **Real-time transport and presence**: broadcast the JSON and who is where (for example the selected block) over WebSockets with a hosted service such as Liveblocks, PartyKit or Hocuspocus. Needs a new service and its own auth, so it is more than the check above.
- **CRDTs**: Yjs or Automerge wrapped around Puck's JSON tree merge concurrent edits without conflicts. The most capable and the most work; it also changes how drafts are stored and how the data migrations (`src/lib/landing/migrations.ts`) apply.
- Puck Cloud's own multiplayer (managed auth, branching environments and versioned page storage are announced alongside it) is tracked in Puck's GitHub issue #207, per the same summary. We don't use Cloud.

### Landing pages: smaller things left _(Idea)_

Custom colours and fonts beyond the theme list (kept to the palette on purpose so text stays readable); per-device layout overrides (hide a block on phones); a spacing and width picker per block (only Section has one); keyboard shortcut list in the editor (undo, redo and save work); a heading outline panel in the editor sidebar (Puck's heading-analyzer plugin shows an "Audit" tab with the page's H1, H2 and H3 as a tree; our publish checks in `src/lib/landing/a11y.ts` already flag a missing or second `h1` and skipped levels, so the plugin would show it while editing instead of at publish; check that it works with our block props and our Puck version before adopting); autosave conflict warning when two admins edit the same page (last write wins today); preview inside the editor matching the public render exactly requires the editor iframe to load the same stylesheet, which Puck does by copying the app's styles; the admin preview route is wrapped in the admin shell, a bare route would match the public look better.

### Landing pages: embed, social icons and a logos strip _(Idea)_

Three blocks left out of the first set of static blocks (Hero, Steps, Stats, CTA banner). Each is its own small piece.
- **Embed.** A generic iframe for things like a Kajabi quiz or a calendar. Deferred because it needs a design first: a strict allowlist of hosts (as `parseEmbed` does for video), the iframe address built rather than pasted raw, a `sandbox`, a title (an a11y error without one), and a decision on whether any allowed host sets cookies or tracks, since the consent banner rules apply (`docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`). Start at `parseEmbed` and `src/lib/landing/a11y.ts`. It would also unblock the quiz page in "a quiz block" below.
- **Social icons** in the page header and footer. **Resolved** for website pages: the site template's social links are icon links using the Social icons block's marks; **Still open:** the same for a landing page's own root header and footer (it has no social links at all today).
- **Logos strip** ("as seen in", partners): a row of pictures with alt text and optional links, greyscale option, wrapping by container query. Low priority because there are few logos to show; it depends on the picture presets that keep a logo's shape ("pictures at the right shape and size").

### Landing pages: blocks that show Hub data _(Idea)_

Every block today is typed in by hand, so a page goes stale. Four blocks would read the Hub's own data instead. Shared rules: the public route already reads on the server with the service role, so each block gets a server-side loader that returns only what is safe for an anonymous visitor (no member names, emails or ids; the repo is public and a page is anonymous by design). Pass the data to a presentational block the way `Countdown.tsx` splits its client part, keep the editor and preview working without live data (placeholder rows), and add the block the usual way (`docs/product/LANDING_PAGES.md`, "Adding a block"). Build the events block first: it sets the pattern for the rest.
- **Upcoming events and prickles.** A list or cards of the next events (title, date and time in `ORG_TIMEZONE`, price, a sold-out state, a button to sign up), with a setting for how many and which kind. Replaces the "event rows with a sold-out state" the Kajabi audit found, and never goes stale. Start at `src/lib/upcoming-prickles.ts`, `src/lib/events-agenda.ts`, `src/lib/event-offer-window.ts` and `src/lib/event-tickets.ts`; decide which events are public (a flag or the audience fields in `src/lib/event-audience.ts`).
- **Book shelf.** A grid of covers with title, author and edition links, for a "books by our members" or a showcase page (the audit found one book grid). Start at `src/components/books`, `src/lib/book-editions.ts`, `src/lib/book-cover-assets.ts`. Needs a decision first: a member's book appears on a public page only with her opt-in (a per-book "show publicly" setting), and its cover asset must be public like any page picture.
- **Course catalog.** Cards for the courses on offer (title, picture, summary, a button), for sales pages. Start at `src/components/courses` and `src/lib/courses`. Needs a decision first: which courses are public and how a page chooses them (a picker of courses, not "all").
- **Testimonials from a shared library.** The Testimonials block takes quotes typed on each page (the audit found nine pages with them), so the same quote is retyped and edited in several places. A library of quotes (text, attribution, optional picture, a consent record that the person agreed to publication), managed once and picked per page, with a way to show a chosen few or the latest. A table with a repository and audit entries, admin only. It overlaps with "saved sections" above: decide whether a quote library or a reusable Section covers both.

### Page block registry: next steps _(Needs Scheduling)_
The page block registry (`docs/architecture/PAGE_BLOCKS.md`) is the first contribution point: the events module adds the Announcement strip, Schedule panel and Coming up. What it leaves, and where to start:
- **Outcome numbers and its live figures** (`src/lib/landing/live-stats.ts`, `live-stats-loader.ts`) stay in the pages code, although they read the Hub's own attendance, writing and book data. They import no events code, so the boundary holds, but a second product would not want them in "pages". Worth moving to a module of their own (a "community" one) when a second app needs the page builder; the loader contract already allows it (`PageDataLoader`).
- **Other module contribution points** are not designed: a module's routes, admin navigation, member pages, database tables and notifications. The events module still lives mostly in `src/lib` and `src/app`. One ADR should generalize from this slice before a second one is built (`docs/decisions/README.md`).
- **The block names in the a11y check and the AI builder** (`src/lib/landing/a11y.ts` switch, `src/lib/landing/ai.ts` `AI_ITEMS`) still list the pages code's own block types by name. The registry gives them item rules and publish checks for blocks it knows (modules' included), but a module's block is not offered to the AI builder with its own description. Start at `PageBlockDefinition` (add an optional `ai` field) when a module block should be offered.

## Data Import

### Data hygiene: Kajabi course imports that match no member _(Needs Scheduling)_

**What is missing:** `/admin/hygiene` (the Data Health Dashboard, with sub-pages for unmatched events, Zoom and Slack, ambiguous names, merge and fix, missing member data and so on) has nothing for the Kajabi course imports. Three of them keep what they cannot match, and none of it is visible afterwards, only as counts in the import's own dry-run or run summary: (1) **quiz responses** whose responder email matches no member (`bronze.kajabi_quiz_responses`, "kept, no member yet"), or matches several (a shared address, skipped, never guessed); (2) **lesson progress** rows whose email matches no member (`bronze.kajabi_lesson_progress`, `keptNoMember` and `ambiguous` in the progress import summary); (3) **lesson comments** whose author name matches no member or more than one (`unmatched` and `ambiguous` authors in the comments import summary, counted and listed by name to the admin who runs it, never to logs). The owner's rule is that losing Kajabi must not lose anyone's writing or progress, so these rows are kept, but staff have no page that says how many are waiting or helps fix the cause (a duplicate to merge, a member to add, an alias to record) so the next run picks them up.

**What to build:** a "Course imports" tile and sub-page under `/admin/hygiene`, in the same shape as the others (counts and a list, one row per unmatched responder or author, each with the fix link: Merge & Fix, the alias tools under `/admin/data/aliases`, the member search). Show **counts per course and kind, and the email or name only to staff who can already read them** (members' data, so behind the permission the other hygiene pages use; never in logs or the repo). Rows for a removed member must not appear (their address is in `removal_tombstones`; the import skips them and so should the page). **Where to start:** the repositories for the three Bronze tables (a read that joins to `members` and the alias map the way `src/lib/courses/quiz-response-import.ts` and `progress-import.ts` do, paged with `fetchAllRows`), the existing `unmatched-slack` page as the pattern, `docs/product/COURSES.md` ("Importing past quiz responses", "Importing Kajabi per-lesson progress", "Lesson comments").
