Test with invented data: a responder with no member, one with a shared address, a removed one (not listed), and a member added later (drops off the list after the next import).

### Zoom accounts: registering an account and matching by host email
- **What shipped:** several Zoom accounts feed the Hub, the secondary excluded until an admin keeps its meetings (`docs/architecture/ZOOM_ACCOUNTS.md`).
- **No screen to register an account.** `zoom_accounts` rows are added with a one-off statement the owner runs (the doc has it). A small admin form (label, policy, credentials prefix, with a check that the env vars are set and a "test connection" button) would let staff do it. Deferred because there is one secondary account and it changes rarely; start from `ZoomRepository.createAccount` and the review page.
- **Owner steps are outside the repo:** the Server-to-Server OAuth app on the second account, its webhook subscriptions and its `ZOOM_SECONDARY_*` values synced to Vercel.
- **Matching a meeting to the account by host email** (for instance suggesting "keep" when the host is one of our hosts and the topic looks like a prickle) is not built: the review page shows topic, time and participant count and the admin decides. If the personal account's undecided list gets long, add a suggestion from `prickle_types` names and the calendar prickles that overlap the meeting.
- **`latestMeetingStart()`** (the "attendance data through" date on the calendar page) still counts every Bronze meeting, kept or not.
- **Not covered:** the nightly reconcile imports the last 90 days of every account in one run; if a second account's volume makes the run slow, give each account its own pass.
- **First run on the real second account (nothing has run against it yet).** After the owner steps: register the account with `default_policy = exclude`, import a short range from the manual admin import, and check in production (read-only, `verify-against-prod.md`) that its meetings are in Bronze under the new account, that none made a prickle or attendance, and that the review page lists them as undecided. Then keep one meeting that really was a Quill & Cup session, reprocess, and check the prickle and its attendance appear; ignore it again and check they go. Read the logs for a run or two before trusting the nightly reconcile with it (`read-only-clis.md`).
- **Zoom side of the owner steps, in detail.** A Server-to-Server OAuth app in the secondary account with the same report and meeting read scopes as the primary app; webhook events `meeting.started`, `meeting.ended`, `meeting.participant_joined`, `meeting.participant_left` and `meeting.chat_message_sent` pointing at `/api/webhooks/zoom`; its secret token goes in `ZOOM_SECONDARY_WEBHOOK_SECRET_TOKEN` (each app signs with its own, and the webhook accepts any configured account's). Zoom's endpoint validation must pass once the variables are synced. Don't rotate a token without updating the variable first, or that account's events get a 401.
- **Check what a kept secondary meeting does to members.** Kept meetings become prickles and so reach check-in and check-out DMs, the dashboard and attendance counts. Confirm the live room (`prickle_join_settings`, `/api/live-room`) and the presence cron resolve a room by meeting id from either account, and that a kept personal-link session isn't offered a join link for the primary room. Untested: the pgTAP and reprocessability tests cover Silver exclusion, not these consumers end to end.
- **Decision hygiene.** A decision keyed on a series (the Zoom meeting id of a personal link) keeps or ignores every future occurrence too, so a "keep this link" rule on a link also used privately would pull private meetings in. If that happens, prefer per-instance decisions for that link, or add a series rule that only applies on days with a calendar prickle. Decisions are audited, so a wrong bulk action can be traced; there is no undo button beyond "Back to default".

### Zoom in-meeting chat (spike built; blocked on Zoom sending the event)
_(Needs Scheduling: a Zoom support request, then 2-3 weeks of data to decide)_

**The idea.** Ingest the chat people type in Zoom during prickles as a new Bronze source of messages, tie each message to its author, and use it in member activity, engagement scoring and so on, the way Slack messages are. Zoom offers both a fetch and a webhook, so it should follow the usual pattern: a webhook for latency and a pull-based import in the nightly reconciliation and the manual admin import. It is a new Bronze source (`bronze.zoom_chat_messages`), a table of its own, not part of `zoom_attendees`.

**What the investigation found (Zoom's docs, 2026-11).**
- **Webhook: `meeting.chat_message_sent`** ("In-meeting chat message received"), for public and private messages in a meeting. Payload (`payload.object`): the meeting `id` and `uuid`, an optional `breakout_room_uuid`, and `chat_message` with `message_id`, `date_time`, `message_content`, `sender_name`, `sender_email` (optional), `sender_session_id`, `sender_type` (`host` or `guest`), `sender_context` and `recipient_context` (`meeting`, `breakout_room`, `waiting_room`), `recipient_type` (`everyone`, `host`, `guest`, `group`, `host_group`), and for a private message `recipient_name`, `recipient_email`, `recipient_session_id`, plus `file_ids` when files are attached. Two sibling events exist ("chat message file received", "chat file downloaded"); the first is documented as only available after approval, and neither is handled.
- **The docs also say** the chat webhook needs "the DLP for in-meeting chat feature" enabled and is "only enabled upon request approval" (contact Zoom support). Whether that applies to every use of the event or only to a DLP integration is not clear from the docs. See the status below.
- **Pull path: only a cloud recording's chat file.** `GET /meetings/{id}/recordings` lists `archive_files` / `recording_files`, and a `CHAT` (or `CHAT_MESSAGE`, JSON) file exists only when the meeting was recorded to the cloud with chat saved. The archived JSON has per message `msg_type` (1 public, 2 private, 3 deleted, 4 emoji, 5 nonverbal feedback, 6 edited, 7 reply), `e_time` (elapsed since the start, not a clock time), `s_name`, `s_email`, `msg_id` and `msg_content`, several of them base64-encoded; the sender's email is empty when hidden. The download needs a token that lasts 24 hours when it comes from a webhook. Nothing else in Zoom's API lists a past meeting's chat.
- **Volume of recordings is tiny.** Production had about 19 cloud recordings, 11 computer recordings and 3 transcripts in the last 12 months, against 50+ prickles a week (roughly 2,600 sessions a year), most with 5 people or fewer. So the pull path would cover well under 1% of sessions, and the webhook is the real source. A message the webhook misses is lost for good, and there is no history before the day it started working.
- **Camera on/off per participant: not available.** Zoom's Meetings webhooks have join and leave events but none for starting or stopping video (the only such event found is in the Video SDK, a different product), and the Dashboard QoS endpoints return the camera hardware, not whether it was on. Realtime Media Streams is the only route that would show it and means processing live media, which doesn't fit. Re-check Zoom's current webhook list before building anything.
- **Zoom account.** One shared Zoom account, so only meetings the community account hosts are seen, which makes the chat effectively public or semi-public within the community. The Marketplace app is account-level, Server-to-Server OAuth, "Hedgie Hub", activated. It uses classic scopes: `meeting:read:admin` is the "Meeting's Read Scopes" the chat events require, and no new scope was offered when the event was added.

**Decisions made (with the owner).**
- **Store everything the webhook sends, including private messages**, but the text is not readable by admins: break-glass or system use only. Same shape as Slack content: text and `raw_payload` are service role only.
- **Who talked to whom is metadata**, readable by staff and the system, shown as an interaction between two members: sender, recipient (name and email) of a private message, time, kind of recipient, whether files. Admins read `bronze.zoom_chat_messages_meta`.
- **Public vs private by prickle type.** Special events and educational webinars are treated like public channels; standard writing prickles like private channels. `prickle_types.chat_visibility` (`private` by default, `public` seeded by name for `educational`, the two parties and the virtual retreat) records it. A private message (`recipient_type` other than `everyone`) stays private whatever the type.
- **Start as a spike**: collect the data first (Bronze table, webhook, tests), then decide whether it is worth building on. Activity and engagement scoring are follow-ups.

**What shipped (PR #1204).** The `meeting.chat_message_sent` handler in `/api/webhooks/zoom` (`src/lib/zoom-chat.ts`, parser tested) records each message once per `(meeting_uuid, message_id)` through `ZoomRepository.recordChatMessage` (in-memory twin and contract tests included); message text is never logged; a failed save is logged and the webhook still answers 200. Migration `20270625000000`: the table (`zmmsg_` ids), column grants and an admin-only read policy, the `zoom_chat_messages_meta` view, `prickle_types.chat_visibility`; pgTAP test `zoom_chat_privacy.test.sql`. Nothing reads the table yet.

**Status: blocked (checked 2026-11-15).** The app has "In-meeting chat message received" subscribed and saved, but `bronze.zoom_chat_messages` has 0 rows in production and the logs show no `meeting.chat_message_sent` in 72 hours, across meetings that started after the save. Joins and leaves keep arriving, so our endpoint works and Zoom isn't sending the chat event. The likely cause is the approval gate above (or an account setting).
- **Next step:** a support request to Zoom: the app name, an account-level Server-to-Server OAuth app with `meeting.chat_message_sent` subscribed and saved, other meeting events arriving, no chat events in several days of meetings with active chat; please enable in-meeting chat message webhooks for the account, or say what setting or approval is missing.
- **Before sending**, confirm people typed in the in-meeting chat (not Team Chat) in a meeting started after the save.
- **Subscribe to that one event only.** Leave "chat message file received" and "chat file downloaded" off: nothing handles them and file events can carry file content.
- **To check:** `supabase db query --linked "select count(*) from bronze.zoom_chat_messages"` (link the worktree to production first; read only) and Vercel logs for `meeting.chat_message_sent`.
- **If Zoom won't enable it, drop the integration** (the migration and the handler; nothing else reads the table).

**Decide after 2-3 weeks of data** (service role queries, counts only: no message goes into this repo, a PR or a doc): messages per week, distinct senders, how often `sender_email` is present, and how many senders match a member the way `zoom_attendees` do. If volume is trivial or most senders can't be matched, drop it; if not, build the rest.

**Still to build if it goes ahead.**
- **A pull-based import.** A rule violation that stays unless this changes: add the recording's chat file to the nightly reconciliation (`/api/reconcile/zoom`) and the manual admin import if recording ever becomes common, upserting on the same `(meeting_uuid, message_id)` key (the archive's `msg_id` is base64; its `e_time` is elapsed time, so the clock time comes from the meeting start).
- **Mapping a chat to its prickle**, so the prickle type (and `chat_visibility`) is known: `meeting_uuid` to prickle, which needs the same room to prickle mapping as live presence (see "Live presence" under the Live Page section). An admin control for `chat_visibility` in the prickle type editor.
- **Author matching.** `src/lib/member-matching.ts`, the aliases and `ignored_zoom_names`, the way `zoom_attendees` are matched. Session ids and display names change per meeting and the email is often empty for people outside the account, so expect ambiguous and unmatched authors (the hygiene queues).
- **Silver and scoring.** A `member_activities` source rebuilt from Bronze (DELETE + INSERT by source and date range, like Slack), with no text for a private type or a private message, as for restricted Slack channels, and a place in engagement scoring. Reprocessability and idempotency tests as for the other sources.
- **Privacy and retention.** `/privacy` and the retention job (`docs/` retention notes) don't mention Zoom chat yet; add it before the data is used for anything a member sees: what is stored, who can read it and for how long. Also update the member-facing wording if chat ever shows in a member's activity.
- **An analytics event or two** (`src/lib/analytics/events.ts`) for whatever members or admins see, once there is something to see.

### Manual-run buttons for the nightly jobs that have none
The import page (`/admin/data/import`) has forms for the Kajabi, Stripe, calendar, Slack and Zoom imports and reprocess buttons for members, calendar prickles and Zoom attendance, which run the same logic as the nightly `/api/reconcile/*` jobs for those sources. No button calls a `/api/reconcile/*` route, and four nightly jobs have no equivalent at all: the Resend email import (`/api/reconcile/email`, which also does the backfill with `?days=`), the login activity mirror (`/logins`), the media sweep (`/media`) and the retention job (`/retention`). Today they run only from the cron, or by an admin opening the URL. Deferred because the page is organized by source, not by job; start by adding a small "Nightly jobs" section to `src/app/(admin)/admin/data/import/page.tsx` with one `ProcessButton` per job (`ManualReprocessingSection.tsx` has the component), the email one with a days field.

### Silver Change Propagation in the Processing DAG
- **Where:** `src/lib/processing/trigger.ts` (`SILVER_DEPENDENCIES`, `getProcessingOrder`, `triggerReprocessing`)
- **Today:** a Bronze/Local change selects the Silver steps that read that table, and Silver→Silver edges only order the selected steps ("if both run, members and calendar before attendance"). Since 2026-09-29, an edge no longer pulls in an unselected upstream step (it used to, so every Slack webhook event rebuilt all members).
- **Missing half: downstream propagation.** When an upstream step's output changes, the steps that read it don't rerun. A Kajabi import that adds a member should re-match that member's past Zoom attendance and Slack activity. `getDownstreamSilverTables` exists but is unused. Instead:
  - `src/app/api/process/members/route.ts` hard-codes a 90-day attendance reprocess in `after()` at the end of every members rebuild.
  - Nothing reprocesses Slack after members change, so a new member's Slack messages stay unmatched until something else reprocesses Slack.
  - `getAffectedSilverTables` special-cases `calendar_events` → attendance.
- **Fix:** make propagation part of the graph. When a step runs, its downstream steps run in the same pass, in topological order, over the same date range (members → attendance and Slack; calendar → attendance). Then remove the hard-coded attendance reprocess and the `calendar_events` special case. Changes to Slack messages/reactions and Zoom attendees stay local, since nothing is downstream of `slack` or `attendance`.
- **Later refinement:** cascade only when the upstream step actually changed rows (each step reports whether it did), so a members rebuild that changes nothing costs nothing downstream.
- **Cause of the members deadlocks / statement timeouts** (see "Members Reprocessing Deadlocks / Statement Timeouts on Concurrent Webhooks", noted in PR #27 review): before the 2026-09-29 fix, every Slack message/reaction webhook and every Zoom import ran a full members rebuild, and each members rebuild then ran the hard-coded 90-day attendance reprocess. A burst of events (e.g. the bot joining 16 channels during an import) ran many at once and hit statement timeouts (`57014`) on `reprocess_members_atomic`. Webhooks no longer start members runs, but members can still run concurrently (Kajabi import + nightly reconcile + a Slack import's user rebuild), and so can attendance (several Zoom `meeting.ended` events). Propagation must not reintroduce the pile-up: serialize or coalesce concurrent runs of the same step (e.g. a Postgres advisory lock), as that entry suggests.

### Slack Webhook: The Per-Event Activity Rebuild, What Is Left
Every Slack event used to rebuild a whole day of Slack `member_activities` (`reprocess_slack_activities_atomic`), after loading and matching every member in TypeScript. Production logs (2026-10-29) showed it failing a few times a day with `57014 canceling statement due to statement timeout`: the reads take ~100 ms, so the 8 s was bursts of events rebuilding the same rows at once and queuing behind each other's row locks (and it would have grown with every message once chat is busy). **Still open (small):** a Slack user who appeared since the last full projection has no activity until the next rebuild (the nightly one); the windowed rebuild (imports, restriction changes, nightly) and a per-message event on the same message at the same instant could briefly insert both, which the next rebuild of that day removes.

### On-Demand Incremental Fetch for Every Source
Rule (.claude/rules/data-pipeline-reprocessability.md, "Nothing through webhooks alone"): nightly reconciliation is the guaranteed path; webhooks and incremental fetches only reduce latency. Add a targeted incremental fetch per source so gaps heal in minutes instead of by the next nightly run:
- Zoom, Google Calendar, Kajabi: equivalent "fetch since the newest record we have" paths, triggered on demand (webhook gaps, admin button, page views).
- Record each gap-fill (source, scope, rows recovered) and surface it on `/admin/data-health`. A fill that recovers many rows means the webhook was down. Checkly can't see this: it monitors endpoints and heartbeats, not processed requests or logs. (Slack's are at `/admin/data-health/gap-fills`; the other sources record into the same table when they get their fills.)

### Admin Calendar Live Attendance: Push Instead of Polling _(Needs Scheduling)_

The admin calendar adds live Zoom presence (`src/lib/live-attendance.ts`) to a running prickle's count and re-reads every 30 seconds while one runs (`src/components/LivePoll.tsx`). It polls because `bronze.zoom_participant_events` is service role only and not in the Realtime publication, so `LiveRefresh` (postgres_changes, RLS) can't see it. Worth doing once 30 seconds of lag or the extra renders matter: have the Zoom webhook send a content-free Realtime Broadcast after `recordParticipantEvent`, with an authorization rule limiting subscribers to admins, and let the calendar `router.refresh()` on it. Don't open the table to admins for this: it holds participant names, emails and raw payloads.

### Secondary Zoom Rooms _(Needs Scoping)_
We have a couple of secondary Zoom links/rooms that aren't imported. The Zoom import (`src/lib/zoom/client.ts`) reads one host's meetings (`ZOOM_USER_EMAIL`), and everything downstream assumes prickles run in that host's room, one meeting at a time. Checked against prod on 2026-10-03: all 417 imported meetings in the last 60 days had the same `host_id`, and only 1 pair of them overlapped in time. The main room's meeting id also changes roughly monthly.

What relies on that, and what to change when a second room is imported:
- **Attendance** (`src/app/api/process/attendance`): matches a Zoom meeting to scheduled prickles by time overlap alone (`findOverlappingPricklesInMemory`). Two rooms running at once would both match the same prickle, and a social room's attendees would count as attending a writing prickle. It needs a room → prickle (or prickle type) mapping, e.g. a meeting id or host per type, and that mapping must survive the monthly meeting id change.
- **Live presence** (`src/lib/zoom-presence.ts`, `bronze.zoom_participant_events`): the participant webhooks may already arrive for every room on the account. `loadPresenceByMember` only counts events from hosts seen in `bronze.zoom_meetings` (`host_id`), so secondary rooms are ignored today. Once one is imported, that filter lets it in, and presence needs the same room → prickle mapping, since it currently treats being in any covered meeting during a prickle as being at that prickle.
- **Check-out DMs** (`dueCheckoutMembers` in `src/lib/prickle-checkin-dms.ts`): inherit both of the above.
- **PUPs**: unscheduled time in a secondary room would become PUPs, which may or may not be wanted.

### Auto-post the daily #prickle-times update _(Needs Scoping)_
Today a person posts "Remaining Prickle Times for TUESDAY, JULY 21" in #prickle-times each morning: the day's remaining prickles (time, type, host as a mention, "HEADS DOWN" for hostless writing sessions) plus the Zoom link, meeting ID and passcode. The monthly passcode change means updating that post template by hand, so post it from the Hub instead, from the same Zoom setting the check-in DMs and calendar feed use (`prickle_join_settings`, edited at `/admin/prickle-join`, `src/lib/prickle-join.ts`).

- **Job**: a cron route (see `/api/internal/*`, wrapped in `withCronHeartbeat`) that runs each morning in `ORG_TIMEZONE`. Idempotent per day: record the posted day and message `ts`, so a retry or overlapping run never double-posts.
- **Content**: that day's prickles from `prickles` (start time at or after the post time, non-cancelled), hosts mentioned by Slack user id (`slack_identities`/`slack_users`), the join link and passcode from `joinAt(settings, <the day>)`. The existing post says "All times in EST", so check what time zone it should name.
- **Post**: a staff-style channel post, so it stays a direct `chat.postMessage` (a shared room, not a member notification; see the exceptions in `.claude/rules/member-notifications.md`). The channel is config: the channel name in `config/app.ts` like `SLACK_INTRO_CHANNEL`, resolved to an id. Needs `chat:write` (and `chat:write.public` or the bot invited) in `config/slack-app-manifest.yml`.
- **Open questions**: keep the existing text ("Wanna prickle at a time not posted? ...") verbatim? Post earlier-than-morning prickles for the next day? Edit the post when a prickle is added or cancelled later in the day? Does the one-off or irregular event (monthly goal review) go in it? Pop-ups hosted in secondary rooms need the "Secondary Zoom Rooms" item above.
- **Test**: with the fake clock (`useFakeClock`), post once per day, skip a second run, and use the link in effect that day. Include a test with more than 1000 prickles' worth of pagination only if the query isn't date-bounded.

### Map every Kajabi offer to one Hub resource

`/admin/offers` lists each Kajabi offer with the events, courses and programs that point at it, and flags offers with none (not in the Hub yet: the open items before leaving Kajabi) or several. It is read-only: the links are still edited on the event, program and course pages, and the three use different keys (events: `community_event_offers` by offer id, one event per offer; courses: `bronze.kajabi_course_offers`, imported; programs: `programs.kajabi_offer_names` by name).

- **Still open:** unify the three into one offer-to-resource table so an offer can be assigned from the Offers page and "exactly one" is enforced; link programs by offer id rather than name; a way to mark an offer as retired/not migrating so it leaves the open list; membership offers (`MEMBERSHIP_PRODUCT_NAMES` in `src/lib/membership.ts`) and Stripe products have no Hub resource yet and show as unmapped.
- **Start at:** `src/lib/offer-coverage.ts`, `src/app/(admin)/admin/offers/page.tsx`.

### Kajabi Data Expansion

**Current state:** Kajabi API sync (`/api/sync/kajabi`, runs daily at 3am via Vercel Cron) fetches contacts, customers, purchases, and offers into Bronze, then processes Silver. Goal is to own the full member lifecycle.

**Products catalog (next step)**
- Create `kajabi_products` table (manual catalog of ~10 known products)
- Create `member_products` junction table (historical product access)
- Update member processing to populate `member_products` from `kajabi_purchases` + `kajabi_offers`
- Known products to seed: Quill & Cup Membership, 180 Program, Mindset Training, Self-Editing Academy, BFF Program, Hedgies on First Orientation, Chicago Retreat
- Enables product-based queries: "Show all members with 180 Program", "When did member join/leave BFF?"

**Transaction history (future)**
- Kajabi API: `/v1/transactions` (if available on plan)
- Bronze table `kajabi_transactions`, Silver rollup for LTV / revenue analytics

**Lead & engagement tracking (future)**
- Form submissions, opt-in reports, product progress — lower priority until core member/financial data is solid

### Importing Kajabi per-lesson progress _(Needs Scheduling)_
Members partway through a Kajabi course should arrive in the Hub with those lessons marked complete in `lesson_progress`. **Still open:** run `--apply` and then the Silver step (dry run first) for Self-Editing Academy when it moves; an admin button for the Silver step; roster paging for a much larger product (unknown); and the member-confirmed percent catch-up below for courses with no per-lesson data.

### `courses:migrate`: add the quiz step and exercise it against real Kajabi _(Needs Scheduling)_
`pnpm courses:migrate` (docs/product/COURSES.md, "One command") chains steps A to D but was only tested with mocks: it has not been run against real Kajabi or production, so the first real run is a rehearsal to read closely. **Still open:** (1) quiz capture, once it lands, is one more object in the `buildSteps()` list in `src/lib/courses/migrate.ts` (between B and C); today quizzes are skipped by step B and only counted in the summary. (2) Draft lessons' videos are never uploaded by the command (billed, some are leftovers); picking them stays on the admin course page. The quiz responses capture (`--responses`) and its import (`POST /api/process/quiz-responses`) are one more optional pair of steps, also untested against real Kajabi (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Importing past quiz responses"). The progress capture, its insert-only import and the comments capture (to Bronze only) are optional steps of the command now, equally untested against real Kajabi: the first real run of those is a rehearsal too.

### Import Kajabi lesson comments into the Hub _(Needs Scheduling)_
`courses:content --comments` captures Kajabi's lesson comments into `bronze.kajabi_lesson_comments`, and the Silver import writes them into `lesson_comments` (docs/product/COURSES.md, "Lesson comments", Kajabi migration). **Resolved:** the `source` and `source_key` columns (unique together), the import (`src/lib/courses/comments-import.ts`, `POST /api/process/course-comments`, step K2 of `pnpm courses:migrate`), authors matched to members, the Instructor badge for staff (to become the course's instructor only: see "Lesson Comments on Courses"), threading, Kajabi's own times, removal and restore, and no follows, notifications, "N new", digest counts or unanswered-question entries for imported rows. **Still open:**
- **Nothing has been run against production.** The owner runs `pnpm courses:migrate` (or the route with `dryRun: true` first) after reading the counts and the listed names; the capture itself is still unverified against the live Kajabi site.
- **Authors are matched by the exact Kajabi contact name only.** The capture reads just the displayed name (no email, contact id or profile link on the member page), so a name that matches no member (a contact renamed since, a nickname) or more than one (two people with the same name) is left out and listed in the dry run, never guessed; `member_id` stays required. **Where to start if the list is long:** extend the capture (`src/lib/kajabi/comments-capture.ts`, `extractCommentsInPage`) to record a stable identifier next to the name if one exists in the page markup (the comment's avatar image address, for example, may carry an email hash; check on one lesson first, and store only the hash, never the address), add a nullable column to `bronze.kajabi_lesson_comments`, and match on it before the name. A lasting alternative is a small table of reviewed name-to-member choices for the ambiguous ones, read by `matchAuthors`.
- **A comment whose Kajabi id was hashed** (the capture's last resort, a `h:` key) gets a new key when its text is edited in Kajabi: the old row is soft deleted and a new one added, so the thread still shows one. The dry run of the capture prints how many ids were hashed.
- **Reading a course's Bronze comments scans by `course_kajabi_id`** (the table's key starts with the lesson). Fine at a few thousand rows; add an index in a later migration if a course has far more.

### Catch-up from imported Kajabi progress _(Needs Scheduling)_
The Kajabi Product Progress import stores only a percentage per student, which cannot say which lessons were done, so nothing writes `lesson_progress` from it. **Why deferred:** it belongs with the Self-Editing Academy migration, when the course exists in the Hub. **What to build:** on the course outline, a member with access, a row from `memberProductProgress()` (`src/lib/courses/product-progress.ts`) and no Hub progress sees what Kajabi said (for example "Kajabi shows you at 62%") and confirms "mark everything up to here as done" (a lesson or module picker, defaulting to the lessons that make up that share of the course). It writes ordinary `lesson_progress` rows as the member, insert-if-absent, marked as imported/self-reported, with no `member_activities` rows. **Decided:** these completions count toward badges and course-completion nudges. Needs the course's lesson order and a decision on how a percentage maps to lessons (by count or by video length).

### Duplicated courses: what the first version leaves out _(Needs Scheduling)_

"Duplicate course" (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Duplicating a course") copies the tree, the videos (same `video_id`), images and downloads (same stored object, a row of its own), and leaves out members' data and the program. Not done, and where to start:
- **Choosing the program on the copy while duplicating.** The copy has no program on purpose (publishing it would give it to everyone who has the program); an admin sets it in the course details afterwards. If admins want it in one step, add an optional program argument to `duplicate_course` and a picker in `DuplicatePanel`.
- **Shared download objects.** A copy's download points at the original's stored object, so removing one is guarded by `CourseRepository.storagePathShared` (the lesson file actions and the Kajabi file step). Two gaps: the Kajabi step re-uploading a changed file over the same path (`upsert`) changes the copies' content too, and deleting a whole lesson or course leaves its objects in the `course-files` bucket (as before). A sweep of unreferenced objects, or copying the object into the new lesson's own path, would close both.
- **A files library with "where this is used", like the media library.** Lesson downloads (`course_lesson_files`) have no catalog, no usage view and no admin page: the only place to see a file is the Downloads panel of the lesson or course that owns it, so nothing says that a copy shares an object with another lesson. `docs/product/MEDIA_LIBRARY.md` already plans `media_assets.kind = 'file'` ("`course_lesson_files` gain a catalog row when the course builder needs to pick them"); this is that step. It would let `/admin/media` list files, show every lesson using one, refuse a delete while one is in use (replacing `storagePathShared`), and let the builder attach an existing file instead of re-uploading. **Why deferred:** the duplicate only needed the guard. **Where to start:** the usage view and `MediaRepository` in `src/lib/data/media.ts`, `src/lib/courses/files.ts`, and the "file" kind section of `docs/product/MEDIA_LIBRARY.md`.
- **The copy's address is a placeholder.** A duplicate gets `<slug>-copy` (`-copy-2`, ...) and, being a Hub course, its address stays editable in the course details. **Resolved:** renaming the copy offers a one-click "Use <address>" for the name's address, and a published (or once-published) course's old addresses now redirect permanently (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Course addresses: suggestions and redirects"). **Still open:** nothing asks for the address in the duplicate dialog or notes on the copy's page that it is still a placeholder. **Where to start:** `DuplicatePanel`, `duplicate_course`, `CourseDetailsForm`.
- **Course address redirects: loose ends.** (1) The course admin page doesn't list a course's old addresses (`CourseRepository.oldAddressesOf` exists for it) and there is no way to release one that was a mistake; today a redirect holds its address until the course goes back to it or is deleted. (2) A Kajabi course's address change, from the import, is not redirected, and an import that would take an address a Hub redirect holds fails on the database's uniqueness rule instead of choosing another. (3) A permanent redirect from a page in a streaming render may arrive as a meta refresh rather than a 308 status (Next's behavior); the course pages have no `loading.tsx` today, so it is a 308, but check it if one is added. **Where to start:** `src/lib/courses/address-redirect.ts`, `supabase/migrations/20270712000000_course_address_redirects.sql`.
- **Courses nested deeper than the editor handles** can't be duplicated (`too_deep`), like a take-over; the fix is the same flattening in Kajabi.

### Delete course: what the first version leaves out _(Needs Scheduling)_

"Delete course" (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Deleting a course") removes a Hub-owned draft course that no member has access to. Deferred, and where to start:
- **Courses imported from Kajabi.** They are refused, because the import would create them again from the Bronze rows (`/api/process/courses`). Removing one for good needs a way to tell the import to skip it (a hidden or ignored flag on `kajabi_courses`, or deleting it in Kajabi and letting the import's soft delete reach Silver). **Where to start:** `src/lib/data/kajabi-courses.ts`, `src/lib/courses/import.ts`, `src/lib/courses/process.ts`.
- **Courses with members.** Refused while anyone has or is due access (progress, comments and chat links alone do not block). A course that was used and is retired should be archived (hidden, kept), not deleted; there is no such state yet. **Where to start:** a soft-delete or archive column on `courses` read by `loadAdminCourses`, if staff ask for it.
- **No undo.** The delete is for good (the dialog says so). A soft delete with restore would need the same column.
- **Stored files stay.** Deleting a course leaves its objects in the `course-files` bucket (the guard `storagePathShared` protects copies that share them); the sweep of unreferenced objects is the one in "Duplicated courses: what the first version leaves out".
- **A once-published course's old addresses** stop redirecting when it is deleted, so a link someone saved to it ends in a not-found page. Acceptable for the draft copies this was built for; revisit if staff delete courses that were live.
- **Removing a grant by hand** has no screen yet ("Course access: an admin screen to grant and remove access by hand"), so a course with a grant can't be deleted until there is one, or by a database change.

### Course videos: a cleanup page for unused and long-draft videos _(Idea)_
Every video in Mux is billed for storage by the minute (about $2/month for the Ready Writer's ~650 minutes, `docs/product/COURSES.md`), and nothing today tells staff which uploads are no longer earning their keep. An admin page (under the Courses area, behind the `courses` flag) would list two kinds:

1. **Not used in a lesson.** A `videos` row that no `course_lessons.video_id` points at: the old asset after a lesson's video was replaced (the plan retires it "once nothing references it"), a lesson that was deleted in Kajabi, or a duplicate upload. `video_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so these accumulate quietly.
2. **Only in draft for N months.** A video whose lessons are all hidden (Kajabi drafts) and that was uploaded at least N months ago. N comes from a filter on the page (default 3 months, say). There is no "became a draft on" date today (a lesson's `updated_at` moves for any edit), so start with `videos.created_at` as the age, and say so on the page. A lesson that is only "Coming soon" doesn't count: those are planned and visible.

**Per row:** Mux asset id, duration, quality (`basic`/`plus`), status, uploaded on, the lessons that use it (linked, with their state), and the stored minutes, with a total at the top ("N videos, M minutes").

**Acting on it:** staff-only, and never automatic. A "Delete from Mux" button per row (and for a selection) that confirms with the minutes it frees, calls Mux's delete-asset API, then removes the `videos` row and its `video_text_tracks`. Refuse when a lesson still references the video (re-check at delete time, since a lesson may have been re-linked since the page loaded). Write each delete to the audit log. Deleting a draft's video means the lesson has to be uploaded again when it is published, so label that clearly.

**Notes:** the same page could also show videos stuck in `preparing` or `errored`. A matching page for `course_lesson_files` (copied PDFs no lesson lists any more) would follow the same pattern, though the files step already removes those itself.

### Video library: what I've watched, and a watch-later list _(Idea)_
Let a member see in the Hub which videos from the library they have watched, and bookmark the ones they want to watch. Not scoped yet: it is unclear whether "library" means the course lessons or a wider set of videos (replays, talks), so decide that first.

**Watched:** the data mostly exists for course lessons. `lesson_progress` has `completed_at` and `last_position_seconds`, and `video_watch_segments` records every stretch watched (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Watch tracking"). A "Watched" / "In progress" / "Not started" mark on each lesson or video in the list comes from those, not a new table. Completion today is the 90%-playhead rule in `LessonCompletion.tsx`; "90% of the video actually watched" from the segments is still open (`docs/product/COURSES.md`) and would make the mark exact. Nothing is written in sudo, so the marks are read-only there like the rest.

**Bookmarks:** a new Local table (say `video_bookmarks`: member, video or lesson, `created_at`, soft delete with `deleted_at`), writable only by the member and read-only in sudo, with a bookmark control on the lesson and library views and a "Watch later" list. Needs the table grants and a pgTAP test per `supabase-table-grants.md`, and `track()` events per the usage-analytics rule.

**Related, same area:** "Continue watching" on the dashboard and a per-member stats view are both listed as not built in `docs/product/COURSES.md`; this would share their reads. Start at `src/lib/courses/member.ts` and the lesson list pages.

### TrackBear Import Gaps

The TrackBear importer (Projects → "Import from TrackBear"; `src/lib/trackbear-import.ts`) brings over
projects, all progress, starting balances, covers, tags-on-entries, and single-project goals (with their
titles, descriptions and show-on-profile). Importing again **overwrites** what is already in Hub with the
export's values (project title, description, phase and visibility, starting balances, entry date, amount,
note and tags, a goal's target, dates and details), matched by the id each record was imported under, and
the summary says how many of each changed. Left alone: covers, a goal's dashboard pin, anything made only in
Hub, a starting balance in a measure the export doesn't list, and anything the member deleted in Hub. It lists
everything it can't bring over per import instead of silently dropping it. Closing these gaps needs Hub features first:

- **Cross-project goals** (a TrackBear goal counting all projects or several): `writing_goals.project_id` is NOT NULL (a goal always has one project, `20270815000000`), and nothing displays or computes a goal with no project, or with several. Supporting them means relaxing that again or linking a goal to several projects.
- **Tag-filtered goals** ("only count entries tagged X"): no tag filter on `writing_goals`.
- **Habit cadences**: "every N days/weeks" (N > 1) and yearly habits; Hub habits are every 1 day/week/month.
- **"Any measure" habits** (TrackBear habit with no threshold): Hub habits need one measure.
- **Habit date ranges**: Hub habits are open-ended; imported habits' start/end dates are dropped.
- **Project "starred"**: no star on Hub projects.
- **Tag colors, unused tags**: Hub tags are plain labels on each entry.
- **Negative starting balances**: `writing_project_starting_balances` has `CHECK (amount >= 0)`.
- Habit weeks start Monday in Hub; TrackBear uses the member's setting (Sunday by default), so weekly streaks can differ.
- **Entry "last edited" time** (`updatedAt`): Hub entries only have `created_at`.
- **Covers**: kept at their original size as the member's own media library image (`src/lib/trackbear-cover.ts`); a cover that fails to copy (or is an SVG, which the library refuses) isn't retried on re-import (the project already exists), so the member has to upload it by hand.
- **Not in TrackBear's export at all** (would need their API or a TrackBear change): account-level lifetime starting balance, week-start and other settings, leaderboards.
- **One-click import**: TrackBear has no OAuth, and its API needs a member-created API key, so import is export-file upload. If TrackBear adds OAuth, switch to it.


### Educational Prickle Guest Speakers: Setup and Gaps

Educational prickles (`EDU-PRICKLE: Title w/Name`) map to the Educational type through `prickle_types.summary_aliases`, and the name after `w/` is stored as `prickles.guest_speaker` while the type's default host hosts (`prickle_types.names_guest_speaker`). Deferred:

- **Titles that don't start with a known type name or alias** (a webinar titled "Quill & Cup [Topic] Prickle: ...") still go to the unmatched queue to be resolved by hand; once resolved, a speaker after `w/` or `with` is read as usual.

### Ambiguous Zoom Names: The Hygiene Count Still Reads the Stored Rows

The Ambiguous Zoom Names page works out from the current members and aliases which stored names are still ambiguous, and derives their counts from Bronze. The Data Hygiene overview (`src/app/(admin)/admin/hygiene/page.tsx`, `ambiguousNamesCount`) still counts the stored unresolved rows, so it can show more names than the page lists until the stale rows are resolved or ignored. Deferred to keep the fix small. Start by moving the "still ambiguous" check into a shared function in `src/lib/` that both call; `ambiguous_zoom_names.occurrence_count` and `candidate_member_ids` are no longer read and can be dropped afterwards.

### Import pre-2026 events _(Idea, very low priority)_

The past events page (`src/app/(member)/events/past/page.tsx`, `PAST_EVENTS_FROM`) hides events that start before 2026, because the older ones haven't been imported properly yet. Deferred until there is time to import them. Start by importing the older events, then remove the `PAST_EVENTS_FROM` filter.

### Copy every course's images out of Kajabi before the subscription ends

Production (2026-11-10): lesson thumbnails are all copied into the media library, but 77 module posters and 7 course covers still show Kajabi's own address, because `courseImageSrc` (`src/lib/courses/image-urls.ts`) falls back to the original link until a copy exists. All of them belong to 7 courses that are still drafts (nothing member-visible is hot-linked), and the per-course "Copy images" step (`copyCourseImages`, `src/lib/courses/images.ts`) has not been run for them. Once Kajabi is cancelled their links die and copying is no longer possible. Do: run "Copy images" for every course while Kajabi is still paid for, re-run the count (course/module/lesson rows with a `*_url` and no `*_asset_id`, read-only against production) until it is 0, then remove the Kajabi-address fallback from `courseImageSrc` so nothing can hot-link again.

### Mark a lesson complete when its video is watched in chat _(Resolved 2026-11-12)_

- **Resolved:** the chat player now sits inside the same `LessonCompletionProvider` as the lesson page when `POST /api/chat/videos/<message id>` says `completion: { completed }` (the viewer has the course, the lesson is open and the view is being recorded, so not in sudo); `AutoCompleteAtWatchedMark` completes the lesson at about 90% while playing, through `setLessonComplete`, which checks access and sudo again. A lesson already complete is not redone, and "✓ Lesson complete" shows under the video (`src/components/chat/ChatVideo.tsx`). A reader without the course watching a share an admin opened to the channel gets `completion: null`: nothing of theirs is completed. The entry `lesson-videos-in-chat`, the help article and `/privacy` (Course videos row) say so.

## Infrastructure & Deployment

### Refresh the aging architecture docs
Two living docs in `docs/architecture/` have drifted from the code. `SEQUENCE_DIAGRAMS.md` (last edited 2026-08-26) lists `/api/analyze/*` and `/api/import/*` routes, some of which may have moved or been renamed since. `ERD.md` says it covers "87 migrations", which is almost certainly far behind now (check `ls supabase/migrations | wc -l`). Both are marked living, so a wrong line is a bug.
- Why deferred: the docs reorganization PR only moved files and added status headers; checking every route and table is its own piece of work.
- Where to start: compare the route list in `SEQUENCE_DIAGRAMS.md` with `src/app/api/`, and the tables in `ERD.md` with `src/lib/data/database.types.ts` (regenerate with `pnpm types:gen` first if a migration is pending).

### Make the Unit Test Job Faster, then Lower Its Timeout Again
`Test (unit)` has a 15 minute limit (it was 10) because the suite takes 5 to 9.5 minutes depending on the runner it lands on, and grows by about 30 test files a day. Measured on CI runs from 13 to 17 November: median 426s, p90 566s, six runs cancelled at the 10 minute limit. Every phase (setup, import, environment, tests) scales together between a fast and a slow runner, so it is runner CPU, not one slow test. About half the worker time is per-file overhead (the jsdom environment for ~400 files, module imports, the setup files), not the tests themselves.
- Shard the unit project (`vitest --shard=i/n`) in a matrix of 2 or 3 jobs. The required check is named `Test (unit)` (`config/github.yml`), so add an aggregator job with that name or rename the check there. The coverage report already merges several `--suite name=dir` inputs, so each shard can upload its own.
- Find what each file pays to start: `tests/setup.ts` runs `vi.mock` with `importOriginal` on `src/lib/slack-bot-token` in every file, so the real module and its imports load 1,000+ times. Measure with and without it. Also look at jsdom versus happy-dom for the ~400 component files, and the slowest files (`EmojiPicker`, `EmojiPickerKeyboardTones`, `care-page`, `MessageBox`: ~87s of test time between them).
- Check the "Show runner size" step's output in the unit job to confirm the core count the estimates assumed (4 vCPU for a standard runner on a public repo), and measure what skipping coverage on `main` (done) saved.
- Why deferred: the timeout bump and the coverage change stop the failures now; sharding changes the required checks, which deserves its own PR.

### `member-matching` and `orphaned-meetings-idempotency` Fail Now and Then in the db Suite
About 1 in 25 db runs in mid-November failed in `tests/member-matching.test.ts` (all 14 tests that need the member it inserts, even the plain email match) or `tests/api/reprocessability/orphaned-meetings-idempotency.test.ts` (`expected 0 to be 1`), on `main` and on unrelated PRs. In both, a member the test inserted a moment before is not in the list the test then loads.
- **Ruled out**: test order (the db files run in the same order every time; the files just before are the same in passing and failing runs), the seed (a handful of members), the API row cap (`max_rows = 5000`), scheduled database jobs (the `pg_cron` jobs only send reminders), a stale cache (no route is involved in `member-matching`).
- **Done**: the orphaned-meetings test loaded `members`, `member_name_aliases` and `prickles` with a bare, unordered `select`, against the pagination rule; it now pages and orders them like the code under test. Both tests now fail with a message that says which case it is: the member is missing although the row exists, the row is gone (with the table's row count), or why the orphaned count is what it is.
- **Still open**: the cause. When it fails again, read that message: "direct lookup finds 1 row" means the loaded list was cut short (look at `fetchAllRows` and paging while other rows change), "finds 0" means something deleted the row (look at what runs in the background of the db job: the backgrounded build and `types:check`, and work the previous test file left unawaited).
- Why deferred: needs one more failing run to tell the two apart.

### Check that a Dropped Column Is No Longer Referenced
Squawk (`config/squawk.toml`, run in the `Check migrations` job) fails any new migration that drops or renames a column, table or function, so a drop needs a deliberate, approved `-- squawk-ignore`. It reads only SQL: it can't tell whether the code still uses the column, which is how `member_books.genre` was dropped in the same PR that stopped reading it and took `/projects` down while the deploy rolled out. Add a script (next to `scripts/check-migration-order.ts`) that, for a migration carrying `squawk-ignore ban-drop-column` (also table and function), extracts the dropped name and fails if the same PR's tree still references it (grep `src/app/`, `src/lib/`, `src/components/`; ignore `src/lib/data/database.types.ts`, which `pnpm types:gen` regenerates), and a stronger variant that checks the previous production deploy's code instead of the PR's. Deferred because the lint plus the approval rule already stops the accident; worth doing if approved ignores start slipping through.

### Test Slack Integrations at the HTTP Level with MSW
Slack tests currently replace the `@slack/web-api` `WebClient` class with a hand-written fake (e.g. `tests/api/slack-sign-in.test.ts`). That skips the real client's request encoding, retries and error handling, and only returns the happy responses each test remembers to stub. Intercept `https://slack.com/api/*` with MSW instead (the shared server is already set up in `tests/setup-msw.ts`):
- A reusable Slack fake: handlers for `users.info`, `views.publish`, `chat.postMessage`, `conversations.*` that record calls and return realistic responses, including `ok: false` errors (`user_not_found`, `invalid_blocks`, `ratelimited` with `Retry-After`).
- Assertions on what was actually sent over the wire (form/JSON bodies, `Authorization: Bearer` token), not on mock call arguments.
- Migrate every Slack-touching test (sign-in, nudges, wheel of wonder, feedback, private channel access) and delete the per-file `vi.mock('@slack/web-api')` fakes.
- Keep the complementary layers: `@slack/types` typing on block builders, Block Kit limit tests (`tests/lib/slack-sign-in-blocks.test.ts`), real HMAC-signed webhook requests. Optionally, a nightly non-hermetic smoke test that publishes to a test user in a Slack developer sandbox workspace, since only Slack itself fully validates Block Kit.

### Enforce "Tier 2 Ids Are Never Exposed" _(After the Short IDs Scheme Lands)_
`.claude/rules/resource-ids.md` ("Resource ID Tiers") and `docs/decisions/SHORT_IDS_PLAN.md` say a Tier 2 table's id must never reach a user; today a reviewer has to catch it. Once the short id scheme is in place, add a check so a violation fails CI:
- Keep the tier lists in one machine-readable place (a small module or the `COMMENT ON TABLE ... 'id prefix: xxx_'` comments the migration adds), not just prose.
- A test that scans `src/app/**/[param]` route segments, `href` / `url` / `ref` builders in `src/lib/notifications` and `src/lib/channels`, Slack block builders and the calendar feed for ids that come from a Tier 2 table, with an explicit allowlist (each entry with a reason, like the admin-route test in `tests/components/pages/admin-auth.test.tsx`).
- Possible shortcut: a runtime guard in the link / notification helpers that rejects an id whose prefix is not a Tier 1 prefix.
- Add the Tier 1 promotion step (readable prefix, migration, tier list) to the PR checklist.

### Rotate the Checkly Heartbeat Ping URLs _(Needs Scheduling)_
`checkly deploy` printed every heartbeat's ping URL into the public CI logs ("Ping URL of heartbeat check ... is https://ping.checklyhq.com/<id>"), and anyone holding one can mark that job healthy. **Still open:** the URLs already in old run logs stay valid. Rotating means recreating each heartbeat monitor (rename its logical id in `src/lib/cron-heartbeats.ts` so Checkly issues a new URL), copying the new URLs into `.env.prod` and `pnpm env:sync`, then deleting old runs' logs (`gh run delete`) if wanted. Do it together with the pre-prickle-nudges rename below, which already needs a new monitor.

### Log Member Merges So They Can Be Undone Exactly _(Needs Scheduling)_
A merge (`POST /api/admin/members/merge`) re-points everything from the absorbed member to
the survivor and then deletes the absorbed member, so nothing records what moved. Undoing
a wrong merge today is a heuristic: the Split button (`/api/admin/members/split`) and
`/api/admin/members/move-aliases` take the name and email aliases the merge left behind and
reprocess attendance, but they can't restore anything that isn't rebuilt from Bronze
(hiatuses, status overrides, program enrollments, hosting, append-only activities such as
writing progress, outreach touches and logins, and external ids copied to the survivor).
`audit_log` records most of those re-points as separate field changes, but nothing ties
them to one merge, `prickle_attendance` and `member_activities` moves aren't audited, and
the route runs about 20 unrelated writes with no transaction.

**Proposed:**
- A `member_merges` table, one row per merge, written in the same transaction as the
  merge: actor, time, primary id, and the absorbed member's full original row as JSON
  (including its id).
- A child `member_merge_moved_rows` table: `(merge_id, table_name, row_id)` for each row
  re-pointed, and the full row for anything the merge deleted because it collided with the
  survivor's (duplicate name aliases, duplicate cohort enrollments). Also record the
  external ids copied to the survivor and the `ambiguous_zoom_names.candidate_member_ids`
  arrays that were rewritten.
- Move the merge into one Postgres function so it is atomic and writes the log itself; the
  route (single and bulk merge both use it) just calls it. A failure partway then leaves
  nothing half-merged.
- An exact unmerge on top of the log: recreate the member with the same id, move the logged
  rows back, restore the deleted ones, put the copied external ids back, then reprocess
  attendance and Slack activity from Bronze. Offer it as an "Undo merge" action on the
  log entry (the log alone, with undo done by hand from it, is the smaller first step).
- Keep the log permanently (admin-read only; no member-facing exposure), so old merges stay
  undoable. The moved-rows table grows with a long-time member's attendance, still small
  next to `audit_log`.
- Needs the usual new-table work: grants in the same migration, RLS, pgTAP coverage, and
  the merge route's existing tests moved onto the function.

Keep Split and move-aliases as the fallback for merges made before the log existed (see
the removal item below).

### Remove Split and Move-Aliases Once the Merge Log Exists _(After "Log Member Merges")_
`POST /api/admin/members/split`, `POST /api/admin/members/move-aliases`, the Split button
and `SplitMemberModal` (`src/app/(admin)/admin/members/[id]/`) exist only to undo merges made
before merges were logged: they guess from the leftover aliases and rebuild attendance,
because nothing recorded what the merge moved. Once merges are logged and "Undo merge"
works, the only merges they could still help with are the old, unlogged ones, so they
become close to dead code. Remove them (routes, modal, button, their tests in
`tests/api/admin/members/` and `tests/components/`) once the unlogged merges that matter
have been checked and fixed, or after a set period with no use; whichever comes first.
Check the `audit_log`/activity feed for recent use before deleting.

Unconfirmed. After "Move ticked names and emails to ..." in the Split dialog, the source
member's Identity tab once kept showing the moved email briefly. It has not been reproduced.
The suspected cause is the client router cache, so `SplitMemberModal` calls `router.refresh()`
before navigating; whether that is needed is unknown. The routes do not call `revalidatePath`
(a route handler's call doesn't clear the client cache, so it was removed). Retry the repro
(move an alias, then open the source member's Identity tab); if it still happens, capture
what the page shows and when, and check whether the alias row is gone at that moment.

### Env Sync Never Deletes: Removed Variables Linger in GitHub, Vercel and Vault _(Needs Scheduling)_
`config/env-vars.ts` is the source of truth for what exists, but the sync scripts only add and overwrite (`scripts/sync-to-github.ts`, `scripts/sync-to-vercel.ts`, which removes a Vercel var only to replace it, and `scripts/sync-vault-secrets.ts`). Take a variable out of the config, or drop a destination from it, and the copy already synced stays where it is until someone deletes it by hand. This came up when the Checkly heartbeat URLs were briefly given a GitHub destination (PR #371, reverted in #378): seven GitHub secrets were left behind and had to be deleted manually. Config as code that can't say "this should no longer exist" has the problem Terraform solves with its state file.
- **Fix:** let each sync compute the diff between the config and what the destination holds. List the destination's names (`gh secret list` / `gh variable list`, `vercel env ls`, the Vault secret names), and report names that are present but not declared for it. Start with a read-only `--prune-check` that prints them (and fails in CI), then an explicit `--prune` that deletes them after a confirmation. Never delete by default: other things share these places (secrets set by hand, Vercel's own `VERCEL_*` and integration-managed vars, Supabase vault entries owned by Supabase), so scope to an allowlist or a naming convention, or keep a committed list of names the sync owns (the state file).
- **Also check:** GitHub Actions secrets and variables are separate lists (a `type` change from secret to config leaves the old kind behind), and Vercel has per-target copies.
- **Docs:** `docs/infrastructure/ENV_MANAGEMENT.md` and `.claude/rules/new-env-var.md` should say that removing a variable means running the prune, until it exists, deleting it by hand in each destination.

### Migrate to Tailwind CSS 4 _(Blocks 28 `npm audit` Findings)_
**Status:** Not started (added 2026-10-21)

The app is on Tailwind 3.4 (`config/tailwind.ts`, `@tailwind` directives in
`src/app/globals.css`). `npm audit` still reports 28 findings (3 moderate, 25 high) after
`npm audit fix`, and `npm audit fix --force` would clear most of them only by moving to
Tailwind 4. They trace to `braces` (via tailwindcss 3 → chokidar/fast-glob/micromatch, and
the `vercel` CLI's ts-morph chain) and `postcss-selector-parser` (via
`@tailwindcss/typography` and postcss-nested). All are denial-of-service advisories in
build/dev tooling that only processes our own source, so the real risk is low, but the
audit stays red until this lands. The `vercel` CLI chain may stay flagged either way.

`.claude/rules/ui-layout.md` already describes v4 features (`@theme`, built-in container queries), so the docs
are ahead of the code.

- [ ] Run `npx @tailwindcss/upgrade` on a branch: config moves into CSS (`@theme`,
      `@plugin`), `@tailwind` directives become `@import "tailwindcss"`, PostCSS uses
      `@tailwindcss/postcss`.
- [ ] Load `@tailwindcss/typography` through `@plugin`; check it still resolves.
- [ ] Review renamed utilities and changed defaults (border/ring colors, `shadow-sm`,
      `rounded-sm`) for visual changes.
- [ ] Check every page at widths either side of each breakpoint (see `.claude/rules/ui-layout.md`,
      Responsive Layout) and in dark mode.
- [ ] Confirm the supported-browser floor (Safari 16.4+, Chrome 111+, Firefox 128+) is
      acceptable.
- [ ] Re-run `npm audit` and record what remains.

### Un-pin CI Runners from ubuntu-24.04
**Status:** Pinned 2026-09-24, needs revisiting after 2026-11-19

`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `checkly.yml`, and `auto-merge.yml` all pin
`runs-on: ubuntu-24.04` instead of `ubuntu-latest`, added to avoid flaky CI during
GitHub's gradual, non-deterministic rollout of `ubuntu-latest` → Ubuntu 26.04 between
Oct 19 and Nov 19, 2026 (`actions/runner-images#14748`).

- [ ] After the rollout completes (~2026-11-19), deliberately test the `test-db` job
      (Docker/Supabase-based, most likely to be sensitive to the kernel/systemd bump)
      against `ubuntu-26.04` explicitly on a branch before switching the pin.
- [ ] Once verified, either switch `runs-on:` back to `ubuntu-latest` (now pointing at
      26.04) or pin directly to `ubuntu-26.04` — same tradeoff as today's choice.

### Re-introduce Test Parallelism
**Status:** Deliberately disabled (2026-09-22), needs a real fix

`config/vitest.mts` sets `fileParallelism: false` because test files running concurrently
against the shared local Supabase instance were racing each other (colliding fixture
names, cross-file data pollution) and causing spurious failures unrelated to real bugs.
Disabling parallelism fixed that but costs real time -- the full suite is already at
~100s and creeping toward the 2-minute mark as more tests are added, which matters once
this suite is gating CI on every PR.

**Real fix options to investigate:**
- [ ] Wrap each test in a transaction that rolls back at teardown, so parallel tests never
      see each other's writes (the standard pattern for this class of problem)
- [ ] Give each parallel worker its own isolated schema/database instead of sharing one
- [ ] Audit remaining shared-fixture-name collisions (e.g. generic "Test Member" style
      names reused across files) and make them unique per file/run as a cheaper partial fix

Don't just flip `fileParallelism` back to `true` without one of the above -- that's what
caused the pollution/flakiness in the first place.

### Re-check the providers' privacy certifications each year _(Needs Scheduling)_

The services section of `/privacy` says each provider is certified under the EU-U.S. Data Privacy Framework (and its UK extension) or that we rely on standard contractual clauses. That was checked on 2026-10-30 against the Data Privacy Framework list: every provider is active except Supabase, which relies on the standard clauses in its data processing addendum (incorporated into its terms, so nothing to sign). Certifications have to be renewed: Google's next due date is 2027-09-13, and Resend is marked "re-certification under review". Look them up again (dataprivacyframework.gov, participant list) each autumn and whenever a provider is added, and reword the sentence if one lapses. Start: `src/app/privacy/page.tsx` (services section) and its test.

### Unmask more of Session Replay if recordings are too opaque _(Needs Decision)_

Replays keep Sentry's defaults (all text, form fields and media masked) except the member and admin navigation and the tab bars, which carry `data-sentry-unmask` (`src/instrumentation-client.ts`, `src/components/Tabs.tsx`, `src/components/MemberNavigation.tsx`, `src/app/(admin)/admin/AdminNavigation.tsx`). That shows where someone went, not what a screen said. Deferred until someone has watched real recordings and knows what is missing. Add the attribute to specific components, never a blanket `unmask` selector: headings and buttons can hold a member's name (the user menu button does), and chat, notes and profile text must stay masked. Whatever is unmasked stays true to the "text and form fields masked" wording on `/privacy`, so reword it if the approach changes.

### Read the rollups for ranges past 13 months _(Due before late 2027)_

Usage events older than 13 months are removed once rolled up (`analytics_rollups`, `src/lib/retention/usage-events.ts`), but the admin analytics pages and their SQL functions (`analytics_funnel`, `analytics_event_summary`, `analytics_top_clicks`, `analytics_notification_rates` and the rest) read `analytics_events` only. The first events reach 13 months in late 2027 (the oldest are from 2026-10-20); from then a range that starts earlier would silently show only the last 13 months. Before then: have those reports read `analytics_rollups` (total, route, control and action dimensions by day, week or month) for the part of a range older than the cutoff, say on the page where a figure comes from rollups (no per-visit funnels from them), and decide whether any report that needs combinations (route by screen size) justifies a combination set with a minimum cell size.

### Raw browser strings in push subscriptions and feedback _(Needs Decision)_

The nightly retention job (`/api/reconcile/retention`) removes the raw IP and browser string from the visit log, the usage log and course viewing after 90 days, which is what the `/privacy` row says. Two other places keep a raw user agent and are deliberately not covered: `push_subscriptions.user_agent` (the device label in Settings > Notifications, kept while the subscription is) and the user agent a member's feedback submission carries (`src/app/api/feedback/route.ts`, for support to reproduce a problem). Decide whether to store only the parsed device (`src/lib/device.ts`) in the first and remove the second after 90 days, or leave both and name them on the page.

**Status:** Ideas from the coverage work (PR #77); none started

Context: API route tests run in-process through `apiFetch` (`tests/helpers/api-fetch.ts`) so
`src/app/api` coverage counts, and per-area floors on the combined unit + db numbers live in
`AREA_FLOORS` (`scripts/coverage-report.ts`). Combined, 2026-10-31: lib ~96% lines, components
~90%, `src/app/api` ~85% (73% branches). `src/app/(member)` and `src/app/(admin)` were ~56% and ~19% on
2026-10-14 and haven't been re-measured.

- [ ] **Floors for `src/app/(member)` and `src/app/(admin)`.** Neither has one; `src/app/(admin)` is the
      biggest gap in the repo. Add the floors once a round of page tests lifts them.
- [ ] **Ratchet the floors.** `AREA_FLOORS` sits ~2 points under the baseline and only moves
      by hand. Have the coverage report note when an area is more than ~3 points over its
      floor, so floors follow the numbers up.
- [ ] **Cover the weakest `src/app/api` routes.** The coverage report's per-file data shows which
      handlers have the most uncovered statements and branches; start with the biggest.
- [ ] **Move the ~32 direct-import route tests onto `apiFetch`.** They call the handlers
      themselves and mock `next/headers` per file. One shared helper removes the mocks.
- [ ] **Audit fire-and-forget mocks for the race fixed in `slack-message-subtypes.test.ts`.**
      A mocked `after()` that does `void callback()` can land in the next test after its
      `vi.clearAllMocks()`. Grep the tests for `after:` mocks and `void` callbacks; make them
      collect their promises and have the test await them.
- [ ] **Automate `pnpm github:config:push`.** It runs by hand because `GITHUB_TOKEN` can't
      manage rulesets. Worth a CI job with an admin-token secret only if the ruleset changes
      often; at minimum a scheduled `github:config:diff` to flag drift.
- [ ] **Clear the remaining uncleared `setTimeout`s.** An uncleared timer that fires after its
      component unmounts sets state on a torn-down DOM; in a test that's an unhandled
      `window is not defined` that fails a run in which every test passed, only on slow runs. #97
      fixed the dropdown-close timer in four search boxes (`src/lib/hooks/useBlurDelay.ts`). Left, same
