      fits), and the "Saved" / "Copied" flashes in `HostVibePanel` (`setSaved`), `CalendarSyncCard`
      (`setCopied`) and `wheel-of-wonder/Wheel.tsx` (`setJustRevealed`, `setStarterCopied`). Keep the
      timer id in a ref and clear it in an unmount effect, and add a test that triggers it, unmounts
      and expects `vi.getTimerCount()` to be 0 (see the blur tests in
      `tests/components/EntitySearch.test.tsx`). Then grep `src/components/` and `src/app/` for any other
      `setTimeout(` that sets state without a cleanup; `SearchPalette` (copied), the hygiene
      buttons, `MatchingGame` and `AliasSearchForm` also use one and weren't checked.

### Support Preview Environments
**Status:** Not started — needs scoping

Right now every merge goes straight to `main`; PR-based Vercel preview deployments have
never really been used (confirmed: `vercel.json` has no preview-specific config, and
there's no workflow that opens PRs instead of merging worktree branches directly). Making
previews actually useful means:

1. [ ] Make Vercel preview deployments work properly with a real PR workflow (currently
       blocked on habit/workflow more than tooling -- previews already happen automatically
       on any branch push once PRs are actually used).
2. [ ] Fix all secrets/env vars for the preview environment, and **disable anything that
       causes real-world side effects from a preview**: outbound Slack messages, Kajabi/Zoom
       API calls, billing-triggering webhooks, email sends, cron jobs. A preview should never
       be able to notify a real user or incur a real charge.
3. [ ] Evaluate [Supabase branching](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/deployment/branching)
       (native per-PR ephemeral DB branches) as the mechanism, instead of the single static
       `.env.preview` Supabase project previews currently point to. Note: that existing devel
       project has no migration-push path today at all (`supabase/config.toml` only has
       `[remotes.prod]`, and `package.json` only has `db:push` targeting `.env.prod`) --
       worth confirming whether Supabase branching solves this automatically or whether it's
       a separate gap either way.
4. [ ] Run Checkly browser checks against each preview URL, including key flows like login --
       this is the "deploy-check" capability discussed when Checkly was first set up, deferred
       specifically pending this preview-environment work landing first.

### Support a Long-Lived Devel Environment
**Status:** Not started — needs scoping

A persistent (not per-PR-ephemeral) staging environment, sitting between preview and
production:

1. [ ] Add a persistent Supabase branch for devel (see Supabase branching link above --
       distinct from today's single static `.env.preview` project, if branching replaces it).
2. [ ] Same secrets/notification-disabling work as preview environments above -- no real
       Slack/Kajabi/Zoom/billing side effects from devel either.
3. [ ] Clarify the relationship to Supabase DB branching from the preview-environments work
       above -- likely the same underlying mechanism, but confirm rather than assume.
4. [ ] Checkly checks against the devel branch too (same browser-check coverage as preview).
5. [ ] Update the git workflow and CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`) to a real promotion
       pipeline: deploy to devel first, run tests, a bake/soak period, then promote to
       production -- rather than today's single `main`-branch-direct-to-prod pipeline.

### Mux configuration as code (`mux:config`) _(follow-ups)_

**Still open:**
- **Enforce the restriction: on for everyone since 2026-11-16 04:07 UTC.** **Resolved:** the id is set in production, it was checked in a real browser (desktop and iOS, Chrome and Safari), and the global switch is on. The first weeks are the watch: Mux Data's Errors page and the staff alert (which now carries the HTTP status and a `restricted` flag) show a failure with its cause, and turning `mux_playback_restriction` off reverts tokens at once. **Still open:** (1) once a few clean weeks have passed, retire the flag as was done for `mux_data` (delete the flag key from `src/lib/features.ts` and the row, make the id the only switch, or drop the id to switch it off); (2) the nightly check's own "checks" environment could get a restriction so it proves enforcement; (3) preview and local use the Local Dev Mux environment, which has no restriction unless one is created with `pnpm mux:config:push:dev`.
- **Checks environment (the nightly stream check).** Today only the always-on webhook check runs nightly (an unsigned POST must answer 401); the two stream checks (a signed link must get a manifest, a request without a link must get 403) are not declared until `MUX_CHECK_SIGNING_KEY_ID`, `MUX_CHECK_SIGNING_KEY_PRIVATE` and `MUX_CHECK_PLAYBACK_ID` exist. **Why deferred:** it needs the owner's Mux account (a separate "checks" Mux environment with its own signing key and a few-second test video that has a signed playback policy) and then `pnpm env:sync:github --only=MUX_CHECK_SIGNING_KEY_ID,MUX_CHECK_SIGNING_KEY_PRIVATE,MUX_CHECK_PLAYBACK_ID` and `gh workflow run checkly.yml --ref main`; the steps are in `docs/infrastructure/MUX_CONFIG.md`. **Also blocked for now:** the Checkly deploy has been failing with "You've reached your Uptime Monitors limit" on some commits (the account limit; raise it or remove monitors), so a new check cannot go live until that is settled. **To make it prove the restriction** too: create a restriction in that environment (`mux:config` against it) and make `config/checkly/__checks__/mux-token.ts` add the same `playback_restriction_id` claim, then add a check that a request with a bad `Referer` gets 403.
- **Local Dev Mux environment: its own restriction.** Preview deployments and local development use the "Hedgie Hub - Local Dev" Mux environment, which has no playback restriction, so `MUX_PLAYBACK_RESTRICTION_ID` is unset there and tokens carry no claim: previews do not exercise the path production now runs. **To do:** with that environment's System token in `.env.local` (one exists, created for CI), run `pnpm mux:config:push:dev` to create the restriction (never run against Local Dev yet: if its token cannot create one, create it through the Mux API by hand), put the id it prints in `.env.local` and `.env.preview`, then `pnpm env:sync --only=MUX_PLAYBACK_RESTRICTION_ID --target=preview`. That environment's only webhook is a developer's tunnel: `mux:config` recognises tunnel addresses and refuses to adopt one (`docs/infrastructure/MUX_CONFIG.md`). Low priority: it only matters for testing the restriction on a preview.
- **Read the production webhook.** Listing webhooks needs a System-permission token (`MUX_SYSTEM_TOKEN_ID/SECRET` in `.env.prod`), so the first diff couldn't confirm the webhook; the restriction part was compared with the app token.
- **Webhook delivery check.** The nightly check can't prove Mux reaches us; that needs a throwaway asset created in the environment each run (a few cents a month) and polling the `videos` row, or a Mux "send test event" if one appears in the API.
- **Signing keys** are created by hand (the private half is shown once); `/system/v1/signing-keys` could list them for a drift check.
- **Mux Data on the other players, and an admin link.** **Resolved:** the member lesson player, the chat video card (`/api/chat/videos/<message id>` returns the consent facts) and the admin lesson builder's player report to Mux Data (`mux_data` flag, `docs/infrastructure/MUX_CONFIG.md`). **Still open:** the webinar archive and any other `MuxVideo` could add `MuxDataMonitor` the same way (with the same consent rule). The staff alert only links the Mux dashboard's root; a deep link to the environment's Errors page would need the environment id, which isn't kept anywhere. **Verified 2026-11-15:** views of production lessons, the chat card and the admin builder appear in the production Mux environment with no `envKey`, so none is needed. The three players report under their own names (`PlayerSurface`, `src/lib/mux-data.ts`).
- **Link a Mux Data view to our own events (undecided; today they match only by playback id and time).** A `course_video_error` event and its #hub-alerts post carry our analytics `session_id`, `lesson_id`, `playback_id` and a time; a Mux view has its own id, `page_url`, `player_error_code` and `custom_1`...`custom_10`, so finding the view for an alert is a search by time. Mux Data is anonymous on purpose (`src/lib/mux-data.ts`: no `viewer_user_id`, cookies off; `/privacy` and `docs/product/ANALYTICS.md` say "no account id"). Options, from least to most identifying: (1) **a fresh random id per page view** as `view_session_id` or `custom_1`, kept in our own event, so an alert links to one exact view and nothing links views together; (2) **our analytics session id** (resets after an idle gap), which ties one visit's views together; (3) **a stable per-member id** (opaque or salted hash) as `viewer_user_id`, which gives per-member patterns and support follow-up but makes the data personal data at Mux, so `/privacy`, the changelog and `docs/infrastructure/MUX_CONFIG.md` change and a removal is a manual email to Mux. Findings from 2026-11-15 (Mux docs): no documented way to edit or delete a stored view, so "scrub the id but keep the view" isn't offered (the Data API has list/get only; `viewerData` can't be updated after monitoring starts); erasure is by email to Mux's GDPR address and removes whole views, and Mux keeps view data up to 100 days. So with 1 or 2, "scrub on request" means deleting or anonymizing our own rows for that id, which leaves the Mux view unlabeled. Where to start: `MuxDataMonitor` in `src/components/courses/LessonPlayer.tsx` (the metadata is built in `muxDataMetadata`), the failure report in `LessonPlayer` (`report`) and `src/lib/operations-alerts.ts` for a link in the alert. Deferred because it is a privacy decision for the owner, not a code question.

### Google Calendar push channel: create and renew it _(follow-ups)_


**Still open:**
- Updates (new, renamed or moved events) are only kept inside the watched window, 90 days back to 90 days ahead, the same as the nightly import; only deletions look further. An edit to an older or further-out event is ignored until the window is widened. Decide the window before relying on far-future scheduling.
- A push sync logs only its summary (`Calendar sync from webhook`). Log how many events Google returned and the timing of each step, so a missing event can be told apart from a slow sync without guessing.
- An event created on the wrong Google calendar (a personal one) is silently never synced. The admin could be shown the newest event Google returned and when, on `/admin/data-health`, so it is clear whether the sync saw it.
- `updatedMin` with `singleEvents` is assumed to surface every instance of an edited recurring series; check against real edits and fall back to Google's `syncToken` if instances are missed. The nightly full sync covers any miss.
- Record each webhook-triggered sync as a gap-fill per "On-Demand Incremental Fetch", and show channel age/expiry on `/admin/data-health`.
- The channel renews only once a day; if Google ends it early (rare) edits wait for the next 02:00 UTC run. A second, more frequent renewal check would close that.

### Move Quill & Cup DNS to Cloudflare _(Needs Scoping)_
The domain is registered at Squarespace, but its DNS records are currently managed at Kajabi. Move DNS to Cloudflare so web and email traffic can be treated differently:

- **Web** (`hub.quillandcup.com` and any other site hostnames): orange cloud (proxied) for Cloudflare's CDN/WAF/DDoS layer. Vercel has its own edge network and documents caveats for proxying behind another CDN (SSL mode must be Full (strict), and Vercel's own caching/analytics see Cloudflare IPs), so confirm that trade-off before flipping the proxy on rather than assuming it's a pure win.
- **Email** (Resend's DKIM/return-path records, MX, SPF/DKIM/DMARC TXT): grey cloud (DNS only). Mail records can't be proxied.
- **Registrar** can stay at Squarespace; only the nameservers change (from Kajabi's to Cloudflare's). Transferring the registration to Cloudflare is optional and separate.

**Before switching nameservers:**
- [ ] Inventory every existing record at Kajabi's DNS (site, forwarding MX, Kajabi, Slack, Zoom verification TXT, the Resend records, etc.) and recreate them at Cloudflare *before* the cutover, so nothing drops. Also check whether Squarespace holds any DNS records of its own.
- [ ] `support@quillandcup.com` is a Squarespace email-forwarding alias (to the team Google Group). Confirm where its MX records live and whether Squarespace forwarding keeps working once nameservers point at Cloudflare; if not, replace it with Cloudflare Email Routing (see next item).
- [ ] Lower TTLs ahead of the cutover; verify the Resend domain, Supabase auth emails, and OAuth callbacks after.

### Email Routing on Separate Subdomains _(Needs Scoping; decided 2026-11-03 to split by kind of mail)_
Everything sends today from `no-reply@hub.quillandcup.com` through Resend: Supabase Auth's emails (SMTP, `supabase/config.toml`), the notification emails (`EMAIL_FROM` in `config/app.ts`) and the launch emails. Footers point replies to the support address. **Decision:** split by kind of mail, because tracking and reputation are per domain in Resend, and the CRM wants clicks on notifications but must never track a sign-in email. Not built; this entry is the plan.

**Why now.** (1) Click tracking is meant to be on for the notification emails, but the switch is per domain, so it would apply to the sign-in emails too (it is not on yet: see "Where this stands"); Resend's guide for Supabase Auth emails says link tracking can corrupt verification links, and no setting on one shared domain can have tracking for notifications and none for auth. (2) Reputation is per domain: a spam-complaint spike from a newsletter must not delay a sign-in code. (3) Replies need a name that can carry an MX record, and `hub` is a CNAME to Vercel. It is cheaper before volume grows and before more links in sent mail depend on the sender.

**Where this stands (2026-11-06).** Resend's webhook is live and the nightly import has run (`docs/architecture/EMAIL_TIMELINE.md`), so sends, deliveries and bounces are on member timelines. **Click tracking is not on**: it was believed to be, but `resend domains list` (Resend CLI, read-only, key from `.env.prod`) shows `click_tracking: false` and `open_tracking: false` for `hub.quillandcup.com`, and a test email's Check in button was the plain Hub URL, so no clicks can arrive. The dashboard's "New tracking subdomain" form had not been completed. Open tracking is left out on purpose (see "Opens" under "Email timeline: what is left"). The domain also has `receiving: disabled`, which replies (`reply.`) will need.

**Enabling click tracking** (on `hub.` now, ahead of the split, or after `auth.` exists; the choice is the risk in the next paragraph):
1. Resend -> Domains -> `hub.quillandcup.com` -> Tracking -> New tracking subdomain: subdomain `links` (neutral; Resend says to avoid negative-sounding names such as "tracking"), click tracking checked, open tracking unchecked, Add domain.
2. Add the CNAME the DNS Record step shows (it will be `links.hub.quillandcup.com`) where the DNS lives (Kajabi until the Cloudflare move; DNS-only, not proxied, once on Cloudflare). It is a new name under `hub`, so it does not touch the `hub` record that points at Vercel. Resend rewrites links only after the record verifies.
3. Verify: `resend domains list` shows `click_tracking: true`; send a test notification (Admin -> Test Notifications), check that the Check in button points at the tracking subdomain, click it and look for a "Clicked a link in..." row on your Activity Log.
4. **Request a real sign-in email and use its link.** Click tracking applies to every email from the domain, Supabase's sign-in emails included, and Resend's guide for Supabase Auth emails says link tracking can corrupt verification links. If a sign-in link breaks, turn click tracking off and do step 2 of "Order of work" first.
5. Update `docs/infrastructure/WEBHOOK_SETUP.md`, step 4, with the subdomain used.

**The split**
| Subdomain | Mail | Tracking |
|---|---|---|
| `auth.` (or `login.`) | Supabase sign-in, invites, the launch emails | none of any kind |
| `hub.` | notifications the Hub sends (reminders, check-ins...) | click tracking on; opens off for now |
| `news.` | newsletters and broadcasts (Resend topics and unsubscribe lists would live here) | clicks and opens, if wanted |
| `reply.` | inbound only (MX), for replies: "Replies (phase 2)" under "Email timeline: what is left" | n/a |

**Order of work**
1. **Check the Resend plan's domain limit first.** The notes from the launch say the free plan is in use; the free plan allows one domain as far as we know (unverified), and this needs three or four. An upgrade may be part of the cost.
2. **`auth.` first**, since it fixes the sign-in link risk without waiting on the rest. Add and verify the domain in Resend (DKIM, SPF and return-path records), tracking off; change the sender in `supabase/config.toml` (`[remotes.prod.auth.email.smtp]`, pushed by CI's "Push Supabase config" step after migrations; run `pnpm config:diff` first, as `.claude/rules/config-as-code.md` says); send a real sign-in email and use its link.
3. **Notifications**: give each notification kind in `src/lib/notifications/registry.ts` a stream that picks its sender, instead of the single `EMAIL_FROM`; keep `hub.` for the notification stream. Per-kind unsubscribe links are unaffected (they point at the app, not the sender).
4. **`news.` and `reply.`** when newsletters and replies are built. Replies also need `Reply-To` on Hub email and, for Supabase's emails, the Send Email hook.
5. **DMARC**: publish a record on the root `quillandcup.com` (subdomains inherit it) before or with the first new domain.
6. **DNS**: the records live at Kajabi today and move in the Cloudflare cutover ("Move DNS to Cloudflare" above). Do the Resend records as part of that move, DNS-only (grey cloud), rather than twice. Each new domain's records are in Resend after adding it.

**Open**
- Whether Supabase's emails can be told apart from ours once they come from `auth.` (they can: the sender address is the stream), which also lets the timeline treat them differently ("Auth and launch email tags" under "Email timeline: what is left").
- Warm-up: a new subdomain starts with no reputation. Volume here is small, so send real mail through it for a few days before relying on it.
- Update `docs/architecture/EMAIL_TIMELINE.md` and `docs/infrastructure/WEBHOOK_SETUP.md` (the webhook covers every domain on the account, so nothing changes there) when it is built.

### Clean Up set-state-in-effect Warnings
**Still open:** `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` is still `warn` in `config/eslint.mjs`, with ~47 sites in ~30 files (run `pnpm lint`). They fall in three groups: fetch-on-mount helpers that call `setLoading(true)` and are also reused after a save (admin `*Client.tsx` pages), reading `localStorage`/`matchMedia`/`window` after hydration (`ThemeSwitcher`, `ConsentBanner`, `InstallAppPrompt`, navigation components), and resetting state when a prop changes (`MergeMemberModal`, `CalendarWeekView`). Deferred because each fix changes when a render happens (`useSyncExternalStore`, keyed children, or a data-fetching library) and needs its own component test. Start with the hydration group using `useSyncExternalStore`, then turn the rule back to `error`. Also check `next dev` and Sentry source maps on the next Vercel preview.

### Data Access Layer: Repositories, Typed Clients, Typed Seed Data _(In Progress)_
Goals: isolate database access so other layers are tested against in-memory fakes (less DB in CI), a clear object model, compile-time column and enum checks, and typed seed profiles for tests, demos and docs. Decision: **repositories on the existing Supabase client with generated types (Option A)**, migrated area by area; Drizzle/Kysely only later, for queries PostgREST handles badly. A direct Postgres connection bypasses RLS and would need a wrapper that sets `request.jwt.claims`, the role and the sudo setting for every user-scoped query (or `audit_row_change` records `system`), so it isn't worth it for the layering goals.


**Still open:**
- [ ] **Type the attendance components**: `PrickleDetails`, `MemberAttendanceView`, `MemberCalendarClient` and the community-stats code take `any[]` / snake_case rows. `src/lib/attendance-view.ts` maps the repository's domain objects to those rows at the page boundary; give each component the domain type (`Attendee`, `AttendedPrickle`) and delete the matching adapter.
- [ ] **Course Silver and Local tables behind a repository.** **Still open:** the device-retention job (`src/lib/retention/network-details.ts`) reads and clears `video_watch_segments` through a table-name variable the guard cannot see, so it still queries the table directly until retention gets its own repository. **Still open:** the import's other Bronze tables (`slack_channels`, `slack_messages`, `slack_reactions`) still use that helper until they get repositories.
- [ ] **The chat tables (~53 queries in 21 files)**: `chat_channels` (8), `chat_channel_members` (11), `chat_messages` (20), `chat_message_contents` (5), `chat_reactions` (2), `chat_slack_authors` (5), `chat_gap_fills` (2). They are bigger than the earlier aggregates because of rules that are easy to break in a move: RLS gates a message's text per channel (`chat_message_contents` is its own table so RLS can hide it; reads run as the caller, writes as the service role), Slack bridging has its own state (`bridge_mode`, `bridge_disabled`, `bridge_status`), several flows go through functions (`chat_post_message`, `search_chat_messages`, `project_slack_chat_channels`), and read state is pulled from Slack (`src/modules/chat/slack/read.ts`). Plan: **(1)** a skeleton PR (`ChatRepository` interface in `src/lib/data/chat.ts`, Supabase and in-memory classes, dataset arrays, `createRepos`, and the seven tables in the guard test with every file that queries them; one real method to prove the shape, e.g. the channel list `src/lib/chat/load.ts` builds). **(2)** Four agents, one per slice, each adding a labelled section: *channels and membership* (`chat_channels`, `chat_channel_members`: `src/lib/chat/load.ts` channel/member reads, `membership-actions.ts`, `chat/actions.ts`, `chat/[id]/page.tsx`, `chat/[id]/members/page.tsx`, the channel parts of `slack-read.ts`/`gap-fill.ts`); *message reads* (`chat_messages`, `chat_reactions` reads: `load.ts`, `search.ts`, the `search_chat_messages` call as a typed wrapper, `src/components/chat/ChatLive.tsx` (a client component: its reads can take the browser client, the realtime subscription itself stays out of the repository)); *message writes* (`slack-post.ts`, `slack-edit.ts`, `slack-react.ts`, `chat/actions.ts` writes, `api/webhooks/slack/route.ts`, `chat_post_message` as a typed wrapper); *content, files, authors and gap fills* (`chat_message_contents`: the two file routes, `file-cleanup.ts`, `slack-upload.ts`; `chat_slack_authors`: `names.ts`, `slack-membership.ts`; `chat_gap_fills`: `gap-fill.ts` and its admin page). **(3)** Rules for all four: the repository takes whichever client the caller has (the privilege is the client's), so a content read stays service-role where it is today; no behaviour change, including which reads swallow errors; the RLS behaviour is covered by the existing pgTAP tests, the repository contract runs as the service role and checks the data rules; external calls (Slack API, `pullReadFromSlack`) stay outside the repository. Merge order: skeleton, then channels (the others join through channel ids), then the rest in any order. Expect the message-reads and writes slices to share helper methods; give them one owner for `chat_messages` row shapes (a `ChatMessageRow` in the skeleton) to avoid two near-duplicate types.
- [ ] **Typed seed profiles**: **Still open:** the demo still writes calendar events and Zoom rows itself and derives prickles and attendance with the app's own processing (on purpose: that is the pipeline under test; a profile-driven Bronze writer would let the profile be the only source); a course-in-progress profile (needs `SeedProfile` fields for courses, lessons and progress); and member-facing help articles that cite the profile instead of repeating names.
- [ ] **Direct SQL path (Drizzle/Kysely)**: revisit when a repository needs joins, CTEs or window functions that an RPC makes awkward. Try it on one read-only service-role route first, with `prepare: false` behind the pooler.

(2) Script-run modules with relative imports that are tested on the real database: `src/lib/kajabi/{lesson-content,course-hero,contact-refresh}.ts`. (3) Composition-root helpers in routes and actions (`src/app/api/webhooks/slack/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/members/[id]/route.ts`, `src/app/(member)/settings/profileActions.ts`, `prickle-picker/actions.ts`, `my-prickles/commitment-actions.ts`), which build their repositories once and are fine as they are. About 17 test files still use `createFakeSupabase`/`useFakeSupabase`: pages that read through an RPC (`member-directory`, `admin-network`, `admin-analytics`, `chat-pages`, `chat-search`), the settings and push-device actions (`NotificationsPanel`, `settings-routes`), `onboarding-server`, `member-profile-page`, `admin-member-detail-page`, `unflagged-pages`, `admin-calendar-page`, `chat-actions`, plus two repository-level tests (`events-member-reads`, `notification-preferences-chunks`) that fake the client on purpose, because they test the Supabase repository classes.
- [ ] **Second consolidation pass over the large repository interfaces** _(Needs Scheduling)_. The first pass (2026-10-31/11-01: chat 47 to 44, writing 47 to 42, courses 37 to 35, kajabi-courses 35 to 32, billing 28 to 26, prickles unchanged) was too cautious: agents left pairs alone on grounds such as "different column list" or "has an inner join" without checking that it changes anything for the real callers. `entriesForPrickle` vs `prickleEntries` in writing was one such pair: same filter and order, the second adds columns and an inner join to `writing_projects`, and the join cannot drop a row because `project_id` is `NOT NULL ... ON DELETE CASCADE` and both callers run as the owning member; it merged afterwards. Interface sizes now: chat 44, writing 41, courses 35 (and course progress 19), prickles 47, billing 26, kajabi-courses 32, members 33, events 32, zoom 30, aliases 29; none was audited under the stricter bar below except as noted. Do it as one agent per repository (a workflow works well), then a skeptic pass, a PR each, pure refactors:
  - **Re-audit the first six** (chat, writing, courses and course progress, prickles, billing, kajabi-courses) and **audit the next four** (members, events, zoom, aliases; `src/lib/data/<name>.ts`).
  - **A pair may be left alone only with evidence**, written in the PR: the exact rows or behaviour that would differ for the actual callers. Check the schema (foreign keys, NOT NULL, row-level security, soft-delete filters) and the callers, not just the SQL text. "Different columns" or "has a join" alone is not evidence.
  - **Cheap extra columns or a primary-key join over a small result do not block a merge:** merge to the larger shape and let the caller read what it needs. Keep separate: wide text columns over large result sets (Slack message bodies, lesson bodies), hot paths, and anything where the larger query changes a plan the callers depend on.
  - **Behaviour-bearing differences stay separate:** fail-closed authorization, atomic claims and leases, single-use token consumption, soft-delete rules, and the privilege of the client the caller passes.
  - **Mechanisms:** options objects with optional filters (`{ projectIds }`, `{ limit }`, `{ past }`), list-taking methods with the single-item call a one-liner at the caller; delete methods with no callers outside tests. No generic `query(anything)`, no column strings or raw PostgREST filters from callers, no union return types that force narrowing.
  - **Skeptic pass:** after each audit, a second agent gets only each "left alone" pair, its callers and the schema, and tries to show the merge is safe; only pairs it cannot merge stay separate. Check, for example, `liveLessons`/`liveLessonOutlines` and the `*Rows` readers in billing, the course access/grant/progress readers, `activeBetween`/`listedInEventsActiveBetween` and `byIds`/`byCalendarEventIds`/`byZoomMeetings` in prickles.
  - **Gates and approach:** the same contract suite must pass on the twin and the database (a case may move, not disappear); no behaviour change (a bug found goes to this file, not into the PR); expect about 1.2M tokens for ten repositories with the skeptic pass. Each agent's brief is the bullets above plus: touch only its repository's file, contract suite and the callers of the methods it changes; rebase on `main` and keep both sides on conflict; a PR each, never stacked.
- [ ] **Type boundary follow-ups in `src/lib/data`** _(small)_. `src/lib/data` has no `as unknown as` left (176 on 2026-10-29) and `tests/lib/data-casts.test.ts` caps the remaining casts at 3, each commented. Still open: (1) the guard does not cover the 23 `.overrideTypes<...>()` calls, which are as silent as a cast when wrong and are only commented by convention (`attendance.ts` has one with no comment): count them in `data-casts.test.ts`, require a reason, add a ceiling that only goes down. (2) About 13 of them repeat one assertion per view (`community_event_details` in `events.ts`, `slack_messages_meta` in `slack-messages.ts`; Postgres views come out all-nullable in the generated types): give each view one typed accessor so the assertion lives once. (3) Three runtime select strings in `attendance.ts` could be static so the client infers the row. (4) The remaining casts: `SlackFile.raw_payload` typed `any` could be `Json` with a runtime narrow where `src/lib/slack-capture.ts` reads it; the two `as never` are low value. (5) Text columns held to a union by a check constraint (some `chat_messages` columns and a few others) are typed by hand; a Postgres enum or domain type would let the generator type them, but that is a migration under expand-then-contract (`.claude/rules/migrations.md`), so only for a column that has caused a bug. Start in `tests/lib/data-casts.test.ts`.
- [ ] **CI: what is left after the 2026-11-01 profile** _(Needs Scheduling)_. **Measured, not fixed:** (1) the `Test (unit)` step went from about 272s to about 382s on `main` at the run for #783 and stayed there (one run at 299s); every Vitest phase grew together (transform x1.25, setup x1.7, import x1.5, tests x1.6, environment x1.75) with the same test count, and the same coverage command takes about 58s locally before and after #783 and today, so it looks like a slower runner, not our code; worth re-measuring before acting, and rechecking whether jobs landed on a different pool. (2) `supabase start` in the db job is about 96-117s, the largest single step; `-x realtime` did not help; try caching the images, a smaller stack, or starting it while the build runs (check what the db tests need first). (3) The unit suite's CPU is mostly jsdom: 306 of 768 files carry the DOM environment and account for about 206 of 237 summed test-seconds (environment alone is 160-280s summed); the slowest files are `tests/components/EmojiPicker.test.tsx` (19s), `EmojiPickerKeyboardTones.test.tsx` (13s) and `tests/lib/outcome-wiring.test.ts` (7s). Levers: component tests that only check logic can be plain unit tests, and the heaviest could run in a separate non-required job (never rename or drop a required check, see `config/github.yml`). (4) `pool: threads` broke the timezone tests and was not faster. Not a lever: loading the repository graph costs about 0.27s per worker, so the twin-based tests are cheap (186 files, 6s of test time). The db suite did get faster (195s to about 132s) because logic tests moved onto the in-memory twin; converting the 17 remaining fake-chain tests (see "Convert the last `supabase`-taking functions") continues that.
- [ ] **Guard agent-made PRs against reverting unrelated files** _(small)_. #728 (a data-layer PR cut before #725 and #726 merged) came out of conflict resolution with its own side of files it never meant to touch: it deleted four repository files and put back the old Squawk config, the old CI job name and the old required-check name in `config/github.yml`. `main` ran without #726's repositories until #738 restored them, and nothing failed (the guard test still passed, because #728's version listed those tables as unmigrated again). Mitigation so far is a manual check of each PR's file list. Options: a CI step that fails a PR whose diff against its merge base removes or reverts lines from files that the base branch changed since the branch point (compare `git diff base...head` with `git diff head base` on the files both touched), or a required check that flags a data-layer PR touching `.github/`, `config/squawk.toml` or `config/github.yml`. Also: agents that cannot force-push merge `main` into the branch, which is fine, but the conflict resolution is where this happened, so review those merge commits. Remote branches `data-layer-slack-bronze-2` and `-3` were left behind (stacked PR that auto-merged into the first one); the user prunes them (`pnpm branches:prune --remote`).

### Faster `main` CI: Serialize Only the Deploy Jobs _(Needs Scheduling)_
Runs of `ci.yml` on `main` queue one behind another: `concurrency` at the top of the workflow is `ci-<workflow>-<ref>` with `cancel-in-progress` false on `main`, so a merge waits for the whole previous run (tests included, ~10-15 min) before its own tests start. Two merges in quick succession (for example a fix right after a broken merge) can leave the second waiting 10+ minutes. Deferred because the group is what keeps migrations, the deploy and the Slack manifest token rotation from overlapping, and a mistake in this change breaks production deploys.

- [ ] Take the run-level `concurrency` off `main` (keep `cancel-in-progress` for pull requests), so tests, build and coverage for different commits run in parallel.
- [ ] Put a job-level `concurrency: { group: prod-deploy, cancel-in-progress: false }` on `push-migrations`, `deploy`, `push-slack-manifest` and the Vercel config push, so only one touches production at a time and none is cancelled mid-flight. GitHub keeps one pending job per group and replaces older pending ones, which is fine: the newest commit contains the older ones' migrations and code.
- [ ] Guard against out-of-order deploys: with parallel tests an older commit can finish after a newer one. Each production job first checks that `$GITHUB_SHA` is still the tip of `main` (`git fetch origin main`, then compare with `git rev-parse origin/main`, or `git merge-base --is-ancestor`) and skips otherwise.
- Check first: the Free plan caps runners at 20 concurrent jobs per account, so parallel runs compete with PR runs and CodeQL for slots and the gain shrinks when busy. `Check migrations` stays outside the deploy group, unchanged. Roll out in its own PR and watch the next two `main` runs (`ship-via-pr.md` step 7). Related and cheaper: run CodeQL only on `main` plus a weekly schedule instead of every PR and push.


### Trim CodeQL Runs _(Needs Scheduling)_
CodeQL is on GitHub's default setup (`gh api repos/quillandcup/hub/code-scanning/default-setup`: languages `actions` and `javascript-typescript`, weekly schedule), which scans every pull request and every push to `main`. Those runs share the Free plan's 20-concurrent-job pool with CI, so during a burst of merges they compete with the required checks for runners (each run is short, about 1 to 10 minutes, so this is a small share next to CI). Deferred because it trades away per-PR scanning, which is a security decision, and because the default setup has no trigger options: narrowing them means replacing it with an advanced setup.

- [ ] Decide whether per-PR scans are worth the runner slots. Alternatives: scan only `main` plus the weekly schedule, or keep PR scans only for paths that change code (`src/app/`, `src/lib/`, `src/components/`, `.github/workflows/`) and skip docs and migrations-only PRs.
- [ ] To change triggers: disable the default setup (Settings -> Advanced Security -> Code scanning), add `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` (GitHub's template, `on: push` to `main`, optional `pull_request` with `paths`, and `schedule`), and keep both languages. Add a repo-config note if a ruleset ever requires the check. The `Analyze (...)` jobs aren't in the required checks today (`config/github.yml`), so removing them from PRs doesn't block merging.
- Related: `Faster main CI: Serialize Only the Deploy Jobs` above. Do that one first; it is the larger part of the queueing.

### Version skew: old tabs call server actions that no longer exist _(Resolved 2026-11-05, watching Sentry)_
- **What was wrong:** A browser tab opened before a deploy keeps the old build's JavaScript and server action ids and posts them to the new deployment, which answers `UnrecognizedActionError: Server Action "..." was not found on the server` (Sentry HUB-A and HUB-6).
- **Resolved:** The build sets a per-deploy `NEXT_DEPLOYMENT_ID` (`.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, #871), so pages carry `?dpl=dpl_...` and `data-dpl-id`, and Vercel's Skew Protection keeps serving an old tab from the deployment it was opened on. Checked 2026-11-04/05: asking `/login` for a deployment 2 to 4 hours old by `x-deployment-id` (or `?dpl=`) returns that deployment, and one older than the maximum age returns the live one; Next's `server-action-reducer.js` sets `x-deployment-id` on action requests, and a real signed-in tab's server-action POST carried the live deployment's id. Vercel counts the maximum age **from the deployment's creation** (docs: "one day from deployment creation"); the project had the old Pro cap of 12 hours (43200 s), which is why HUB-6 recurred from a tab open about 17 hours. It is now `skewProtection.maxAgeSeconds` in `config/vercel.yml` (7 days; production deployments are kept 30 days, the upper limit), applied by `scripts/vercel-config.ts` after the deploy that merges it. `AppUpdatedBar` (`src/components/announcements/AppUpdatedBar.tsx`, member and admin `AnnouncementStack`) tells a page 12 hours before its build's deadline (`src/lib/skew-deadline.ts`, from the build time and the max age that `next.config.ts` bakes in) to reload when done and submit its work first; if an action is refused anyway it says what happened and that what was typed is still on the page.
- **Still open:** nothing, unless HUB-6 reopens. Not built, on purpose for now: a `/api/version` route that would tell a tab whether a newer deployment exists (today the notice shows for any page old enough, even on a quiet week, which is rare with several deploys a week); first see whether HUB-6 recurs. If it does, check the max age took effect (`pnpm vercel:config:diff`) before anything else. Recovering what a member typed after a refused action is only "copy it, then reload"; a real fix would keep form drafts across a reload.

### Adopt Cache Components (`cacheComponents`) _(Needs Scoping, not urgent)_
Next 16 has a new caching model behind `cacheComponents` in `next.config.ts` (the `"use cache"` directive, `cacheTag`/`cacheLife`/`updateTag`, Partial Prerendering and Partial Prefetching); the docs say it becomes the default in the next major release. We are not on it. Chat's cross-request lookup cache (`src/lib/chat/lookup-cache.ts`) uses the older `unstable_cache`, which still works. Looked at while speeding up chat switching, and decided not to adopt it for that: (1) every one of the ~112 pages (and the layouts) reads the signed-in user from cookies, and under Cache Components request-time data outside a `<Suspense>` boundary fails the build, so most routes need restructuring, not a flag flip; (2) the 8 route segment config exports (`dynamic`, `revalidate`, `runtime`, find them with `grep -rnE "export const (dynamic|revalidate|runtime|fetchCache)" app`) must be migrated and `runtime` is unsupported; (3) the `unstable_cache` calls move to `"use cache"` (small); (4) the gain is a static shell plus partial prefetching, but our pages are per-user and database-bound (production chat renders spent 100-500ms per query, not rendering a shell), so it would not shrink the measured cost. Nobody has tried a build with it, so the size of (1) is an estimate. Start from the docs' "Migrating to Cache Components" guide (`node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/migrating-to-cache-components.md`) and a throwaway branch with the flag on, counting build errors, before scheduling it; revisit when the next Next major lands.

### Media library follow-ups

The media library foundation is in (`docs/product/MEDIA_LIBRARY.md`): table, bucket, repository, upload and serving routes. Left for the PRs that add its first users:

- **Analytics**: the picker and `/admin/media` record `media_picker_opened`, `media_picked`, `media_cleared`, `media_uploaded`, `media_edited`, `media_deleted` and `media_downloaded`. Each new surface (book covers) extends the `surface` list in `src/lib/analytics/events.ts` and `src/lib/media/surfaces.ts`.
- **`/admin/media` is staff only and shows the shared library.** Usage lists events, books and (unnamed) projects; each feature that gets an `*_asset_id` column adds itself to `MediaRepository.usages`.
- **Members' image control is built; staff's picker is still staff-only.** **Still open:** `GET`/`POST /api/media` and the staff library stay staff-only, which is right for the shared library; a member image for anything but a book cover needs its own `own`-style routes (see "Member uploads other than book covers").
- **Orphaned objects** (a file with no `media_assets` row, a `renditions/<id>/` folder for an id that no longer exists) are not swept: the nightly sweep (`src/lib/media/sweep.ts`) goes from rows to files, not the other way. They can only come from a failed insert after an upload or a hard delete in the database; list the `originals/` and `renditions/` folders against the table if the bucket ever grows unexplained.
- **A cover adopted while the member changed it is left unused in their library.** `adoptLegacyCovers` (`src/lib/media/adopt-covers.ts`) stores the downloaded image, then writes the cover only if the old link is still there; when the member changed the cover in between, the write is skipped but the new `media_assets` row stays, unreferenced, in that member's library. Harmless and rare (it needs a change during a run of the admin script), but nothing removes it; a follow-up could soft-delete the asset when the conditional write loses. Start in `adoptLegacyCovers` and `WritingRepository.adoptCover` / the books equivalent.
- **`WritingRepository.adoptCover` has no contract case.** The conditional cover write was added with the https-only `fetch-cover` change; the in-memory twin is unit tested and the Supabase class only through `tests/api/media/adopt-covers-db.test.ts`. Per the repository rule (a new method goes in the interface, both classes and the contract suite), add a case to the writing contract (`tests/helpers/`, run by `tests/lib/data/` and `tests/api/data/`): it writes when the old link matches and returns "not written" when it does not.
- **Deleted images can't be restored from the UI.** Within the 30 days before the sweep removes the files, staff can clear `deleted_at` in the database; `/admin/media` has no "deleted" view or undo.
- **Member uploads other than book covers.** `POST /api/media` and the picker are staff-only; only `POST /api/bookshelf/cover` takes a member's upload, as a multipart request up to 4 MB (a request body limit). Bigger covers would need the signed-URL path (`/api/media/uploads`) for members.
- **Making the Bookshelf public: resolved.** Each book has a level (`member_books.visibility`) and the **Hedgie Bookshelf** page block shows the books at the globe on any landing page (`docs/product/WRITER_PROFILES.md`, "The public Hedgie Bookshelf"). The `bookshelfPublic` switch is gone: a cover is served to visitors while a live book at the globe uses it. **Still open:** a dedicated public route and a redirect from the old `/hedgie-books` address wait for the root-host work (PRs "Public pages live on the root host" and "Serve the public site on the root host"); until then the admin builds the page with the block (`/p/<slug>`). A member's own books are not on their writer profile (`/w/<handle>`) yet.
- **Staff can't see a member's covers** in `/admin/media` (it lists the shared library); a cover shows up there only as a usage when a shared image is used by a book.
- **SVG** is refused. Worth accepting for logos on the marketing site (sanitized, served with a sandboxing CSP); see `docs/product/MEDIA_LIBRARY.md`, "Upload safety".
- **Videos and files in the catalog.** `media_assets.kind` allows `video` and `file` but only images are created. The course builder's migration relaxes the image-only NOT NULLs per kind and links `videos` (see "One catalog" in `docs/product/MEDIA_LIBRARY.md`).
- **Paperback and hardcover covers** need a full wrap (back, spine, front) with a spine width from page count and paper type: a cover template, not a crop. Out of scope until asked.
- **A free-form crop rectangle.** Crops are a preset size around a focal point (`Adjust crop` sets the point and, for covers, how a picture of another shape fits); there is no draw-a-rectangle crop. Deferred because one focal point fixes every shape at once (event card, hero, shelf, Amazon) and a rectangle fixes one. If someone needs exact framing, add an optional per-use crop stored as fractions (x, y, w, h) that overrides the focal point; the original stays untouched. Start in `src/lib/media/render.ts` (`settingsFor`) and `src/components/media/MediaEditor.tsx`.
- **The "Add event" form can't pick from the gallery.** The Event photos tab needs an existing event with imported photos, and a new event has none. Offering it there would mean creating the event first (or importing photos before saving), a different flow; the admin event page has it (`MediaPicker`'s `eventPhotos`).

### Intermittent failures in the full unit suite _(Needs Scheduling)_

Seen 2026-10-29 on clean `origin/main` (2 of 4 full `pnpm test:unit` runs on a laptop with the local Supabase stack running) and on feature branches; each file passes alone, so it shows only under full-suite load. Two causes so far:

- **`tests/components/pages/admin-calendar-data.test.tsx`** raises an unhandled `TypeError: The "event" argument must be an instance of Event` from undici's WebSocket (`fireEvent` in `undici/lib/web/websocket/util.js`): something it renders opens a real Supabase Realtime WebSocket (the prickles realtime change, `20270224300000_prickles_realtime.sql`) under jsdom, where jsdom's `Event` and Node's differ. The run fails even though every test passes. Start by mocking the realtime client in that test (or in the component's data hook) so a unit test never opens a socket, which also makes it independent of whether a local stack is up.
- **`tests/lib/commitments.test.ts`** and **`tests/lib/outcome-wiring.test.ts` (`saveCheckin reports checkin_saved`)** each failed once in a full run and passed alone and on re-run. Not diagnosed; check for real waits or shared state (`.claude/rules/testing-requirements.md`: tests use the fake clock, no real waits).

CI runs on a machine without the stack and hasn't shown this, so it is cheap to fix now and expensive to find once it starts failing there.

### Alerts that don't depend on the app being healthy

Every alert today (`src/lib/operations-alerts.ts`: failed DMs, `section_failed`, `client_crash`, cron problems) is sent by the app itself, through its own database (`rate_limit_hit`), Slack token and Resend. When the database is timing out or the deploy is broken, the alerting path is the thing that is broken, and a crash the app can't report stays silent. Sentry would be the independent path but its Slack integration needs the Team plan (about $26/month), which is more than this is worth. Deferred because it needs a choice of mechanism. Options to evaluate, none verified yet: a Sentry internal integration with a webhook on the alert rule action pointed at a Slack incoming webhook (Sentry's docs say rules can send to webhooks on the free plan; confirm, and that a payload Slack accepts needs a small formatter); Sentry's own email alerts on new issues (included in every plan, and independent of the app); Checkly, which already watches production (`config/checkly/__checks__/`), alerting `ALERTS_EMAIL` or Slack from outside the app on a failing check; a free serverless function as the Sentry-to-Slack formatter. **Still open:** decide whether Slack also needs an independent path (the options above), and keep the primary address in mind when the Sentry account changes hands. Then update `docs/infrastructure/SLACK_OPERATIONS_ALERTS.md`, which says the app posts its own alerts.

### Page sections that fail alone, and tracing beyond the member page

The admin member page loads about fifteen queries in one server render, so a single statement timeout (57014, seen once in production) blanks the whole page. The PR adding `src/lib/query-trace.ts` names the failing query in Sentry, logs sections over 2s and adds `error.tsx` pages with a digest, but the page still fails as a whole. Deferred because it needs a decision per panel about what an empty state should say. Start in `src/app/(admin)/admin/members/[id]/MemberDetailPage.tsx`: wrap the non-essential panels (Slack feed, activity feed, membership history) so an error shows "couldn't load" for that panel only. Then use `traced()` on the other heavy pages (`/admin/activity`, the member dashboard) and watch the `[slow section]` log lines for what to index.

### Client bundle check that doesn't depend on a list of packages

`tests/lib/client-server-boundary.test.ts` walks our own imports and flags a client file that reaches a "server-only" one, but what counts as server-only is a hand-written regex of package names (`SERVER_ONLY_PACKAGES` in `tests/helpers/client-server-boundary.ts`). A server SDK missing from it passes CI and breaks pages in the browser: #615 pulled `@slack/web-api` (and through it `node:fs`) into every page with the record-history button, and CI was green (fixed in #626; the list was extended afterwards). Deferred because the fix is a different kind of check, not another entry. Start by scanning the built client output instead: after `next build` (the `Test (db) & build` job already builds), fail if any file under `.next/static/chunks` references `node:fs`, `node:child_process`, `node:net` or other Node-only modules, which catches any package, direct or transitive, with no list to maintain. Keep the import walker for its readable chain ("A -> B -> C") and for the server-imports-client rule.

### Production database memory, and the jump from Small to Medium

Statement timeouts (57014) kept hitting unrelated queries in production: the admin member page's `count_activity_feed` (Sentry HUB-G), the calendar import and its reprocess, and the Kajabi reads. It was not a bad query. For the member in HUB-G the three branches of the count use their indexes and read from cache (about 1s in total when idle, against the 8s limit), and the function was already reworked for planning in `20270306100000`. The Supabase dashboard (Reports → Database, one hour on the day it was looked at) showed memory, not CPU or disk: total RAM 408 MB with 843 MB in swap (206% of RAM) and memory commitment marked SEVERE at 1.76 GB against a 1.4 GB capacity, while CPU was about 92% idle, disk IOPS about 12 of 3,000, connections 20 of 60 (PostgREST 11), and the database 0.37 GB. `shared_buffers` is 224 MB and `max_connections` is 60, the Nano profile. Anything that needs memory (a window sort, a big scan) waits on swap, and a heavy job overlapping an admin page tips it over the 8s statement timeout.

**Still open:**
- Read the dashboard's memory chart after a day of normal use: swap should be well below RAM and commitment out of SEVERE (the database CLI can't show swap). Swap should fall well below RAM and commitment leave SEVERE; if not, the load is heavier than it looks.
- **The next step up is a cliff.** Medium (4 GB) is about $59.18/month, four times Small, so memory use is worth keeping down before more members arrive. Start with what reads the most: the Kajabi bronze tables are read in full 20k+ times with `OFFSET` paging (`pg_stat_statements`: about 330 ms mean per page, on `bronze.kajabi_contacts` and `bronze.kajabi_purchases`; the stats have run since June, so means are blended), PostgREST's connection pool size, and imports and reprocessing that overlap each other and admin use. Compare `pg_stat_statements` (read-only, `supabase db query --linked --project-ref ...`) before and after to see what changed.
- **Compute size is not config as code.** `supabase/config.toml` has no key for it. The Management API can set it (`PATCH /v1/projects/{ref}/billing/addons` with `addon_type: "compute_instance"` and a variant such as `ci_small`, needing the `infra_add_ons_write` permission), like the "Enable leaked password protection" step in CI. A change restarts the database, so a step on every push would have to read the current size and only write when it differs; unverified whether re-applying the same variant is a no-op. A cheaper start is a read-only drift check that fails when the live size differs from a value in the repo. Not built because the size changes rarely; build it if it drifts or when a second environment appears.

### One feature manifest that drives tests, demos, help and the changelog, with a CI check that keeps them current _(Needs Scheduling)_

**What is missing.** Four things already describe the same feature separately and are tied together only by convention: the browser test and demo (a Playwright scene in `tests/demo/scenes/`, which doubles as an end-to-end test and takes the screenshots), the Hedgie Help article (`src/content/help/<slug>.md`, `src/lib/help.ts`), the changelog entries (`src/content/changelog.ts`, where a scene's file name must equal an entry's slug) and the feature flag (`src/lib/features.ts`, `feature_flags`). Nothing says "this feature has a scene, a help article and a changelog entry", so each can drift: a release goes out without an entry, a help article keeps describing an old screen, a flagged feature has no test. The goal is one record per feature that all four read, and a CI check that a change to a feature updates what members read about it, for improvements and bug fixes as well as new features (a fix needs a changelog entry even when no help article changes).

**Shape (a sketch, to be decided).** A manifest, for example `features/<id>.ts` or one `src/lib/features-manifest.ts`: `id`, `name`, the flag key if it has one, the routes and code paths it owns (`src/app/(member)/courses/**`), its scene file(s), its help article(s), and the changelog slugs it has (many, over time). Scenes and help articles name their feature; changelog entries gain an optional `feature` field. Help is written as **questions and answers** ("How do I check in late?"), one or several per feature, with screenshots reused from the scenes (today help images come from a separate capture set, `docs/product/DEMO_AND_HELP.md`).

**The CI check, in stages.**
1. *Consistency (blocking from day one):* every flag in `FEATURE_PREVIEWS` and every `@changelog`/help scene has a manifest entry; every file or slug a manifest entry names exists; every product entry's `feature` exists. Cheap and no false positives.
2. *Coverage:* every manifest feature has at least one scene that runs in CI (so it is an end-to-end test) and, if member-facing, at least one help question.
3. *Freshness (report-only first):* a pull request that changes files a feature owns must also add or edit a changelog entry for it, or say why not (a `Changelog: none (reason)` trailer in the PR, reviewed like `skip-patch-coverage`: never self-approved). Docs-only and pure-refactor PRs are exempt; an engineering entry satisfies it for non-member-visible changes. A scene that starts failing marks its help article as stale until it is looked at.

**Risks.**
- *Over-building:* a fifth thing to maintain. Mitigate by deriving what we can (scene and help names from the feature id), generating stubs for a new feature, and starting with stage 1 only.
- *The path map rots,* so the freshness check mislabels PRs. Keep ownership coarse (a directory per feature), review it when a feature moves, and run it report-only (a PR comment) for a few weeks to measure false positives before it blocks anything.
- *Friction and gaming:* a blocking check on every small fix invites a reflexive "none". Keep the opt-out visible and reviewed, count how often it is used, and make adding an entry fast (the reader-review skill and entry template exist).
- *Entries nobody reads:* a check can force an entry to exist, not to be good. The member reader review (`.claude/skills/member-reader-review`) and `.claude/rules/changelog-writing.md` still apply.
- *Flagged vs unflagged features and shared code* (a button component used by ten features) do not map one-to-one; decide how an unowned or shared path is treated before the check blocks.
- *Frozen vs live:* changelog screenshots are frozen at ship time and help screenshots should follow the current UI; the manifest must not merge the two into one image set.

**Where to start.** `docs/product/DEMO_AND_HELP.md` (scenes, help images), `docs/runbooks/CHANGELOG_PROCESS.md`, `src/lib/features.ts`, `src/lib/help.ts`, `tests/lib/` for the existing "every X has a Y" tests (`site-pages`, `staff-access`), and `scripts/docs-only.ts` for how a CI job decides what a change touched. Write a short design doc first (`docs/FEATURE_MANIFEST.md`), then stage 1.

**Related: the nightly changelog drafter.** A prototype exists (`pnpm changelog:draft`, `src/lib/changelog/draft.ts`, `docs/runbooks/CHANGELOG_PROCESS.md`, "Drafting entries"): it groups merged PRs into story drafts but guesses the grouping from PR titles and bodies. A feature manifest would make the grouping deterministic (PRs touching a feature's paths belong to its story), so build stage 1 first. **Still open:** `LIVE_FLAGS` (`src/lib/changelog/live-flags.ts`) starts empty, so every flagged PR is held until someone lists its flag (fill it from the audit log, read locally; CI must never read the production database), and the drafter does not yet pull in all of a long-flagged feature's earlier PRs by flag key), run it on a schedule in CI (a `claude -p` step with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` as an Actions secret, opening a draft PR), have it write the demo scene and capture the screenshots (needs the demo stack in a CI runner), run the member reader review on the draft, and read the day a flag went on for everyone from the audit log instead of leaving it to a human.

### Outbound User-Agent: the clients that could not be augmented

`docs/infrastructure/OUTBOUND_REQUESTS.md` lists what sends our identity. Still sending only their library's own User-Agent: the Sentry SDK's transport (no option to add to it), and the token request Google's auth library makes for the service account (`google-auth-library` builds it itself; only the Calendar calls carry our directive). Deferred because neither talks to a host that asks who we are; worth revisiting if Google or Sentry ever blocks on it. Start at `src/lib/google-calendar/client.ts` (a custom `GoogleAuth` transporter) and `src/instrumentation-client.ts` / `sentry.*.config.ts` (`transport` option).

### Flaky unit tests under load, and the unit job's 10-minute limit _(Needs Scheduling)_
The unit job (`Test (unit)` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `timeout-minutes: 10`) normally takes 6 to 8 minutes, but on 2026-11-17 three of twelve recent runs were cancelled at the limit (one on `main`, two on PRs; the re-runs passed in 6 to 9 minutes), and `main` runs also failed on `MessageUnfurlRemove` and `AdminEventAttendeeTickets`. Locally, the whole component and lib suites in parallel fail a different handful of tests each time (`BlockedPreviewsList` and `MessageUnfurlRemove` "when the action throws", `AnimationsSetting`, `link-unfurl-metadata`, `outcome-wiring`) that all pass when run alone. So the tests depend on timing or on shared state under load. **Where to start:** run the suite a few times with `pnpm test:unit` and list which tests fail; look for real timers, un-awaited promises and module-level state (`vi.mock` state not reset); then either raise `timeout-minutes` (a CI change to approve) or split the job in two, and keep the suite well under the limit. Deferred because it needs a decision on the limit and is separate from the pages being built.

### Supabase Performance Advisor Warnings

`supabase db advisors --linked --type performance` reports 99 warnings in production (2026-11-17, `docs/checklists/SUPABASE_PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md`): 30 `auth_rls_initplan` (RLS policies that call `auth.uid()` or `current_setting()` directly, so Postgres re-evaluates them per row; wrapping them as `(select auth.uid())` fixes it), 68 `multiple_permissive_policies` (several permissive policies on one table and role, each run for every row) and 1 `duplicate_index`. Deferred because none is an error and nothing has been traced to them; worth doing because the database is short on memory and CPU (see "Production database memory"). Where to start: the advisor output lists each policy and table; fix in a migration (expand only, policies are replaced in place), run `pnpm test:pgtap` since these are RLS policies, and drop the duplicate index after checking nothing names it.

### Load test the Hub before the next big announcement

The Supabase production checklist asks for a load test, ideally on staging. There is none, and no staging database; the 2026-11-02 launch went out untested. Missing: a script (k6 or similar) that signs in a few hundred test members and loads the busiest pages (home, calendar, chat), plus a safe target. Deferred because the member count is about 80 and nothing has failed under real load apart from the memory issue above. Worth doing before a large announcement or an import of many more members. Where to start: `docs/checklists/SUPABASE_PRODUCTION_CHECKLIST.md`, "Performance"; run it against a local stack or a Supabase branch (see the branching entry below), never production.

### Realtime cost and limits

Realtime is used for chat (`src/components/chat/ChatLive.tsx`, `ChatSidebar.tsx`), prickles and `LiveRefresh`, and three tables are published (`prickles`, `chat_messages`, `chat_reactions`). In production, the Realtime WAL-polling query is the top entry in `pg_stat_statements` by total time (about 1.9 million calls, 14,000 s, 7.5 ms mean, stats since June), more than any app query, on a database that is already memory-bound. Not yet checked: Supabase's Realtime limits (connections, messages per second, channel joins) against our use, and whether every subscription is needed (an unfiltered `postgres_changes` on a table is read by every client). Deferred because chat is new and the cost is shared with the memory item above. Where to start: https://supabase.com/docs/guides/realtime/limits, the dashboard's Realtime report, and `LiveRefresh.tsx` (which tables and who mounts it); consider Broadcast from the server instead of `postgres_changes` if the polling cost matters.

### One definition per resource, and a module manifest that declares resources _(Idea)_

Each kind of resource is spread over many places that are kept in step by hand and by a drift test each: the repository in `src/lib/data` (about 70 of them, each with its own paging, filter and sort), the permissions (`src/lib/permissions.ts`), the Activity Log sentence and label (`describeRow`, `ENTITY_LABELS`), the id prefix (`resource-ids.md`), the tracking-plan events and the `withAction` wrapper, the lifecycle actions, the RLS policies. Idea, taken from how Kubernetes' API machinery works: write **one definition per resource** (kind, table, id prefix, schema, the scope it lives under, the verbs and named actions it has, which fields are filterable, sortable, redacted or written only by the system, the audit sentence, the tracked events, any external versions) and derive the rest from it. Deferred on purpose: the permissions model, the authorization ADR (`docs/decisions/0001-authorization-orgs-and-scopes.md`), the page block and chat transport seams and several-roles-per-user are being finished first, and a framework drawn before they settle would be drawn blind. It is also the same thing as the module manifest's `resources` field, so the two should be one ADR.

What to take, in the order that pays off:
1. **A conformance suite every resource must pass**, before any generator: the role-by-permission matrix, RLS denies what it should (pgTAP generated from the definition), an audit entry with a sentence exists, every filterable or sortable field has an index, paging is stable and has no gaps over 1000 rows, writes are idempotent. Useful even with hand-written repositories, and the cheapest first step.
2. **The definition itself**, as metadata that describes a table the migrations already create (a test checks it against `database.types.ts`), feeding permissions, audit labels, ids and analytics. No generated SQL: migrations, RLS and squawk need judgement, so at most print a standard migration for review or add SQL helpers (`attach_audit(table)`, policy builders).
3. **A repository factory** with keyset paging (an opaque continue token, not an offset), filter and sort only on declared and indexed fields, optimistic concurrency (a version column, update with if-match, a conflict refusal), a scope filter for collections, and an escape hatch for custom queries.
4. **Named actions next to CRUD** (publish, merge, reorder, bulk send) in the same registry, permissioned, audited and tracked the same way; domain commands are not forced into create/update/delete.
5. **Spec versus status**: fields only the system writes (pipeline output, derived columns) get their own permission and RLS, so staff cannot edit them. Matches the Bronze/Local/Silver split.
6. **External versions at the edge only** (import and export files, webhook and API payloads, stored JSON documents), each converting to and from one internal type, with round-trip tests and unknown fields preserved. `src/lib/landing/migrations.ts` and the page export's `format`/`version` are small cases already. Not for the database: expand and contract does that.
7. **Cross-module hooks** (validation or defaults a module adds to a resource it does not own), cascade and cleanup declared on the definition, and an OpenAPI description for consumers.

Not to copy: forcing everything into CRUD, generating hard SQL silently, retrofitting the existing ~300 tables at once (pilot on one small resource and adopt as tables are touched), or building the framework before the conformance suite. Where to start: `.claude/rules/permissions-for-resources.md` (the permissions half), `docs/architecture/PERMISSIONS.md`, `docs/architecture/PAGE_BLOCKS.md` (the registry and contribution pattern), `src/lib/data/` (a few repositories to find what repeats) and `tests/lib/permission-guards.test.ts` (the shape of a drift test). Pick a small, self-contained table as the pilot.

### Unordered aggregates feeding an ordered result
`jsonb_agg`, `array_agg` and `string_agg` without an `ORDER BY` return rows in whatever order the plan produces, so a list shown to a member or asserted by a pgTAP `is(...)` can flip when a planner choice changes (a join strategy, statistics, a new index). This broke `main` once (`get_profile_writing`, fixed in `20271030000000_profile_writing_stable_order.sql`, which also pins the order in `writing_three_level_visibility.test.sql` under three join strategies). Not yet checked one by one: the pgTAP files that compare a multi-element aggregate with no `ORDER BY` in the query (`audit_log`, `audit_member_actions`, `chat_search`, `chat_unread_channel_ids`, `chat_mark_unread`, `get_record_history`, `in_app_notifications`, `member_interactions`, `member_notes_and_directory` (directory names), `member_works_dated`, `search_member_aliases`, `admin_search_matches_all_aliases`, and the other `get_profile_writing.test.sql` assertions, now deterministic), and the SQL functions behind them. Deferred because each needs a decision on the intended order and many are single-row. To do it: for each, either sort in the test (`ORDER BY` inside the aggregate, when the order is not the product's) or in the function (when it is), and look for the rest with `git grep -nE "jsonb_agg|array_agg|string_agg" -- supabase/migrations` on the latest definition of each function.

### Page checks: what is still in the baseline, and what they do not reach _(Needs Scheduling)_

The browser page checks (`tests/demo/`, `docs/product/DEMO_AND_HELP.md`, "Page checks") run nightly (the page-checks jobs of `.github/workflows/help-screenshots.yml`) and fail on anything `tests/demo/baseline.json` does not list. Deferred, in the order they are worth doing:

- **A video's play button is covered** (`edge:control-covered` on `/chat/:id`, every size but phone). On a chat message that shares a lesson video, the card's own `lesson-play-video` button sits under the media player's Play button, so it can never be clicked. Seen by the `share-a-lesson-video-in-chat` scene. Where to start: `src/components/chat/` (the shared-video card) and `LessonPlayer`'s overlay; either let the player's button be the one control, or stop the card button from being drawn under it. Delete the six `/chat/:id` entries when it is gone.
- **Realtime in the demo stack.** Chat's live updates log a warning and a refused websocket when the local stack's realtime service doesn't answer, so `isEnvironmentNoise` (`tests/demo/checks.ts`) drops those two messages. Find out why the demo stack's realtime isn't reachable (the demo config is generated from `supabase/config.toml` by `tests/demo/stack.ts`), fix it, and remove the filter so a real live-updates failure shows.
- **Not reached by the schedule.** (1) `@changelog` scenes are not on it. Three of them fail on a clean stack for reasons of their own (`check-in-usual-feelings`: the "Frequently used" heading never shows; `sidebar-modes`: the sidebar mode button is not found; `sign-in-with-an-emailed-code`: it needs `DEMO_MAIL_URL` for the demo stack's mail port). (2) The schedule checks a production build, so React's development-only warnings (the detailed hydration mismatch, empty `src`) only show in a local `pnpm demo:app` run; if they come back, run the page checks once against the dev server. (3) Nobody has seen the schedule run yet: the first nights may show baseline gaps that only a clean runner reveals (a local run on a loaded machine timed out on sign-in); fix them as they come, and lower the entries as pages are fixed.

## Security & Access Control

### Two notions of "who is on the team": the staff roster and the account role _(Needs Scheduling)_
The Hub has two overlapping answers to "who is on the team". The `staff` table (roles owner, staff, contractor; it gives members their role and hire date in the members processing and is linked to a member by email) is a roster. `user_profiles.role` (admin, assistant, community_manager, member) is the account role that decides permissions. A person can be in one and not the other (a contractor with no account, an admin with no row in `staff`). `staff_member_ids()` (`StaffRepository.memberIds()`) joins them for one purpose, "this member is staff" (Hedgie Care's DM list), and never decides who may read anything. **Why deferred:** the owner asked for the single definition now and left the unifying for later. **Where to start:** decide which is the source of truth (probably the account role, with the roster kept for hire dates and contractors), then let the members processing read the role from there, drop the email-based link in `StaffRepository.linkMembers`, and keep `staff_member_ids()` as the one place the answer is computed.


### Back the assistant role in the database _(Needs Scheduling)_
The `assistant` role (`src/lib/permissions.ts`) holds the admin's permissions, minus the Hedgie Care private reads (`ASSISTANT_WITHHELD`), so it passes every page, action and route guard. Row-level security still treats only `role = 'admin'` as `is_admin()` (`20260515000002`), so an assistant's own client reads nothing of the admin tables and the pages come up empty or fail. **Why deferred:** no account holds the role (prod, 2026-11-21: admin, community_manager and member only), and widening `is_admin()` to a second role touches every admin policy and function, an owner decision (`never widen is_admin()` is the current rule). **Where to start:** decide whether the assistant is real; if so, a migration that makes `is_admin()` (and the functions that repeat `role = 'admin'`) accept it, a pgTAP test, and move `profiles.isAdmin` and the member-app `role === 'admin'` checks (`src/app/(member)/layout.tsx`, `src/lib/sudo.ts`, `src/lib/search/context.ts`, `src/lib/courses/caller.ts`) onto `can(...)`. Until then, don't assign the role.

### Map each mutating permission to its Activity Log entry _(Idea)_
`.claude/rules/permissions-for-resources.md` says every mutating permission names the Activity Log entry it produces, but nothing checks it: a permission can ship whose action writes no entry, or one `describeRow` has no sentence for. **Why deferred:** the registry (`PERMISSIONS` in `src/lib/permissions.ts`) holds only a description today, and adding the entry name touches every mutating permission, which is a sweep of its own and an open question for actions that write several entries. **Where to start:** give each mutating permission an `audit` field (the entity type or event it writes, or an explicit "none, because ..."), then a unit test that every one names an entry `describeRow` (`src/lib/activity-feed.ts`) has a sentence for, using the Activity Log docs (`docs/architecture/ACTIVITY_AND_AUDIT_LOG.md`) for the event list.

### Guard coverage for member-facing resources _(Idea)_
`tests/lib/permission-guards.test.ts` scans only `src/app/(admin)/`, the staff action files and the staff API folders. A page, action or route outside them (a member-facing resource, a staff operation exposed under `src/app/(member)/` or `src/app/actions/`) can ship with no `can()` or ownership check and nothing fails. **Why deferred:** member-facing code mixes ownership checks (`getEffectiveIdentity`), public routes, webhooks and cron with their own secrets, so a blanket scan needs a list of how each kind is guarded first. **Where to start:** inventory `src/app/actions/*.ts` and `src/app/api/*` by guard kind (staff permission, ownership, public, secret), then extend the guard test to require one of the kinds per file, with an explained allow-list like `NOT_GUARDED` there. Until then `permissions-for-resources.md` says to check in the page or action and test the denied case.

### Guard a data export with `data.export` _(Idea)_
`data.export` and `changelog.edit` are reserved permissions (`RESERVED_PERMISSIONS` in `src/lib/permissions.ts`): no staff page, action or route exports data or edits changelog entries today (the changelog is a file; the only download is a member's own project export). **Why deferred:** nothing to guard yet. **Where to start:** the first admin CSV or download takes `requireApiPermission(request, "data.export")` (admin and assistant only; the conversion specialist is denied by design) and comes off the reserved list, and its own client reads only what RLS allows. `tests/lib/permission-guards.test.ts` fails on a stale reserved entry.


### Supabase security checklist follow-ups

From `docs/checklists/SUPABASE_SECURITY_CHECKLIST.md` (2026-11-17). Deferred because each needs the dashboard, an owner decision or a design. (1) **Resolved 2026-11-18** (owner-reported): MFA is required for the Supabase, Vercel and GitHub organizations, and each has a second owner with MFA. **Still open:** (2) **Resolved 2026-11-18:** OTP expiry is now 3600 ("Lower the email link lifetime"). **Still open:** (3) Realtime uses `postgres_changes` and `realtime.messages` has no policies; messages and reactions both soft delete (corrected 2026-11-18: reaction removal is an UPDATE), so only a hard delete by a maintenance job sends a DELETE event and this is low priority; revisit with "Realtime cost and limits". (4) **Staff two-step sign-in: built 2026-11-18, requirement not on.** Settings has the enrol screen and `/mfa` the code check; staff with a factor are held to aal2 in the proxy, the page and action guards and `requireAdmin` (`docs/trust/STAFF_MFA.md`). **Still open:** the owner sets `staffMfaRequiredFrom` in `config/app.ts` once every admin and community manager has enrolled, which makes a factor mandatory; and a lost device is reset by hand (the doc has the steps). Not built, deferred until the requirement is on and we see how it goes: recovery codes, more than one authenticator app per account, WebAuthn keys, and a fresh code before the most dangerous actions (starting sudo, imports).

### Move member sign-in and MFA behind our own server

Today the browser calls Supabase Auth directly (`signInWithOtp` in `src/app/login/LoginForm.tsx`, `signOut`, and the staff MFA enrol, challenge and verify calls in `src/components/mfa/`), through `src/lib/supabase/client.ts`. **Why deferred:** it works, RLS and the publishable key are what protect data, and proxying would not allow IP restrictions (Supabase has none for its HTTP APIs, see `docs/decisions/NETWORK_RESTRICTIONS.md`). **What it would buy:** one place to rate-limit and log sign-in attempts, one hostname fewer in the CSP `connect-src`, and a browser that no longer needs the Supabase URL for auth. **Where to start:** server actions that call `src/lib/supabase/server.ts` (which sets the session cookies) for sign-in, sign-out and each MFA step; take care with the MFA cookie round-trip and the session refresh in `src/proxy.ts`. Realtime and uploads stay as they are (see the same doc).

### Threat model follow-ups

From `docs/trust/THREAT_MODEL.md` (2026-11-17). (1) **Resolved (2026-11-18):** `tests/lib/api-route-auth.test.ts` lists every `src/app/api/**/route.ts` with its credential kind and fails on a route with none or a stale allow-list entry (static text: it does not prove every code path reaches the check). (2) **Resolved (2026-11-18):** `tests/lib/safe-fetch-callers.test.ts` classifies every server file that makes a request (a fixed provider host, with the reason, or `safe-fetch`); a new caller or a stale entry fails it. No member-chosen address bypassed the guard. **Hardening left open:** `src/lib/slack-capture.ts` sends the bot token to the `url_private` address in the Slack file payload with the platform `fetch`; the value comes from Slack, but a host check (`*.slack.com`) before sending the token would remove the trust.
