The Slack photo, emoji and Kajabi image and file copiers (`src/lib/chat/avatar-copy.ts`, `emoji-copy.ts`, `src/lib/courses/images.ts`, `files.ts`) also use the platform `fetch` on provider-supplied URLs, so they miss the private-address checks and the User-Agent; move them to `safeGet` if those sources are ever less trusted. (3) **Sanitizer half resolved 2026-11-21:** the allow-lists of `src/lib/courses/sanitize.ts` and `src/lib/landing/sanitize.ts` were read line by line against the `sanitize-html` 2.18.2 behaviour and tested with 148 payloads (script and event-handler tricks, javascript, data, vbscript and entity-encoded links, frames, forms, svg, math, template, noscript, comment and style mutation vectors, nested and unclosed markup) through both sanitizers, `sanitizeLandingData` and the `RichHtml` render, checking the output as `parse5` reads it and that cleaning twice changes nothing (`tests/lib/html-sanitizers-xss.test.ts`). No bypass found, nothing tightened. Every stored-HTML path sanitises at render, and the lesson and landing writers also at write (Kajabi import, the lesson editor, the landing editor, the Kajabi page importer, templates and the AI draft); Bronze keeps Kajabi's raw HTML, which nothing renders. Help, changelog and prickle text are markdown without raw HTML. **Allow-list concern Resolved (2026-11-21):** a read-only check of production found every lesson image and link absolute (10 images, 70 http(s) links, 2 mailto) and no links or images in landing rich text, so both sanitizers now drop a site path in an image unless it is under `/api/courses/image/` or `/api/media/` and drop any link path under `/api` (`src/lib/sanitize-urls.ts`; counts and method in `docs/trust/SANITIZER_URL_CHECK.md`). Nothing in production changes. **Structured links Resolved (2026-11-22):** the structured link props of landing blocks (`href` and `url`, through `safeHref` in `src/lib/landing/data.ts`) now use the same rule as stored HTML (`allowedHtmlHref`): a path under `/api` is dropped, and so are `//`, backslashes, `..`, encoded dots and slashes and control characters. A read-only check of production found 0 link-like values under `/api` in landing pages, their versions and the site template; the only relative paths were four site pages. **Still open, low:** confirm that `/api/media/<id>/<preset>` and `/api/courses/image/` serve only images (stored HTML images may still name them), and keep it so when either route changes. **The `SECURITY DEFINER` half is Resolved (2026-11-21):** `docs/trust/SECURITY_DEFINER_REVIEW.md` has every one of the 54 definer functions that signed-in users can execute (read from production, read-only, and hash-matched to the migrations): none builds SQL from caller input (two use dynamic SQL or `set_config` safely: `member_owned_row_counts`, `duplicate_course`), every one pins `search_path` and checks the caller, none needed a fix. The advisor's one other finding (a trigger function without a pinned `search_path`) is fixed in `20270874000000_pin_search_path_member_activities_stamp_sudo.sql`. `security_definer_review.test.sql` adds guards: every function in `public` pins its `search_path`, a callable definer function with dynamic SQL must be on a reviewed list, and one member cannot list or revoke another's sessions. **The three low-severity decisions were settled 2026-11-22 (details in the review's "Decisions"):** `chat_find_or_create_dm` now refuses members who are not active and does not reopen a conversation the other person left (`20271005000000_chat_find_or_create_dm_tighten.sql`); `pg_temp` is deliberately not appended to the older plain `public` search paths (not reachable through PostgREST), and `security_definer_review.test.sql` requires it of every new `SECURITY DEFINER` function, with the older ones on a shrink-only allow-list (remove an entry whenever you replace one of those functions); the mentor match counts are deliberate and consistent with what members were told (the Big Sister badge on a profile is disclosed on `/big-sisters`; no page says matches are private). (4) **Resolved (2026-11-18):** the code was scanned for logged tokens beyond the webhooks (the Calendar webhook logged its channel token until this review; it was rotated 2026-11-18). One more leak: the Kajabi OAuth failure logged the whole response body (fixed, now field names only; no credential is known to have been logged, since the log fires only when `access_token` is missing). Sentry events carried the calendar feed token (in the path) and sign-in or unsubscribe link values (in the query): `src/lib/sentry-scrub.ts` now redacts them in all three `Sentry.init` calls. `tests/lib/no-secret-logging.test.ts` guards against logging a whole headers object, the environment or a serialised request. **Still open:** Vercel's request logs record paths, so the calendar feed token appears there for the feed's requests; the token is the feed's only credential, so move it out of the path only if that matters (a query value would still be logged). (5) **Resolved (2026-11-18; removed 2026-11-23, replaced by the OSV scans):** `.github/workflows/audit.yml` ran `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high` weekly and on lockfile changes, `continue-on-error`, not a required check. First run found 9 advisories, all in transitive packages: **high** picomatch GHSA-fc5q-39xc-vjpf (no patched version; through `@tailwindcss/typography`'s tailwindcss); moderate fast-uri GHSA-w74x-v6f9-j3xg and GHSA-wjr9-j795-jxcr, postcss-selector-parser GHSA-rj75-hqrm-r3gf, qs GHSA-6pq8-jhm2-2vcw (through googleapis), glob GHSA-3g3m-2fjr-6pw4 and minimatch GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249 (through react-email); low fast-uri GHSA-xhq7-xmqj-769h and GHSA-333x-w4r6-6crp. Most are build or email-template tooling; none was assessed as reachable from request handling. **Open:** take the fixes as the parents release them (Dependabot), **Update 2026-11-20:** the audit now fails scheduled and manual runs (a pull request still only reports), and the findings above were cleared by dependency bumps; making it a required check was left open and is moot now that it is removed. (6) Alert on repeated failed webhook verification and on a burst of sign-in emails: see "Alerts for abuse: failed webhook verification and sign-in email bursts". Start at the document's A01, A03, A05, A06, A09 and A10 rows.

The evaluation in `docs/checklists/SUPABASE_SECURITY_CHECKLIST.md` (2026-11-17) left four items open. Deferred because each needs the dashboard, the owner's decision or a design. (1) Unchecked from the CLI: MFA on every Supabase account with dashboard access, MFA enforcement on the organization, more than one Owner, and any test phone number set only in the dashboard. (2) **Resolved 2026-11-18:** OTP expiry is now 3600. (3) Realtime uses `postgres_changes`, whose `DELETE` events RLS doesn't filter and `realtime.messages` has no policies; consider server-sent Broadcast for chat with "Realtime cost and limits". (4) Write a short threat model and an OWASP Top 10 pass. Start at the checklist doc.

### Alerts for abuse: failed webhook verification and sign-in email bursts _(Needs Scheduling)_
From the threat model (`docs/trust/THREAT_MODEL.md`, A09 and the sign-in flood abuse case). Two detections are missing; both only tell staff something is happening in #hub-alerts, and neither changes what the routes accept. (1) **A provider's webhook verification fails repeatedly:** alert when the rejections for one provider (Slack, Zoom, Mux, Resend, Google Calendar) reach a threshold within a window; the message says provider and count only, never a header, body, token or address. (2) **A burst of sign-in emails:** alert when the hourly count passes a configurable share of the overall cap (150 an hour), since a flood from one source can block everyone's sign-in for an hour; the message says count and cap, never an address. Deferred because the thresholds should come from real launch traffic, not a guess, and a counter shared across serverless instances needs a place to live (memory is per instance; look for an existing database counter or a small service-role-only table first). Where to start: `src/lib/operations-alerts.ts` (the rate-limited alert helper, `docs/infrastructure/SLACK_OPERATIONS_ALERTS.md`), `src/lib/webhook-auth.ts` and the routes under `src/app/api/webhooks/`, `src/lib/rate-limit.ts`, the sign-in code in `src/app/login/actions.ts` and its log. Put thresholds in `app.config.ts`, alert once per window, and test crossing, staying below, the dedup window and that no personal data is in the message.

### External security scanners: what is left _(Needs Scheduling)_

Added 2026-11-20 as scheduled, informational workflows (none is a required check, none blocks a merge, like `osv-scan.yml`): `openssf-scorecard.yml` (OpenSSF Scorecard), `gitleaks.yml` (gitleaks over full history), `osv-scan.yml` (OSV-Scanner on `pnpm-lock.yaml`) and `zap-baseline.yml` (passive ZAP baseline against production; there are no preview deploys to aim it at). Their findings are not triaged yet: **after the first runs, read each one, fix or rotate what is real, and list what is accepted here.** Then decide which to make blocking. Not done:
- **First scanner runs, 2026-11-20.** gitleaks: 23 findings, accepted in `config/.gitleaksignore` by fingerprint so a new finding still fails. Re-checked: 22 are false positives (the public local Supabase demo keys, fake test tokens and ids). **One was real when written:** a database password in an early version of `docs/infrastructure/DEPLOYMENT.md` (gone from the current file, still in history; fingerprint in `config/.gitleaksignore`). **Resolved:** the owner rotated the production database password long before this (confirmed 2026-11-20), so what is in history no longer opens anything; the allowlist entry stays so the scan keeps passing. ZAP baseline: 0 failures, 10 warnings. **Resolved 2026-11-20:** `poweredByHeader: false` in `next.config.ts` (no more `X-Powered-By`); the cross-domain warning on built JS/CSS, `icon.png` and the manifest (Vercel's `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on public static files, nothing credentialed) is kept out of the scan by `config/zap-baseline.tsv`, which also lists, with reasons, what is accepted: Subresource Integrity (the only third-party script is the Google Analytics tag, which changes without notice and can't be pinned), Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy (`require-corp` or `credentialless` would break third-party images and embeds, and we need no cross-origin isolation) and CSP directives with no fallback (decided not needed: our members use current browsers; `'strict-dynamic'` is already assumed). The rest are informational (report-only CSP, cache headers, session cookie identified) and need no action. Revisit the report-only CSP under "CSP for scripts and styles". **OSV-Scanner:** the first run found 28 known vulnerabilities in 12 packages; the dependency bumps in #1565 cleared 26 of them, and a fresh run on `main` the same day found 1 package with 2 (both "sandbox breakout" advisories in the npm package `sandbox`, with no fix published, reached only through the dev-only `vercel` CLI), which `config/osv-scanner.toml` accepts until 2027-02-20 (reason and expiry in the file). Revisit when `vercel` drops or upgrades it, or when the expiry passes.
- **Scorecard, first triage (2026-11-20, score 4.8).** **Resolved:** every action pinned to a commit SHA with its version in a comment (Dependabot keeps both current), no workflow grants write at the top level (only the jobs that move a tag or push a branch), `SECURITY.md` added, and all 21 `pnpm audit` findings cleared by dependency bumps and `pnpm-workspace.yaml` overrides (all in Vercel and Checkly tooling; drop an override when its parent releases a fix). **Still open, on purpose:** Code-Review and Branch-Protection stay low because the `main` ruleset requires no approvals (a solo maintainer with auto-merge; requiring one would block every merge), and it does not apply to administrators; the one `npm install -g vercel` in `deploy.yml` was unpinned by hash (since replaced by the lockfile's `vercel`, `pnpm exec vercel`); Fuzzing, the OpenSSF best-practices badge and Contributors are not pursued.
- **Fuzzing (Scorecard "Fuzzing" check).** **Resolved:** `fast-check` is a dev dependency and `tests/lib/url-sanitizers-fuzz.test.ts` runs property tests over the URL sanitizers (`allowedHtmlHref`, `allowedHtmlImageSrc`, `safeUrl`). **Still open:** the same style for the other code that takes untrusted input: the HTML sanitizers, the Kajabi/Slack/Zoom/Stripe importers, webhook payload parsing and the CSV and photo-import parsers ("never throws", "output has no script", "re-running gives the same result"). One test file each in `tests/lib/`.
- **Property-based tests: where they pay most (2026-11-24).** The rule is `.claude/rules/property-based-testing.md`; the pattern is `tests/lib/url-sanitizers-fuzz.test.ts`. Ranked by untrusted input and wrong-data cost first, then by how cheap the function is to test (all pure; each already has an example test unless noted, so the gain is the fuzzing). Convert one per PR; a found bug is fixed with its counterexample added as an example test.
  1. `src/lib/slack-signature.ts` `verifySlackSignature`: never throws; valid only when the HMAC matches within 5 minutes. Likely finding: `parseInt` of a non-numeric timestamp gives NaN and `Math.abs(NaN) > 300` is false, so the age check is skipped (low severity: the signature must still match the same string). Gates Slack webhooks and sign-in links.
  2. `src/modules/chat/slack/escape.ts` `escapeForSlack`: no unescaped `&`, `<`, `>` in the output except recognized tokens (`<@U…>`, `<#C…>`, links); `<!channel>` can never be produced from member text. No dedicated test today.
  3. The HTML sanitizers `src/lib/landing/sanitize.ts` (`sanitizeRichText`, `sanitizeLandingData`) and `src/lib/courses/sanitize.ts` (`sanitizeLessonHtml`): idempotent, and the output has no `<script`, `on*` attributes or non-allowlisted scheme. Idempotence is the new part (`tests/lib/html-sanitizers-xss.test.ts` has the examples).
  4. `src/lib/email-unsubscribe.ts` token sign and parse: round trip; any tampered, wrong-kind or secretless token gives null; never throws. Unauthenticated, changes email preferences.
  5. `src/lib/sudo-cookie.ts` `parseSudoCookie` / `signSudoCookie`: round trip; arbitrary strings give null and never an identity without a valid HMAC. Admin impersonation.
  6. `src/lib/chat/search-syntax.ts` `parseSearch`: never throws; the `tsquery` has only quoted word tokens and the operators `& | ! <-> :*`, so it is always safe for `to_tsquery`.
  7. `src/lib/courses/product-progress-csv.ts` `parseProductProgressCsv`: throws only `ProgressCsvError`; `rows + invalid == rows read`; emails unique and lowercased; percent within 0 to 100.
  8. `src/lib/landing/slug.ts` `slugify`: empty or matches `SLUG_PATTERN`, at most 80 characters, idempotent (reserved words are the one exception to "valid"). Only tested indirectly.
  9. `src/lib/prickle-schedules.ts` `zonedTimeToUtc` and `generateScheduleInstanceDates`: formatting the result back in the zone gives the input wall time; dates stay inside the month. Check the single offset correction on DST gap and overlap times near 2am (a wrong prickle hour).
  10. `src/lib/permissions.ts` `roleChangeRefusal` / `changeableRoles`: a null refusal never lets an actor grant or remove a role at or above their own rank; the two functions agree. Privilege escalation.
  11. `src/lib/supabase/paginate.ts` `chunk` and `fetchRowWindow`, `src/lib/pagination.ts` `pageBounds`: chunks concatenate back to the input and are at most `size`; windows return exactly `count` rows with no overlap; `firstItem <= lastItem <= total` with a clamped page.
  12. `src/lib/streaks.ts` `computeStreaks` / `computePrickleStreaks`: current <= longest; invariant to order and duplicates; timezone-aware week index.
  13. `src/lib/member-matching.ts` `normalizeName` / `aliasKey`: idempotent; `aliasKey` ignores periods, apostrophes, hyphens and spacing, and must stay in step with the SQL `name_alias_key`.
  14. `src/lib/chat/unfurl-dedupe.ts` `normalizeLinkForMatch`: idempotent; http and https equal; `www.`, fragments, tracking parameters and parameter order don't matter.
  15. `src/lib/nudge-jitter.ts` (`sendHourFor`, `jitteredGapDays`, `calendarDaysBetween`): hour within 9 to 17; gap within ±15% and at least 1 day; `calendarDaysBetween` antisymmetric and ignoring time of day.
  Smaller: `src/lib/community-stats.ts` `etHour`/`etDate`, `src/lib/ical.ts` `foldLine`/`escapeText` (75-octet lines, no split multi-byte characters), `src/lib/slack-sign-in-code.ts` code normalization. Not candidates: anything that imports the Supabase client (`src/lib/timezone.ts`); extract the pure part first.
- **Scorecard, what is left after the quick wins (2026-11-23, 7.4).** Vulnerabilities (8/10) stays: OSV lists two advisories against an npm package named `sandbox` that the `vercel` dev dependency pulls in at 4.1.0; they have no fixed version, so only dropping `vercel` from `package.json` (and calling it with `pnpm dlx` or the pinned global install) would clear them; accepted in `config/osv-scanner.toml` meanwhile. **Resolved:** `deploy.yml` runs the CLI from the lockfile (`pnpm exec vercel`) instead of a global `npm install`, so Pinned-Dependencies has no unpinned command left. The OpenSSF best-practices badge needs a FLOSS license, so the proprietary license rules out a Passing badge. Code-Review and Branch-Protection need an approver other than the author (a review bot that really reviews and approves, or a second maintainer); `dismiss_stale_reviews_on_push` is already on.
- **OpenSSF best-practices checklist.** `docs/checklists/OPENSSF_BEST_PRACTICES.md` scores us per criterion and ends with the ordered list of what is worth doing without the badge (triage the scanner results, lint warnings to zero, enforce the CSP, publish combined coverage, a one-year roadmap, property-based tests). Pick items from there.
- **Manual one-off grades** (the owner runs them, results are not in the repo): Mozilla Observatory and securityheaders.com on the production host, and the email side of the sending domain (SPF, DKIM, DMARC, MTA-STS) with a checker such as MXToolbox or Hardenize.
- **Supabase advisors** (Security and Performance, dashboard or the CLI's advisor command against the linked project): flags tables without RLS, `SECURITY DEFINER` functions and mutable `search_path` across the whole schema, beyond what the pgTAP tests cover. Read-only; run it, then fix findings through migrations.
- **GitHub secret scanning and push protection** are repository settings; check they are on (Settings, Code security). `config/github.yml` declares only `settings:` and rulesets, so decide whether to add them there.
- **Semgrep** (`p/nextjs`, `p/typescript`) next to CodeQL, and **Socket** for malicious or typosquatted packages (advisories miss them). Not added: each needs an account or a token and an owner decision.
- **A logged-in ZAP scan (not built).** The scheduled baseline (`zap-baseline.yml`) is passive and signs in as nobody, so it only sees public pages: the member and admin areas, where the data and the permission checks are, are never scanned. A logged-in (and later active) scan belongs on a preview or demo environment with test accounts, never production (an active scan sends attack payloads and writes data). **Why deferred:** there are no preview deploys yet (see "Make Vercel preview deployments work"), and sign-in is a one-time emailed link or code, so ZAP needs a session set up for it. **Where to start:** once previews exist, point ZAP's automation framework at the preview URL with a member and an admin test account, signed in through a stored session cookie or a test-only login route that exists only in that environment (never in production); until then the local stack with the demo community (`demo/`) can run the same scan from a workflow with Docker. Keep the passive baseline on production as it is. Findings follow the same rule as the baseline: fail only on FAIL-level alerts, post to the alerts channel.
- **Badges that need an account or a decision (README has the account-free ones: CI, Scorecard, the audits).** Coverage, maintainability, tech debt and readability scores need a service (Codecov for coverage from the CI report; SonarCloud or Code Climate for maintainability and debt), each with an account and a token in the repo's secrets, and a decision on what they may read. A "version" badge is not useful yet (`package.json` is 0.1.0 and nothing is released by version); the deployed commit is the `production` tag (`docs/infrastructure/DEPLOYMENT.md`). The license is proprietary (`LICENSE`, 2026-11-20) and the README has a static badge for it. A "compliance" badge would claim a certification we do not have: skip it unless one is earned.
- **A gate for agent skills.** Nothing stops a third-party skill being committed to `.claude/skills/`. skills.sh shows Gen, Socket and Snyk audit results at install time (the `skills` CLI prompts on a Fail), but only for GitHub-sourced skills on that site and only on the installing machine. Start with a CODEOWNERS entry for `.claude/**`, `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` (and "require review from code owners" in the `main` ruleset), a manifest recording each skill's source and pinned commit with a CI check, and a rule against fetch-and-execute in skills. Check whether skills.sh has a machine-readable audit API before promising a CI gate.

### Code quality: decide what to gate _(Needs Scheduling)_

Added 2026-11-20 as report-only tooling (`.github/workflows/code-quality.yml`, not a required check, never fails on findings): `pnpm knip:report` (unused files, exports, types and dependencies; `config/knip.json`), `pnpm dup:report` (copy-pasted code; `config/jscpd.json`), `pnpm lint:quality` (ESLint complexity and duplicate-branch rules; `config/eslint-quality.mjs`, kept apart so `pnpm lint` is unchanged) and `pnpm quality:report` (the markdown summary, plus the findings on lines a PR added via `scripts/quality-new-code.ts`, which shares `parseAddedLines` with the coverage gate). Deferred because nobody has seen the size of the backlog yet. **Backlog on 2026-11-20 (local run):**
- **knip: 51 findings.** 19 duplicate exports (the same thing exported under two names), 15 unused exported types, 9 unused exports, 3 unused files, 5 unused or unlisted dependencies. Small, because tests count as entry points (an export only a test uses is not reported) and a Next.js route or page file is an entry. Most in `src/app/(admin)/admin/stats/CommunityStatsCharts.tsx`, `src/lib/domain/prickle.ts`, `src/lib/search/universal.ts`, `src/lib/config.ts`, `src/lib/slack-matching.ts`.
- **jscpd: 607 clones, 8,671 duplicated lines (2.0% of 430k).** At 70 tokens and 8 lines minimum. Most in `src/app/api/admin/apply-aliases/route.ts` (35 clones), `src/app/api/admin/events/[id]/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/programs/route.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/events/route.ts`, `src/app/api/import/slack-api/route.ts`, `src/app/api/import/calendar/route.ts`, and the reprocessability and idempotency tests under `tests/api/`.
- **ESLint quality rules: 767 warnings.** `complexity` (over 15) 388, `sonarjs/cognitive-complexity` (over 15) 286, `max-params` (over 5) 39, `max-depth` (over 4) 30, `sonarjs/no-identical-functions` 13, `max-nested-callbacks` (over 4) 10, one all-duplicated-branches. Most in `src/lib/activity-feed.ts`, `tests/helpers/client-server-boundary.ts`, `src/app/(member)/chat/actions.ts`, `src/app/api/webhooks/slack/route.ts`, `scripts/coverage-report.ts`, `src/app/api/import/slack-api/route.ts`, `src/lib/data/events.ts`, `src/lib/upcoming-prickles.ts`.

**Resolved (2026-11-20, the owner's decision): a gate on new code, only the cheap and unambiguous rules.** `pnpm quality:gate` (`scripts/quality-gate.ts`, the `Code quality gate` job in `code-quality.yml`, pull requests only, `contents: read`) exits 1 on: (1) `sonarjs/no-identical-functions` or `sonarjs/no-all-duplicated-branches` on a line the PR added (the same added-lines notion as the coverage gate; the existing findings never fail anyone); (2) a knip unused file or unused, unlisted dependency, binary or unresolved import that is not in `config/knip-baseline.json` (8 entries today: 3 unused files, 4 unused dependencies, 1 unlisted), and a baseline entry that is no longer a finding, so the list only shrinks (delete the line when you fix one). A tool that breaks also fails the gate. **To suppress an intentional exception:** `// eslint-disable-next-line <rule> -- <reason>` for the ESLint rules; for knip, `ignore`, `ignoreDependencies` or `ignoreBinaries` in `config/knip.json` (never add to the baseline). **Stays report-only:** `complexity`, `sonarjs/cognitive-complexity`, `max-depth`, `max-params`, `max-nested-callbacks`, `no-duplicated-branches`, `no-identical-conditions`, jscpd clones, and knip's unused exports, types and duplicate exports. **Still open:** the job is not a required check yet (owner's call): add `Code quality gate` to the required checks of the `main` ruleset in `config/github.yml` and run `pnpm github:config:push` (only the owner runs it). The gate has not yet failed in a real PR run (it is proven by unit tests, `tests/lib/quality-gate.test.ts`). Whether to gate more (jscpd on added lines, complexity with a higher threshold, new unused exports) waits on seeing how the gate behaves. Do not try to clear the backlog first. Also open: `knip` ignores exports that tests use (drop `tests/**` from its entries to see what only tests keep alive), `jscpd` reports exact clones only, and the findings on a function's body are matched by the line ESLint reports (the function head), so a changed body under an old signature is not caught.

### Things to do only if the repo stays public, and what going private changes _(Needs Scheduling)_

The plan is to make the repository private eventually, after development slows enough that CI compute stops being a large cost (the owner, 2026-11-20). Until then it is public, so a few free services are available that stop being free or useful once it is private. No paid services (the owner's rule); prefer tools that run inside our own CI over ones that need a token and read the repo.
- **Only worth it while public: coverage and quality dashboards.** Codecov (coverage, uploaded from the CI report; its 2021 incident was a modified upload script that sent CI secrets out, so if used, take the official action with a repo-scoped token and nothing else in the job's environment) and SonarQube Cloud (maintainability, duplication, tech debt; free for public repositories and, per Sonar's own wording, not tied to a license; confirm on their plans page before signing up). Both need an account and a token. Neither is needed for the enforcement we have: the required `Coverage report` check holds new code to `MIN_PATCH_SCORE` with per-area floors.
- **Only possible while public: the OpenSSF Scorecard published score and its badge** (`openssf-scorecard.yml` has `publish_results: true`, which needs a public repository; the README badge reads the published result). Publishing buys only the badge and the public viewer page (and an API other tools can read, which nobody does for an app we do not distribute). The `floor` job in `openssf-scorecard.yml` reads that API today for the score alert, so when the repo goes private: set `publish_results: false`, have the action also write `results_format: json`, and read `.score` from that file instead of the API; drop the badge; keep the SARIF upload only if code scanning is available (below). First published score, 2026-11-20 (before the license): 4.8. Weak checks: Code-Review 0 (no approvals are required; one-person team), Token-Permissions 0, Security-Policy 0 (no `SECURITY.md`), License 0 (fixed by `LICENSE` since), Pinned-Dependencies 3 (actions pinned by tag, not by commit hash), Vulnerabilities 0 (21 known, from the OSV data), Branch-Protection 3, Fuzzing, CII-Best-Practices, Packaging and Signed-Releases not applicable or not worth it. Cheap next wins: a `SECURITY.md`, `permissions:` at job level in the workflows that lack it, and pinning actions by hash (Dependabot keeps hashes current). The alert floor is `scorecard.minScore` in `config/app.ts`; raise it as the score improves.
- **What going private changes** (check each before the switch): rulesets on a private repository need a paid GitHub plan, and the org is on the free plan, so the `main` ruleset in `config/github.yml` (required checks, resolved threads, no direct pushes) would stop being enforced (`.claude/rules/config-as-code.md`); CodeQL code scanning and secret scanning with push protection are paid features on private repositories, so the required `CodeQL` check would go; GitHub Actions minutes are limited on private repositories (the weekly scanner workflows and the long test jobs count against them); `gitleaks` over history and `OSV-Scanner` keep working because they run in CI. The proprietary `LICENSE` and README badges stay valid; the CI, audit and scanner badges point at workflow results and stop working for readers who cannot see the repo.

### Community manager: narrower billing reads, Activity Log, and the write page
The community manager role (`src/lib/staff-access.ts`) reads Kajabi and Stripe Bronze tables whole (raw payloads, every customer) because Resubscriptions and the membership timeline read them that way; a view or SQL function returning only what those two need would narrow it. The Calendar View still links to admin-only hygiene pages. Deferred to ship the read-only role first. Still to build: the Outreach / Work Queue page for at-risk members they can write to (needs `requireActionPermission(...)` beside `requireActionPermission(...)` and write policies). Start at `supabase/migrations/20270430000500_community_manager_role.sql` and `src/lib/admin-auth.ts`. **Resolved (2026-11-11):** `requireActionPermission(...)` exists (`src/lib/admin-auth.ts`) and the first write page is Hedgie Care (`docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md`, the `care.write` staff feature, RLS with `is_community_manager()`); `audit_row_change()` counts a community manager as staff. **Resolved (2026-11-17):** the member page's Activity tab, limited to the member's own activity (the staff feature `member.activity_tab_limited`; `get_activity_feed` and `count_activity_feed` serve a community manager only with a member and only the `activity` kind; no audit rows, page visits or staff notes), the owner's decision; `docs/architecture/ACTIVITY_AND_AUDIT_LOG.md`, "The community manager's Activity tab". **Still open:** the Outreach / Work Queue write page above, and the narrower billing reads. **Owner decision open: an audit subset for her Activity tab.** What she misses today are the audited changes an admin makes in sudo as a member and the audited changes staff make to a member's record (the document lists the entity types and which sudo actions leave only an audit row; in production there have been about a dozen sudo audit rows besides one schedule backfill, plus page trails). A safe subset would be an allowlist of member-written entity types with the field names only, never the before and after values, and never the identity types (`member`, the alias, status, profile and join-date overrides); it is a new branch in the two functions for `is_community_manager()` and a sentence in `describeRow` that names no values. Not built, because the owner has not chosen it.

### Hedgie Care: what is left _(Needs Scheduling)_
Designed 2026-11-11 in `docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md` for the community manager's reach-outs, replacing her spreadsheet; it replaced a first build called Conversations in the same week. **Built:** `requireActionPermission(...)`, the `care_engagements` table (one row per reach-out period, renewals linked, RLS for admins and the community manager, audit, the `member_data_handling` row), `CareRepository`, the page at `/admin/care` (Active) and `/admin/care/earlier`, manual logging, the `care.write` staff feature, the measured results (before, during, after, counted on request) and the tracking-plan events. Whatever else is not merged is open:
- **An "ask" flow for hand-overs (decided 2026-11-18: skip for now).** Today a care plan changes hands only by the owner (or an admin for them) offering it and the receiver accepting, or by an admin taking it over. There is no way for someone to ask the owner for a plan and have the owner approve. If it is wanted later, reuse the pending-offer columns (`handover_to`, `handover_by`, `handover_at`) with a direction flag, show it under "Waiting for you" for the owner, and give the Activity Log a sentence ("asked for the plan"); the guard on `owned_by` stays as it is. Start at `supabase/migrations/20270726000000_care_handover.sql` and `src/lib/care-handover.ts`.
- **Confirm the numbers with her.** `careEngagementDays` (30), `careLapseGraceDays` (14), `careQuietDays` (14), the three measured windows (28, 30 to 60 days) and the lists of reasons and the four endings are starting points; the numbers are `config/app.ts`, the lists are `src/lib/care-values.ts` plus the migration's `CHECK` constraints (changing a list after it ships is a new migration: expand, then contract).
- **Import her spreadsheet** (about 11 rows, real names). A local one-off to run against the database and delete afterwards, never committed (member names stay out of the repo, `.claude/rules/public-repo-hygiene.md`); use the service role, set `opened_by` to her account, map her "type of check-in" to a `reason` (her dropdown was Introduction, General Check-In, Writing Check-In, Encouragement, Welcome), compute `period_ends_on` from `opened_at`, and set `last_staff_message_at` / `last_member_message_at` from her reply dates.
- **Slack DMs fill status in: built (2026-11-11); what is left.** Built: `care_tracked_dms` (a staff member's own list of the DMs read for her plans, RLS to the owner, not audited), `CareTrackedDmRepository`, the derivation (`src/lib/care-dm.ts`, `src/lib/care-dm-rules.ts`: the latest message each person wrote in a tracked 1:1 DM since the start of the Eastern day the engagement opened, forward only, metadata only) run when she opens the page, when she starts a care plan or accepts one handed to her, and after the nightly DM pull. **Resolved (2026-11-14, the owner's decision):** tracking is automatic with no per-DM choices: the "Your DMs" tab, "Stop tracking this DM", "Read this DM again" and the rule that remembered a stop are gone; a plan she holds is the reason a DM is read. Still open: (1) **To turn it on for her** (operational, not code): the `slack_dm_bridge` flag is off globally in production and attached to one segment, so an admin adds her to that segment (or gives her a preview), and she connects Slack from the banner on the Active tab or the note on a plan (it asks for the DM scopes). Nothing here changes a flag. (2) **`/privacy` and consent wording**: the DM pull reads **all** of a connected member's 1:1 and group DMs, so what Hedgie Care uses is narrower than what is pulled; the page says what it reads, but `/privacy` is the owner's to word. Also decide whether the member on the other side of a DM should be told that the staff member's engagement follows it (it stores only who wrote last and when). (3) **Live update from the webhook** is not wired: the projection reads Bronze when the page opens and nightly, which is as fresh as nothing-notifies-anyone needs; if "needs your reply" ever drives a notification, call `projectCareFromDms(..., { channelId })` after the `message.im` event is stored (`src/app/api/webhooks/slack/route.ts`, `decideDmEvent` `keep`/`pull`). (4) **Group DMs** are out of v1. (5) **Who tracks**: the derivation uses the tracked DM of the plan's owner (`owned_by`; a plan with no owner reads no one's DM). **Resolved (2026-11-12 and 2026-11-14):** a DM is tracked because a care plan exists with the member (`docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md`, "Slack DMs fill it in"), whatever the plan is about; a new plan is owned by whoever starts it, and the receiver of an accepted hand-over has their own DM with the member tracked at once. (6) **Staff counterparts** are the `staff` table's members and the members behind admin or community manager accounts; two community managers who are in neither would see each other listed. (7) **The community manager's own client can already read Slack DM metadata** (`20270430000500_community_manager_role.sql` gives `is_community_manager()` SELECT on `bronze.slack_channels` and `bronze.slack_messages`, whose column grants leave text, files and the payload to the service role): who wrote in which DM and when, for every DM in Bronze, not only hers. Hedgie Care itself reads through the service role and only her own DMs, but that policy is wider than the DM-privacy story elsewhere (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`); narrow it (exclude `is_im`/`is_mpim` conversations and the restricted channels) if she does not need it.
- **Email** through Resend: her own address on the `reply.` subdomain through Resend inbound, forwarded to her inbox, and outgoing through Resend SMTP. Verify first that SMTP sends fire webhooks and match the member by address. Shares the work in "Email timeline: what is left", "Replies (phase 2)".
- **The cohort and comparison view** on `/admin/at-risk`: engagements against similar at-risk members she has not contacted (a random pick from her weekly list would create that group without leaving anyone out for good). Not built; the per-engagement results are.
- **Reminders and alerts for overdue engagements (not built; lapse is only shown on the page today).** A reminder to the **owner** when a period reaches "review due", and an alert to **admins** when one lapses. Each must go through `createNotifier` (`src/lib/notifications/notify.ts`) as a kind registered in `src/lib/notifications/registry.ts` (with its sample in `src/lib/notification-test.ts` and an email template), never a direct Slack DM; **start in dry run behind a feature flag per kind** that says who gets it for real (`liveFor` in `src/lib/member-reminders.ts`, the `prickle_reminders` flag in `src/lib/features.ts`), logging the owner, kind, why and message and writing no dedup row; claim the send in `NotificationLogRepository` (`claimDm`-style dedup keyed by engagement and kind). Needs a daily job (the "review due" and "lapsed" days are derived: `careStatus`, `src/lib/care.ts`) that reads engagements with the service role. Start in `src/lib/care.ts`, `src/lib/member-reminders.ts` and `/api/internal/member-reminders`. Decide first who is told (see "Who owns it, still open" in `docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md`).
- **Follow-up that opens a new engagement (not built).** Two kinds exist: inside one (`follow_up_on`, built) and when to open a NEW one, mostly trigger-based: stopped attending prickles, engagement dropping against their own baseline, check-in ratings falling, hiatus milestones, new arrivals, hedgieversaries, plus a `revisit_on` date set at close. Planned as a **derived suggestions list** with the reason shown, suppressing members with an open or a recent engagement, with dismiss and snooze stored. Consider a new queue type in `admin_work_queue_completions` (`AdminWorkQueueRepository`) instead of a new table; **verify first**: it is admin-only today (RLS), so the community manager cannot write it. **Members who qualified but were not contacted are the natural comparison group** for the measured results (the rules can be recomputed for any past date). Shares code with the cohort view above.
- **Who owns engagements (partly resolved).** She, and possibly others (admins, a second community manager), are the owners; `owned_by` supports several. **Resolved (2026-11-14):** a new plan is owned by whoever starts it (no silent continuation of the previous owner), and a plan changes hands only through a hand-over the receiver accepts. **Still open:** whether admins own work day to day or only offer plans on someone's behalf, and who the reminders go to.
- **Business outcomes beyond what the Results panel shows.** The goals are churn reduction through engagement and belonging (primary) and upsell into courses, programs and opportunities that fit where a writer is in their journey (secondary). Built, from existing repositories and no new table: graded retention (active, on hiatus or cancelled at each `careRetentionCheckpointDays` checkpoint, "Not yet" until it has passed; the Membership Timeline's engine, `memberStatusTimeline` in `src/lib/member-status.ts`; a member on hiatus is roughly half a churn, so the three states are shown apart), the first time they went on hiatus or cancelled after it opened (`firstLapse`), and the programs they started or held, with each cohort's own length (`ProgramRepository.enrollmentsOf`). **Not derivable cleanly yet:** (1) **A subscription's commitment.** There is **no annual plan yet** (the owner, 2026-11-11). The longer commitments today are the courses The Ready Writer and Self-Editing Academy (6 to 12 months, membership included), so **program length is the commitment signal for now**, and the Results panel already shows it from each cohort's own dates. Annual plans are planned: when they launch, take a dated look at how they are sold (Stripe's price interval is structured, `bronze.stripe_subscriptions.data.items.data[].price.recurring.interval` and `interval_count`, and all 393 subscriptions in production on 2026-11-11 were monthly; Kajabi offers, `bronze.kajabi_offers.data.attributes`, carry only `subscription`, `one_time` and `recurring_offer` booleans and a free-text `price_description`, so a Kajabi annual plan can't be told from a monthly one) before parsing anything. Start then with `BillingRepository.stripeSubscriptionsByCustomers` (it returns `data`) for members with a Stripe customer, and ask the owner how Kajabi annual offers are priced before reading `price_description`. (2) **A course the member started.** `course_access.starts_at` says access began (a membership grants many courses), not that they started one; "started" would be the first `lesson_progress.created_at` in a window, which has no dated read (`CourseProgressRepository` reads by lesson ids) and is not trustworthy for Kajabi-imported rows (`imported_from`). Start by adding `CourseProgressRepository.firstProgressBetween(memberId, from, to)` over rows with `imported_from IS NULL`, plus an RLS check that the community manager may read it (she cannot read `lesson_progress`; the Results action already uses the service role for the membership read). (3) Retention reads the membership history known **today**, so a cancellation recorded later changes an old checkpoint; a cohort view that needs a fixed answer would have to store it. (4) The checkpoint days are a starting point to confirm, and any weighting of the three states for a cohort view must be config.
- **A section on the member's profile.** The lists edit in place and there is no detail page; a member's own engagements (`CareRepository.forMember` exists) could show on `/admin/members/<id>`, which a community manager may open.
- **A member merge drops their engagements** (`care_engagements` is in `NOT_MOVED`, `src/lib/member-merge-coverage.ts`): moving them needs the one-open-per-member rule handled (a clash stays with the duplicate, as `MentorshipRepository.moveMemberRecords` does for matches).
- **The Activity Log shows a start or close twice** to an admin (the audit row of the table's own change, and the `member_activities` row on the member's timeline), as the design asked; decide which one the feed should keep if it reads as noise.
- **`get_activity_feed`'s page-view branch still labels a community manager's visits as a member's** (`actor_kind` there lists only `admin` and `assistant`, `20270503000000`); `audit_row_change()` was fixed in `20270624000000`. She cannot read the feed, so nothing is wrong on screen today.
- **A funnel for the events** (`care_engagement_started`, `care_message_logged`, `care_engagement_closed`, `care_engagement_renewed`) in `src/lib/analytics/funnels.ts`, on `/admin/analytics`.
- **The community manager can read `member_interactions` rows** (who, when, whether in private; never text) through the read-only policy added for the measured results, though the page only shows counts. Narrow the policy (a view or a function that returns counts) if that is more than she should see.
- **Why the results use the service role after the staff check.** The community manager's own client can read the membership timeline's billing Bronze (`kajabi_customers`, `kajabi_purchases`, `kajabi_offers`, `stripe_customers`, `stripe_subscriptions`), the hiatus history and `members` (`20270430000500_community_manager_role.sql`), but no policy found gives her `member_status_overrides` or `member_program_enrollments`, which the membership at each checkpoint and the programs started need. So `getEngagementResults` hands the service role to `loadCareResults` after `requireActionPermission(...)` and `care.write`, and only the derived answer leaves it. Granting her read access to those two tables (a view or function with only what is needed) would let the results use her own client.
- **Naming may still change: "care plan".** The words went from "engagement period" to "care period" and then to **care plan** (2026-11-12: a plan has an explicit timeline, and when it ends there is simply no explicit plan for the member, not "we stop caring"). Only the words changed: the table, columns, routes, config keys (`careEngagementDays`, `careLapseGraceDays`...), events and code are still `care_engagement*` and `engagement`, nothing in the database was renamed. If the term changes again, the copy is in `src/app/(admin)/admin/care/` and the action messages in its `actions.ts`; renaming the identifiers would need the expand-then-contract two-PR path.
- **Plan messages: built (2026-11-14, draft PR #1251, wording for the owner's review); what is left.** The Messages panel on a care plan shows the full messages of every DM tracked with the member (tracking exists only because a plan does: made when a plan is started, a hand-over accepted or an ownerless plan claimed) sent inside the member's care plans' windows, to admins and community managers, per member across plans and owners, with an audit entry (`docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md`, "Plan messages"). Still open: (1) **Summaries of a conversation** (a short account of how a plan went, instead of reading it) are a future idea that needs an AI SDK, which the Hub does not have and which would send members' private messages to a third party: not built, not to be added without the owner's explicit decision and a change to `/privacy`. (2) **Messages after a hand-over** appear once the receiver's own DM with the member is tracked, which accepting the plan does; until the receiver has such a DM (or Slack connected) only the earlier owner's DM is read. (3) **Files** in a plan message are only marked "(with a file)"; their content is not shown. (4) **Threads** are shown flat by time. (5) **Group DMs** are still out of Hedgie Care. (6) The panel shows the current text of an edited message, not its history. (7) **Legacy tracking rows** from the opt-in design that have no plan of that staff member behind them would show their DM inside the member's plan windows; check production (`care_tracked_dms` against `care_engagements`) before it ships.
- **The start form's "When did this start?" could default from the tracked DM** _(Needs Scheduling)_. When she picks a member whose Slack DM she tracks, prefill the day her latest run of messages with them began (an editable prefill with a short hint, "from your DM"): a new run starts after a gap longer than a config value (`careConversationGapDays` in `config/app.ts`, read through `src/lib/config.ts`, starting at 7), never earlier than the DM's first message and never in the future; a pure, tested function over message times. Deferred because it is not small: it needs the times of all of a DM's messages (today `SlackMessageRepository.lastWritesIn` returns only the latest per person, through the service-role-only `care_dm_last_messages` function, because these are private DMs), so a new service-role-only database function of the same shape (metadata only, a migration with grants and a pgTAP test, `pnpm types:gen`), a repository method in both implementations and the contract, a staff action called when the member is picked (only for her own tracked DM, re-found in her own DM list like `findOwnDm`), and the form wiring (`StartEngagementForm.tsx`, `slackDmMemberIds` already tells which members are tracked). For a Slack DM she has not tracked yet (starting auto-tracks it) the same lookup could use `findOwnDmWith`.
- **The engagement score is written in two places.** `src/lib/member-engagement.ts` (`computeMemberEngagementMetrics`, `engagementScoreAt`) and the SQL function `admin_member_engagement` (`20270516000000`: `least(100, sum)`, 50 and 10 for the tiers) repeat the rule (30 days of points, capped at 100; highly engaged from 50, active from 10). The care rows use the TypeScript one and the Members list the SQL one; they agree today. Not changed here (it feeds the admin Members page): if the thresholds ever change, change both, or move the list onto the TypeScript rule.
- **"Last in the community" is a list in `src/lib/care-participation.ts`.** It counts the member-made activity types listed there (prickle, Slack message or reaction, lesson, check-in or check-out, prickle commitment, writing log), never logins, feedback, taking a check-in back, or anything staff or the system wrote. A new kind of member activity does not count until it is added there; the owner may want a different set (reactions, writing logs and commitments are the debatable ones). Hub-only chat messages and DMs that never reach Slack write no activity row, so they do not show.
- **A care plan's first message is a placeholder.** Starting one writes "somebody wrote" at its start (now for today, the start of the Eastern day for an earlier day, so a real DM from that morning can replace it). Times that came from a log or a tracked DM are never moved by "Change start date", and moving the start later does not unwind times a DM already filled in (they only move forward).

- **Notes on the first build's `conversations` table:** dropped in `20270626000000` with an approved squawk ignore (empty in production).


### Hedgie Care: notify the receiver of a hand-over (createNotifier kind, dry run first) _(Needs Scheduling)_
A care plan changes hands only when the receiver accepts (`docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md`, "Owners"), and the receiver is told **only by the page** ("Waiting for you" on Hedgie Care) for now: nothing is sent. A notification must go through `createNotifier` (`src/lib/notifications/notify.ts`) as a kind registered in `src/lib/notifications/registry.ts`, with its sample in `src/lib/notification-test.ts` and an email template; **start in dry run behind a feature flag** that says who gets it for real (`liveFor` in `src/lib/member-reminders.ts`, the `prickle_reminders` flag in `src/lib/features.ts`), logging the receiver, kind and message and writing no dedup row, then widen. Link to the exact thing: the Hedgie Care page (where the offer waits under "Waiting for you"), never a page that merely relates to it, and say what and ask the question ("Cam would like you to take over the care plan with a member. Accept or decline"), with no member name beyond what the page shows to staff; claim the send in `NotificationLogRepository` keyed by plan and offer time, so a re-offer notifies again and a refresh does not. The receiver is a staff member, so check the kinds' audience rules (the notifier is built for members) before assuming the registry fits; and tell the proposer when the offer is answered. Start at `propose_care_handover` (`supabase/migrations/20270726000000_care_handover.sql`), `proposeImpl` in `src/app/(admin)/admin/care/actions.ts`, and the "Reminders and alerts for overdue engagements" entry above, which needs the same kind of staff-facing notification.

### Hedgie Care: tell the previous owner of a takeover (createNotifier kind, dry run first) _(Needs Scheduling)_
An admin may take an open care plan over from its owner without their agreement (`docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md`, "Owners"; `claim_care_plan` in `supabase/migrations/20270801090000_care_admin_takeover.sql`), and the previous owner is told **only by the page** (the quiet "Taken over" section on Hedgie Care, `TakeoverNotices.tsx`): nothing is sent, so an owner who is away may not hear for days. A notification must go through `createNotifier` as a kind registered in `src/lib/notifications/registry.ts` (sample in `src/lib/notification-test.ts`, email template in `src/emails/notifications/`) and **start in dry run behind a feature flag** that says who gets it for real (`liveFor` in `src/lib/member-reminders.ts`), logging the recipient, kind and message and writing no dedup row. Link to the exact thing (the Hedgie Care page, where the notice waits), say what and who ("<admin> took over <member>'s care plan"), claim the send in `NotificationLogRepository` keyed by plan and `taken_at`, and stop when the owner dismisses the notice (`taken_seen_at`). The recipient is a staff member, so check the kinds' audience rules before assuming the registry fits, as in "Hedgie Care: notify the receiver of a hand-over" above, which needs the same kind of staff-facing notification.

### Hand-overs need both sides: places that still reassign without consent _(Needs Scheduling)_
`.claude/rules/hand-overs.md` ("Hand-overs need both sides") says a thing that belongs to one person changes hands only when the receiver accepts, with the database refusing a direct change of owner. Hedgie Care is the first user. Noticed while building it, **not changed**, each to be decided with the owner (some are rightly admin decisions, not hand-overs between two people):
- **Big Sister matches** (`src/app/(admin)/admin/mentorship/actions.ts`, `MatchesPanel.tsx`, `MentorshipRepository`): an admin picks the big sister for an arrival and "Reassign" moves it, and the big sister is not asked (members volunteer into a pool, `big_sister_volunteers`, but a specific match is made without her yes). A mentorship is the example the rule names for later.
- **Prickle hosts** (`assignHostToUnhostedPrickles` and `setDefaultHosts` in `src/app/(admin)/admin/data-health/missing-hosts/actions.ts`; the schedule's host): an admin assigns a host to prickles without the host accepting.
- **Anything staff "own" through an admin-only write**, such as an inbox thread or a work-queue item if one gains an owner: build it on the same shape (a proposal on the row, an accept function, a trigger that refuses a direct change), not a free `UPDATE`.
The shape to copy is `supabase/migrations/20270726000000_care_handover.sql` (`propose_`, `accept_`, `decline_`, `cancel_` functions as `SECURITY INVOKER`, a `BEFORE UPDATE` guard keyed on a transaction-local setting) with `src/lib/care-handover.ts` for what the page shows.

### Hedgie Care: what is left in the unified timeline _(Needs Scheduling)_
**Resolved (2026-11-14):** the plan card's **Activity during this plan** lists the member's own activity and the Hub's emails to them inside the plan's window, with the plan's own care rows, newest first and paginated, for admins and the community manager (`ActivityRepository.planActivity`, `src/lib/care-activity.ts`, `docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md`, "Activity during this plan"); and every event of a plan reads as a sentence in the Activity Log ("Care plans in the Activity Log"). **Still open:** (1) **emails she receives and sends outside the Hub** (Resend `email.received` and her "send as", `docs/architecture/EMAIL_TIMELINE.md`, the "Email" section of `docs/product/HEDGIE_CARE.md`): nothing of them is in `member_activities` yet, so the list cannot show them; once Resend rows with her address exist, add the types to `PLAN_EMAIL_TYPES` (`src/lib/care-activity.ts`) and decide whether a sent reply is the member's or staff's row (`actor_kind`); (2) **Instagram DMs**, the same way once they reach the Hub; (3) **the plan's Messages panel** from draft PR #1251 (the tracked DM, read inside the plan by the team): a privacy decision of the owner, and its privacy text must be rewritten for automatic tracking first (see "Hedgie Care: rewrite draft PR #1251's privacy text for automatic tracking"); the activity list shows where and when a Slack or chat message happened, never its text, and should link to the DM only through "Open the DM". **Privacy points that still hold:** only staff-to-member communication and the member's own activity, never a DM between two other members; nothing a member typed into a place they believe is private may reach staff who do not already have it.

### Hedgie Care: manual touches carry a channel when more channels exist _(Needs Scheduling)_
Plans no longer have a channel, and the manual log buttons ("They replied", "I replied") only move two times. When emails and Instagram DMs reach the Hub, a manual touch (a phone call, a prickle chat, an email she sent outside the Hub) may need to say which way it happened, so the unified timeline can show it. Add the channel to the touch (a row of its own or a column on a touch log), not back onto the plan. Start from `CareRepository.logMessage` and the `care_message_logged` event; decide the list of channels together with the unified timeline above. A manual log writes no row in the Activity Log today (it moves a time on the plan, as a tracked DM does, and is not an event someone did to the member's record); once a touch has a channel and a row of its own, give it a sentence in `src/lib/care-events.ts` like the other events.


### Screenshots for changelog entries that still have none _(Needs Scheduling)_
Entries shipped 2026-11-16 to 2026-11-18 with no image: the product entries `goal-start-date-starting-count-note`, and the staff entries `landing-page-build-with-ai`, `duplicate-course`, `course-address-redirects` and `member-activity-date-range`. Why deferred: the demo stack took several tries to start on a busy Docker host, and a scene has to wait for slow first loads (the two-step scene waits for the QR picture, see `staff-two-step-sign-in.spec.ts`). Where to start: `pnpm demo:stack reset && pnpm demo:stack up` (give it its own `DEMO_STACK_ID` and `DEMO_PORT_SHIFT` beside other worktrees' stacks), `pnpm demo:seed`, then write one `tests/demo/scenes/changelog/<slug>.spec.ts` per entry (see `security-page.spec.ts`) and run `pnpm changelog:capture <slug>`. For the goal note, a project with a starting count and a goal with a start date needs adding to the demo seed (`demo/member-content-seed.ts`).

### `pnpm demo:app` and the other `dotenv -e` scripts fail with the `dotenv` 18 bin
**Resolved (2026-11-19):** the `dotenv-cli` package was removed and every script now calls the `dotenv` 18 bin as `dotenv run -f <file> -- <cmd>` (`-f` repeats for several files; a variable such as `NEXT_DIST_DIR` is set before the command). The original note follows.

`package.json` has both `dotenv` (18, which came in with the dependency bump) and `dotenv-cli` (11), and both install a `dotenv` command. In a fresh install `node_modules/.bin/dotenv` printed the `dotenv run [-f <paths>] -- <cmd>` help and exited 1 for `dotenv -e demo/.env.demo -- next dev`, so `pnpm demo:app` (and `demo:build`, `demo:serve`, and the `-e .env.prod` scripts such as `db:push` and `config:push`) did not start. Worked around by running `dotenv run -f demo/.env.demo -- next dev -p 3100`. Where to start: check which package owns the bin in `pnpm-lock.yaml`, then either drop the `dotenv` package if the app does not import it, or call `dotenv-cli` explicitly in the scripts; confirm with `pnpm demo:app`.

### Hedgie Care screenshots for the staff changelog entry _(Needs Scheduling)_

A screenshot needs a demo scene that carries care plans and the final UI: the demo community has no care plans, and the UI (the start dialog, the tabs, the results panel) was still changing. Where to start: write `tests/demo/scenes/changelog/hedgie-care.spec.ts` tagged `@changelog` (see `whats-new-changelog.spec.ts`), seed a few care plans for fictional members in the demo seed (`pnpm demo:seed`), sign in as the demo community manager, then run `pnpm changelog:capture hedgie-care`, look at every image, and reference them from the entry (fictional demo community only). ### Hedgie Care: member announcement entry at launch _(Needs Scheduling)_
Members should hear about the Hedgie Care program when it launches: a `product` entry (no screenshots) that follows `.claude/rules/changelog-writing.md` and is run through the `member-reader-review` skill before it goes in. It is **not** written yet on purpose: an entry publishes to members, the weekly digest and the announcements post when it merges, so it waits for the owner to say the program is announced. The privacy rules it describes (the Messages panel, `/privacy`) have been live since 2026-11-18, so the facts below can be checked against `main` now. The staff entry `hedgie-care` is dated 2026-11-12, the day the first care plan was created in production. It must cover, each only as far as it is verified in the code and docs on `main` at that time: what a care plan is (the community manager reaching out to a member, for a stretch of time), what is recorded (who spoke last and when comes from who wrote and when in the DM; the messages themselves are read only as described below; the member's timeline shows that a plan started, was renewed or closed; her note is not shown), who can read the messages of a Slack DM in a plan (the facts as built in the Messages panel: see below), a link to `/privacy`, and, only if checked (no AI service in `package.json`, no SDK or API host for one, nothing sending member text to a third party), a calm sentence that no AI touches their words. Date it the day the program goes live for members; `changelogDigestStartsOn` and the digest flag decide who is told.
**Privacy facts the entry must reflect (as built in the Messages panel, draft PR #1251; re-verify against `main` before writing).** (1) Starting a care plan tracks the staff member's one-on-one Slack DM with the member when the Hub has it, automatically: there is no choice to make and no way to stop it or opt out. Accepting a plan handed over tracks the receiver's own DM with the member too. (2) Admins and community managers (not assistants) can read in Hedgie Care the full messages sent in the tracked DM(s) from the start of each of the member's care plans to its close (or until now while it is open), including earlier plans whoever held them (a plan handed over or taken over keeps the earlier owner's DM readable; whoever holds the plan sees the earlier plans' messages); deleted messages, messages outside a plan and files' contents are not shown. (3) Opening them writes an audit entry (who looked, whose plan, when; never the messages), shown in the Activity Log's Privacy view, and nothing is shown if the entry cannot be written. (4) Hub Chat shows a note at the top of such a DM, for everyone in it: "Our community manager and admins can read what is sent in this conversation during a Hedgie Care plan, our way of staying in touch with members." Slack's own app shows no note. (5) Every other DM with a staff member is treated like a DM between members: break-glass access only; no chat policy changed. (6) Members cannot opt out of their plan's DM being tracked or read: nothing in the code offers it, so the announcement must not promise it (the owner's call whether to offer one). (7) `/privacy` has the section "Messages in a Hedgie Care plan" (`#care-plan-messages`); the announcement links to it. Words for the member: say "special access" for break-glass, "private message" is fine for DM, name "our community manager and admins" rather than "the team", say "a log of who looked" rather than "audit log" (the member-reader-review's advice on the first draft).

### Stale private-channel memberships in the chat mirror: what is left

### Removal on request for records with a required member id _(what is left)_

`/privacy` promises that when a member asks us to remove something we "either delete it or take your name off it". The routine (`remove_member_data`) and the staff entry point ("Remove on request" on the admin member page) exist; what is left is below. For usage events the design is a scrub that nulls the identity columns and keeps the row (`docs/architecture/EVENT_LOG.md`, "What a scrub does"). Records a member owns by a required id (writing goals, progress entries, books, awards, check-ins, commitments, notes) have `member_id NOT NULL` and can't be scrubbed that way.

**Decided: re-point them at a placeholder "former member" row.** That keeps the record's shape and loses the person. The catch: the content itself can identify someone (a book title, a note in their own words), so a table whose content identifies gets its rows deleted instead; decide that per table when building, next to its retention in the event catalog.

**Order of operations:** a scrub or re-point is irreversible and untraceable (afterwards we can't find a person's rows to delete them later or to answer an access request), so a request for true deletion must happen before any anonymising.

**Needed before launch (owner, 2026-11-25): the Hub must be GDPR compliant, so the items below that hold personal data are launch work, not nice-to-have.** Two things follow from the owner's review. **First, the source systems.** A copy kept in Bronze cannot be removed for good while the source still has it: the next import brings it back (the fingerprints in `removal_tombstones` stop only what the removal already knows by address or key). So a removal is not complete until the person is also removed or anonymised upstream, and the panel should say that as a checklist the staff member ticks, one line per system that holds something (Kajabi: contact, comments, quiz submissions; Stripe; Zoom; Slack), shown after the removal and recorded in the request log below (a tick and a date, no personal data). **Second, a former member's lesson comments:** see "Removal on request: lesson comments keep their text" below.

**Still open:** (1) **Bronze copies of the other imports are not covered**: the Kajabi course capture (quiz responses, progress, matched comments) is, but the Kajabi contact, customer, purchase and member copies (`bronze.kajabi_contacts`, `kajabi_customers`, `kajabi_purchases`, `kajabi_members`, `subscription_history`), Stripe, Zoom and Slack imports are keyed by email or an external id, so until they are handled the person has to be removed at the source first, or the next import brings them back (they would arrive as a new member, not as the placeholder). The panel says so after a removal. **Where to start:** the pattern in `supabase/migrations/20271127000000_removal_covers_quiz_and_kajabi_capture.sql` (`member_removal_emails`, deletion by those addresses in `remove_member_data`, a line in `member_removal_extras`, a fingerprint in `removal_tombstones`); the catch is that a returning customer is legitimate there (a contact or payment for the same address is how a member is made), so a fingerprint can't simply block those tables: decide what "removed" means for them first; (2) usage events and login history are deleted with their user (their column is NOT NULL), not scrubbed as `docs/architecture/EVENT_LOG.md` describes, so only the rollups keep their counts; (3) files in storage other than the media library and feedback screenshots are not deleted (book covers of deleted books in `book-covers`, event or chat files that name them), and the media library's sized copies are left to the daily sweep; (4) copy the member's name out of chat and Slack-derived content if it should go too (`chat_messages` keeps the author as unknown only).

### Removal on request: lesson comments keep their text _(Needs Scheduling)_

**What is wrong:** `docs/product/COURSES.md` ("Privacy and retention", under lesson comments) says that on a deletion request the author and text are cleared and the replies keep a tombstone. What the removal does today (checked 2026-11-25) is different: the comments a member wrote stay under the "Former member" placeholder **with their text** (`member_data_handling`, row for `lesson_comments`; the quiz and Kajabi-capture removal left them as they were). A comment's words are the member's own writing and can identify them (names, details, style, the thread around it), so "Former member" is not by itself the same as removed. The owner wants replies to stay inline in their thread, which is compatible with erasure: **keep the row as a tombstone** (no author, no text, "This comment was removed"), so the thread's shape survives. **Not legal advice; confirm with counsel** whether keeping text under a placeholder is acceptable for a member who has not asked for erasure (a member who only left) and require clearing it when they ask.

**Where to start:** `member_data_handling` and `remove_member_data` (`supabase/migrations/20271127000000_removal_covers_quiz_and_kajabi_capture.sql` is the latest edit), `lesson_comments` (`body`, `author_member_id`, soft delete columns and the retention job that clears the text of deleted comments after 30 days), the thread renderer for a removed comment (it already shows deleted ones as a tombstone). A removal on request should clear `body` and the author for the member's comments and their replies' quotes of them, keep replies, and the Kajabi capture's tombstone keys already stop the Bronze copy from coming back. **Also:** the comment's `source_key` ties it to Bronze; the Hub-side clearing and the Bronze deletion must stay in step. Add a test: a removed member's comment shows as removed, its reply is still in the thread, and re-running the comments import does not restore the text.
