### OSPS Baseline: what is left _(Needs Scheduling)_

`docs/checklists/OSPS_BASELINE.md` (assessed 2026-11-23, catalog `v2026.08.28`) maps every control. The cheap ones were fixed with it (the "Access" and "Dependencies" sections of `.github/GOVERNANCE.md`); what needs a decision or the owner is left:
- **Workflow token default** (`OSPS-AC-04.01`). **Resolved (2026-11-23):** set to `read` by hand in Settings, Actions, General (live `default_workflow_permissions` is `read`). Every workflow declares its own `permissions:`, and the one that opens PRs uses `GH_TOKEN_AUTO_MERGE`, so nothing relied on the default. Also **resolved:** it is now in code (`workflow_permissions:` in `config/github.yml`, applied by `scripts/github-config.ts` through `PUT repos/<repo>/actions/permissions/workflow`, with `can_approve_pull_request_reviews` kept `true`; `pnpm github:config:diff` shows drift). **Still open:** nothing.
- **`CODEOWNERS`.** **Resolved (2026-11-23):** `.github/CODEOWNERS` is `* @quillandcup/eng` (the engineering team owns everything), and the team has Write on the repository. `require_code_owner_review` stays off (zero approvals and auto-merge), so it asks for review without requiring it. **Still open:** confirm `gh api repos/quillandcup/hub/codeowners/errors` shows no errors once the file is on `main`; split the file by path when a second team owns part of the repository.
- **Remediation thresholds** (`OSPS-VM-05.04`, `OSPS-VM-06.01`). **Resolved (2026-11-23):** `.github/SECURITY.md`, "How fast findings are fixed" (Critical 10 days, High 30, Medium 60, Low 90); the owners should confirm the numbers. **Resolved (2026-11-23, license policy, `OSPS-VM-05.05`):** `license-scan.yml` and `config/license-policy.json`; the first scan found 0 findings with 8 accepted exceptions (4 LGPL packages, one fair-source license, one data license, one bare "BSD", one with no license metadata), all expiring in 2027. The scan reads the installed packages, so platform-specific ones differ between a laptop and the Linux runner; the first CI run may need an exception added. **Still open:** the pre-release rule (`OSPS-VM-05.02`) is Partly until the ruleset with the new `OSV diff / osv-scan` check is pushed (next item). The deadlines are a target, not a gate: nothing computes how old a finding is, the weekly scans just keep failing and alerting while one is open.
- **Blocking dependency checks** (`OSPS-VM-05.03`). **Resolved (2026-11-23):** `.github/workflows/osv-diff.yml` runs OSV-Scanner's diff mode on every PR and fails only on a vulnerability the PR introduces; `OSV diff / osv-scan` is a required check in `config/github.yml` and, since the owner ran `pnpm github:config:push` (2026-11-23), in the live `main` ruleset; `OSPS-VM-05.02` and `-05.03` are Met. **Still open:** **Known bypass:** the check reads `config/osv-scanner.toml`, so a PR can add an ignore for the very vulnerability it introduces and pass. Only review notices it. Not fixed because nothing in the repository can tell a justified ignore from a convenient one; the options are to require owner review for that file (`CODEOWNERS` is `* @quillandcup/eng`, but code-owner review is off) or to have the diff job read the ignore list from the base branch, so a PR cannot loosen the rules it is judged by. Start in `.github/workflows/osv-diff.yml`.
- **Dependabot security updates.** **Resolved (2026-11-23):** enabled (live `gh api repos/quillandcup/hub/automated-security-fixes` returns `enabled: true`). `auto-merge.yml` doesn't merge a security major: it enables auto-merge for a Dependabot pull request only when `fetch-metadata` reports a patch or minor update, and an empty or major result leaves it off (read 2026-11-23, not exercised on a live security update yet). **Still open:** check the open alerts against the `npm audit` findings.
- **What the OpenSSF scanner found (2026-11-23, `pvtr-github-repo-scanner` v0.33.2).** Added `.github/security-insights.yml` (validated with the schema's CUE definition) and a build heading in the README for it. **Still open:** (1) `can_approve_pull_request_reviews` **Resolved (2026-11-24):** `config/github.yml` declares it `false` (no workflow approves a pull request, and the two that open or merge one use their own tokens) and the owner applied it with `pnpm github:config:push`; the live setting is `false` and `pnpm github:config:diff` shows no change; (2) `OSPS-QA-03.01` **Resolved (2026-11-24):** Met. The checks that can fail because of a change are all required; the five that are not (the `Analyze` jobs, `Detect docs-only change`, `Code quality report`, `auto-merge`, the screenshot cleanup) are plumbing or report-only, and a check with a path or event condition cannot be required (one that never reports blocks the merge). The OpenSSF scanner will keep failing it; (3) re-run the scanner after the Security Insights file is on `main` and record which Security Insights dependent checks now pass; (4) the scanner reports `OSPS-DO-03.01` and `-DO-03.02` failed because there is no `signature-verification` link; we publish no signed releases, so there is nothing true to link, and the rows stay N/A. If releases ever exist, write that guide and add the link. Re-run recipe: download the release, check it against `checksums.txt`, read its config, and pass the token as JSON in the `PVTR_VARS` environment variable of that one process, for example `PVTR_VARS="$(jq -n -c --arg owner OWNER --arg repo REPO --arg token "$TOKEN" "{owner: \$owner, repo: \$repo, token: \$token}")"` (no file; a named pipe races). The scanner takes `owner`, `repo` and `token` from its `vars`, and Privateer reads `PVTR_*` environment variables ahead of the config file.
- **Licenses at pull request time** (`OSPS-VM-05.05`). Today `license-scan.yml` checks every installed package against `config/license-policy.json` once a week and gates nothing, so a pull request that adds a GPL package merges and is found within a week. Blocking it in the PR (a required check that fails on a package the PR adds, for example `actions/dependency-review-action` with `deny-licenses`, or the same policy file read in a diff against the base branch) is its own decision: it adds a required check, and the policy file is editable in the same PR, so it would be the same bypass as the OSV ignore list above. Worth doing if a dependency with a bad license is ever added by accident; start in `.github/workflows/license-scan.yml` and `config/license-policy.json`.
- **Not pursued, by nature of the project**: an OSI license (`OSPS-LE-02.01`), a DCO or CLA (`OSPS-LE-01.01`) and a second human approver (`OSPS-QA-07.01`).

### OSPS Baseline: the three design rows _(Needs Scheduling)_

Left Partly on purpose (2026-11-24) because a thin version would be paperwork. Worth doing because each would be used, in the order below.

- **`OSPS-SA-02.01`, describe the real external interfaces.** `docs/architecture/API_ROUTES.md` lists all 173 route handlers and who may call each, which guards the credential check (a test fails on a route with none) but says nothing about what to send or get back. 157 of the routes are called only by the app's own pages and need no more than that. The 16 reachable without a session (7 webhooks, 2 cron, 2 public forms, 2 one-time-token links, the calendar feed, a signed link, the sign-in code exchange) are the real external interfaces: describe each with its request, response and error cases, in OpenAPI or plain tables. Nothing in the repository validates request bodies with a schema (no `zod` or similar in the routes), so there is nothing to generate it from: write it by hand, and add a test that every one of the 16 is described, so a new external route fails until it is. Start from `docs/infrastructure/WEBHOOK_SETUP.md` and the credential kinds in `scripts/api-route-inventory.ts`.
- **`OSPS-SA-01.01`, actors and actions.** Who can do what is spread over `docs/architecture/PERMISSIONS.md`, `src/lib/permissions.ts` and the sequence diagrams. A generated actor-by-action table from the permissions registry (guest, member, each staff role, the Slack, Zoom, Kajabi and calendar integrations, the cron jobs), with a test that fails when a permission has no row, would be used when adding a feature. Start in `src/lib/permissions.ts`.
- **`OSPS-SA-03.02`, attack surface of the critical code paths.** `docs/trust/THREAT_MODEL.md` has the OWASP view and "Entry points and how each is guarded", but no separate ranked analysis of the paths where a bug costs the most: sign-in and session, the service-role client, webhooks, stored HTML rendering, outbound fetches, `SECURITY DEFINER` functions, file and image routes. A short document per path (what reaches it, what guards it, what test proves it, what is left) would turn the existing reviews into one place to read before touching them.

### GUAC: considered, not planned

GUAC (Graph for Understanding Artifact Composition, from OpenSSF) ingests SBOMs, attestations and scanner output from many projects into one graph to answer supply-chain questions. Not planned: this is a single project with no corpus of SBOMs or attestations to analyze, and OSV-Scanner and Dependabot already answer "which of our dependencies are known-vulnerable". Revisit if the number of repositories or published artifacts grows, or once SBOMs and build attestations exist for more than one thing.

### Deploy provenance: what is left _(Needs Scheduling)_

**Resolved (2026-11-23):** the deploy signs a build-provenance statement and a CycloneDX SBOM statement for each production deploy (`attest` job in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, how to verify in `docs/infrastructure/DEPLOYMENT.md`, "Verifying what was deployed"). The first run on `main` passed and both `gh attestation verify` commands succeed.

**Still open:**
- **A policy gate.** Nothing checks an attestation before or after a deploy; the signature is a record, not a control. Worth doing if a deploy path other than CI appears, or when a second consumer needs to trust the build. Start with `gh attestation verify` in a post-deploy step and the `--signer-workflow` check.
- **A reproducible build.** Needed for anything above SLSA Build L2 (the claim in `DEPLOYMENT.md` is L1 plus the signing properties of L2). Worth doing only if a requirement asks for L3; the build runs in an ordinary workflow job today.
- **Matching a signed manifest to a deployment's files** is manual. Worth automating if anyone starts relying on the manifest.

### GitHub App instead of personal access tokens _(Needs Scheduling)_

Three workflows act as a person through fine-grained personal access tokens: `auto-merge.yml` (`GH_TOKEN_AUTO_MERGE`), `disposable-email-domains.yml` (`GH_TOKEN_DISPOSABLE_EMAIL_DOMAINS`) and the Slack manifest job (`GH_TOKEN_SLACK_MANIFEST`). They exist because the workflow's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` can't do what they need: a pull request it opens doesn't start the required checks, a merge it makes doesn't start the `main` run (migrations, deploy), and it can't write secrets. An organization-owned GitHub App would do the same jobs without a person's account behind them. Not done because it costs a one-off setup (create the App, install it on the repository, store its id and private key as secrets, mint a token per run with `actions/create-github-app-token`) and the tokens work today. Worth doing when one of them expires or breaks unexpectedly, when the person who owns them leaves, or when a second owner needs to rotate them.

What an App gives that the tokens can't:
- **No person behind it.** A personal token is the creator's access: their account leaving the organization, losing access or having 2FA reset breaks every workflow that uses it, and everything it does (auto-merge, the refresh PR) is attributed to them. An App is owned by the organization and acts as its own bot.
- **Short-lived credentials.** The App's installation token is minted for each run and lapses within the hour, so a leaked log line or secret is nearly worthless. A personal token lives until its chosen expiry (up to a year), when it silently starts failing the workflows and someone has to remember to rotate it.
- **Narrower and separate by design.** Permissions are set on the App and on the repository it is installed on, independent of anyone's own access, and one App can carry exactly the permissions of all three jobs without a per-token copy to keep in sync.
- **A real audit trail.** Pull requests, merges and secret writes show as the App, so the Activity Log and the audit log show automation separately from a person's work.

What it would not change: a merge or pull request made with an App token starts the same workflows as one made with a personal token (that is the property we need), and it does not remove the need to guard the App's private key (which sits in a GitHub secret like the tokens do today).

A second use: the weekly OpenSSF scan (`pvtr-scan.yml`) runs with the built-in token, which cannot read branch rules, so `OSPS-AC-03.01`, `-QA-03.01` and `-QA-07.01` read Needs Review in CI and Failed when an administrator runs it (`config/pvtr-baseline.json` accepts either). A built-in token cannot be given the administration read it would need, and a new personal token with it would be one more long-lived credential. An App installation token with only Administration: read, minted for that one job, would let the scan see the rules, and the three baseline entries could then record one exact result each, so a weakened ruleset shows up as a change.

### Repo Security Settings and Scans _(Needs Scheduling)_
Found 2026-10-22 by comparing the live repo's `security_and_analysis` (read with
`gh api repos/quillandcup/hub`) against what GitHub offers for public repos. Secret scanning and push
protection are already on; these four are not. All are free on a public repo and none changes code,
so none was urgent. Deferred to batch them.

- [ ] **Secret scanning: non-provider patterns.** Off today. Adds private keys, connection strings
      and generic high-entropy keys to what push protection and alerts catch (provider-pattern
      scanning only covers known token formats). Settings → Advanced Security → "Scan for
      non-provider patterns". Expect some false positives on test fixtures and the local-dev demo
      Supabase key; dismiss those with a reason ("used in tests"), or add narrow `paths-ignore`
      entries in `.github/secret_scanning.yml` (it also skips push protection for those paths, so keep it specific).
- [ ] **Secret scanning: validity checks.** Off today. GitHub tests a detected credential against
      the provider and marks the alert `active`, `inactive` or `unknown`, which tells us whether a
      leak needs rotating now (`docs/runbooks/SECRET_ROTATION.md`) or is already dead. Same settings page.
- [ ] **Dependabot security updates.** Enabled 2026-11-23; the "doesn't merge a security major" check is done by reading `auto-merge.yml`, only the alerts-against-`npm audit` comparison is still to do. It was disabled, though `.github/dependabot.yml` already
      runs weekly version updates (npm and github-actions) and `auto-merge.yml` merges the
      patch/minor ones. Security updates are a separate setting: they open a PR as soon as an
      advisory affects a dependency, instead of waiting for the weekly run, and can raise a
      version the weekly grouping wouldn't. Enable it, then check the open alerts alongside the
      `npm audit` findings in "Migrate to Tailwind CSS 4". Majors still need a human; confirm
      `auto-merge.yml` doesn't merge a security major.
- [ ] **Run the built-in `/security-review` skill on a schedule?** Undecided; the question is
      whether it finds enough to be worth the tokens. Try it once on the whole repo and on a recent
      auth- or RLS-touching PR, and record what it found that `pnpm test:unit`, the pgTAP suite
      and `/code-review` didn't. If it earns its keep, run it before merging changes to auth, RLS,
      `src/proxy.ts`, webhooks or anything that exposes ids, rather than on a calendar (a cron
      agent via the `schedule` skill is the alternative). If it only repeats what the checks above
      already enforce (`requireAdminPage` test, Tier 2 id rule, Slack-content grants), drop this
      item.

Where to start: check whether the `settings:` block of `config/github.yml` (applied by
`scripts/github-config.ts`) can carry these. If it can't, toggle them in the GitHub UI and note
here that they live outside the config-as-code file.

### User Invitations
- **In-app invite management**
  - Admin page to send invites with pre-set roles
  - Email templates for invitations
  - Track invite status and expiration

### Row Level Security (RLS)
- **Role-based restrictions**
  - Add assistant/member roles when we have non-admin users
  - Update policies to restrict based on role
  - Test thoroughly before granting access to non-admins

### Supabase Security Advisories (Skipped)
**Resolved 2026-11-18 (review and guard):** the advisor reports 51 "signed-in users can execute a SECURITY DEFINER function" warnings, not two, plus `is_admin()` for `anon`. Each of the 51 was read on 2026-11-18: each checks the caller or is a deliberate, limited read of other members' data (member directory aliases and their search, a member's chosen profile writing, per-mentor match counts) or a wrapper over a checked function. `supabase/tests/database/security_guards.test.sql` now fails CI for a new one with no recognizable check, without a pinned `search_path`, or runnable by `anon` (`docs/trust/RLS_SECURITY.md`, "Security definer functions and the guard"). **Still open:** the guard proves a check exists, not that it is right, so a person reads each new definer function in its PR; and confirm the mentor match counts (readable by any signed-in member) match what members were told. The advisories below are the original two, kept for history.

Two low-severity advisories from `supabase db advisors --linked --type security` were left unresolved after the 2026-08 RLS/search_path hardening pass (see `supabase/migrations/20260720034138_enable_rls_on_exposed_tables.sql` and `20260807023835_harden_search_path_and_definer_grants.sql`):

- **`anon_security_definer_function_executable` / `authenticated_security_definer_function_executable` on `is_admin()` and `create_user_profile()`** — both are `SECURITY DEFINER` and still grant `EXECUTE` to `anon`/`authenticated`. Not revoked because:
  - `create_user_profile()` is the `on_auth_user_created` trigger function that provisions a `user_profiles` row on signup — revoking risks breaking signup.
  - `is_admin()` is called inside the `user_profiles` RLS policies (`USING (is_admin())`) for role `{public}` — revoking `authenticated`/`anon` EXECUTE would likely break those policies for logged-in users, not just tighten anon access.
  - Real-world risk is low: `is_admin()` just returns a boolean based on `auth.uid()` (returns `false` for anon since `auth.uid()` is null), and `create_user_profile()` is a trigger function that errors if invoked directly outside trigger context.
  - **If revisited:** switch `is_admin()` to `SECURITY INVOKER` (it only reads `user_profiles`, which already has its own RLS) instead of touching grants, and/or restrict `create_user_profile()`'s grant to just the role Postgres uses to fire the trigger.

### Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)
Define user roles and permissions:

1. **Member/Customer** (future)
   - View own profile and attendance history
   - Cannot access other members' data
   - Cannot access admin tools

2. **Admin** (current default)
   - Full access to all features
   - Import/process data
   - View all members and prickles
   - Edit members, prickles, aliases

3. **Assistant** (future)
   - Read-only access to member data for support
   - Can view attendance and engagement metrics
   - Cannot edit or delete
   - Cannot access import/process tools

4. **More granular roles** (future consideration)
   - Content Manager - manage prickles/calendar only
   - Analytics Viewer - read-only dashboards
   - Onboarding Specialist - member CRUD only

**Implementation:**
- Add `role` column to user profiles table
- Create RLS policies per role
- Update UI to show/hide features based on role
- Add role management interface for admins

### Scrub personal data from public git history _(Needs Scheduling)_

The repo is public, and some old commits (messages, a seed file, a couple of fixtures and docs) still hold real member or staff names, personal emails, Slack handles and member ids. An old credential is also still in history; the owner confirmed it was rotated, so it is a cleanup, not an emergency. The owner reviewed the commits by hand and withdrew several candidates that turned out to be fine, so the list is theirs: it is in `/tmp/history-private-review.md` on the owner's machine (commit list with GitHub links, subjects with real names already removed), and the strings to replace must be rebuilt from it with the owner. Neither the list nor the strings go in the repo, a PR or a commit message (`public-repo-hygiene.md`).

**Why deferred:** rewriting history needs a force-push to `main`, which the ruleset forbids and only the owner (repo admin) can lift, and it orphans every open branch and worktree. The owner wants it timed for when other PRs have slowed down.

**Plan**
1. Install `git-filter-repo` (not installed). Work on a fresh `git clone --mirror` in a scratch directory outside the repo, never in a worktree.
2. Write a replacements file (`literal==>replacement`, one per string) outside the repo, from the reviewed list. Include the credential, emails, handles and member ids. Use `--replace-message` for commit messages and `--replace-text` for file contents, and `--path` rules for files that should disappear from history entirely (a seed file with real handles; a tracked local settings file and a one-off seed script were suggested as the cheap alternative: untrack them going forward and leave history alone).
3. Dry run: check that no listed string survives (`git log --all -p -S<string>` and `git grep` over every ref for each one), that the tip's tree is identical to the current `main` apart from the replaced strings (`git diff` of the two trees), and that migration filenames and timestamps are unchanged.
4. When PRs have settled: tell the owner, who lifts the `main` ruleset's force-push block (`config/github.yml`; do not change it from a session). Push the rewritten refs with `--force-with-lease` from the mirror. Then the owner restores the ruleset (`pnpm github:config:push`).
5. Every worktree and open branch must be rebased onto the new `main` or recreated; stale clones must not push the old history back. Merged branches' PR refs (`refs/pull/*`) still hold the old commits.
6. Ask GitHub Support to remove cached views and the PR refs for the rewritten commits and to run garbage collection; until then the old commits stay reachable by hash.
7. Rotate or revoke anything that was live in the old history, whether or not it is believed to be rotated already.

**Do not**: force-push from a session without the owner present, lift or edit the ruleset yourself, or paste any flagged string into a commit, PR, issue or log. Report counts only.

`/compliance`, `/security` and the staff pages `/admin/compliance`, `/admin/privacy` and `/admin/security` are live (`src/content/security.ts` feeds both; shared pieces in `src/components/trust/Trust.tsx`). **Still open:** (1) **Privacy's list was separate from the page: resolved 2026-11-17.** The member sentences are data in `src/content/privacy.ts`, `/privacy` renders them, and each row of `src/lib/privacy-promises.ts` names the items it reads (`reads`), so `/admin/privacy` shows the page's own text as "Members read" (`tests/components/pages/privacy-one-source.test.tsx`). **Still open:** sensitive notes (vendor contract status, audit dates, incidents) would need a database-backed register with its own migration, repository, RLS and audit entries, which was deliberately left out. (7) **Browser headers: resolved 2026-11-17**: the policy is enforced and `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `X-Frame-Options` and `Permissions-Policy` are sent (`SECURITY_HEADERS` in `src/lib/csp.ts`). Scripts and styles are covered by the nonce-based policy tracked in "CSP for scripts and styles (nonces)". Compliance and Security name none. `tests/lib/trust-no-provider-names.test.ts` holds both rules. **Decided 2026-11-17:** Stripe, Kajabi, Google Calendar and Google Photos all stay generic: members don't interact with Stripe directly, Kajabi is a white-label site for them, the calendar feed is a standard ICS link that works in any calendar app, and only staff pick photos in Google Photos (the Google sign-in for it is admin-only; members only see a link to an event's album). The sentence about the photo service used to say "only when you choose photos to add", which read as if members choose; it now says staff do.

### Security audit follow-ups _(Needs Scheduling)_

Found by a security review and not done in the hardening PR that fixed the small items (anon privileges, a logged webhook token, exact secret matching, render-time lesson sanitizing). Each is small to start.

- **Audit advisories in build and tooling packages.** `pnpm audit` reports about a dozen advisories, all in transitive packages (follow-redirects, picomatch, fast-uri, glob, minimatch, qs, postcss-selector-parser). Deferred because none is in the request path and each needs a parent bump or a `pnpm.overrides` entry checked against the build. Start: `pnpm audit --prod` and `pnpm why <package>`; then run `pnpm audit --prod` in CI so new ones fail a PR.
- **Tables every signed-in member can read for all members.** Member engagement, member metrics, the staff list and awards are readable by any signed-in member through RLS. Probably intended for some, but nobody has reviewed each against what `/privacy` promises. Deferred because it is a product decision per table. Start: list tables whose select policy is `authenticated` with no per-member scope (`pg_policies`), and decide for each: keep, narrow to the member's own rows, or move behind a service-role read.
- **Public storage buckets without limits.** **Resolved for `member-photos`** (2 MB, WebP only, with a pgTAP test). The public buckets for Slack avatars and Slack emoji have no file size or MIME type limits. Deferred because they are written by server code that already checks, so this is a second layer. Start: set `file_size_limit` and `allowed_mime_types` on the buckets (`supabase/config.toml` for local, a migration or the dashboard for production) and check the upload paths still pass.
- **`bronze` is an exposed API schema.** `supabase/config.toml` (and production) list `bronze` in the API's exposed schemas, so PostgREST can address the raw-import tables for any role that holds a grant. Anon no longer holds one, but the app only reaches `bronze` through the service role. Deferred until someone confirms nothing browser-side needs it. Start: remove `bronze` from `[api] schemas`, check `src/lib/data/` repositories still work (they use the service role), and change the production setting through the config-as-code flow.
- **`clientIpFrom` trusts the first `x-forwarded-for` entry** (`src/lib/rate-limit.ts`). That is safe on Vercel, which overwrites the header, and wrong anywhere a client can set it. Noted so it is not copied to another host; if the app ever runs behind a proxy that appends, take the last trusted hop instead.

### Check the first CSP health alerts against reality _(Needs Scheduling)_

The daily check (`/api/internal/csp-health`, `docs/infrastructure/CSP_ALERTS.md`) went in at the same time as the first report-only week, so its thresholds come from one day of production data (about 20 active members a day). **Still open:** (1) the rule for "never ran our scripts" counts a member as running them if they called our API or the router prefetched three pages in one second; after a few weeks, look at the days it alerted and whether a real browser problem was behind each, and tune `config/app.ts` `cspHealth`. (2) Opera, Samsung Internet and other Chromium forks are not judged against a minimum version, because their numbers don't map onto Chrome's; add a mapping if one shows up in the access log. (3) The cron has no Checkly heartbeat (the plan limit that holds back the other newer jobs); `CHECKLY_HEARTBEAT_CSP_HEALTH` is declared and the route pings it once a monitor exists (`src/lib/cron-heartbeats.ts`, drop `unmonitored`). (4) The new Sentry alert rules were written against the workflow API's documented shapes and read-only calls; the first push by CI is the first write, so read its log (`push-sentry-config`) and `pnpm sentry:config:diff` afterwards.

### Make the sign-in cookies HttpOnly _(Needs Decision)_

The Supabase sign-in cookies are `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax` and host-only (`src/lib/supabase/cookie-options.ts`), but not `HttpOnly`: the browser client reads the session from the cookie, so a script injected into a page can read the refresh token. Supabase's own guidance is that this is by design: its docs say making them `HttpOnly` "is not necessary", and not feasible for an app with client-side JavaScript, since only the server could then read and refresh the session (the server-side auth guide's FAQ and the sessions guide). The Hub uses the browser client in seven places (`src/lib/supabase/client.ts`: sign-in and sign-out, chat Realtime in `ChatLive`/`ChatSidebar`/`LiveRefresh`, direct uploads in `MediaUploadForm` and the lesson files field), so it is not a flag to flip.

A custom path exists but is outside what the library supports: sign in and out through server actions; the server hands the page a short-lived access token (an hour) for Realtime and uploads, through `createBrowserClient` with an `accessToken` callback that asks a small route; then set `httpOnly: true` in `SUPABASE_COOKIE_OPTIONS`. What it buys is narrow: an injected script can no longer copy the long-lived refresh token and use it from elsewhere. It could still act as the member from inside the page and would still see the access token for its hour. Decided to wait until the nonce-based Content-Security-Policy for scripts lands (the stronger defence against an injected script, tracked in "CSP for scripts and styles (nonces)"), then decide whether the remaining risk justifies the custom work. If it does: check Realtime reconnects when the token renews, and test sign-in, sign-out and an upload.

### A stricter cookie for sensitive actions _(Low priority)_

Only the sudo ("view as a member") cookie is `SameSite=Strict`, `HttpOnly` and signed (`src/app/actions/sudo.ts`). Everything else relies on the `Lax` session cookie plus Next's same-origin check on server actions. A second short-lived `Strict` cookie that sensitive actions require would stop a request that comes from another site. That is all it does: a script injected into our own pages runs on our site, so the browser sends a `Strict` cookie along with its requests, and it is no defence against an injected script (see "Confirm before the most damaging actions" for that). The `Lax` cookie and the origin check already cover cross-site requests, so the extra cookie adds little. Revisit only if a cross-site request problem shows up.

### Confirm before the most damaging actions (audit and extend) _(Needs Scheduling)_

A script running in a signed-in member's page can act as them, and it cannot read their inbox. Asking for a fresh sign-in (an emailed code) before the few most damaging actions is what stops that. Today it exists for one action only: switching the primary email requires a sign-in within the last 10 minutes, else it sends a code and asks for it (`hasRecentSignIn`, `REAUTH_WINDOW_MS`, `sendReauthCode`/`verifyReauthCode` in `src/app/(member)/settings/identityActions.ts`, `ReauthModal.tsx`). No audit has been done of the other actions, so none of them can be assumed covered.

Start with the audit: list the server actions and routes whose misuse does lasting harm and rank them. Candidates to look at: the admin actions behind `requireActionPermission(...)` that remove, merge or delete members, change roles or admin access, start sudo, revoke other people's sessions, delete courses that have enrolments, turn a feature flag on for everyone, send to many members, or export data; and the member actions that change how they sign in or what is connected to their account (adding or removing an email alias, disconnecting Slack, deleting their account). For each: does it ask, and should it. Then move the check out of the member settings page into a shared helper (a `src/lib/` function plus one confirmation dialog) so an action opts in by calling it and returning `needsReauth`, and add a test per guarded action that a stale sign-in is refused. Decide the window per action (10 minutes today), and what an admin who signs in by code from the same inbox adds (it proves access to the inbox, not more).

### Data requests: record each one and its deadline, and answer access requests _(Needs Scheduling)_

The Privacy page promises that a member can ask us to show what we hold about them, correct it, remove it or stop using it for usage statistics, and that we answer as soon as we can and within a month. Requests arrive by email to the support address and are handled by a person. **What exists:** removal has a staff tool ("Remove on request" on the admin member page: a dry run of what would go, the member's name typed to confirm, `remove_member_data` in one transaction, then their sign-in and stored files; `src/app/(admin)/admin/members/[id]/RemoveMemberPanel.tsx`, `src/lib/member-removal.ts`; what is left of it is in "Removal on request for records with a required member id"), and turning off usage statistics is a switch on the Privacy page.

**What is missing:** (1) **A record of each request**: nothing says that a request arrived, when it was answered or what was done, so the one-month promise cannot be checked, and the removal tool writes no such record of its own (`removal-actions.ts` only records that the action ran, as usage analytics). A removal also removes the audit entries about the person, so the record has to live apart from them and hold no personal data: the type of request, received and answered dates, who handled it, no member id, name or email (`public-repo-hygiene.md` applies to the repo, and the same care applies to this table). **The catch-22 (a removal erases the person, yet we must show we did it), settled by the owner on 2026-11-25:** record the removal without naming the person: the kind of request, the dates, the staff member, **how many rows of each kind the removal deleted or cleared** (the counts the dry run already computes, never content), and the ticks of the upstream checklist (see "Removal on request for records with a required member id"). Give the request its own fresh random reference (a new id prefix, not the member's `mbr_` id, which other records may still carry), put it in the reply to the member, and keep nothing that links the reference back to them. Whether a returning person was removed before is answered by the fingerprint in `removal_tombstones` (an address hash, no name), not by this log. (2) **Access**: nothing shows a member what we hold. The dry run counts what a removal would touch but is not a copy of the data. A per-member read-only export (reading through the repositories, the `member_data_handling` catalog as the list of where their data is) would answer it. (3) **Correction** has no path of its own beyond staff editing the member's record.

Start with the log and the deadline (a small admin list: type, received, answered, handled by, overdue marked), then the export. Write the steps down first: what an export contains, who may run it, and the order with removal (an access answer has to be given before any removal, since afterwards nothing can find the person's rows).

**What an access answer needs (our understanding, to be confirmed with counsel before it is relied on):** the dry run is a staff-facing count per table, not an answer. Under GDPR (Article 15, if we have EU or UK members) a person who asks is owed confirmation that we process their data, a copy of the data itself (in a commonly used electronic format when they ask electronically), and the context: why we use it, the kinds of data, who receives it, how long we keep it, where it came from, and their other rights. The deadline is one month, extendable by up to two more for a complex request if the member is told; it is free; and we may verify identity, as much as is reasonable (sending the export to the address on file helps). Portability (Article 20) adds a structured, machine-readable form for data the member provided. California's CCPA/CPRA has its own right to know with a 45-day clock, but only above its thresholds, which are not reviewed yet (see the Compliance entry on state laws). The law does not require a self-service button.

**Plan: a staff action "Export what we hold" on the admin member page, next to "Remove on request".** (1) Read each table the `member_data_handling` catalog lists for the member, through the repositories (not a bare `select("*")`), and write one JSON or CSV file per table; send a plain-language cover sheet with it (purposes, recipients, retention and sources: most of this is already on the Privacy page, so reuse that text rather than writing it twice). (2) **Include the Bronze copies** of the member's records from Kajabi, Stripe, Zoom and Slack that sit in our database: it is data we hold, which is the opposite of removal, where those copies are left alone. (3) **Other people's data stays out**: for chat and DMs, export the member's own messages and the conversation names, not what others wrote. (4) **Staff notes about the member** (care engagements and similar) are generally personal data about them; whether they go in, or which exemption applies, is a decision for counsel and the owner, not an engineering default. (5) **Deliver by a secure expiring link** (a signed storage URL that is deleted after a week or two), never as an email attachment; write the log entry (received, answered, handled by) from the same action. (6) Test that the export reads the catalog's tables, leaves out other members' messages, and writes the log entry. A member-facing "Download my data" button is a later step, once this works by hand.

### Sharper logo in authenticator apps _(Needs Scheduling)_

Authy has no logo catalog we can submit to: it runs a Google Images search on the QR code's issuer and shows the first image as a small thumbnail (Twilio's support articles on Authy logos; nothing in the QR code, the otpauth URI or the app can carry an image). We tried issuers on a phone: "Hedgie Hub" gave LEGO, "Quill & Cup Hedgie Hub" a stock hedgehog, "Quill & Cup" and "Quill and Cup" a small blurry wordmark, `quillandcup.com` our real logo, also blurry. `mfaIssuer` in `config/app.ts` is `quillandcup.com` for that reason: our own logo, as sharp as Google's copy allows.

**Why it is blurry:** the public site (`quillandcup.com`, on Kajabi today) gives Google little to rank. Its only logo is a 360x80 wordmark PNG with the alt text "Header Logo", the favicon is 32x32, and the page has no `og:image` and no schema.org markup. **Why deferred:** all of it lives in Kajabi's site settings, not in this repo, and Google decides when to re-rank (days to weeks, not guaranteed).

**Where to start (in Kajabi):** (1) upload a large logo, at least 512x512 on a transparent or white background, as the header logo, with the alt text "Quill & Cup logo"; (2) set the site's social sharing image (`og:image`) to it; (3) add Organization markup in the site's custom header code: `{"@context":"https://schema.org","@type":"Organization","name":"Quill & Cup","url":"https://www.quillandcup.com","logo":"<URL of the large logo>"}` in a `application/ld+json` script. Then re-test with the QR codes (an otpauth URI with `issuer=quillandcup.com`, any fake account) a week later. When the landing pages move off Kajabi (see "Landing pages (page builder)"), carry the same three things to the new site, so it is not lost with Kajabi.

### Vetting for a public "drop in" button on the home page _(Needs Scheduling)_

A "drop in" button on the public home page would let someone who is not a member join a live prickle. The risk is a stranger who joins to harass or expose themselves to a room of women. **Why deferred:** nothing is built, and the amount of friction (the card step in particular) is a product decision, since it costs sign-ups and is also the strongest deterrent. **Principle:** the button never hands out a room. It starts a request, and a room opens only to someone who has passed a few cheap checks, so a casual troublemaker finds it not worth the effort.

**Plan (layers; none is enough alone):**
1. **No public link.** The button goes to a short "Request a drop-in" form for a specific prickle. Each approved person gets their own join link (Zoom registration with manual approval issues one per person), so a shared or leaked link opens nothing for anyone else.
2. **Friction that removes anonymity and zero cost.** Verify an email (phone by text message is optional, only if abuse shows up); ask for a real name and a question or two about their writing, which also checks fit; require a card on file with no charge (a Stripe setup intent), which deters most casual abusers and gives a card fingerprint to ban on; and a code-of-conduct checkbox saying they can be removed and banned.
3. **Approval.** A host or community manager approves each request ahead of the prickle, or the checks above approve it automatically and staff review only flagged ones. Choose by expected volume.
4. **Room settings.** Waiting room on, with the host admitting only people whose registered name matches; no renaming, screen share or annotation for participants; first-timers admitted with video off until the host does a short check-in; a co-host present who can suspend participant activities and remove someone in one click.
5. **Sponsorship.** The strongest trust signal, and it matches the invite-only model: a member can bring a guest and is accountable for them. Offer "Ask a member to bring you" next to the open request path.
6. **Afterwards.** A ban list keyed on email, card fingerprint and phone; audit-log entries for bans (`audit-and-activity-log.md`); a written procedure for hosts to report and remove.

**Recommended first version:** verified email, card on file with no charge, per-person registered Zoom links, waiting room, and first-timers admitted with video off, plus the sponsorship path. **Where to start:** decide the friction level with the owner; then a `drop_in_requests` table with a repository (new-table rules in `CLAUDE.md`), the Stripe setup step, Zoom registration through the API, an admin approval list under `requireAdminPage()`, and notices to the host through `createNotifier`. Whether a guest counts in attendance and stats needs its own decision.

## Login & Access History

### Session Replay (PostHog bolt-on)
Per-user login/access history (`access_events` table + `get_access_sessions()`, surfaced via a "History" view on `/admin/users`) tracks when a user came back and what pages they visited, sourced directly from Supabase — see `supabase/migrations/20260827140000_create_access_events.sql`. That covers "when did they come back" and "what pages did they view," but not literal visual playback of what happened on screen.

**Idea:** Add PostHog's free tier (1M events/mo, session replay included) as a bolt-on for that specific need — `posthog.identify(userId)` client-side, tied to the same Supabase auth id. Not a replacement for the custom table: PostHog's dashboard is a separate view, not integrated into `/admin/users`. Self-hosting PostHog is ruled out.

**Why deferred:** The custom table already answers the primary ask (login history + page trail inside the existing admin UI) with no new infrastructure or third-party script. Session replay is a nice-to-have for UX debugging, not a blocker.

### Email sign-in link: a one-click `/auth/confirm` with the Slack "Continue" guard
**Resolved (2026-11-18):** the sign-in, invitation and recovery emails now link to `/auth/confirm?token_hash=...&type=...` (`src/app/auth/confirm/route.ts`, `src/lib/auth-confirm.ts`). It checks the token on the server with `verifyOtp`, so the link needs no secret held by the browser that asked and works on any device. It copies the Slack link's guard (`src/lib/browser-navigation.ts`): a real top-level navigation (`Sec-Fetch-Mode: navigate`, `Sec-Fetch-Dest: document`) signs in at once with no extra page, anything else (a mail scanner, a link checker, a prefetcher, a client without the headers) goes to `/auth/confirm/continue`, whose button posts back and is the only thing that spends the token. Recorded as `signed_in` method `email_link`, or `email_continue` for the detour. The templates are built from `supabase/emails` by `scripts/_email-templates.ts` and pushed with the Supabase config on merge; the link is `{{ .RedirectTo }}?token_hash={{ .TokenHash }}&type=...`, and the code asks for `${APP_URL}/auth/confirm` (login form, the identity re-check, invitations). `/auth/callback` still handles PKCE `code` links sent before the change and forwards any `token_hash` it receives to `/auth/confirm`. The typed code stays in the sign-in email. Staff with a two-step factor still go through the code check afterwards (the link only gives a first-factor session).

**Still open:** read the first days of real use (`vercel logs --query "signin:"` for `confirm_refused` and `confirm_no_token`, `signin_failed` events, the share of `signed_in` with method `email_continue`); the trade-off accepted is that the link works from any device for whoever holds the email, as the code already does. Dashboard-sent invites and Supabase's own `email_change` and `confirmation` emails are not changed (`email_change` still uses Supabase's address). Lowering the link lifetime is the next entry.

### Lower the email link lifetime (`otp_expiry` to 3600)
**Resolved 2026-11-18:** `otp_expiry` is 3600 (one hour, was 86400) in `supabase/config.toml`, applied to production by the CI config push on merge. It covers the emailed link and the typed code in the same email, invitations and recovery. The magic link, invite, recovery and launch emails say one hour. A spent or expired link has a way back: the link opens `/auth/confirm` (only a real browser spends it) and the login page sends a new link or code, or a fresh invitation to someone invited who never accepted (`resendPendingInvite`, `find_pending_invite`, a 60 second cooldown). Effect: a link requested in the evening is dead by morning, and an invitation must be used within the hour, or the invitee asks for a link on the login page (staff can also re-send the invite from the member's page). Send the launch emails (`pnpm launch:email`) when members are ready to sign in, since the code in each one now lasts an hour. **Still open:** nothing found fragile; if invitees keep missing the hour, consider a "send when ready" invite flow (the provider has one lifetime setting for every kind of email).

### Sign-in health: anonymous counters for failures and attempts
`signed_in` (by `method`) and `signin_failed` (reported when the member then gets in; carried a week in a cookie) are in the usage events (`docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`, "Sign-in"). They cannot show **people who never get in**, or **failures as a share of attempts**, because a failed or abandoned sign-in has no member and `analytics_events.user_id` is required. The server log lines (`vercel logs --query "signin:"`) cover them only for as long as Vercel keeps logs.

**Build:** the anonymous, server-side collection path that `docs/architecture/EVENT_LOG.md` already designs ("Statistical-purposes exceptions": no user id, session, cookie or IP; the server only increments rollup counters and stores no event). A table of counts per day, `reason` or `method`, and a coarse device class (iOS, Android, desktop; not the raw user agent), incremented where the failure happens (`/auth/callback`, `verifyEmailCode`, `/auth/slack`, the Slack code action) and for **link requested** (the browser asks Supabase directly today, so `LoginForm` would send a one-line beacon to a route that increments the counter), each through a repository (`.claude/rules/data-access-layer.md`) with a contract suite, plus a "Sign-in health" table on `/admin/analytics`: per day, links requested, sign-ins by method (from `signed_in`), failures by reason, and the failure share. Add a sentence to the privacy page (`/privacy`) saying the Hub counts failed sign-ins with no identifying data. Needs a migration (new id prefix, table grants, RLS: service role only; `supabase-table-grants.md`) and a note in `docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`. **Why deferred:** it needs the migration, the repository and the privacy-page change in one go; the cookie-carried events cover the people who do get in until then.

---

## Notifications

### Close the Slack DM of other notification kinds when they are handled in the Hub
Check-in and check-out DMs now collapse and clear their unread badge when answered in the Hub (`src/lib/prickle-checkin-dm-close.ts`, `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Read state shared with Slack"). Other Slack DMs the app sends (prickle reminders, chat notifications, hand-over requests) stay as they are once the member acts in the Hub. Deferred because each needs its own "handled" signal and a closed-note wording, and the check-in DMs were the ones a member reported. To do it: have the kind's sender keep the message with `notifier.send`'s `onReceipt` (as `keepDmMessage` does), then call a shared close from where the in-app notification is resolved (`resolveInAppNotifications`). Also open: a Submit or Skip pressed in Slack doesn't stamp the row's `closed_at`, so a later Hub edit re-sends the same collapse (harmless, one extra `chat.update`); and a DM answered question by question in Slack without Submit stays live by design.

### Unsubscribe: say who was unsubscribed and offer "that wasn't me" _(Needs Scheduling)_

Unsubscribing from a notification email needs no sign-in: the footer link and the mail app's one-click button carry a signed token for one member and one kind (`src/lib/email-unsubscribe.ts`, `src/app/unsubscribe/page.tsx`, `src/app/api/email/unsubscribe/route.ts`). The page after it says "You're unsubscribed" with a "Turn these emails back on" button, and nothing else tells the member it happened. The token never expires, so a forwarded email lets whoever holds it switch that kind off for that member. Worth doing: after an unsubscribe, show which address it applied to and a plain line, "Didn't mean to unsubscribe you@example.com? Turn these emails back on", next to the button. The link is a bearer token and can be forwarded, so show the address masked (the first letter and the domain), never in full. Start in `src/app/unsubscribe/page.tsx` (the `subscribed` branch) and test the masked address.

### Unsubscribe: a confirmation email, or a note in the Hub _(Needs Decision)_

Nobody is told when email for a kind is switched off by an unsubscribe link: no email, and no in-app notification. A member who did not do it, because the link was forwarded or opened by mistake, finds out when emails stop. Two ways to close it: (1) one short email to the same address saying "You won't get emails about <kind> anymore. Didn't mean to? Turn them back on", with a resubscribe link and a link to the notification settings, and nothing promotional in it (it is a reply to the member's own action, so it should count as transactional like the sign-in emails, but check this against the CAN-SPAM guidance before sending to an address that has just opted out of that kind); (2) an in-app notification, which needs no email and cannot reach the wrong inbox, but only reaches a member who opens the Hub. Decide which, or both; if the email, send it through `src/lib/email.ts` directly like `src/lib/email-change-notice.ts` (not through `createNotifier`, which would respect the very preference just turned off) and write it as a template in `src/emails/account/`. Also in the same area: the two unsubscribe endpoints have no rate limit (`src/lib/data/rate-limit.ts` has the helper), and the token never expires on purpose so old emails keep working.

### Notification link tags: custom `nk`/`nc` or standard UTM? _(Needs Decision)_
Every Hub link in a Slack, email or push notification carries `?nk=<kind>&nc=<channel>`; the page reads them on landing, records `notification_opened` in `analytics_events` and strips them (`src/lib/notifications/attribution.ts`, `src/components/AnalyticsEvents.tsx`, `docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`). They are custom parameters, not UTM, and no recorded reason says why: the change that added them (#210) does not mention UTM, and nothing in the repo uses `utm_` parameters. Left as is for now. Likely reasons, none confirmed: all usage analytics is first-party (no outside tool reads these links, so UTM's reach does not help), `nk` and `nc` carry our own kind and channel ids from the registry, which have no UTM field of their own, and custom names keep notification traffic out of any Google Analytics campaign report if it ever loads inside the Hub (the marketing site sets `_ga`).

Decide whether to keep them or move to `utm_source` (the channel), `utm_medium=notification` and `utm_campaign` (the kind). Worth switching only if an outside tool should understand the links. The change is small: `KIND_PARAM` and `CHANNEL_PARAM` in `src/lib/notifications/attribution.ts`, the reader in `AnalyticsEvents`, `docs/product/ANALYTICS.md` and the attribution tests. Emails and Slack messages already sent keep the old parameters, so the reader would have to accept both for a while.

### Channels, Notifications & Messaging _(v1 live: Slack, in-app (flagged), browser push (flagged), email + per-kind notification settings; more channels next)_
One system for everything the Hub sends members, instead of each feature hand-rolling a Slack DM. Three layers (this replaces the CRM section's "Messaging Abstraction Layer" item):

- **Channels** (`src/lib/channels/`): the delivery systems (Slack, in-app, browser push and email today; SMS, WhatsApp next). Each adapter resolves a member to an address and sends an `OutboundMessage` (`text`, `url`, per-channel rich bodies like `slackBlocks`, `footerLinks`, and `timeSensitiveUntil`: how long it's worth interrupting for, which each channel interprets, e.g. in-app banner vs bell only). Shared by both layers below; nothing above it talks to a provider directly.
- **Notifications** (`src/lib/notifications/`): the app reaching out, governed by the member's per-kind channel choices. Mostly one-way, but some ask for an answer (check-in/check-out selects); the reply comes back over the same channel's inbound webhook.
- **Messaging** (planned): two-way conversations, e.g. in-app chat bridged to Slack (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`), and later SMS/WhatsApp threads. Uses the same channel adapters, plus the inbound half they don't have yet. Members won't move off Slack for chat without notifications for new messages, mentions and DMs, so chat events become notification kinds too.


**Next channels** (each = `CHANNELS` entry + adapter + whatever it needs to resolve an address; every notification kind gets it at once):
- In-app: mark one unread again

- Browser/OS: roll Web Push out past the flag; let members name their devices. Settings → Notifications already has a device list (remove one remotely) and a test button; the admin member page shows enrolled devices. iOS needs a home-screen install (16.4+), prompted by `InstallAppPrompt`
- Mobile push: native app later; Web Push covers it until then
- SMS / WhatsApp: important for non-US hedgies; needs a provider (e.g. Twilio), a verified phone number per member, and explicit opt-in (carrier/WhatsApp template rules)

**Still to build:**
- Inbound on the channel layer: a shared shape for replies (member, channel, conversation ref, text or action) so Messaging and interactive notifications don't each parse provider webhooks. Today only Slack replies exist, handled in `src/app/api/webhooks/slack/interactions/route.ts`.
- Move remaining hand-rolled member messages onto `createNotifier`: payment-failure DMs (below), Host Confirmation Flow (CRM section). Wheel of Wonder's group intro and the Slack sign-in DM stay direct: one is a shared room, the other a transactional reply the member just asked for.
- Staff channel posts as admin notification kinds, so each admin picks their channels: new feedback (`src/app/api/feedback/route.ts`). They post to a shared Slack channel today.
- A `notifications` outbox with async fan-out, once a channel is slow or needs retries (email, push)
- Quiet hours / time zone, and per-member frequency caps across kinds
- Escalation and dedupe: don't email what was already seen in-app; delay-then-send (e.g. email only if a DM is still unread after N minutes)
- Digests: batch low-priority events into a daily/weekly email

- A per-message "Stop these" Slack button that writes `notification_preferences` (the settings link covers it for now)
- Show on the settings page when a channel can't reach the member (e.g. no matched Slack account)

### Getting started: point the intro step at #start-here, and verify Slack identity _(Idea)_
The tour's newcomer step is "Say hello in Slack" and its reminder links to the Slack channel (`src/lib/onboarding.ts`, `src/lib/member-reminders.ts`). Follow-ups:
- **Rename it "Say hello in #start-here"** and link to the in-Hub Chat channel (the read-only mirror of the Slack channel, `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`) instead of the Slack deep link, once members can post from there. The intro-channel name (`slackIntroChannel` in `config/app.ts`) already drives the text; the link needs the chat channel's route.
- **A "Connect Slack" step in the tour**, using the existing Slack connect OAuth (`src/lib/slack-connect.ts`, `member_slack_connections`) so we know each member's Slack user id for certain, not by email/name matching (`src/lib/slack-matching.ts`). Then resolve Slack ids from the verified connection (and `slack_identities`, `src/lib/slack-sign-in.ts`) before falling back to matching, and use the id from the OAuth/Slack sign-in handshake to confirm it matches what we'd inferred.
- The intro reminder skips members we haven't matched to a Slack user, since we can't see whether they've posted; a verified connection brings more of them in.

### Finish renaming "pre-prickle nudges" to prickle check-ins _(Cleanup)_
Migration `20261003130000` renamed the code, route, cron job and dedup log to check-in/check-out names. Two names stayed because changing them needs steps outside the repo:
- **Checkly heartbeat**: `CRON_HEARTBEATS["pre-prickle-nudges"]` (`src/lib/cron-heartbeats.ts`) and `CHECKLY_HEARTBEAT_PRE_PRICKLE_NUDGES`. The key is the monitor's logical id (`cron-pre-prickle-nudges`), so renaming it (e.g. to `prickle-checkins` / `CHECKLY_HEARTBEAT_PRICKLE_CHECKINS`) replaces the monitor. Deploy the checks, copy the new ping URL into `.env.prod` under the new var, run `pnpm env:sync`, then remove the old var.
- **Vault secret** `writing_nudge_cron_secret` (cron → route auth; `config/env-vars.ts` destination for `CRON_INTERNAL_SECRET`). Rename the `vaultName`, run `pnpm env:sync:vault`, then a migration that reschedules `send-prickle-checkins` to read the new name. Delete the old secret only after that migration is live.

### Slack DM bridge: what is left _(Needs Scheduling)_

**Pull volume: done (2026-11-10), for the DMs and group DMs the bot is not in.** A pull is bounded: a conversation read in the last 10 minutes (6 hours for the nightly run) by anyone is not read again, only the member is put in it (`bronze.slack_channels.last_pulled_at`, `planPull` in `src/modules/chat/slack/dms.ts`); opening chat reads the most recently active first and skips ones with no activity for 30 days, ones found empty before, and any first read beyond 20 never-read conversations (the next pulls carry on); a DM with nothing in it creates no chat conversation (remembered, and read again by the nightly run); the nightly client waits on Slack's `Retry-After`, the on-open one fails fast, and a nightly run out of time reports how many members were left partly read. **Still open:** (a) `search:read` was left out on purpose (a new permission for a gain the above mostly gets); (b) the same marker must serve private channels when they are bridged (below); (c) the first real run (2026-11-10) let one member use the whole 200 s budget (3 deferred); each member now gets a fair share, (d) **found 2026-11-11 with ~24 covered members:** Slack rate limits `conversations.list` (about 20 a minute, shared by every member's token), and every member's pull lists their DMs and group DMs on every run, so a run of 24 members spends most of its time waiting. The waits are now bounded by the deadline, but the cost is still there: skip listing for a member whose last pull finished (not `incomplete`) within the freshness window, which needs a completion marker beside `dms_pulled_at` (that is stamped when a pull starts), or list only the types that have changed. Look at a real run in the logs and the Checkly heartbeat for how many members end `incomplete`, and tune `OPEN_NEW_CONVERSATIONS` / the windows from that.

Steps 1 and 2 (a member's Slack 1:1 DMs pulled with their own token into chat, and posting in them and starting one from "Message", behind `slack_dm_bridge`) are built; the plan and the decisions are in `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Slack 1:1 DMs through members' own tokens". Untested against real Slack until the flag is on for someone in production: check that a message sent from the Hub shows once (the pull must fold it into the row `markSent` stamped), and that a DM with someone who hasn't connected Slack opens. Step 3 (live delivery of DM messages: `user_events` `message.im`, gated per member by `slack_dm_bridge`, `src/modules/chat/slack/dm-events.ts`): confirm after the manifest push that Slack accepted `message.im` as a user event, and that a message to a covered member arrives without opening chat. Step 4 (the nightly pull, `/api/reconcile/slack-dms`): after its first deploy put the new Checkly monitor's ping URL in `CHECKLY_HEARTBEAT_RECONCILE_SLACK_DMS`. **Not done, in order:** (group DMs without the bot are built, step 6; untested against real Slack: check that Slack accepts `message.mpim` as a user event after the manifest push, that `conversations.members` works with a member's token for a group the bot isn't in, and that a group the bot is later added to flips from token-only; leave detection runs only when a member pulls, and the sidebar label for a token-only group with several unmatched people is not looked at); (the sidebar fetches the list again when a message arrives, over Realtime, in a conversation it doesn't list: `useUnreadCounts` in `src/components/chat/ChatSidebar.tsx`; a pull that finds a DM with no message newer than the member's last visit doesn't announce it until they navigate); (`/privacy` was rewritten for the switch-on 2026-11-03). **Why deferred:** each is its own PR, and none should ship before the pull has run for a few members in production.

### Slack bridge: private channels the bot isn't in _(Needs Scheduling; wanted, decided 2026-11-10)_

With 1:1 DMs and group messages bridged through members' own tokens, **private channels Billie Bot is not in** are the last Slack conversations that never reach the Hub (`/privacy` says so today: "Slack group DMs or private channels without Billie Bot ... never reach the Hub"). For the Hub to replace Slack for a member they need to be here too. **Why deferred:** it needs another Slack permission (every member reconnects once more), a `/privacy` change, and a decision on who staff can read; none of that was in the DM work.

**Design sketch (reuse what the group DMs built):** (1) **Scope: asked for ahead of time (2026-11-10)**, so members reconnect once with the other new permissions: `groups:history` is a user scope (`SLACK_PRIVATE_CHANNEL_SCOPES`, `hasPrivateChannelScope`, the manifest, the "latest permissions" reconnect bar and Settings note), nothing uses it yet, and `/privacy` says it is asked for ahead of private channels. A member without it stays bridged for everything else. (2) **Read:** `conversations.list` `types=private_channel` with the member's token returns the private channels they are in; the `bronze.slack_channels.token_only` marker (set when a member's pull records a conversation the bot hasn't; the bot's import flips it to bot-read) already says "only member tokens read this" for any conversation, not just group DMs, and `isMemberTokenConversation` asks the stored conversation, not an id prefix (Slack's ids start with `C` on newer private channels and groups alike). Members from `conversations.members`. Messages, replies, reactions, edits, deletes and files go through `pullConversationMessages` / `storeAndProject` like a DM's, typed `private_channel`. (3) **One read per conversation:** the per-conversation "last read from Slack" marker planned in "Pull volume" above, so ten members of one channel are one read, not ten. (4) **Visibility:** the same rule as DMs: a token-only private channel is in the chat of the members who have pulled, and of nobody just for being in it (`slack_dm_member_pulled`; generalize the closing step in `project_slack_chat_channels`). (5) **Live events:** `message.groups` and the reaction events as user events (the webhook gate `decideDmEvent` already handles group DMs by looking up the stored conversation). (6) **Staff access, to decide:** a private channel the bot IS in is readable by staff unless marked restricted (`/admin/data/slack-channels`); one only members' tokens read has no staff in it, so default it to **restricted** (staff only through break-glass, metadata always) rather than readable. (7) **When the bot is invited later,** it flips to bot-read and the bot's import takes over (tested for group DMs; test it for channels). (8) Sidebar: it is a `channel`, listed under Channels by its name (a private channel's name is not secret to its members; the bot's own channels already show theirs). **Where to start:** `src/modules/chat/slack/dms.ts` (`pullOneGroupDm` and `pullConversationMessages`), `supabase/migrations/20270610000000_slack_group_dm_bridge.sql` (the marker and the projection), `src/modules/chat/slack/dm-events.ts`, `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`. **Privacy:** rewrite the `/privacy` rows and the "Your one-on-one Slack DMs" detail to say private channels are brought in too, restricted like the rest, in the same PR that turns it on.

### Hub chat notifications: replacing Slack's _(Needs Scheduling; decided needed 2026-11-10)_

Chat has no notifications: an unread badge is all a member gets. While the bridged DMs and channels are also in Slack, Slack notifies the recipient, so the Hub needn't. It must once Hub chat replaces Slack for a member (or for those who leave it), so this is needed, not optional. **Design so far:** (1) notification kinds through `createNotifier` (`src/lib/notifications/registry.ts`, never a direct Slack DM), with sample messages (`src/lib/notification-test.ts`) and email templates (`src/emails/notifications/`): a new DM message, an @mention, and a message in a channel the member follows at "all"; per-conversation mute and level on `chat_channel_members` (`muted`, `notify_level`, already in the design doc), one notification per conversation until it is read (a burst is one), a link to the exact conversation or message, and **no message text** in the notification (DMs are restricted content). (2) **A switch "Hub chat is my main place"** (Settings → Notifications) that turns the chat kinds on for the member's chosen channels (in-app, browser push, email), off by default so nobody is told twice while Slack still tells them. (3) Rollout in dry run first, behind a feature flag with `liveFor` (`src/lib/member-reminders.ts` is the pattern).
**What can't be done, checked against Slack's docs 2026-11-10:** copying a member's Slack notification settings into the Hub in one click, and turning their Slack notifications off in one click. Slack's public API has **no method to read or change per-channel or global notification preferences**: `users.prefs.get` / `users.prefs.setNotifications` exist only as undocumented browser-session endpoints, which a Slack app's token can't call and which Slack can change at any time. The one supported lever is `dnd.setSnooze` (user token with `dnd:write`, i.e. another reconnect; no documented maximum, only a `too_long` error): it pauses Slack's notifications for a stretch, it doesn't switch them off. **So instead:** the Hub's defaults follow Slack's own (DMs and @mentions notify, channels only on mention), and the switch above shows the member the steps to turn Slack's notifications off, with a link to Slack's help page; a "pause Slack notifications" button through `dnd.setSnooze` is worth a spike (how long a snooze can be) before promising it. **Where to start:** `src/lib/notifications/registry.ts` and `src/lib/notifications/notify.ts` (the `createNotifier` rules in `.claude/rules/member-notifications.md`), the `muted` / `notify_level` columns, and the Realtime INSERT path the sidebar already listens to for what counts as a new message.

### Hub direct messages: what is left _(Needs Scheduling)_

Hub DMs between two members exist behind the `chat_dms` feature (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Hub direct messages"); a member starts one with the "Message" button on a profile. **Deferred, and why:** (1) **Notifications.** **Resolved** by "Hub chat notifications: what is left" below (dry run). Was: a new DM only showed as an unread badge in chat. It needed a notification kind in `src/lib/notifications/registry.ts` sent through `createNotifier` (sample message in `src/lib/notification-test.ts`, email template in `src/emails/notifications/`), linking to `/chat/<id>`, with a dedup so a burst of messages is one notification and a dry-run flag per `liveFor`; held back to agree the wording and batching. (2) **Attachments.** `chat_post_message` refuses files in an `app_only` conversation: staged uploads are only moved to Slack by `src/modules/chat/slack/upload.ts`, so a Hub-only path needs its own place to keep and serve them. (3) **Members who can't sign in.** The sidebar's New message picker (`searchDmCandidates`) already leaves out members who aren't active or have no Hub login, **Resolved 2026-11-22:** `chat_find_or_create_dm` refuses a member who is not active (`chat_dm: not_active`) and a conversation the other person left (`other_left`), with plain messages in `dm-actions.ts`. **Still open:** the profile's Message button still shows for such members and only explains the refusal when clicked; hide it if that confuses people. (4) **Group DMs in the Hub**, and blocking or reporting a member (the report path is also open in the design doc). (5) **Break-glass for DMs** still has no tool; the text is unreadable to staff until it exists.

### Big Sisters: message in the Hub _(Hub DMs now exist; button not built)_

The dashboard card and `/big-sisters` link a new member and their big sister to each other's **Slack** DM (`getSlackDmLinksForMembers`), because Hub Chat can't start a conversation yet: `src/app/(member)/chat/actions.ts` only posts into existing channels, and `chat_channels` has a `dm` kind (Hub-native, always `restricted`, see `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`) with nothing that creates one. When Hub DMs exist: add a "Message in the Hub" button to `src/components/member/BigSisterCards.tsx` (find-or-create the DM with the other member and link to `/chat/<id>`), make it the primary action and keep Slack as the fallback for members who aren't in Hub Chat, and point the `big_sister_match` introduction's `url` at the DM. Gate it on the `chat_posting` feature like the rest of chat.

### Big Sisters: member-facing changelog entry when the flag goes on _(Needs Scheduling)_

The Big Sisters program is built behind the `big_sisters` preview flag and has only an engineering changelog entry (`big-sisters-groundwork`). Nothing tells members about it yet because it is not on for everyone, and a flag that is off is not a product update (`.claude/rules/changelog-writing.md`). Start: when the flag goes on globally (read the date from the production audit log, `entity_type = 'feature_flag'`), add a `product` entry dated that day in `src/content/changelog.ts` describing what members can do, and link it from the engineering entry. See `docs/product/BIG_SISTERS.md`.

### Chat Files: What Is Still Missing _(Needs Scheduling)_
Chat shows images, GIFs and other files under a message (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, 2026-10-21). Left for later: files over 20 MB and files hosted outside Slack aren't copied, so their chip answers 404 (link them to Slack's own URL instead, from `bronze.slack_files.raw_payload`); no audio/video players or PDF previews; Slack's legacy `attachments` (link unfurls, bot cards) aren't rendered. Start at `src/components/chat/MessageFiles.tsx` and `src/app/api/chat/files/`. Sending from the Hub (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, 2026-10-22) also lacks: an upload progress bar, showing the message in the list while its files upload (the composer waits), and a bot-path upload that keeps the member's name and photo (Slack's upload API can't set them; the comment carries their name instead, which can leak into the Hub's copy of the text when Slack's echo is projected).

### Keyboard GIF Panel On Android Says "Can't enter this content here" _(Needs Scoping)_
Reported on Android with the Samsung keyboard: picking a GIF in the keyboard's GIF/sticker panel shows Android's toast "Can't enter this content here." That toast comes from the keyboard when the focused editable does not declare image MIME types (`EditorInfo.contentMimeTypes`) for the commitContent API; Chrome decides that, not the page, and a web page cannot set it. The composer already takes pasted images (`Composer.tsx`); the fix shipped with this entry only widens what counts as a file (`filesOf`, `beforeinput` with files) for keyboards that do commit. Not a motion issue: the animation setting (`src/lib/animations.ts`) only stills GIFs on display and never disables the composer. Still open: confirm on a real device whether Chrome's editable advertises image types, and if not, hint at the 📎 button (or a "GIF" picker such as Tenor/GIPHY search) on Android.

### Prompt Members To Fix Their Own Setup (Reconnect Slack And Similar) _(Needs Scoping)_
When a change needs something from each member, they only find out by hitting the symptom. Example: Slack connections made before `files:write` joined `SLACK_USER_SCOPES` (`src/lib/slack-connect.ts`) silently upload files through the bot, so the message shows as "Billie Bot" with a "Name:" prefix instead of as the member. The only prompt is a "Reconnect Slack" link on `/settings` (`src/app/(member)/settings/SlackConnectPanel.tsx`, shown when `hasReadSyncScopes` is false), and its text only mentions read state. The generic "needs attention" list (push subscription gone, no Slack account matched) is still open. Add a proactive nudge when a member's setup is out of date: an in-app notification kind (through `createNotifier`, with `ref` and `resolveInAppNotifications` so it clears once they reconnect) and/or a step in the Getting started tour (`src/lib/onboarding.ts`) that counts as done from the member's own data. Make it generic (a list of "needs attention" checks: Slack scopes, a dead token revoked by `slack_connection_revoke`, browser push subscription gone, no Slack account matched) rather than one-off, and have the reconnect copy say what the member gains (files, read sync) instead of naming only one. Also consider an inline hint in the composer when a file send falls back to the bot (`postedVia = "bot"` in `src/modules/chat/slack/upload.ts`). Start a new automated sender in dry run per the notifications rules in `.claude/rules/member-notifications.md`.

### Retire Slack Name Matching For Chat _(Needs Scheduling)_
Chat requires a connected Slack account (`requireChat`, `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md` "Access control").

**Still open (small):** two members connected Slack before rotation was switched on and hold non-rotating user tokens (checked in production, 2026-11-01: 4 live connections, 2 rotating); they should keep working, but if either reports a Slack posting error, ask them to reconnect. **Still open:** (4) **Retire name matching** (`src/lib/slack-matching.ts`): measured 2026-10-25 in production, **1 of 81 active members has connected Slack** (all 81 are matched by name), so matching is still how nearly everyone is linked; revisit once most have connected.

### Mark Chat Read While Reading, Like Slack _(Needs Scoping)_
The Hub marks a conversation read only when the reader leaves it (`src/components/chat/ChatLive.tsx`: switching conversations, tab hidden, page unload), through the newest message that was on screen. Slack marks read continuously while the window is focused and the newest messages are in view. So reading in the Hub and then switching apps (Cmd-Tab) leaves the Hub marker, and therefore Slack's, where it was, and the Hub hides the open conversation's badge so it looks read. Options, cheapest first: also mark on window `blur` (the same `markChatRead` + Slack push); or mark while focused once the newest message has been on screen for a couple of seconds (more writes, one per view, and the "New" line must not jump while reading, which is why it marks on leave today). Deferred (2026-10-22) because switching conversations already syncs both ways; start at `ChatLive.tsx` (the "counts as read when the reader leaves it" effect) and `markChatRead` in `src/app/(member)/chat/actions.ts`.

### Chat Link Preview Blocks: Slack Still Unfurls A Mixed Message _(Needs Scheduling)_

A member's block ("Remove and Block" in the Remove preview dialog; `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Link previews") stops the Hub's own preview for a blocked link and turns Slack's unfurl off when **every** link in a message is blocked (`sendChatMessageToSlack`). Still open: (1) a message with a blocked link and another link still unfurls as a whole in Slack (no per-link switch on `chat.postMessage`), and Slack's card for the blocked link then shows in the Hub, because only the Hub's own previews consult the blocks. Fix by dropping, at read time in `withLinkPreviews`, a Slack card that matches a `blocked` row of the author's, or by reading the author's blocks in the loader (the viewer cannot read them under the owner-only policy, so it needs the service role or a `blocked` marker on the stored row). (2) An edit that adds a blocked link to a message already in Slack: `chat.update` has no unfurl switch, so Slack may unfurl it. (3) The blocks apply to messages written in the Hub only; a message written in Slack is unfurled by Slack with no Hub rule involved.

### Chat Link Previews: Reserve The Picture's Real Size _(Needs Scheduling)_
A card's picture now holds a fixed-height placeholder while it loads (`src/components/chat/UnfurlPicture.tsx`), fades in, and shows nothing if it fails, but the card still resizes once from the placeholder (10rem) to the picture's own height (up to 18rem).
