A stable aspect ratio needs the picture's width and height known before it loads: store them on `chat_link_previews` (two nullable columns, an expand migration) when `src/lib/chat/link-previews.ts` fetches the page, probing them from the first bytes of the image (PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP headers) or from `og:image:width/height`, carry them on `CardUnfurl`, and set `aspect-ratio` on the placeholder. Deferred because it needs a migration and a new byte parser for a one-time half-height shift; the wrong-picture bug and the empty card were the problems that mattered.

### Chat Underline And Lists: What They Don't Cover Yet _(Needs Scheduling)_

**Still open:** (1) Only a message with an underline is read from its blocks; the rest of Slack's rich text (formatting inside a link's label, code language) is still flattened to the message's mrkdwn text, so the chat shows it as before. Reading every message from its blocks would fix that but is a larger change to the projection. (2) `blocks` is now selected for every message the chat loads (`MESSAGE_COLUMNS` in `src/lib/data/chat.ts`), though only used for underline: if message pages get slow, add a generated "has underline" column and fetch blocks only for those. (3) Slack's lists are flat (every item has its own indent, so backing a middle item out leaves the items under it where they were, with a level skipped); ours are nested, so they come with it. That is a deliberate difference, not a gap (decided 2026-11-03; it is commented in `message-box-extensions.ts` and pinned by a test), and the chat closes up a skipped level in a message written in Slack the same way. (4) The blocks path (every message with a list or an underline, with or without files, and edits) has not been tried against a real workspace; the plain-text fallback on `invalid_blocks` is the safety net, and it is reported when it fires (an alert in #hub-alerts, a Sentry event tagged `chat_blocks_refused` with the message id, and the `Slack refused the blocks` log line). The member is not told their message went without its formatting: add a note on the message (it needs a marker on the row) if the reports show it happens. Start at `src/lib/chat/rich-blocks.ts`.

### Move The Remaining List Popups Onto `useComboboxKeys` _(Needs Scheduling)_
`src/lib/hooks/useComboboxKeys.ts` is the one place for Arrow/Enter/Tab/Escape handling and the ARIA wiring of a list under a text box (`EntitySearch`, `MultiEntitySearch` and the message box's suggestions use it; `MultiEntitySearch` and its wrappers were moved over in the combobox keyboard PR, with Home/End, Backspace on an empty box and toggling Enter). The palette still has no `combobox`/`listbox` roles or `aria-activedescendant` (its rows are links with `aria-current`, in sections, with copy and delete buttons beside recents), which is why it was left out of that PR. `src/components/search/SearchPalette.tsx` (rows with their own Enter actions and a modal Escape) and the emoji picker's cell grid (`src/components/chat/EmojiPicker.tsx`, a 2-D grid with Home/End and row-aware arrows) still hand-roll theirs. The palette likely fits the hook with a per-row action; the grid needs a grid variant. Deferred to keep the formatting change focused; check `MultiEntitySearch`/`MultiSelectSearch` and other pickers for hand-written key handling at the same time.

### Chat Box: Settings For What Enter Does _(Needs Scheduling)_
Slack has two per-person preferences (Preferences, Advanced; [help article](https://slack.com/help/articles/115005523006-Set-your-Enter-key-preference)): "When writing a message, press Enter to" (send the message, or start a new line and send with Cmd/Ctrl+Enter) and, under Input options, "When typing code with ```, Enter should not send the message" (checked: Enter is a new line in a code block and Shift+Enter sends). The Hub's chat box always does the first option of each: Enter sends, in a list and a code block too, and Shift+Enter is a new line (the next item in a list, a new line of code in a code block), which is what both accounts we checked on 2026-11-03 have (two people's Preferences, Advanced screens showed "Send the message" selected and the code-block checkbox unchecked; Slack's help doesn't say which values are the defaults, but two accounts agreeing, and the second setting being worded as an exception to "Enter sends", make them very likely so). A member with either setting the other way would expect the other behavior. Not built because nobody has asked and they are per-member preferences to store and test. Start in `src/components/chat/MessageBox.tsx` (the Enter branch of the key handler) and the member's preferences (`/settings/preferences`); `Composer` and `MessageBody` (edit box) decide what Enter does with the key, so both need to read them.

### Chat Box: A Setting For Smart Quotes And Symbols _(Needs Scheduling, cosmetic)_
**Resolved (2026-11-06):** the message box does what Slack's "Enable smart quotes and symbols" does: `"` and `'` turn curly (an apostrophe is the closing single quote), `--` becomes an em-dash, `...` an ellipsis, `->` and `<-` arrows (`src/lib/chat/smart-typography.ts`, applied as you type by `convertTyped` in `src/components/chat/MessageBox.tsx` beside the emoticon conversion). Not in code (a code block, or text marked as code) and not inside an address (a `--` or `...` in one stays: with a scheme, `www.`, or a bare domain with a path such as `example.com/a--b`; a quote typed after an address closes the quote around it). Right after a conversion, Ctrl/Cmd+Z takes back only that (the curly quote goes straight, the em-dash goes back to `--`, an emoticon's emoji to the emoticon), and the same for an address that was just made a link by typing a space after it: the link comes off and the words stay (`UndoConversion` in `src/components/chat/message-box-extensions.ts`; the editor's own undo would take back the whole burst typed in the same half second). Once anything else is typed or the caret moves, Ctrl/Cmd+Z is the editor's own undo again. It is always on.

**Still open:** Slack has this as a per-person checkbox (Preferences, Advanced, Input options), and the Hub has no setting: someone who types `--flags` or `->` in plain text, outside backticks, gets an em-dash or an arrow. Add a per-member setting (with the Enter settings, see "Chat Box: Settings For What Enter Does") if anyone asks. Also not converted: `(c)`, `1/2` and other symbols Slack's setting may cover beyond the ones its screen lists.

### Chat: A File Just Sent From The Hub, Before Slack Knows It _(Resolved 2026-11-06; one thing open)_
**What happened (seen 2026-11-04):** after a Hub upload, `sendChatFilesToSlack` asks Slack for the file's message (`files.info`, `lookupFileMessageTs`) to learn the message's timestamp, and only then saves its own copy of the bytes (`keepCopies`, which needs the timestamp for the `bronze.slack_files` row, whose `message_ts` is NOT NULL). For a couple of seconds after an upload Slack answers `file_not_found`, so it returned early without a copy, and the chat's image request (`/api/chat/files/...`) asked Slack for the file and failed the same way until Slack's own event arrived: a broken picture right after sending.

**Resolved:** (1) when the timestamp isn't found yet, the message's file entries keep the path of the staged bytes (`staged`; `sendChatFilesToSlack`), and the file route serves a file the Hub sent from, in order, our permanent copy (once Slack's event has made one), then the staged bytes, and **asks Slack only if neither is there** (the staged bytes were cleared away): the Hub never asks Slack for a file it sent itself while it has the bytes (`src/app/api/chat/files/[messageId]/[fileId]/route.ts`; the first version, #1077, asked Slack first and only then fell back, which still made the failing `files.info` call). (2) The picture tile also asks again after 1.5, 3, 6 and 12 seconds if it fails to load (`IMAGE_RETRY_DELAYS_MS` in `src/components/chat/MessageFiles.tsx`), for any other reason a picture is briefly unavailable.

**Still open:** the permanent copy still waits for Slack's own event in this case (the staged bytes are cleared after a while, so the entry's `staged` path goes stale, which the route tolerates). Keeping a permanent copy at once, without the timestamp, would need `bronze.slack_files.message_ts` to allow null (a migration, expand then contract, and the regenerated types), with Slack's event filling it in. It's stage 4 of "Own The Bytes" below.

### Chat: Own The Bytes, So What Members See Never Depends On Slack's Servers _(Needs Scheduling; staged plan, 2026-11-06)_
**Why:** the Hub is meant to be the permanent archive and, eventually, to be able to replace Slack (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`: Slack's free plan hides history and deletes files after a year). Most of the bytes are already ours; a few things still come from Slack's servers or CDN, and would break if the workspace went away or Slack changed a URL. **The rule to reach:** the Hub never asks Slack for something it has, or sent itself, and Slack is asked at most once, to copy something that arrived from Slack before we had it.

**Where we are (production, 2026-11-06, counts only):** 541 files recorded, 510 copied into the private `slack-files` bucket (about 709 MB) on ingest (webhook, nightly import, or on first view) and served from there through the access-checked `/api/chat/files/...` route. Not copied: 8 files over 20 MB (about 1.4 GB, skipped on purpose: `MAX_SLACK_FILE_BYTES`), 22 hosted outside Slack (Google Drive and the like, skipped: not Slack's), 1 hidden by Slack's history limit. Custom emoji: done (#888, 2026-11-02): all 494 emoji that have an image (the other 4 of 498 are aliases) are copied into the emoji bucket and served from it; none pending. Slack profile photos are the fallback for a member with no Hub photo in two places: chat (`slackUserPhotos`, `src/lib/chat/names.ts`) and `members.photo_url`, which the members processing fills Kajabi, then Slack, then Gravatar (`toKajabiPhotoUrl`, `src/lib/member-avatar.ts`). Production: 359 Slack users have a photo URL (84 live, non-bot); 218 of 946 members show a `avatars.slack-edge.com` photo, 88 a Kajabi CDN one, 640 a Gravatar one (the last two are other providers' and out of this plan).

**Stages, in order (each its own PR, each reading production numbers first):**
1. **Custom emoji images. Done (#888, checked in production 2026-11-07: 494 of 494 copied).** What was planned: copy each emoji's image into a bucket when the emoji sync records it (`src/lib/slack-capture.ts`, `upsertEmoji`), serve ours (a member-readable route or a public bucket: they are workspace decoration, not member content), and keep `image_url` as the source and for re-copying. Small (498 images), cheap, and it also removes the "an emoji whose host sends no CORS headers" limit that the animations-off still-frame code works around (`src/components/EmojiImage.tsx`). Aliases already resolve to a name, so only the image URL changes. Check how an emoji that changes or is deleted in Slack is refreshed.
2. **Slack profile photos. Done (#1123, #1129; checked in production 2026-11-08 after the nightly import).** The import copies every user's photo (deactivated users' too: former members still show in the directory and chat history) into the public `slack-avatars` bucket (`src/lib/chat/avatar-copy.ts`; path `<Slack user id>/<content hash>.<ext>`; `bronze.slack_users.image_path` and `copied_from_url`). Chat (`slackUserPhotos`) and `members.photo_url` use our copy, Slack's URL until copied; a changed photo replaces its copy, and a copy is removed only when Slack drops the photo. Production: 359 of 359 copied, none pending, 0 members on `avatars.slack-edge.com` (218 before), no copy failures logged. Public bucket, like Slack's own avatar CDN and the emoji bucket (owner's decision). **Left:** the column comment on `bronze.slack_users.image_path` still says a copy is cleared on deactivation (fix in the next migration that touches the table); `/privacy` doesn't mention profile-photo copies yet (check what it says about Slack data before the next member-facing change).
3. **Files over 20 MB. Left open on purpose (owner, 2026-11-07): probably don't copy them.** Production (2026-11-07, types and sizes only): all 8 are MP4 videos, 69 to 656 MB. If they are copies of course or membership videos we already hold in Mux (`videos`), a second copy in Storage is waste, and the answer is to link the Mux video instead. **First step:** find out what the 8 are (an admin looking at them in chat; don't put titles in the repo) and whether each has a Mux counterpart. **Likely feature (own entry: "Chat: Watch Videos In The Conversation, With Watch Stats"):** a way to share a Hub video into chat, so a message points at a Mux video (signed playback, the same access rules as the course) and not at a file. Questions for it: who may see a video shared in a channel (course access vs channel membership), how a Slack-side message with a Mux link reads, and what the 8 old messages show (a link to the Mux video, or "video not copied"). If some of the 8 are *not* in Mux, the original options still apply: a streaming background copy with a higher cap (`ensureSlackFileCopied` and the nightly copy read the whole body into memory today; read the storage plan's size and egress limits first), or a stored thumbnail plus a link. Not before the privacy item below.
4. **A permanent copy of a Hub-sent file at once**, not when Slack's event arrives: `bronze.slack_files.message_ts` allows null (a migration), `keepCopies` runs without the timestamp, and Slack's event fills it in. See the entry above. Only worth it if the staged bytes ever turn out not to cover the gap.
5. **Link previews and other Slack-hosted pictures** when the chat starts drawing them (Slack's legacy `attachments` and unfurls are not drawn yet; `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Not yet").
**Stays a link, by decision:** files hosted outside Slack (the 22): they were never Slack's to keep and may be someone else's to share.

**Each stage must follow the rule files already follow for deletion:** when a file is deleted (in Slack or in the Hub) its bytes are removed from the bucket and its row soft-deleted (`removeChatFileBytes`, `removeFilesGoneFromSlack` and the retry `sweepDeletedFileBytes` in `src/lib/chat/file-cleanup.ts`; the `file_deleted` webhook is the fast half and the import the pull-based one). A copied emoji image needs the same when the emoji is deleted or replaced in Slack (the emoji sync already soft-deletes the row, `liveEmojiNames`/`markEmojiRemoved`), a copied profile photo when the member leaves or changes it, and a larger-file copy the same as any file. Say in `/privacy` what is kept after a delete, as it does for messages.

### Chat: Watch Videos In The Conversation, With Watch Stats _(Started: sharing and playing a lesson video is built behind `chat_videos`, no stats yet)_
**Resolved (first PR):** a member can share a course lesson's video into a Slack-bridged channel they have joined (lesson page → "Share this video in chat", `shareLessonVideoInChat`; later replaced by "Copy link" and pasting the address, see "Chat video card starts at the shared timestamp"); the message shows a card, and pressing play mints playback links for that viewer through `POST /api/chat/videos/<message id>` after the channel check and the course-access check; Slack gets the text plus a link to the lesson. Stored in `chat_message_videos` (`ChatVideoRepository`), behind the `chat_videos` feature, off for everyone. Design and behavior: `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Lesson videos in messages". Decided for now (the stricter reading): a reader without the course sees "part of a course you don't have access to", not a preview.

**Share panel (done, fourth PR):** the conversation is picked from the shared searchable `EntitySearch` (new `browse` option lists every conversation on focus) instead of a plain dropdown, and nothing is preselected unless there is only one. **Lesson links pasted into Slack (done, seventh PR):** a Slack message naming a lesson page becomes the card in Hub Chat when its author (mapped to a Hub member) could share the lesson, including an admin's `?share=open`; webhook plus a nightly three-day catch-up. **Still open:** messages that arrive through the Slack DM bridge's own pull (`src/modules/chat/slack/dms.ts`) and through gap-fill are converted by the nightly pass, not at once; removing the address in a later edit does not remove the card. **Slack link and admin menu (done, sixth PR):** the Slack "Watch it in Hub Chat" link is now the message's permalink in the chat search's shape (`?thread=<root or itself>#m-<message>`), and the lesson builder's Video actions menu has the two paste links. **Message permalink (done, ninth PR):** the Slack link and "Copy link" are now `/chat/<channel>?m=<message>` (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Message permalinks"): the server reads it, so it survives sign-in (the `#m-` fragment never did), scrolls to the message however far back it is, and opens a reply's thread. **Still open:** a message deleted since the link was made scrolls nowhere (the conversation just opens); links in notifications, the dashboard and chat search still use `?thread=`/`#m-` and could move to `?m=`. **Paste a lesson link (done, fifth PR):** "Copy link to paste in chat" on the lesson page copies the lesson's address, and sending it in any message (a thread reply or a direct message too) turns it into the video card when the sender could share that lesson (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Paste a lesson link, anywhere"); admins also get a copy button whose link opens the share to everyone in the conversation (honored only when an admin sends it). **Still open there:** the share panel itself only posts top-level channel messages; a "Share a course video" button in the chat composer (no copy and paste, the conversation implicit); a chip in the composer saying a pasted address will become a card (today it looks like a link until sent); and converting an address added in an *edit*. (The old four-argument `chat_post_shared_video` was dropped in a follow-up migration, with approval.)

**Still open, in order:**
1. **Watch stats from chat. Done (second PR):** a nullable `video_watch_segments.chat_message_id`, carried by the player tracker and `/api/courses/watch` (accepted only for a readable message that shares that lesson), the card records like a lesson view and resumes from the lesson's saved position, and the staff lesson stats show a separate "Watched in chat" section while every other number stays about the lesson. **Left:** `track()` events for chat plays and shares (the share is an outcome through `withAction`; plays rely on the autocaptured click and the segments); the course-level stats page and over-time chart do not include chat; the lesson's own completion rule (90% of the playhead on the lesson page) does not run from chat, so watching in chat does not mark a lesson complete; `/privacy`'s text about course viewing data should be checked to cover chat viewing before the flag goes on for everyone (the owner's call).
2. **Race: done (third PR).** The message and its video row are written in one transaction by `chat_post_shared_video`, so the message is never visible without its card.
2b. **Who may watch: done for the base case, preview still open.** Playing needs the course (playback route and watch route), unless an admin opened the share to the channel (`open_to_channel`, admins only, off by default). **Idea (not built):** let members without the course watch a short preview, for example the first 5 minutes, or a chosen excerpt, instead of nothing. It would need a preview length per video or lesson (a field on `videos`/`course_lessons`, decided by staff), a way to end playback there (not checked: whether Mux can limit a signed stream to a time range; options to look at are a separate short clip asset made from the video, or stopping the player at the limit and treating the limit as soft; read Mux's docs first), and a prompt to get the course at the end. Decide soft or hard limit first. **Also open:** the Slack message links to the message in Hub Chat as well as to the lesson, so the open share works from Slack; what a Slack reader gets from that link: signed out, they are sent to /login, the link (query included) is remembered and used after sign-in, tested in `tests/lib/proxy-chat-link.test.ts`; signed in with no member record, `requireChat` sends them to /admin (then /no-access), tested in `tests/lib/chat-access.test.ts`; signed in as a member who hasn't connected Slack in the Hub, `requireChat` sends them through the connect-Slack gate (`src/lib/gates.ts`), which carries the page they asked for through Settings and Slack's approval page and back, and shows "Not now" (`tests/lib/chat-access.test.ts`, `tests/lib/slack-connect-return-to.test.ts`, `tests/components/SlackConnectPanel.test.tsx`). **Still to decide:** whether a Slack reader should be able to open this one message without the Slack connection at all.
3. **Direct messages and group messages: done by the paste path.** The conversation picker (and `src/lib/chat/share-targets.ts`) was removed; pasting the lesson address works in any channel, thread or direct message.
4. **The other sources:** a video uploaded from the Hub composer (direct-to-Mux upload, its own `videos` row owned by the message, a length cap) and an MP4 that arrives from Slack (the 8 large ones in "Own The Bytes", stage 3). Find out what the 8 are and whether Mux has them first.
5. **Card polish: done (eighth PR).** The card shows the lesson's thumbnail, title and course up front, says when a viewer can't watch it, and opens and starts the player with one click (`GET`/`POST /api/chat/videos/<message id>`; `LessonPlayer` `autoPlay`). The player is given the card's thumbnail as its poster, so opening it doesn't swap to a different frame first. **Still open:** the first frame of the video itself still replaces the poster when playback starts (a lesson's thumbnail is usually a title slide, not frame one); a short fade would soften it. Autoplay can be refused by some browsers (Safari is stricter about starting playback after an asynchronous request), which leaves the big play button; the card plays inline at the width of the message, with no larger or pop-out view.
6. **Still unbuilt from the plan below:** deleting a shared message should say what happens to the share row (today the row stays; the card is hidden with the content), a help article and demo scene, a member-facing changelog entry when the flag goes on for everyone (read the day from the audit log), and the `/privacy` check.

**Original plan (kept for the sources and decisions not built yet):**
**What:** a message can carry a video that plays inside the chat, in the Hub chat and, if it can be done, from Slack as well, and the watching is counted like course lessons are. **Why deferred:** nothing plays video in chat today (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`: "Not yet: video/audio players"; `src/components/chat/MessageFiles.tsx` shows a download chip), and the decisions below are the owner's. It is also the likely answer to "Own The Bytes", stage 3 above (the 8 MP4s over 20 MB that we don't copy): point a message at a Mux video instead of storing a second copy.

**Which videos (decide first).** Three sources, each different:
1. **A Hub video shared into chat** (a lesson's, or later a library video): the message holds a reference to a `videos` row (Mux, signed playback), no file. The player is the course one (`src/components/courses/LessonPlayer.tsx`, Video.js v10 on `MuxVideo`), so speed, captions, resume and storyboard previews come along.
2. **A video uploaded from the Hub composer.** Needs the direct-to-Mux upload the course builder has (`lesson-video-actions.ts`, `@mux/upchunk`) rather than the 20 MB Storage path, and a `videos` row that belongs to the message (so it is billed, retained and deleted with it). Decide who may upload (Mux bills by minute stored and delivered) and a length or size cap.
3. **A video that arrives from Slack** (an MP4 file on a message, or a link to YouTube/Vimeo/Loom): the MP4s are the files we skip over 20 MB today. A small one could play from the `slack-files` copy through the access-checked `/api/chat/files/...` route (needs `Range` support and a `video/*` allowlist next to the raster-image one, since only images are served inline today); a large one could be copied to Mux by the import. Pages hosted elsewhere stay links (or a click-to-play embed; third-party players load the provider's scripts, so check `/privacy` and the CSP first).

**Who may watch.** A video shared in a channel is seen by the channel's members, but the course rule is "has access to the course". Decide whether sharing a lesson video into chat grants playback to people without the course (a preview-length clip?), or shows a locked card ("part of the Ready Writer") to those without access. The access check lives where the bytes are served, not in the message: playback tokens are minted per viewer after the channel check **and** that decision, like `POST /api/courses/playback-tokens`. Default to the stricter reading until the owner chooses.

**Stats.** Reuse `video_watch_segments` (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Watch tracking"; one row per continuous stretch, a `viewed` marker, not written in sudo, first-party and not consent-gated per `docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`, "Course viewing data") and the same player reporter, so the staff stats pages, resume and "% watched" work unchanged. What is new: segments are keyed by video, not by lesson, and a stat row has no place to say *where* it was watched. Add a nullable context (a lesson, or a chat message) on the segment or a `surface` column, and have the stats page split or filter by it, so a lesson's numbers aren't mixed with chat views of the same video (Kajabi's "location" filter has no equivalent today, see `docs/product/COURSES.md`). A member's playhead should resume per video, whichever surface they used. Add `track()` events for chat plays (a share, a play, an upload) in `src/lib/analytics/events.ts`, and keep the `/privacy` text about course viewing data true for chat.

**Slack side (optional, probably a link only).** Slack can't run our player or report plays. What it can do: a Hub-shared video posted to Slack as a link to its Hub address (`/chat/...` or a `/watch/<message id>`-style page that asks for sign-in and plays), which carries no thumbnail unless the public preview page (like `src/app/lesson-preview/`) gives Slack a card with a poster; **plays from Slack are counted when the member opens the Hub page**, not inside Slack. Never put a playback token or a Mux playback id in a Slack message: those links must not work for anyone who isn't signed in and entitled (Slack unfurling is off for our sends, `src/lib/slack.ts`). Decide whether an incoming Slack MP4 is shown by the Hub only, since Slack's own player already handles it there.

**Also settle.** Deleting the message (in the Hub or from Slack) must remove or retire the video it owns, with the same bytes-follow-the-row rule as files (`src/lib/chat/file-cleanup.ts`: `removeChatFileBytes`, `sweepDeletedFileBytes`) and the nightly pull-based path that catches Slack-side deletes; a shared lesson video is only referenced, never deleted by this. Notifications and the search index treat the message as having a video (no text to search). Sudo: nothing is written, as for lessons. A phone with the chat open in the background shouldn't keep playing a clip when the member scrolls away (pause when it leaves the viewport).

**Where to start:** (1) find out what the 8 large MP4s are and whether they are in Mux (stage 3 above), and get the owner's answers on the two decisions (which sources first; who may watch a shared lesson video). (2) The smallest useful slice is source 1 behind a feature flag: a "Share video" action on a lesson, a `video` entry on `chat_message_contents.files` (or a sibling column; keep the RLS that gates the text gating it), a `MessageVideo` component in `src/components/chat/` that reuses `LessonPlayer`'s pieces and asks a token route that repeats the channel check and the course-access check, and `video_watch_segments` rows with a chat context. Then the Slack link form, then uploads. Tests: the token route's refusals, the segment write with context, the stats split, a Hub-sent message showing the player, and the delete path. Help article and demo scene after it works (`src/content/help/`, `tests/demo/scenes/`).

### Chat video card starts at the shared timestamp
**Resolved:** a lesson link with `?t=95` pasted into chat (the Hub box or Slack) now starts the shared video at that second for everyone who plays it, over their own saved position, with the same rule as the lesson page (`parseLinkSeconds`, `src/lib/courses/watch.ts`: a whole number, 1 to 86400). Stored in `chat_message_videos.start_seconds`, carried by `chat_post_shared_video`'s new `p_start_seconds` argument, returned by `GET`/`POST /api/chat/videos/<message id>`, shown on the card ("Starts at 1:35") and kept on the lesson link in Slack's copy (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Lesson videos in messages").
**Still open:** the five-argument `chat_post_shared_video(text, text, text, boolean, text)` is kept (now a thin call to the six-argument one) so the code live during the deploy that added `start_seconds` keeps working. Once that deploy is live, drop it in a follow-up migration (needs the user's approval for `ban-drop-function`, with the PR that removed the last caller; the only caller, `sendChatMessageImpl`, already uses the six-argument form). `tests/lib/chat-share-video-action.test.ts` and `supabase/tests/database/chat_post_shared_video_thread.test.sql` still exercise the five-argument form.

### Chat Drafts, Synced With Slack _(Slack sync blocked)_

**Still open:** syncing with Slack's own drafts. Checked 2026-11-02 (Slack's method reference and OpenAPI spec): no `drafts.*` methods are documented. `chat.postMessage` does take `current_draft_last_updated_ts` and lists `draft_*` errors, so the client uses drafts internally; third-party tools reach them with a browser session token (`xoxc` plus a cookie), not the bot or member OAuth tokens the Hub holds, so using them would mean storing members' session credentials. Slack's MCP server advertises "draft messages" for AI clients; whether that writes to a member's Drafts & sent is unchecked. Worth revisiting only if Slack publishes a drafts API (watch the changelog); then a draft would be pushed from `saveChatDraft` (for connected members, production only, like `src/modules/chat/slack/read.ts`) and pulled on focus like the read marker. **Also open:** drafts never expire, so the files of an abandoned draft stay in the staging folder for good (an age limit on drafts, deleting their files with them, would fix it). The sidebar list follows this browser's boxes only; a draft written in another tab or device shows after a reload (Realtime on `chat_drafts`, own rows, would make it live).

### Chat: Files While Editing, Unverified Against A Real Workspace _(Needs Verification)_
**Still open:** (1) Slack's docs only say `file_ids` is "Array of new file ids that will be sent with this message"; whether it replaces or appends to a message's existing files was not verifiable without a real workspace. We name the existing ids too, which is right if it replaces and may be harmless if it appends. Try it once on a real message and adjust `updateChatMessageInSlack`. (2) Check that the webhook's `message_changed` and the nightly import leave the same file list in `chat_message_contents.files` for Hub- and Slack-written messages. (3) Removing a file on Save is not atomic with the edit (files go first). (4) `Composer.tsx` still has its own copy of the staged upload code; move it onto `src/lib/chat/upload-client.ts` (`uploadStagedFiles`).

### Chat Message Menu: Edit/Delete And The Rest _(Needs Scheduling)_

**Still open:** Slack's other entries aren't supported: Mark unread, Add to saved, Add a reminder, Get notified about new replies, Pin to channel, Connect to apps, and the hover bar's Forward and Add to-do. Each needs its own feature first (the read marker only moves when the reader leaves a conversation, there are no saved items or pins), so add them one at a time as those land.

### Chat Emoji Picker: Skin Tones And Keyboard Grid _(Needs Scheduling)_


**Still open:** true virtualisation if a workspace passes tens of thousands of custom emoji; toned emoji inside message text (`:name::skin-tone-N:` still renders untoned); recents live in localStorage per browser, not per member.

**Still open (small UX):** the picker shows "Loading…" instead of its Frequently used row until the big emoji data (`emojibase-data`, a dynamic `import()` in `loadCatalog`) arrives, although recents are just shortcodes and need no catalog to render. Draw the recents (and the custom emoji) right away and load the catalog only for browse and search. Found while fixing a `MessageList` test that raced that import (PR #372). Start at `src/components/chat/EmojiPicker.tsx` (`catalog` state, the `!catalog` branch near the bottom); a component test can assert the recents render before the catalog resolves.

### Chat Custom Emoji: One Request Per Image _(Needs Scheduling, only if it becomes slow)_
`Emoji` (`src/components/chat/ChatText.tsx`) renders each custom emoji as its own `<img>` pointing at Slack's CDN URL (`bronze.slack_custom_emoji`, via `src/lib/chat/names.ts`), lazy and async-decoded, and the picker (`src/components/chat/EmojiPicker.tsx`) only renders chunks near the viewport. Standard emoji are Unicode text and cost no requests. Prod has 464 custom emoji (checked read-only), so scrolling the Custom tab means a few hundred small requests spread over the scroll; fine today. Deferred because it only matters if the set grows by an order of magnitude or Slack's CDN is slow. If it does, in order of effort: (1) serve thumbnails through our own resizing proxy or `next/image` so every image is a small, cacheable file we control; (2) generate a sprite sheet of the custom emoji at import time (`src/lib/slack-capture.ts` already copies files) and render with background-position; (3) true virtualisation of the Custom tab (render only visible rows). Start by measuring request count and bytes for the Custom tab before choosing.

### Chat Thread Replies Aren't Grouped _(Needs Scheduling)_
In the conversation, a message from the same person within five minutes of the previous one drops its avatar and name and shows its time on hover (`groupByDay` in `src/lib/chat/days.ts`, used by `src/components/chat/MessageList.tsx`). Replies in a thread panel (`src/components/chat/ThreadList.tsx`) never get that, so every reply repeats the avatar and name. Reuse `groupByDay`'s follow-up rule there (no day markers) and pass `compact` to `MessageItem`.

### Chat Visual Tests: Day Marker And Hover Bar _(Needs Scoping)_
The sticky day marker (opaque, flush to the top of the message pane) and the hover action bar are layout and CSS behavior that the jsdom component tests can't see: two regressions there (a transparent marker, a gap above it) were only caught by looking at the real page. There are no Playwright or other browser tests in the repo today. Decide whether to add a small Playwright suite (a seeded conversation spanning several days, scrolled past a day boundary, viewport widths either side of the sidebar breakpoint, hover on a message) or screenshot checks; start from `src/components/chat/MessageList.tsx` and `src/components/chat/MessageActions.tsx`.


### Chat Switching Speed: What Is Left _(Needs Scheduling)_
Switching conversations was a full server render with nothing on screen until it finished. **Still open:** (1) measure on a phone; the logs only cover the server. (2) The bigger step is a client-driven shell: messages fetched per conversation on the client with a per-conversation cache that is shown at once and revalidated, which is what makes Slack feel instant; only worth it if (1) shows the server render still dominates. (3) The `staleTimes` window can show up to 30s old data on any page whose data changes without a server action calling `revalidatePath` (API routes, webhooks, and member actions with none: `src/app/actions/in-app-notifications.ts`, `src/app/(member)/wheel-of-wonder/actions.ts`); audit those, or move the window to a per-route `cacheLife` stale time if the app adopts Cache Components. (4) When the unread line moves after a cached catch-up the reader is not scrolled to it. (5) Mobile browsers discard background tabs and reload on return; check that chat responses don't send `Cache-Control: no-store` (which keeps a page out of the back/forward cache) and that a cold load restores the last conversation (the `LAST_CHAT_COOKIE` already does).

### Chat Group Messages: Adding People _(Needs Scoping)_
The Hub offers no controls to add or remove people in a group message (`src/lib/chat/membership-abilities.ts`, `src/app/(member)/chat/membership-actions.ts` refuse `group_dm`), but Slack's own app does let you add someone to a DM. As far as we know it does that by creating a new group conversation (hence its "Include conversation history?" step), which the API can't do to an existing one, and the bot can't make a replacement under the same Hub conversation id. Deferred: this is the safe behaviour until it is verified, and it only costs members a trip to Slack. Not verified: whether `conversations.invite` really refuses an `mpim`, and whether a new `mpim` can be opened with `conversations.open` listing everyone and the history copied. **Start here:** check both against the Slack docs and a test workspace; if a new conversation is the only way, the Hub would need to open it, link it to the old one in `chat_channels`, and decide what the old one shows. Billie Bot is deliberately left out of a group message's label (`groupDmLabels` in `src/lib/chat/load.ts`): it is in every group and isn't a Hub member.

### Lesson Comments on Courses _(Needs Scoping)_
Ania wants comments on lessons, mainly so instructors can answer questions in place. The design is in `docs/product/COURSES.md` ("Lesson comments") and its six decisions were made on 2026-10-28 (see "Decisions" there), so it is ready to build but not started. Start with phase 1 of that plan (`lesson_comments` table with the `cmnt_` id prefix, RLS and pgTAP, the thread on the lesson page, the unanswered-questions admin list) behind a feature flag. Phase 4 (polish) is built except per-comment read state and Realtime, below. The `cmnt_` prefix is chosen (`cmt_` is `prickle_commitments`). Decided 2026-11-02: after access ends the course overview and progress stay visible, and comments (like lessons, videos and downloads) are closed. **Kajabi comments will be imported (decided 2026-10-30)**, not skipped: see "Kajabi migration" under "Lesson comments" in `docs/product/COURSES.md`. It needs phase 1's `lesson_comments` table first, and a source nobody has looked at: Kajabi's public API has no comments, so the first step is `courses:content --probe` on the admin page where comments are listed or moderated, then a `--comments` capture like `--progress`. Until then an admin view must show "not imported", never 0.


**Phases 1, 2 and 3 are built (flag `lesson_comments`, off for everyone; phase 3's sending is in dry run until the `lesson_comment_notifications` flag is on for someone).** Deferred from them, and where to start:
- **Resolved (phase 3):** follows, reply and instructor-reply notifications, the daily digest, the staff note about reported and held comments, "N new since your last visit", and the `?comment=` scroll and highlight (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Phase 3 (notifications), as built"). **Still open from it:**
- **Switching the notifications on.** Watch the logs of `postLessonComment` and `/api/internal/lesson-comment-notifications` for a run or two (member id, kind, why, channels, message), then widen the `lesson_comment_notifications` flag: yourself, staff, a segment, everyone. The route has no Checkly heartbeat yet (`unmonitored` in `src/lib/cron-heartbeats.ts`, the plan is at its monitor limit): add one with the same steps as the other unmonitored jobs once there is room.
- **Staff hear about new questions, not just the queue.** The design also gave staff new top-level questions and an instructor digest; today staff get a daily count of reported and held comments, and find questions in the unanswered list. A digest of new unanswered questions per course is a second kind and a second section of `sendModerationDigest`. The course page also still has no count badge beside its Comments link (`CourseCommentRepository.moderationBacklog` is the read).
- **Resolved (phase 4):** @-mentions with their own notification kind, video timestamps in comments, "N comments, M new" per lesson in the course outline (staff also see unanswered), the "Instructor answers" filter, and the unanswered and moderation lists linking with `?comment=` (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Phase 4, as built"). **Still open from phase 4:**
- **Mentions: loose ends.** A mention token holds the member id, so if two members are merged the old id is no longer a course member and the mention reads "@someone" (the merge doesn't rewrite comment text; add a pass over `lesson_comments.body` to the member merge if it matters). Mentions are not in the daily digest. The mention list needs one character after the `@`; offering the thread's participants for a bare `@` would need the thread's authors passed to the composer. An edit that removes and re-adds someone doesn't tell them twice (the claim is per member and comment).
- **Read markers are per lesson.** "N new" counts comments since the last visit to the thread, and a refresh after posting resets it, so a member who opens a thread and leaves has seen everything as far as the marker goes. Per-comment read state (a row per member and comment, or a "last seen comment" cursor) would let the thread mark each unread comment and keep "new" until it is scrolled to. **Why deferred:** the design's phase 4 list names only counts in the outline, the per-lesson marker already feeds them, and a per-comment table grows with every comment times every reader. **Where to start:** `lesson_comment_reads` and `CourseCommentRepository.markRead`/`outlineCounts` (add a cursor column, expand-only), `countUnread` in `src/lib/courses/comments.ts`.
- **Realtime updates (new comments without a reload).** Not built. **Why deferred:** a comment is readable only by members with current access to the lesson's course, and held and hidden ones only by their author and staff, so a broadcast of the row would leak unless the channel is private with a `realtime.messages` policy that repeats `has_course_access` and the held and hidden rules, and the broadcast trigger would have to send no text (just "something changed in lesson X", so the page refreshes through the normal read). That is a migration, a policy and a pgTAP file for a polish item, and members mostly reply after the instructor does, not within minutes of each other. **Where to start:** `.claude/rules/use-realtime.mdc` (a `broadcast` trigger on `lesson_comments` with a private topic `lesson:<id>:comments`), a policy on `realtime.messages` for `topic` like that, a client hook that calls `router.refresh()` and an `aria-live` note; test that a member without access, and a held comment's reader, get nothing.
- **Replies to others in a thread.** Only the author of the comment being replied under (and, for an instructor, the lesson's followers) is told; other members of the thread are not, unless they follow the lesson and the digest reaches them. Add "participants" as an audience if members expect it.
- **Followers without access are skipped; staff who follow need no access** (they are always eligible).
- **Verifying the follows table against the real stack.** The new migration's pgTAP file passed in a rolled-back transaction on a local database that had phases 1 and 2 applied; the repository contract suite against Supabase (`tests/api/data/course-comments-repository.test.ts`) and `pnpm types:check` were left to CI.
- **Resolved (phase 2):** the link-count hold, reports, staff hide, unhide, delete and approve, dismissing reports, the moderation queue, "Mark handled", and one audit entry per staff action with no text (`moderate_lesson_comment`; `docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Phase 2 (moderation), as built"). **Still open from it:**
- **Resolved (phase 3): staff are told about new reports and held comments** by the daily `lesson_comment_moderation` note (dry run first). **Still open:** the count badge beside the course's Comments link.
- **The Instructor badge should mean the course's instructor, not "any admin".** **Resolved:** a course has a list of instructors (`course_instructors`, edited in the course settings), the flag is computed from "the author is one of this course's instructors" when a comment is posted and in the Kajabi import, recomputed for the course's existing comments when its instructors change, and staff who are not instructors comment as ordinary members (no badge, no answer, plain reply notification). See `docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Course instructors, as built". **Still open:** rename the `lesson_comments.is_staff_reply` column to say what it means (`is_instructor_reply`), expand then contract in a second PR (the TypeScript field is already `isInstructorReply`); until then the column keeps the old name with its comment explaining the meaning.
- **Staff employment dates, so an old or future staff role doesn't badge comments.** A person who was staff only for a period, or becomes staff later, should not carry the staff or instructor badge on comments outside those dates. **Why deferred:** the `staff` table (`StaffRepository`) has a `hire_date` but no end date, and the flag is stored per comment when it is written, so imported and older comments keep whatever it said then. **Where to start:** add start and end dates to staff (expand-only), keep the badge stored when the comment is written, not derived on every read (decided 2026-11-24: cheaper): check the author's role against those dates at write time, and in the import against the comment's original Kajabi date, and recompute a person's stored flags when their dates are corrected (the same recompute the course's instructor list uses when it changes). Cover it in the same change as the instructor item above, since both decide what the badge means.
- **Undoing a staff delete.** Delete is a soft delete and the text stays (30 days by design), but there is no action to bring a staff-deleted comment back, and the moderation queue lists only open reports and held comments, not what staff already removed. Add a `restore` action to `moderate_lesson_comment` (the guard trigger already lets admins clear `deleted_at`) and a "Recently removed" list.
- **Reporter feedback and abuse.** A reporter sees "Reported" until the page reloads, then the Report button is back (the page doesn't know their open reports), and a second try says "already reported". Add `reportedByMe` to `ThreadComment` (one read of the viewer's open reports) if it confuses members. Reports are rate limited per member (10 an hour, 30 a day); there is no staff view of a member who reports a lot.
- **Held comments by staff or on edit.** Staff are never held, and an edit that would add links is refused instead of held; if that proves too strict, hold the edit too (it needs the previous text kept, since the comment is already visible).
- **Reports and the data export / `/privacy` line.** Reports are in `member_data_handling` (deleted with the reporter) but the member data export and the privacy page do not list comments or reports yet (see the retention item below).
- **Quoting the author when replying to a reply** (the design says a reply to a reply attaches to the parent "quoting the author's name"); today it just attaches.
- **Deep link to a comment.** **Resolved (phase 3):** `?comment=<cmnt_…>` scrolls to the comment and marks it, and notifications link that way. **Resolved (phase 4):** the unanswered and moderation lists link with `?comment=` too.
- **Retention of deleted text (30 days), "Former member" after 90 days, the data export and the privacy page line** are not built (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Privacy and retention").
- **Funnel** for lesson viewed, comments seen, comment posted, instructor replied (`src/lib/analytics/funnels.ts`); the four events exist.
- **Counts in the course outline for staff** are built (**Resolved (phase 4):** total, new and unanswered badges per lesson in the outline). **Still open:** the design's sortable per-lesson table for staff (`SortableTh`, `DataTablePager`) under `/admin/courses/<id>/comments`; the badges cover finding the lessons that need an answer, so add the table only if staff want to sort or export.
- **Verifying the db suite locally.** The repository contract suite against the real database and the pgTAP file were only partly run on the author's machine (pgTAP passed against a fresh local stack; the PostgREST contract run timed out under load); CI is the first full run.

### Resubscribe link on an ended course _(Needs Scheduling)_
When a member's access to a course has ended, the course stays in `/courses` and its overview page shows the outline and their progress with every lesson closed (decided 2026-11-02). The overview should say so at the top: "Access ended: resubscribe to this course here", with a link. Today it shows only "Your access ended on DATE. Your progress is saved below, but the lessons are closed." **Why deferred:** the right link is not settled (the course's Kajabi checkout or offer URL, a Hub page, or the support address), and checkout stays on Kajabi for now. **Where to start:** the `access.kind === "ended"` block in `src/app/(member)/courses/[slug]/page.tsx`; a per-course resubscribe URL would live next to `courses.access_duration` (a column or a setting on the admin course page), read as config, never a literal. Related: the reminder before access ends (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Later").

### Reminder before a course's access ends _(Needs Scheduling)_
A member is told some weeks before their access to a course ends, with a prompt to continue with membership billing. It matters because access to a limited-term course simply stops: The Ready Writer is one year and includes membership for that term, and afterwards membership continues only if the member subscribes separately (decided 2026-10-18; the course overview then shows "access ended", with their progress and every lesson closed, `docs/product/COURSES.md` "Member experience"). **Why deferred:** it is a notification feature, not part of the launch, and two things are unsettled: how many weeks before (one reminder or two, say four weeks and one week), and where the prompt leads (the same resubscribe or membership-billing link as "Resubscribe link on an ended course", which checkout stays on Kajabi for now). **Where to start:** a notification kind in `src/lib/notifications/registry.ts` sent through `createNotifier` (never a direct Slack DM), with its sample message in `src/lib/notification-test.ts` and an email template in `src/emails/notifications/`; choose the members with `course_access.ends_at` inside the window and, for a course whose program includes membership (`programs.includes_membership`), who are not already on membership billing (derive that from membership history, never store it); a dedup log so a member gets each reminder once per `(member, course, ends_at)`; dates in the member's own time zone (`src/lib/timezone.ts`, falling back to `ORG_TIMEZONE`); a feature flag with `liveFor` so it starts in dry run (`src/lib/member-reminders.ts` is the pattern); the message links to the exact thing (the course page or the resubscribe link), with no link preview; declare its events in `src/lib/analytics/events.ts`. Tests: the window edges, the dedup, a member who already subscribed, and a course with no end date (never reminded).

### Pop-Up Prickle ids change while a meeting is still running, which breaks check-ins _(Needs Scheduling)_
A member answering a check-in or check-out DM, or dismissing one, sometimes fails with a foreign key violation (`prickle_checkins_prickle_id_fkey`, `prickle_checkout_dismissals`), logged by `/api/webhooks/slack/interactions` as "not saved" and "dismiss checkout: insert failed"; the two prickle ids seen on 2026-11-03 and 2026-11-04 are not in production, and one of them was still viewable about 90 minutes before. **Cause (found in production, not yet proven for those two ids since the rows are gone):** zoom-sourced prickles ("Pop-Up Prickle", purpose `writing`, so they get check-in and drop-in DMs; 68 in the last 14 days) are keyed on `(zoom_meeting_uuid, start_time, end_time)`. `reprocess_prickle_attendance_atomic` deletes every zoom prickle in the window whose tuple is not in the new data, and `splitMeetingIntoSegments` (`src/app/api/process/attendance/route.ts`) takes the end from the meeting's end, which moves while a meeting is running and when a scheduled prickle splits it. So the row is deleted and a new one with a new id is inserted. Every table that references `prickles` cascades (`prickle_checkins`, both dismissal tables, `prickle_checkin_dm_log`, `calendar_feed_items`, `prickle_attendance`), so the member's saved check-in answers and the DM dedup row go with it (the dedup loss can send a second DM for the new id). Calendar prickles are not affected: they upsert on `calendar_event_id`. **Why deferred:** a fix changes the prod processing function and needs a decision. **Where to start:** keep a pop-up's id when only its end moves (update the end of the row with the same `zoom_meeting_uuid` and `start_time` before the delete/upsert, in a new migration of `reprocess_prickle_attendance_atomic`; read the latest definition first), with a reprocessability test in `tests/api/reprocessability/` that reprocesses a growing meeting and expects the same id and its check-in still there. Also: make the Slack interaction handlers answer "that prickle isn't there any more" instead of failing silently (`src/lib/data/prickle-activity.ts` `saveCheckin`/`dismiss`), and consider not offering check-ins to a pop-up that is still running. **Correction (checked in production, 2026-11-04):** the end does not move while a meeting runs: in the last 30 days all 220 meetings arrived in a single import after the meeting ended. What changes the end is which run processes the room: a webhook or nightly run whose date range stops at midnight UTC (19:00 Eastern in winter) cannot see a prickle starting after it, so it builds one whole-meeting Pop-Up Prickle, and a later run with a full range replaces it with the right one under a new id. The cascade list is also longer: `ignored_zoom_appearances` and `prickle_popularity` cascade too, and `member_activities` and `writing_progress_entries` lose their link (set null). See "Check-ins and check-outs for Pop-Up Prickles" below. **Resolved (range):** attendance processing now reads every meeting a run touches whole (`loadWholeMeetings`, `src/lib/processing/attendance.ts`) and the Zoom import covers a date-only end day whole (`importReprocessRange`), so the range a run was asked for no longer changes a Pop-Up Prickle's start or end, and a room is processed within minutes of its end instead of whenever a wide enough run happens along. **Resolved (ids):** `reprocess_prickle_attendance_atomic` keeps a Pop-Up Prickle's id when only its end moves (migration `20270512000000_stable_pup_ids.sql`: it updates the end of the row with the same meeting and start in place; test `tests/api/reprocessability/stable-pup-ids.test.ts`), so a later end change (a calendar prickle added inside the meeting, a member newly matching a late joiner) can't send a second check-out or lose answers. **Resolved (Slack reply):** answering, skipping or submitting a DM about a prickle that no longer exists now replaces the message with a plain "out of date" note instead of failing silently (`PRICKLE_GONE`, `goneDmBlocks` in `src/lib/prickle-checkin-dms.ts`; the prickle is looked up first, since the real database refuses the row). **Still open:** a Pop-Up Prickle whose *start* moves (an earlier joiner newly matched) is still a new row under a new id; the `ON DELETE CASCADE` of `prickle_checkins` and the dismissal tables, for a real delete.

### Check-in pills show on work prickles; the DMs don't _(Needs Scheduling)_
The Check in / Check out pills (`checkinOffered`, `src/lib/upcoming-prickles.ts`) and the search palette offer one for any prickle the member has a tie to (hosting, a commitment, added to their calendar, in the room), whatever its type, while the check-in and check-out DMs and the dashboard's "How did it go?" card cover writing prickles only (`offersCheckins`, `src/lib/prickle-checkins.ts`). In the last 30 days that is 82 prickles of "work" types (Study Hall, Brainstorm, Pitch, Story Lounge, Book Club and similar) against 3,051 writing ones and 1 social. **Why deferred:** decided on 2026-11-06 to keep the pills as they are and only pause Pop-Up Prickles in them (`checkinsPausedFor`), because hiding them would remove something members may use and widening the DMs would send more; whether a work prickle should offer a check-in at all is a product decision. **Where to start:** decide per purpose (writing, work, social, mixed), then make the pills, the palette, the DMs and the card ask one function (`offersCheckins`), with a test per purpose; the check-out DM's progress questions only make sense for writing.

### Check-in and check-out design for a continuous stay across several prickles _(Needs Scheduling)_
A member who writes through a Pop-Up Prickle and then a Progress prickle is one stay in the room but, today, several prompts: in the case that started this, one evening produced four check-out notifications, the member answering only the one for the scheduled prickle. Members asked for fewer prompts, and for each "flavor" of prickle to keep its own check-out, which pull in different directions. The check-ins are for the writers' own reflection, but the plan is also to use the data to help them, so what a prompt is saved against decides what can be learned. **How the data may be used (the owner's examples, as given to the members):** notice that 7pm prickles are too late and leave a member more tired, while 7am prickles leave them energized even if they were tired at check-in; that a particular host's vibes work better for a member; that a particular kind of prickle (sprints, say) exhausts them; and recommend in the moment, "you're always super productive when a particular member is on and is running a Pop-Up Prickle right now, want to join?". With back-to-back prickles of one kind and the same (or no) host, one check-in at the first and one check-out at the last could be attributed as "you often feel X in this kind of session"; with very different kinds, or different hosts, it is harder to learn from and to make recommendations. It is admitted that it is not yet known what the analysis and recommendations should look like: for now the aim is to gather data in a way that stays meaningful whichever it turns out to be, which argues for keeping the kind, host, time of day and minutes for each prickle of a stay and not only a combined answer. **Decided so far (2026-11-06):** quiet hours are out, since the midnight crew should be asked as they finish; a late guard is not needed, since the late sends seen after the range fix were the neighbouring-prickle bug (fixed); drop-in check-ins are on for everyone, so a member who joins without a plan gets the check-in and the check-out as a pair (the `checkin_dropins` flag stays as the off switch); Pop-Up Prickle check-ins stay paused until this is settled. **Asked of the members (#hub-feedback, replies pending):** one check-out at the end of a stay, or one per prickle. **Open questions:** (1) what is analysed, the stay or the prickle: per-prickle answers give clean time-of-day, kind and host attribution but force several prompts in a stay; one answer per stay is lighter but a mixed stay (kind or host changes) must be attributed by minutes spent in each, or only counted when the stay is uniform; (2) in a mixed stay, one prompt or one per flavor, or one prompt that can optionally be split where the kind or host actually changes; (3) which prickle an answer is saved against, and what a progress entry such as a word count attaches to when the check-out covers several; (4) the gap that still counts as one stay (existing numbers: 10 minutes for a bathroom break, `EARLY_LEAVE_GRACE_MS`, and 15 for the edge-stay rule, `EDGE_STAY_MS`). **Leading option, not decided:** one check-in at the start of a stay and one check-out at its end, saved against the stay's last prickle with the stay's other prickles and the minutes in each recorded alongside, keeping the per-prickle tables and flows (`prickle_checkins`, the dismissals) and adding the attribution; a per-prickle prompt only where the kind or host changes. Other options weighed: a combined check-out and check-in at each transition of a multi-prickle session, which takes 6 prompts in a 3-prickle run-on to 4 (start, middle, middle, end) but needs the member at the keyboard at each switch; and per-prickle prompts with suppression rules (smallest change, a pile of special cases). Rules the owner floated as examples, none chosen: send no check-in or check-out when a session holds two Pop-Up Prickles; skip the final check-out when the member stayed under 30 minutes in the last prickle; check in at the first prickle and out of the last when they are the same kind with the same (or no) host. The owner's closing position: tone it down for back-to-back prickles somehow, and open the thinking to the members, especially those with data backgrounds. **Where to start:** get the members' answer and the owner's on (1) and (2); then define a stay once (sessionize presence and attendance with the gap in (4), in `src/lib/zoom-presence.ts` beside `staysOf`, so the DMs, the card and the pills share it), decide the storage for (3) (a column or a small `stays` table, with a migration and the expand-then-contract steps), update `checkoutAnswered` and the prompts' wording, then bring Pop-Up Prickle check-ins back through `checkinsPausedFor`.

### Check-ins and check-outs for Pop-Up Prickles _(Needs Scheduling)_
Paused as of 2026-11-04 (`offersCheckins` in `src/lib/prickle-checkins.ts`: no check-in or check-out DM, no "How did it go?" card entry, for a prickle with source `zoom`). **Why paused:** a member got four check-out notifications for one continuous evening of writing (a Pop-Up Prickle, then a Progress prickle). Checked in production: (1) a Pop-Up Prickle's row only exists once some run has covered its room, so it is created hours after it ends (median 5.1 hours when it starts before 19:00 Eastern, 18.9 hours after; 64 of 129 in the last 30 days were created more than 6 hours after they ended, past the 6-hour check-out lookback, and only 25 of 129 have a check-out sent); the early-leaver rule (10 minutes after leaving) cannot fire for one, because it works from prickle rows; (2) the row is rebuilt under a new id when a run with a different date range processes the room again (see "Pop-Up Prickle ids change..." above), so the same member gets the same check-out again and saved answers are lost (28 check-outs since 1 October point at a prickle that no longer exists, all sent between 19:00 and 01:00 Eastern); (3) nothing stops a check-out at midnight or hours after the end, and a continuous stay across a Pop-Up Prickle and a scheduled prickle gets one prompt per prickle. Every Pop-Up Prickle check-out goes to everyone in the room, since a Pop-Up Prickle has no host, commitment or calendar tie. **Why deferred:** the processing range is fixed now (see the entry above), which makes most Pop-Up Prickles exist within minutes of their room ending, so turning check-ins back on would send many more than before; the rules need a decision first. **Where to start:** keep a Pop-Up Prickle's id when only its end moves (the entry above), then settle the stays design ("Check-in and check-out design for a continuous stay", next entry up) before turning it back on by changing `checkinsPausedFor` (`src/lib/prickle-checkins.ts`). Dropped since this was written: quiet hours (a member writing at midnight should get the check-out as they finish) and a late guard (the late sends found after the range fix were a different bug, fixed by keeping the neighbouring prickle in the cron's window). A longer-term option is a live Pop-Up Prickle made from presence events (someone in the room outside any scheduled prickle), so check-in and the early-leaver check-out work for it too.

### Audit the app for aggregating in JavaScript what the database should aggregate _(Needs Scheduling)_
Pages and routes pull every row of a large table into the server (paginated 1000 at a time, one request after another) and then count, group and bucket them in JavaScript. The Slack engagement page (`src/app/(admin)/admin/insights/slack-engagement/page.tsx`) read about 9k messages and 47k reactions this way, ran past its 60s limit on 2026-11-04 and made the browser crash; caching the date formatters (#1004) only lowered the cost per row. **Why deferred:** each page needs its own SQL function or view, repository method (both classes), contract test and migration; it is a sweep, not one change. **Where to start:** grep for `fetchAllRows` and `.range(` callers in `src/app/` and `src/lib/` whose result is then reduced to counts, maps or buckets (`src/lib/slack-engagement-stats.ts`, `src/lib/community-stats.ts`, the other `/admin/insights` and `/admin/stats` pages), rank them by the size of the table in production (`select count(*)` through `supabase db query --linked`), and move the biggest to a database-side aggregate that returns the few hundred rows the page shows; start with Slack engagement, which has its own entry below (counts by channel, by Eastern hour and weekday, by emoji, last activity per channel). Pages that need a time zone aggregate with `AT TIME ZONE` naming `ORG_TIMEZONE`.

### Slack engagement page: aggregate in the database, and count Hub chat too _(Needs Scheduling)_
`/admin/insights/slack-engagement` pulls every live Slack message (all time, for the dormant-channel numbers), every reaction in the date range (year to date by default) and the channel list into the server, one 1000-row page after another, and groups them in JavaScript (`src/lib/slack-engagement-stats.ts`; `liveMessagesBetween`, `liveReactionsBetween` and `liveMessageTimes` in `src/lib/data/slack-messages.ts`). In production on 2026-11-04 that was about 9k messages and 47k reactions, and the page ran past its 60 s limit (Sentry HUB-X, resolved afterwards; #1004 only made the per-row Eastern-time formatting about 45x cheaper, and the row count grows with the community). **Why deferred:** it needs a migration, a repository method on both classes and a contract test, and the page is about to change shape: the stats should later include Hub chat as well as Slack, so the aggregate should be designed around that, not as a Slack-only patch. **Where to start:** one SQL function (or view) that returns the few hundred rows the page shows instead of the raw rows: per channel (messages, thread replies, reactions, distinct people, last activity, all-time message count, which replaces `liveMessageTimes`), per Eastern weekday and hour (`AT TIME ZONE` with `ORG_TIMEZONE`, never the server's zone: `dates-and-timezones.md`), top emoji, and the hero numbers; behind `SlackMessageRepository`, in the interface, both classes and the contract suite. Keep the privacy rules: only metadata (the `slack_messages_meta` view, no text), the function runs as the caller (or checks `is_admin()` / community manager itself), and the page's rule that group DMs and 1:1 DMs count in the totals but never appear as channels. **Hub chat:** the `chat_*` tables (`chat_channels`, `chat_messages`, `chat_reactions`; `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`) mirror bridged Slack conversations today, so adding them to a Slack count would count the same message twice; decide the unit first (one "conversation activity" shape with a source column, bridged messages counted once, Hub-native ones as their own source) and write the aggregate over that. **Tests:** more than 1000 rows (the pagination rule), 23:30 and 00:30 Eastern and the two daylight-saving changes, a restricted channel, and a DM; and measure the same job against production's row counts before and after (`verify-against-prod.md`), not against the local seed. Part of the wider "Audit the app for aggregating in JavaScript" entry above.
### Reloading the page after a server action cancels its response (Firefox, Safari) _(Resolved 2026-11-07)_
- **What was wrong:** Sentry HUB-T (Firefox, "Error in input stream"), HUB-5 and HUB-D (Safari, "Load failed") on `/projects` and `/projects/<id>`, and the matching "Page /projects crashed" alerts in #hub-alerts. `ProjectsClient` and `ProjectDetailClient` ran `window.location.reload()` as soon as a server action returned. The reload cancels the action's response while the browser is still reading it (the POST and the page prefetches show as `NS_BINDING_ABORTED` in Firefox and `cancelled` in WebKit), which those browsers raise as a TypeError; Chromium doesn't, which is why it was never seen locally. The data was saved each time.
- **Reproduced** with Playwright in Firefox and WebKit against a production build of the demo stack (create a project through the form, eight times each): both browsers logged the exact production messages and the aborted POSTs; after replacing the reload with `router.refresh()` there were no console errors and no failed requests, and each new project showed in the list.
- **Resolved:** both clients call `router.refresh()` (the action already revalidates the path); the detail page keys its client on the two values it copies into state (`phase`, `showOnProfile`) so it starts again when a change moves them. Tests: `tests/components/ProjectsRefresh.test.tsx`, `tests/components/pages/project-detail-page.test.tsx`, `tests/components/ProjectGoalsPlacement.test.tsx`.
- **Hosting:** `HostingScheduleManager` (the Hosting tab of My Prickles) had the same reload and the same errors in Firefox and WebKit (reproduced: six `Error in input stream` / `Load failed` in six request-and-withdraw rounds, none after). It now reads the page's list directly, closes the request form and calls `router.refresh()`. **Still open:** nothing, unless HUB-T, HUB-5 or HUB-D reopen (resolved in the release with the projects fix, so a new event from a newer release reopens them). The admin buttons that reload after a plain `fetch` (`ProcessOrphaned*`) are not server actions and are unaffected. A new `window.location.reload()` after a server action brings the problem back: use `router.refresh()`.

### Hub chat notifications: what is left _(built in dry run; Needs Scheduling to widen)_
**Built** (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Notifications"): the kinds `chat_dm`, `chat_mention`, `chat_channel_message`, the "Hub chat is my main place" switch with Slack's side as guided steps (Slack has no API for a member's notification preferences), per-conversation mute and level, one notification per conversation until read (resolved by reading in the Hub or in Slack), all behind the `chat_notifications` feature in dry run. **Still open:** (1) Read the dry-run logs for a run or two (`[chat-notifications] dry run`), then widen: yourself, a segment, everyone. (2) Notify when someone replies in a thread the member took part in (today a reply notifies only when it mentions them, or in a direct message). (3) A digest or quiet hours for a member who turns on email: one email per unread stretch can still be many a day. (4) Reading in Slack resolves a conversation's in-app notifications when Slack's marker is pulled (`pullReadFromSlack`), which runs on opening the Hub or a chat; a member who only reads in Slack and never opens the Hub keeps the in-app notification until then. (5) Sample notifications link to the chat list, not a conversation, as there is no real one to name. (6) Decide whether the switch should also set Slack-side hints per conversation once Slack offers an API.

### Chat: quote or share a Hub message _(Needs Scheduling)_

Hub Chat draws a Slack message that was shared (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Shared Slack messages"), but a member cannot yet quote or share a Hub message themselves. **Deferred:** it is a feature of its own, and the data model is open. **Design note:** a share should be a row, like `chat_message_videos`, not text (`message_id`, `quoted_message_id`), because the trigger that rewrites a message's content from Slack's copy would wipe a Hub-only entry. The card shows a copy of the quoted message stored with the share, to everyone who can read the sharing message (as for Slack shares, `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`), and the sharer must be able to read what they quote. **Requirement found while changing the Slack rule: the sharer must be warned before sending.** Slack does this and the Hub must too, because the copy reaches everyone who can read the new message: when the quoted message is from a private conversation (a private channel, a group DM or a DM, and any restricted channel), show "Share this with N people in #channel? You're about to share a private message from X. Make sure you're not sharing sensitive info before sending." with "Edit message" and "Send" before anything is saved. A message from a public channel needs no warning. Decide with the owner whether a share from a DM into a channel is refused outright (probably) rather than warned. To Slack it goes as the message's permalink (`/chat/<channel>?m=<id>`) in the text, like the lesson-video line. Also open: sharing from a DM into a channel (probably refused), and quoting a thread reply. Start at `src/components/chat/SharedMessageCard.tsx` (the card already takes a readable/private source) and `src/lib/chat/shared-message.ts`.

### Move the per-kind send logs onto `notification_log` _(Needs Scheduling)_

`notification_log` (`NotificationLogRepository`, `src/lib/data/notification-log.ts`) is the one log of what the app sent, keyed by recipient (a member or a shared place), kind and a dedup key; the changelog digest is its first user. Two older logs still do the same job per feature: `member_reminder_log` (intro and prickle reminders, which also use its `sent_at` history for backoff, `src/lib/member-reminders.ts`) and `prickle_checkin_dm_log` (check-in and check-out DMs, `src/lib/prickle-checkin-dms.ts`). Deferred because moving them rewrites live senders and needs the expand-then-contract steps (backfill the rows into `notification_log`, switch the readers and writers, then drop the old tables with the user's approval of each drop). Start with `prickle_checkin_dm_log`, whose claim is the same shape; the reminders also need a "when was the last one sent" read added to the repository.

### Changelog screenshots from the demo scenes
**Resolved:** `pnpm changelog:capture <slug>` runs an entry's demo scene (`tests/demo/scenes/changelog/<slug>.spec.ts`, tagged `@changelog`) on desktop light and writes the screenshots into `public/changelog/<slug>/` as small WebP files; the validator fails an entry whose image file is missing; the scheduled help-screenshots run skips `@changelog` scenes. See `docs/runbooks/CHANGELOG_PROCESS.md`, "Screenshots". **Still open:** scenes for the entries after the first one are written as each entry ships; the capture needs the demo stack and app running by hand, so it is not part of CI.

### Usual feelings in Find a Prickle's check-in _(Needs Scheduling)_

The check-in and check-out modals and the Log Progress modal list the member's most named feelings first (`frequentEmotions` in `src/lib/prickle-checkins.ts`, `PrickleActivityRepository.frequentEmotions`, the Slack DMs' "Frequently used" group), but Find a Prickle's wizard (`src/app/(member)/prickle-picker/PrickleWizard.tsx`) still shows the plain wheel because it has no server data to carry the list. Deferred to keep the first change small. Start by calling `getMyFrequentEmotions` (`src/app/(member)/prickles/checkin-actions.ts`) when the wizard opens and passing the result as `frequentEmotions` to its `CheckinHalfFields`. Also open: letting a member pin a feeling by hand, if the automatic list proves not to be enough.

### Calendar loader still queries per member for commitments and added items _(Needs Scheduling)_

The prickle check-ins and member reminders load each active member's calendar with `loadCalendarFeedPrickleIds` (`src/lib/calendar-feed.ts`). Sentry HUB-1X (an N+1 on the check-in route) came from its prickles reads, which now happen once per run (`preloadPrickleReader`). What is left is per member: `prickleActivity.commitmentsOf` and `calendarFeed.itemsOf` (plus `events.visibleEndingFrom` for added events), roughly three small queries per active member, in groups of 10 (`CALENDAR_LOAD_CONCURRENCY`). Deferred because they hit other tables and Sentry has not flagged them. Start with batch variants taking a member id list (chunked per `data-access-layer.md`) on both repositories and their contract suites, then group by member in memory.

### Checkly heartbeat for the changelog digest _(Needs Scheduling)_

`/api/internal/changelog-digest` (weekly) has its heartbeat switched off (`unmonitored` in `src/lib/cron-heartbeats.ts`) for the same reason as `/api/reconcile/email`: the plan's 10 uptime monitors are used. Remove that line once a monitor is free (see "Monitor the nightly import" in the email timeline notes for the steps).

### db tests that read the whole members table unpaginated _(Needs Scheduling)_

`tests/member-matching.test.ts` and `tests/api/reprocessability/orphaned-meetings-idempotency.test.ts` load members with a bare `.select('id, name, email')`, which PostgREST caps at 1000 rows. The shared test database grows as the suites run, so when it nears 1000 members the test's own member can fall outside the first 1000 and the match comes back null ("expected null not to be null", "expected +0 to be 1"). It surfaced on a PR whose only change was a new db test that briefly added two members (the same suites passed on `main` just before), which fits that cause but was not proven; the PR's test was changed to reuse existing members. Deferred because it needs a look at how many members the suites leave behind. Start by paginating those two reads with `fetchAllRows` (`src/lib/supabase/paginate.ts`) or filtering to the test's own rows, and check which suites leak members.

### Slack reachability on the admin member Notifications tab _(Needs Scheduling)_

The admin member page's Notifications tab (`src/app/(admin)/admin/members/[id]/MemberNotificationsPanel.tsx`) shows the browser devices, the email on record and each kind's channels against the defaults, but not whether the member can be reached on Slack. Deferred because the answer is not a simple lookup: the notifier only sends a Slack DM when the member matches exactly one Slack account by the safe match (alias, then email, then normalized name, deactivated accounts ignored), and an ambiguous match is rejected, logged and reported rather than guessed (see "Slack notification delivery" in `.claude/rules/member-notifications.md`).
