Showing a different rule on the tab would tell staff a member is reachable when the notifier would refuse to send. Start with the member-to-Slack resolution in `src/lib/slack-member-ids.ts` (the one `createNotifier` and `src/lib/member-reminders.ts` use): expose a read that returns the same result for one member (matched, none, or ambiguous, without sending or alerting), load it in `MemberDetailPage.tsx` only when the Notifications tab is open, and show it beside the Browser and Email lines. Read-only, behind the same `member.push_devices` staff feature, and no Slack ids on screen.

### Rich-text box for lesson comments _(Needs Scoping)_

Lesson comments are written in a plain `<textarea>` (`Composer` in `src/components/courses/CommentThread.tsx`, with its own hand-rolled `@mention` picker), while Hub Chat has the TipTap message box (`src/components/chat/MessageBox.tsx`: format toolbar, mentions, emoji, smart typography). Members who write a longer reply or question to an instructor would benefit from the same bold, lists, quotes and links, and the two boxes should feel alike. Deferred because it is more than a swap, and nobody has asked yet.

Where to start and what to decide:
- **Storage: keep plain text, reuse chat's mrkdwn dialect** (`docToMrkdwn` and `mrkdwnToDoc` in `src/lib/chat/rich-text.ts`). Existing comments, the Kajabi import (`src/lib/courses/comments-import.ts`) and mention tokens (`@[mbr_...]`) stay valid with no migration, since plain text is valid mrkdwn. Storing HTML or JSON would need a migration and sanitizing.
- **Rendering:** `Body` in `CommentThread.tsx` would use chat's renderer (`src/lib/chat/render.ts`) but must keep the clickable video timestamps and the `@[mbr_...]` to display-name lookup (`mentionNames`, `richParts` in `src/lib/courses/comment-text.ts`).
- **Mentions:** the chat box takes its suggestions from `suggestMentions` (`src/app/(member)/chat/mention-actions.ts`); comments must offer only people who can see the lesson (`src/lib/courses/comment-mentions.ts`). The shared box needs a pluggable mention source and trigger set (no `#channel` or custom emoji in comments, which are not bridged to Slack).
- **Emails and notifications:** the five `lesson-comment-*` emails (`src/emails/notifications/`) and any Slack or push text need formatting rendered or stripped, as chat already does for its excerpts.
- **Weight:** many edit boxes can be open in one thread, so load the editor lazily.
- Likely shape: extract a shared box core from `MessageBox` (mention source, triggers, toolbar preset) and use it in comments first; tests for the round trip of old plain-text comments, mentions and timestamps; a member-facing changelog entry when it ships. Related: the lesson text editor is a separate TipTap setup (see "Lesson editor" in the UI section); unify only if a shared rich-text core is wanted.

## Hub Chat: Beyond the First Unfurl Pass

### "Jump to latest" pill in chat _(Needs Scheduling)_

The "N new messages" pill (`src/components/chat/NewMessagesBanner.tsx`) only covers the unread above the reader when they open a conversation. A message that arrives live while the reader has scrolled up waits silently (it does not change the pill's count, on purpose). Slack shows a "Jump to latest" pill for that. Deferred to keep the first pill small; start in `MessageList` (scroll position vs. the bottom) and `ChatLive`, which already knows when the reader is at the bottom.

### Check-in DM controls in chat: follow-ups _(Needs Scheduling)_

Billie Bot's check-in and check-out controls work in Hub chat (`src/components/chat/DmControls.tsx`, `src/lib/chat/dm-controls-press.ts`, shared handlers in `src/lib/prickle-dm-interactions.ts`). Still open:
- **Fast double answers.** A press rewrites the Slack message from the blocks the Hub has stored; if a second answer comes before the Slack copy has come back through the mirror, it is written onto the older blocks and the first highlight can briefly revert in Slack (the saved answers are right). Read the current blocks from Bronze (`SlackMessageRepository.rawPayload`) or serialize presses per message.
- **The feeling list** is `EntitySearch`: the core feeling is the second line, there are no group headings, and the list before typing shows 50 options. A grouped variant of the picker would match Slack's list.
- **Not verified in a real browser**: the Chrome extension could not attach to localhost in the session that built this; checked in component tests only. Compare the bold, link and context sizes with Slack on a real DM.

### Full Block Kit, proxied pictures and interactivity in chat _(Needs Scheduling)_

The first pass (`src/lib/chat/unfurls.ts`, `src/components/chat/MessageUnfurls.tsx`) draws legacy attachments (link previews, app cards) and a small Block Kit subset (header, section, context, divider, image, link buttons), and shows one "Open in Slack" link for any other block. Deferred, worth doing when members ask for it or a common app's cards read badly:

- **More block types**: add a parser to `BLOCK_PARSERS` and a renderer to `BLOCK_RENDERERS` for `video`, `table`, `rich_text` inside an app's section, `context_actions`, `input` (display only), and accessory elements (menus, date pickers) shown disabled.
- **Pictures through our own address** — **Resolved:** unfurl pictures (card and block pictures, app logos, quoted authors) go through `GET /api/chat/unfurl-image` (`src/lib/chat/unfurl-images.ts`, `src/lib/net/safe-fetch.ts`; `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Pictures under messages"). **Still open:** see "Chat unfurl pictures: re-encode and keep a copy" and the CSP entries below.
- **Interactivity**: buttons that post back to an app (`action_id`) are not shown. Our own bot's messages could handle them Slack-independently (see "Interactive elements" in `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`); third-party apps stay "Open in Slack".
- **Search results** use a narrower content select, so cards are not shown there.
- **Message-level unfurl toggles** — **Resolved:** the author removes a preview (Hub's or Slack's) with the X on the card (`chat_hidden_unfurls`; `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Link previews for messages written in the Hub"). **Still open:** animated/video unfurls (`video_html`).
- **Embedded media and other apps' previews** (not tried, not built). A link Slack previews as something richer than one title-and-picture card: a YouTube, Vimeo or Loom link that Slack turns into a playable video (the attachment carries `video_html`), a social post, or a card an installed app (GitHub, Notion) supplies through `link_shared`/`chat.unfurl`, which can replace or add to Slack's own and may use blocks. Today the Hub draws what it understands from the attachment (title, text, picture, fields) and ignores `video_html`, so such a link shows a still or nothing. Worth doing: a click-to-play embed for the common video hosts (third-party players load the provider's scripts, so check `/privacy` and the CSP first: see "Enforce the Content-Security-Policy"), and an oEmbed fetch for Hub-written links to those hosts (see "Chat link previews: undo, a Hub-made preview for Slack-origin messages, richer pages"). Also check the author's X on such a message: removal sends the message's other attachments back to Slack in documented fields only (`src/modules/chat/slack/unfurl-removal.ts`), which may not round-trip an app's card or a video. Start in `src/lib/chat/unfurls.ts` (`parseCard`) and `src/components/chat/MessageUnfurls.tsx`.

### Chat link previews: undo, a Hub-made preview for Slack-origin messages, richer pages _(Needs Scheduling)_

The Hub makes link previews only for messages written in the Hub (`src/lib/chat/link-previews.ts`, behind `chat_link_previews`). Deferred: **undo** for a removed card (a DELETE policy for the author on `chat_hidden_unfurls` and a toast); **previews for links in messages that came from Slack when Slack made none** (a link in a private conversation, an app that disables unfurling): needs a decision on fetching for others' messages and a pull-based path; **re-fetching** a page whose preview failed or went stale (today an address is looked at once per message); **oEmbed** and rich players for video and social links; **a pull-based path**: previews are made right after a send and an edit, and a failure is not retried by the nightly run, so a transient network error leaves a plain link; **a copy of the picture** (see "Chat unfurl pictures: re-encode and keep a copy"). Start in `src/lib/chat/link-previews.ts` and `src/lib/data/chat-link-previews.ts`.

### Chat: more kinds of Hub link card _(Needs Scheduling)_

Cards for links to the Hub (`docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Hub links", behind `chat_link_cards`) cover a quoted message, a prickle and a member profile. Not done, and why: **books and projects** (a book's page and a project's page are not both open to every member, and the shape of a book's address and what its card may show needs deciding first); **events** (`/events/<id>`: check who may open an event, and whether tickets or registration details are on the page); **course lessons** (a lesson shows a course the reader may not have, as `src/lib/chat/video-access.ts` handles for shared videos); and **chat search results**, which do not draw cards at all (they read a narrower content select). Also open: the card shows a quoted message's text only, so files, reactions and a message's own cards are left out, and a reply is quoted without its thread. Start in `src/lib/chat/hub-links.ts` and `src/lib/chat/hub-link-card.ts` (the tutorial is in the doc).

### Slack: unfurl Hub links _(Needs Scheduling)_

Slack readers still see Slack's own preview of a link to the Hub (the sign-in page) for a Hub message sent with unfurling on, and for a link someone pastes in Slack. The fix is for the Slack app to register the Hub's domain and answer `link_shared` with `chat.unfurl`, carrying only what is safe for everyone in the conversation (a Slack unfurl is seen by all of them, not by one reader). Needs the manifest and scopes changed and the app re-approved in the workspace, so it is its own change that the owner approves (a draft pull request holds the design and the privacy decisions).

### Chat link previews: limits of the Slack-side removal _(Needs Scheduling)_

When the author removes Slack's preview inside Slack on a message written in the Hub, the Hub now hides its own preview of that link (`src/modules/chat/slack/side-unfurl-removal.ts`; `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Link previews for messages written in the Hub"). **Resolved:** confirmed in production by the owner (2026-11-12) with a Hub-written link and a two-link message: Slack's "remove preview" arrives as the same message with an attachment fewer, and the Hub's card disappears live, without a reload. **Still open:** (1) a link Slack never unfurled gives no signal, so its Hub preview can only be removed with the X; (2) the poster is not checked against the Slack user who acted (the event does not name one); Slack lets only the poster remove a preview.

### Chat shared messages: other shares survive a Slack-side removal _(Needs Scheduling)_

When the author of a Hub-written message removes one shared Slack message with the X, the Slack copy is updated with the message's other attachments in documented fields only (`KEPT_FIELDS` in `src/modules/chat/slack/unfurl-removal.ts`). A second shared message on the same message would go back without `is_share`, `channel_id`, `channel_name` and `message_blocks`, so Slack may stop drawing it as a share. Not checked against Slack (no way to try it from a session), and a message with two permalinks is rare. Worth doing: try it with a message holding two permalinks, and add those fields to the kept list if Slack accepts them. Also: the card's "Posted in" line uses the attachment's `channel_name`, else its `footer`; confirm against real attachments which of the two Slack sends.

### Chat unfurl pictures: re-encode and keep a copy _(Needs Scheduling)_

**Resolved:** the picture proxy keeps a server-side copy: each distinct source address is fetched once, stored in the private `unfurl-images` bucket keyed by a hash of the address, kept 7 days, swept nightly by `/api/reconcile/media`, with a 5 minute in-instance memory of failures (`src/lib/chat/unfurl-image-cache.ts`; `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Pictures under messages"). **Still open:** the proxy does not decode and re-encode the bytes (a crafted file that is a valid PNG header with a hostile body still reaches the browser, inert behind `nosniff` and a sandbox CSP, and is now also stored). Worth doing: re-encode through `sharp` (as `src/lib/media/` does for uploads) with a pixel cap, before storing, so the copy is the re-encoded one. Also open: the failure memory is per server instance, so several instances can each ask a failing host once per 5 minutes. Start in `src/lib/chat/unfurl-image-cache.ts` and `src/lib/net/safe-fetch.ts` (`fetchImage`).

### Enforce the Content-Security-Policy

**Resolved (2026-11-17):** enforced as `Content-Security-Policy` (`src/lib/csp.ts`) after a week of reports; the one missing picture host (course thumbnails on `/courses`) went into `config/app.ts` `csp.imageHosts`, and `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `X-Frame-Options` and `Permissions-Policy` were added (`SECURITY_HEADERS`). **Still open:** watch Sentry for new CSP reports after the deploy (a member photo or cover on a host not listed shows up as a blocked picture): add the host or fix the data. Only `example.com` (a test) and the Kajabi storage host appeared in the first week, so a report from another host means a screen nobody visited yet. Also check `form-action 'self'` against any sign-in flow that posts a form and then redirects to another site.

### CSP for scripts and styles (nonces)

**Context:** the enforcing `Content-Security-Policy` covers only images, objects, base, forms and framing; it has no `script-src`, `style-src` or `connect-src` yet, so an injected script would not be blocked by it. The script policy described below is the way there.

**Report-only again, with a meta tag for pictures (2026-11-18):** `scriptStyleCsp` and `generateNonce` (`src/lib/csp.ts`) build the policy: `script-src 'nonce-…' 'strict-dynamic'`, `style-src 'self' 'nonce-…'`, `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` (about 60 components set `style={{…}}`, which a nonce can't cover), `worker-src 'self' blob:`. `src/proxy.ts` sends it as `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` (`reportOnlyCsp`) and hands Next the same policy on the request. That one policy also repeats the enforced picture, plugin, base, form and framing directives (so their violations are reported) and adds `connect-src`, `frame-src`, `media-src` and `font-src` from `config/app.ts` `csp.connectHosts`, `frameHosts` and `mediaHosts` (starting lists from what the code references; Supabase and Sentry are added from the environment), which nothing enforces yet. **Why the picture policy is a meta tag for now:** Next takes the nonce for its scripts from the request's `content-security-policy` header first, and on Vercel a header of that name from `next.config.ts` or from the proxy is copied onto the request, so a nonce that sits only in the report-only header is never read (found with a temporary diagnostic, 2026-11-18). So pages carry the enforcing picture, plugin, base and form directives as `<meta http-equiv>` (`buildMetaCsp`, `src/app/layout.tsx`) and no policy header; paths the proxy skips (static files, pictures, the Sentry tunnel, the service worker) still get the header from `next.config.ts`. The meta tag cannot carry `frame-ancestors` (`X-Frame-Options` still says the same) or `report-uri`, so its own violations are not reported; the report-only copy above reports them instead. **Still open:** (1) after a week of Sentry reports, fix what is blocked (the Google Analytics tag, Vercel Analytics and Speed Insights, session replay, TipTap, any library that injects a `<style>` tag; trace one report of a blocked string-evaluation call on `/admin/events`: which library, allow it or replace it); (2) then send **one** `content-security-policy` header with the picture directives (with `frame-ancestors` and `report-uri` back) and the script policy, and delete the meta tag and `buildMetaCsp`; (3) read the first reports for `connect-src`, `frame-src`, `media-src` and `font-src`, add the hosts they name to the config lists, and enforce them in the same header; (4) update the trust pages (`src/content/security.ts`, `src/content/compliance.ts`) when it is enforcing; (5) `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'` can go only after the inline styles move to classes or CSS variables set from a nonced rule. The Sentry issues from the first day (HUB-15 to HUB-1H, HUB-1D, HUB-16 to HUB-18) are resolved in the release that carries this, once nothing sends them.

**Week-one reports, read 2026-11-20:** **Resolved:** the Mux CDN hosts (`*.mux.com` in `connectHosts`, `image.mux.com` in `mediaHosts`) and the Google Analytics beacon (`https://www.google.com/g/collect` in `connectHosts`, pinned to the path because Google's own CSP guide allows all of `*.google.com`; if Sentry later names another `www.google.com` path, such as an ads or consent one, add that path, not the host) were added (HUB-1V, 1W, 1Y, 1Z, 1T, 20); the page builder now loads Puck's `no-external.css` instead of `puck.css`, which imported Inter from a font site (HUB-1R, 1S: resolved in the release that shipped it after a browser check showed no request to the font site, and no report since; Puck's own script carries the same `@import` and injects it only when its static stylesheet is not applied, which `no-external.css` prevents by setting the `--_puck-styles-loaded` marker, so if a report ever returns, check that the stylesheet loads before Puck first renders, or patch the package with `pnpm patch`); the blocked `eval()` (HUB-1E) came from browser copies of Node's `crypto` and `vm` modules that the bundler added because five repository files imported `node:crypto`, so they now use Web Crypto (`src/lib/random-int.ts`) and `tests/lib/client-node-builtins.test.ts` fails on a new client path to a Node built-in. **Still open:** (a) HUB-17 (inline `<style>`) came from headless Chrome on preview URLs in every event read (the screenshot and demo runs); confirm no real browser sends it, or filter that browser out of the report; (b) once the release is live, check Sentry for new `script-src` and `connect-src` reports for a few days before step (2); (c) the Inter font the editor used to fetch is now the app's own font stack, so look at the page builder once for any visible difference.

**Google Analytics cookie gap, read 2026-11-20 (not a CSP effect: the script and style policy is report-only):** about 13 of 91 active members never get the `_ga` cookie on any page view (2 of 7 on Firefox, 3 of 9 on desktop Safari, 6 of 37 on Chrome, 3 of 32 on iOS), which is why Firefox and Safari desktop show 72% and 35% of page views without it; the other members get it on every view (a new browser's first view has none, so a few "mixed" members are normal). It is not the EU consent default (the missing views are from outside the EU and UK, and EU visitors have the cookie), not the "leave me out of statistics" switch (none of those members switched it on), and not a data artefact (`access_events.ga_client_id` is empty before mid-October 2026, so earlier weeks say nothing). That leaves content blockers and browser tracking protection, which is a guess: the data cannot tell a blocked tag from a missing cookie. **Resolved:** the page-view beacon now says whether the tag was running (`access_events.ga_tag_state`: `tag_running`, `not_loaded`, `not_applicable`; `src/lib/analytics/ga-tag-state.ts`). **Still open:** read it once it has a few weeks of rows (NULL before it shipped and for older tabs), then decide whether to respect the blocking or do something about it, and ask one of those members which extension or setting they use. Read-only, counts only: members with page views but no `ga_client_id` in the last 30 days, by what their beacon said: `select count(*) filter (where tag_running_views = 0 and not_loaded_views > 0) as tag_blocked, count(*) filter (where tag_running_views > 0) as tag_running_no_cookie, count(*) filter (where tag_running_views = 0 and not_loaded_views = 0) as not_applicable_or_unreported from (select user_id, count(*) filter (where ga_tag_state = 'tag_running') as tag_running_views, count(*) filter (where ga_tag_state = 'not_loaded') as not_loaded_views from access_events where is_page and created_at > now() - interval '30 days' group by user_id having count(ga_client_id) = 0) m;` Agreement check (a running tag with analytics allowed should set the cookie): `select ga_tag_state, count(*) as views, count(ga_client_id) as views_with_cookie from access_events where is_page and ga_tag_state is not null and created_at > now() - interval '14 days' group by 1;`. Slow-tag check: `not_loaded` views that still carry a cookie mean the tag was only late, so the wait (`graceMs` in `watchGaTag`, 2 s after the page finished loading) is too short. The daily health check compares each member against their own history, so these members do not trip it. The daily health check compares each member against their own history, so these members do not trip it.

**Decided 2026-11-20, policy alignment with Google's strict-CSP guidance (web.dev/articles/strict-csp):** `base-uri` is `'none'` as Google recommends (nothing in the app or its libraries uses a `<base>` tag). `script-src` is the nonce and `'strict-dynamic'` alone, as in Google's example (changed from first keeping `'self'`, the same day): MDN says a browser that knows `'strict-dynamic'` ignores `'self'`, host sources, scheme sources and `'unsafe-inline'`, so `'self'` did nothing for any browser our members use (the oldest in the last 30 days is Chrome 150, Firefox 153, Edge 155, iOS 18.5, Safari 18, against Safari 15.4 as the first with `'strict-dynamic'`, going by web.dev's support versions), and it only mattered for a browser too old for it. The policy is still report-only, so nothing is blocked either way. **What to watch:** a browser without `'strict-dynamic'` would now report our own chunks as `script-src` violations, so an old browser shows up in Sentry (and in the daily browser check) instead of being quietly covered; the visitors we have not measured are people on public pages (`/p/…`, `/w/…`, the sign-in page), because `access_events` only covers signed-in members, so look at the browser of any such report before deciding whether to add a fallback when this is enforced. For public visitors the browser data already exists outside our database: Vercel Web Analytics (on every page, cookie-free, not part of the opt-out switch) stores browser, browser version and operating system with each page view and has a Browsers panel (whether the dashboard can split by version, not only by browser name, is not confirmed), and Google Analytics has a browser report for visitors who allowed it; look there for Safari below 15.4, Chrome or Firefox below 52 and Edge below 79 on `/`, `/p/…` and `/w/…`. **Decided 2026-11-20: we do not log anonymous visits ourselves.** It would be an unauthenticated write to our database from the open internet (needing rate limiting, batching and bot filtering) plus a new line on the privacy page, to repeat what the hosting provider's counts give. Not added: the `https:` and `'unsafe-inline'` fallbacks (Google calls them optional, "no need" for current browsers) and Trusted Types (`require-trusted-types-for 'script'`), which is a larger change to look at after the policy is enforcing.

**Script and style policy enforced, 2026-11-23:** Sentry read first (14 days, `logger:csp`): no `script-src`, `connect-src`, `frame-src`, `media-src` or `font-src` report since 2026-11-20; the only unresolved issue was HUB-17 (inline `<style>`), from headless Chrome on preview URLs plus three events from a real Chrome on the production page builder, all from a tab opened on an older build (the chunk named in the report no longer exists, and the `rsms.me` font reports, HUB-1R and HUB-1S, came from the same tab). **Resolved:** items (2), (3) and (4) of the first list: `src/proxy.ts` sends **one** `content-security-policy` header (`enforcedCsp`: script and style policy, picture, plugin, base, form and framing directives, connection, frame, media and font sources, `report-uri`), on the request (so Next finds the nonce) and the response; the meta tag, `buildMetaCsp` and the Report-Only header are gone; `next.config.ts` keeps its picture-and-framing header only for the paths the proxy skips; the trust pages say so. `tests/lib/csp-evaluator.test.ts` runs the policy through Google's CSP Evaluator in CI and fails on any finding. **Deployment-URL noise, 2026-11-23:** HUB-24, HUB-25 and the `pptr` events of HUB-17 (and earlier HUB-15 to HUB-1Q) were a headless Chrome that opens `/login` on each new deployment's own URL (production-target deployments: previews are off; the visitor is probably Vercel's deployment check, unconfirmed): a new host each deploy, and Vercel's deployment protection redirecting `/monitoring` to its sign-in page, which `connect-src` blocks. A first fix keyed on `VERCEL_ENV === 'preview'` did nothing (those deployments are `production`); resolved by sending no `report-uri` unless the request host is the host of `appUrl` (`src/proxy.ts`, `docs/infrastructure/CSP_ALERTS.md`). Resolve HUB-24 and HUB-25 in the release that ships it. **Still open:** (a) **check the first deploy in a real browser**: sign in, open the page builder, a lesson with video, the chat and an upload, and read the console and Sentry (`logger:csp`); a `script-src` report naming our own chunks means the nonce did not reach Next on Vercel, and a `connect-src`, `frame-src` or `media-src` report names a host to add to `config/app.ts` `csp.*Hosts` (rollback is reverting the PR); (b) **HUB-17 (inline `<style>`) was TipTap's own style tag**, which the editors (chat message box, lesson text, page-builder rich text) now create with the page's nonce (`injectNonce: documentNonce()`, `src/lib/csp-nonce.ts`, `tests/components/tiptap-nonce.test.ts`); resolve it in the release that ships that, then watch for any other library that injects a style or script without a nonce (a `style-src-elem` or `script-src-elem` report on an enforced page); (c) **`style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'`** (item 5) is unchanged and is the weakest part left: about 60 components set `style={{...}}`; it cannot run script, but it allows CSS-based data exfiltration and UI redressing. Start by counting the inline styles that depend on a runtime value (needs a CSS variable) and the ones that are static (a class).

### Mark unread: replies, the U shortcut, a real-database contract test

"Mark unread" (`markChatUnread`, `chat_mark_unread`) is offered on top-level messages only, because the unread counts and the "New" line skip replies (a thread has no marker of its own). Still open: marking a reply unread (needs a per-thread marker), Slack's `U` keyboard shortcut on the hovered or focused message (the message menu has no key handling yet; start in `src/components/chat/MessageActions.tsx`), and running `ChatRepository.markUnread` against the real database in the shared contract suite (`tests/helpers/chat-contract.ts`): the database function is bound to the signed-in caller, so it is covered by pgTAP (`supabase/tests/database/chat_mark_unread.test.sql`) and the in-memory twin's own test instead.

### Chat jump to a day: thread panel and in-window live updates

Jump to a day (`?from=`, `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`) loads one window of messages: new messages are not merged into it live (like `?before=`), opening a thread keeps the window (`closeHref`) but the thread link itself drops `from`, and "Show newer messages" starts a fresh window instead of appending to the one on screen (the list remounts, older messages above are reloaded by "Load older messages"). Deferred because appending newer pages in place needs a `loadNewerChat` action and scroll bookkeeping in `src/components/chat/MessageList.tsx`, which other chat work was changing at the same time. Also a "Jump to..." entry for `chat_jumped` in `src/lib/analytics/funnels.ts` if staff want to see use.

### Chat core and Slack adapter: next steps _(Needs Scheduling)_
Chat core talks to a transport port and Slack is the adapter behind it (`docs/architecture/CHAT_MODULE.md`). **Resolved:** step 1 (the port, the registry, the lint rule and `config/chat-core-baseline.json`), step 2 (the outbound Slack code under `src/modules/chat/slack`, core records delivery in `src/lib/chat/delivery.ts`, membership, the DM controls and the mentions' Slack ids on the port, neutral `CHAT_FILES_BUCKET` and `MAX_CHAT_FILE_BYTES`) and step 3a (the webhook handling, gap-fill, DM pulls and event decisions, unfurl removal from Slack's side and lesson links moved into the adapter; core's `chatInbound` implements `ChatInboundSink`; `catchUp` is a port method) and step 3b (`bodyFromBlocks`, `fetchFile`, `messageLink`, `requiresLink`/`hasFullLink` on the port; neutral `CHAT_AVATAR_BUCKET` and the emoji shortcode map in core; the profile-photo copy job and file copy moved into the adapter). **Still open**, in order:
- **Step 3c: the nightly projection.** The last entry in `config/chat-core-baseline.json` is `src/app/api/process/chat/route.ts`: it mixes Slack user matching (`matchSlackUsersToMembers`) and lesson links pasted in Slack (`shareRecentLessonLinks`) with core's projection, outbox retry, file sweep and staging purge. Split the Slack steps into an adapter function the route calls through the port, keeping the route idempotent, batched and reprocessable (`.claude/rules/data-pipeline-reprocessability.md`). Also open: the Slack-keyed lookups in `src/lib/chat/names.ts` (`slackUserNames`, `slackUserPhotos`, `slackAuthorIdsByTs`, channel names) read the Slack repositories directly from core; an author resolver owned by the transport (an `authorRef` plus a resolver) would replace them. The sink's `kind` is passed but core treats all four the same today; the projection RPC and the notification lookup are keyed by Slack channel id and ts (`projectChatMessage`, `notifyChatMessageBySlackTs`).
- **Names that are still Slack's in core.** The bucket is still called `slack-files` in storage (a rename is a storage move), the repository methods and columns are `slack_*` (`markSent(messageId, slackTs)`, `slack_sync_status`), a core log line says "projected one Slack message", and member-facing copy says "Slack" ("Connect Slack in Settings to post", the reconnect notice in `membership-actions.ts`): the copy should come from the transport.
- **Several transports at once** (a transport per conversation) is not designed; `createChatTransportRegistry` takes the first. Decide it when a second transport exists.
- Each step removes entries from `config/chat-core-baseline.json`; the test fails on a stale one.

## Slack Bot Channel Access

### Auto-invite Billie Bot into private channels using a personal Slack token
Billie Bot (this app's own Slack bot) can't invite itself back into a private channel it's been removed from — Slack has no self-invite API, and an app also can't discover private channels it's not currently a member of. `src/lib/private-channel-access.ts` works around the discovery half by detecting staleness in `bronze.slack_channels.imported_at` (a private channel that stops getting refreshed by the nightly Slack import means Billie Bot lost access) and surfacing it on `/admin/hygiene` — but that's alert-only; nothing can currently fix it automatically.

**Idea:** Use a personal Slack user token (a `xoxp-...` token for Cody's or Ania's own account, pulled from the browser session rather than a proper OAuth flow) to close the gap. A real user's token can see every private channel *they're* personally in via `conversations.list`, and `conversations.invite` only requires the inviter to already be a member — so it could add Billie Bot into any private channel the token's owner is in that Billie Bot is missing from, without waiting on a human to do it by hand in Slack.

**Why this isn't built yet:**
- Much less automatable than the bot-token flows elsewhere in this app — a personal token isn't provisioned like `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`; getting one means manually extracting it from the browser, and it's tied to a real account (breaks on password change, "log out everywhere," etc. — no clean rotation story).
- Only covers private channels the token owner happens to be in, not necessarily every private channel Billie Bot should be in.
- Reads more like a manual/one-off remediation script than infrastructure that belongs on an unattended nightly cron.

**If pursued:** probably a manual/on-demand admin action (e.g. a button on `/admin/hygiene` next to the stale-private-channel list, invoked with a short-lived personal token pasted in at the time) rather than another cron, given the personal-token risk above.

### Support more than one Slack workspace
Slack is wired for a single workspace (one bot token, no workspace column on any Bronze table, Slack ids assumed globally unique). The 180 Program workspace (shared with Ready Writer) should be tracked and counted in engagement, with its people matched to their existing Kajabi-backed members, and later sign-in, notifications and the chat bridge. Deferred: it is a four-PR change that touches production keys on large tables, and it was put on hold. Full findings, the decisions made and the plan are in `docs/decisions/SLACK_MULTI_WORKSPACE.md`; start with phase 1 (the `slack_workspaces` table, a per-workspace bot token and workspace ids on the Bronze tables). An unreviewed earlier attempt is on the branch `slack-multi-workspace-1`.

## Gamification / Badges

### More Celebrations in #hedgiehype
New books and awards are celebrated in #hedgiehype (`celebrateNewBook`, `celebrateNewAward` in `src/lib/slack.ts`). Other wins worth cheering in the same channel, each posted the same way (through `postToSlackChannel`, escaped, display name, link to the public `/members/<id>` page, never an admin page):
- **Badges**: a member earns one, the Published Author badge first (`src/lib/badges.ts`).
- **Writing goals**: a goal finished or a project published (`src/lib/writing-journey.ts`, `src/lib/writing-projects.ts`).
- **Anniversaries**: membership anniversaries (the admin Hedgieversaries page, `src/lib/member-tenure.ts`).
- **Big Sister outcomes**: a trial member who stays on after the 30 days. Whether a trial converted is derived from membership history in `src/lib/mentorship.ts`, never stored.

Deferred because each needs a decision on privacy first: anniversaries and mentorship outcomes are personal, so ask members (an opt-in, or a setting) before posting, and honor the usage-statistics opt-out where it applies. Start with badges and writing goals. A new automated sender starts in dry run behind a feature flag (`.claude/rules/member-notifications.md`, "Notification Messages: Links, Copy, Rollout"). Each kind is a name in `slack.celebrationChannelOverrides` (`config/app.ts`), so one that gets noisy can move to its own channel without code. Related: "Staff channel posts as admin notification kinds" above.

### Community Builder Leaderboard
Recognize members who contribute most to community engagement — inspired by Long Angle's leaderboard (https://my.longangle.com/leaderboard). **Note:** Long Angle's own leaderboard ranks by number of members invited/referred, not general engagement — worth deciding whether to mirror that directly (an invites leaderboard) or broaden to community engagement generally.

Possible signals to rank on:
- Invites/referrals sent (Long Angle's actual model)
- Prickle attendance count or total hours
- Slack messages/reactions
- PUPs started or hosted
- Streak length (consecutive weeks active)
- Composite engagement score (see CRM Features / Engagement Scoring)

Considerations:
- Member-facing (public to the community) vs. admin-only view
- Time window options (all-time, last 30/90 days, current month)
- Opt-out mechanism so members who don't want to appear can hide themselves

### Verified Service Provider (VSP) Badges (Needs Scoping)
Ania wants a badge recognizing members who are verified service providers to the community, abbreviated per category — e.g. "VSP Editor", "VSP Coach" — rather than one generic "VSP" badge.

**What already exists (reusable):** the full badge pipeline from the Multi-Product Support & Badges work above — `badge_types`/`badge_levels`/`member_badges` schema (`supabase/migrations/20260831000001_create_badges.sql`), `src/lib/badges.ts` (earned-badge computation, manual vs. automatic), `src/components/BadgeChip.tsx`, and the `/admin/badges` CRUD UI. No VSP-specific code exists yet — this would be a new `badge_types.category` value plus one or more seed rows, not new plumbing.

**Needs scoping before implementation:**
- `badge_types.category` is currently a `CHECK` constrained to `('milestone','community','course','retreat','special')` — needs a new value (e.g. `service_provider`) added to the constraint, or decide `special` is close enough to reuse
- One badge type per category (`VSP Editor`, `VSP Coach`, ...) vs. a single "VSP" badge type with a category sub-label — affects whether this is a handful of new `badge_types` rows or a schema change to `member_badges`/`badge_types` to carry a free-form category alongside the type
- Verification process: who marks a member as a verified provider, and for which categories — likely manual (`is_automatic=false`) like other non-criteria badges, awarded via the existing `/admin/badges/[id]/recipients` flow, but the list of valid categories needs to come from Ania
- Display: profile-only, or also surfaced on admin member list/detail and the community directory (if one exists)?

### PUP-Starter Badge
Recognize members who drive spontaneous collaboration by being the first to join (or most frequently starting) Pop-Up Prickles.

Two possible definitions:
1. **First joiner** — member with the earliest `join_time` in `attendance` for a given PUP
2. **Most frequent caller** — member who initiates the most PUPs (may need Zoom host data or a `called_by` field on prickles)

### Retreat attendees: use the ticket source _(Needs Scheduling)_
`event_attendees.ticket_source` (comped or gifted; a purchase is derived from Kajabi, never stored) and `ticket_note` now record how an attendee got in; every existing row is still NULL. A comped or gifted ticket leaves no Kajabi purchase, which is how a summer 2026 attendee was missed and went without the badge.

**Resolved:** an event can be linked to the Kajabi offers that sell it (`community_event_offers`, `EventRepository`), and "Find Kajabi matches" on the admin event page lists buyers who are not attendees (to add as `purchased`), purchases with no member, and attendees with no purchase and no recorded source (`src/lib/kajabi-event-matching.ts`). Use it on each past retreat as the one-time backfill review.

**Resolved (gifted by):** a gifted attendee has a structured giver, `event_attendees.ticket_giver_id` (a member; cleared with the source, never the attendee's own id, repointed by a member merge). "Find Kajabi matches" uses it: a buyer who is not an attendee and whose every ticket went to a gifted attendee moves to "Tickets passed on" instead of "Bought a ticket, not an attendee", and each multi-ticket buyer shows who the extras went to and how many are not accounted for.

**Resolved (giver required):** a gifted ticket must name its giver: the add form and the row dropdown wait for one, the routes answer 400, and `setAttendeeTicket` returns `giver_required` (a note edit on an older gifted row without a giver still saves). It is not a database CHECK: the giver link is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, so deleting or merging a member nulls it and a CHECK would make that fail.

**Resolved (purchase window):** a linked offer can have a purchase date window (`community_event_offers.purchased_from` / `purchased_until`, inclusive Eastern days, either end optional), because offers get reused for a later event and Kajabi's purchase records carry no retreat reference. Matching, "Purchased" and the places left count a purchase only when its Eastern day (`created_at_kajabi`, else `effective_start_at`) is inside the window of the link it was bought on (`src/lib/event-offer-window.ts`), and an offer's windows on different events must not overlap. The live grant check can't be dated, so it answers "unknown" for an offer with a window or shared by several events.

**Resolved (offer on several events):** an offer can be linked to more than one event when the links' purchase windows don't overlap (the old `community_event_offers_kajabi_offer_id_key` constraint was dropped in `20270721000000`; the `(event_id, kajabi_offer_id)` unique index keeps one link per event). **Still open:** make `loadOfferCoverage` (`src/lib/offer-coverage.ts`) show an offer linked to several events.

**Resolved (not attending):** staff can mark a buyer who is not attending (`event_buyers_not_attending`, one row per event and member; reason `forfeited` for a deposit not refunded, or `other` with a required note). A marked buyer leaves "Bought a ticket, not an attendee", so the bulk add cannot pick them, and shows under "Not attending" with the reason and an Undo. A mark for someone who is an attendee is refused (409) and ignored if they become one later. A member merge moves the marks.

**Resolved (transferred):** a ticket bought for another event and moved to this one is a third source, Transferred, with a link to the origin event (`event_attendees.ticket_from_event_id`; same model as a gift, giver and recipient being one person across events). The origin is required while the source is Transferred (add form and row dropdown wait for it, the routes answer 400, `setAttendeeTicket` returns `origin_required`, `unknown_origin`, `same_event` or `not_transferred`), and changing the source clears it. Like the giver it is not a CHECK: deleting the origin event nulls the link. The origin event derives its side (`EventRepository.ticketsMovedAwayFrom`): a buyer who is not an attendee there and holds a transferred ticket elsewhere that names this event leaves "Bought a ticket, not an attendee" and shows under "Not attending" as "Ticket moved to <event>", read-only (order: passed-on gift, moved, mark). A transferred attendee is never "no ticket on record". A member merge carries the link, since it is to an event.

**Still open:**
- Existing gifted rows keep their free-text note and have no structured giver yet, so the matcher explains nothing for them. A reviewed one-off, run by hand from the notes (no names in a migration or script), can fill `ticket_giver_id`; the admin event page can also set it row by row.
- Add buyers automatically: in the Kajabi processing step (pull-based, repeatable), for linked offers, with a ledger of handled purchases so an attendee staff removed is not re-added, refunded or deactivated purchases flagged rather than added or removed, and a feature flag that starts in dry run (`liveFor`, as `src/lib/member-reminders.ts`).
- Backfill the older retreats from the organizers' notes (comped and gifted tickets for the 2024 and 2025 virtual retreats were missing from the roster when compared in prod). Do it by hand in the admin event page once the organizers confirm which retreat each ticket was for; no member names in a migration or script.
- Run that comparison on a schedule and tell staff (an admin alert), so a gap shows up before a member asks.
- Import each Kajabi contact's offers, so grants show up in Bronze like purchases. An offer granted from Kajabi's admin leaves no purchase, and the contact import keeps only a link to the contact's offers (`relationships.offers.links`), not the list. "Find Kajabi matches" now asks Kajabi live (`include=offers`, `contactOfferIds` in `src/lib/kajabi/client.ts`) for the few attendees with no purchase, but a nightly import would let every retreat and the auto-add see grants without live calls. Needs one `GET /v1/contacts/:id?include=offers` per contact (about a thousand, rate-limited) or a bulk shape if Kajabi has one; keep it in the nightly reconciliation ("Nothing through webhooks alone").
- Show the source on the badge recipients page and in the member's admin profile if staff want it there.
- Tighten the database to match the app: `event_attendees.ticket_source`'s CHECK (migration `20270504000000`) and its column comment still allow `donated`, which the app no longer offers (gifted covers it; no row uses it), and `purchased`, which the app now ignores (it is derived from Kajabi; rows written by the old add button still hold it, harmlessly). Dropping the constraint needs the user's explicit squawk approval (`ban-drop-constraint`), so it was left for a migration that also has another reason to touch the table.

**Why deferred:** the column and the admin controls were the part that stops the problem recurring; the backfill needs answers from the people who ran the retreats.

---

### Awards & Competition Tracking
Track external competitions (awards, contests, anthologies, showcases) that members submit their writing projects to. Members currently share these informally in the `#hedgie-classifieds` Slack channel; no structured tracking existed before Phase 1 below.

**Phase 2 — submission tracking, not just wins:** record *submissions* too, so both per-member and aggregate win-rate stats become possible ("submitted 8, won 2"). Needs a submission status (submitted / longlisted / shortlisted / won / rejected) rather than a boolean, and a submission date separate from any result date.

**Phase 3 — internal award list / PR campaign tracker:** a curated list of known competitions (name, submission deadline, eligibility, categories) members can track against as part of their own PR/marketing push, and staff can use to prompt members ahead of deadlines. Could be seeded from what shows up in `#hedgie-classifieds`, but that's manual curation, not an automated import — the existing Slack import ingests channel messages into Bronze, but "is this message announcing a competition with a deadline" isn't something the pipeline classifies today.

**Badge tie-in:** an "Award-Winning Author" badge, reusing the existing badge pipeline (`badge_types`/`member_badges` — see Multi-Product Support & Badges and VSP Badges above), likely auto-derivable once Phase 1's win record exists, the same pattern as the retreat/program badges.

**Sequencing:** Phase 1 (mark wins) stands alone and should ship before Phases 2-3; win-rate stats and the internal deadline-tracking list are natural follow-ons once win-tracking exists, not prerequisites for it.

**Priority:** Not scoped yet — idea only, no code started.

---

## Member Profiles

### After the Kajabi photos are copied: drop the Kajabi photo path
`pnpm photos:adopt` (`scripts/adopt-member-photos.ts`, `src/lib/members/adopt-photos.ts`) copies member photos from the Kajabi CDN into the `member-photos` bucket and remembers each in `member_profile_overrides.photo_path`, which `/api/process/members` prefers over the Kajabi, Slack and Gravatar address. Open items once it has been applied in production and Kajabi is being retired:
- Re-run it (it only touches photos still on the Kajabi host) until a dry run reports 0 photos, since a member can still get a new Kajabi avatar until the import stops.
- Remove `https://kajabi-storefronts-production.kajabi-cdn.com` from `csp.imageHosts` and `kajabiPhotoHosts` in `config/app.ts` (the book covers imported from Kajabi need their own move first), then delete the `KAJABI_CDN` and avatar branch of `toKajabiPhotoUrl` in `src/lib/member-avatar.ts` along with the rest of the Kajabi member import.
- Member removal (`remove_member_data`) deletes the override row, so the copy in `member-photos` (`<member id>/<hash>.webp`) is left behind: add that bucket to the files it returns next to the media library and feedback ones (`supabase/migrations/20270412000000_remove_member_data_paths_by_bucket.sql`, then the code that deletes them).
- A member-facing "upload my own photo" setting does not exist; when it does, it should write `photo_path` the same way, so the one mechanism covers both.
- **Resolved:** the member-facing upload (`POST /api/profile/photo`) writes `photo_path` the same way. Photos uploaded or copied from Gravatar are deleted on replace or removal, but a member who is removed from the Hub still leaves their file in `member-photos` (the item above covers uploads too now).
### Release the milestones changelog entry
`milestones-on-your-profile` in `src/content/changelog.ts` is held (`held: true`, no date) until writer profiles are open to everyone (`docs/runbooks/CHANGELOG_PROCESS.md`, "Holding an entry"). Before releasing it: read the day the `writer_profiles` flag went on for everyone from the audit log and set `date`; re-read the text against what is deployed; add a badges bullet only once the Finisher and Author badges show real counts (the entry has none now, because the count change was not deployed when it was written); recapture the screenshots (they were taken before the rows stopped repeating their title in parentheses and before the consent text moved behind the info icon, and the milestones are moving to My Writing), and delete the ones the final text does not use (13 are committed so the owner can look at every state, the entry shows 8).

### Public Pen Names ("Also writes as …") _(Partly Resolved)_
Let members choose which of their alternate names show on their public profile. Pen names live in `member_name_aliases`, mixed in with internal Zoom/Slack matching names that can include typos or legal names, and RLS only lets a member read their own aliases (`20260831180000_add_alias_self_service.sql`) — so they can't be shown to other members as-is.

**Resolved (writer profile foundation, `docs/product/WRITER_PROFILES.md`):**
- `member_name_aliases.is_public` (default `false`, a check constraint allows it only for a name the member added themselves) and a "Show on my writer profile" switch in Settings → Identity next to those names.
- The read path is the writer profile repository (`src/lib/data/writer-profiles.ts`): it shows a pen name only when it is the member's own, marked public, active and added by them, and nothing loosens the alias table's RLS. The member picks which one heads their public page ("Name shown").

**Still open:**
- List the other public pen names on the page under the heading as "Also writes as …", and use them on the member profile inside the Hub (the Hub profile still shows only the display name). Each book shows its own pen name (see "Writer profiles").

### Member Emergency Contact _(Idea, Not a Priority)_
A member (early adopter) pointed out that if a Hedgie has an urgent health issue or emergency on a Zoom, hosts wouldn't know who to alert or how. Idea: members optionally save an emergency contact in settings, hidden from other members, which a host can reveal in an emergency without waiting for staff. Nothing built; deferred as not a priority.

Decisions so far:
- **Storage:** nullable `emergency_contact_name`, `_phone`, `_relationship`, `_note` columns on `members`, not a new table. `members` is already own-row-or-admin RLS (`20260926000700_restrict_member_readable_data.sql`), so other members can't read them, and Hub-owned columns that `reprocess_members_atomic` ignores have a precedent (`20260906020000_add_member_birthday.sql`). Keep them out of `member_directory` and the shared profile column list in `src/lib/data/members.ts`; give the repository its own read/write method.
- **Admins** may see it (members aren't worried about that); the concern is other members.
- **No country / local emergency number:** we can't call another country's emergency services anyway, so only the contact is useful.
- **Reveal:** a host (or admin) of a prickle can reveal the contact of someone currently attending it, with no staff in the loop. Hosts can't read `members` rows, so it is a server action using the service role after checking host/admin and an open attendance record. A prominent warning dialog comes before the reveal (real emergency only, the member will be told, it is recorded).
- **After the reveal:** a row in a service-role-only `emergency_contact_accesses` table (who, whose contact, which prickle, when); an immediate, non-opt-out notification to the member through `createNotifier` (new kind, with its sample message and email template); a staff alert (pattern in `docs/infrastructure/SLACK_OPERATIONS_ALERTS.md`). The member can see their own access history in settings.
- **Docs:** a host emergency protocol (local emergency services first, then the contact, then tell staff) in `/help` and `docs/`. If this is built, the protocol ships with it.
- **Plan:** two PRs: (1) columns, settings form, docs; (2) reveal, access log, notification, alert. Tests: pgTAP for the columns and log table, server-action tests for refusal (non-host, not attending), log, notification and alert, and an analytics event with no property that could hold the values.

**Priority:** Not a priority; pick up when scheduled.

### Writer profiles: public author pages, Finishers, three-level visibility _(Needs Scheduling)_
One home on the web for each writer, opt-in, indexed by search engines, with the writer choosing what shows. Split into packages, each its own PR and brief; this entry holds the decisions so each brief can point here.

**Decided (owner, 2026-11-19)**
- **Visibility** has three levels, one click-cycle control: lock (private), house or hedgehog (inside Quill & Cup, signed-in members), globe (open to the internet). **Resolved (package 1):** `src/components/writing/VisibilityControl.tsx` cycles private, community (house) and public (globe) on projects and goals, stored in `visibility` (`supabase/migrations/20270831000000_writing_three_level_visibility.sql`); nothing reads `public` yet, so no one outside the community sees anything new. A goal is never more visible than its project (a goal write is clamped; the profile reads the lower of the two; lowering a project leaves its goals' own setting, as in 20270523000000). **Still open:** the public profile and its readers (packages 2 and later), and dropping `show_on_profile` (next entry).
- **Books and awards** are public by default, no per-item switch for now (revisit if a member asks). They still show publicly only if the author turned their public profile on.
- **Profile is opt-in as a whole**, and **indexed** (no noindex): it is the writer's home on the web. Needs a sitemap, canonical URL, social card and structured data for a person.
- **Name:** the display name by default; the writer should be nudged to pick from their pen names (`member_name_aliases` gets `is_public`, see "Public Pen Names"). Each book shows its own pen name.
- **Handle:** a chosen handle in the URL that members can change, so a handle needs history: the old URL redirects, old handles stay reserved for a while, and reserved words are refused.
- **Three milestones** (owner): (1) wrote THE END, (2) revised to be publish-ready, (3) published. They match the end of the courses: The Ready Writer or The 180 Program, then Self-Editing Academy, then the publishing courses planned for later. The titles are Writer (everyone), Finisher, Author and Published Author, with the rules in `docs/product/WRITER_TITLES.md`; the name of the milestone 2 stamp is open. Milestones 1 and 2 are stored; milestone 3 is derived from `member_books` so the two cannot disagree. A course completion may pre-fill the question but is never the proof (people also finish outside our courses): each milestone keeps an optional source (project stage, course, staff-added). Staff can add entries too, hidden until the member confirms; backfill as much as possible, starting from published books (a published book implies all three) and course completions.
- **Templates:** extend the versioned page template system another session is building (saving, overwriting, versions) with a gallery of 5 to 10 starters for writer profiles. Check its branch before starting so the two do not collide.
- **Fact thresholds** ("only above 150 prickles", "2+ years") are rules for the schedule panel's claims about a host, not controls for authors. On their own page, authors show what they choose; share one fact formatter with a `minToShow` option the panel sets and the profile does not.
- **Lapsed members** cannot sign in, so their profile's fate is part of "What may leads, former members and members on hiatus do?".

**Status:** package 2 (public profile foundation) is built: `docs/product/WRITER_PROFILES.md`. The page shows the name, an optional photo and a bio; it shows no writing yet.

**Packages (order)**: (1) three-level visibility (shipped, see Resolved above), migrating the boolean (backfill `true` to the house level, which is today's real behavior; clamp a goal to its project; expand then contract), with pgTAP; (2) public profile foundation: opt-in switch in Settings → Profile, handle, public read through the service role, preview as visitor; (3) profile facts (the ten formats, one fact kind per ticked box); (4) Finishers record: milestones 1 and 2, date, photo, before/after story, consent per use (profile, homepage, testimonial), self-report, staff backfill, a public Finishers page, homepage live data; the trigger is the project's stage change (below); (5) public Bookshelf; (6) a profile block set for the page editor, member-authored, with a security review; (7) the template gallery.

**Finishers and the project stage:** **Resolved:** the prompt is built (`docs/product/FINISHERS.md`, "The project-stage prompt"): leaving Drafting for a later stage asks THE END, reaching Publishing asks publish-ready (a jump asks both), reaching Complete with no book offers the Publish flow, once per project and kind; going back or to Hibernating never cancels a milestone. **Still open from the original idea:** the prompt asks no photo (photo upload is its own entry) and the name of milestone 2. Original note: the project Status list (Planning, Outlining, Drafting, First read-through, Revising, Developmental edit, Beta readers, Line & copy edit, Final read-through, Final beta read, Publishing, Complete, Hibernating) is the natural trigger, like Publish collecting more data. Moving a project from Drafting to anything after it asks "Did you write THE END?" (date, photo, story); reaching Publishing or Complete asks for the second milestone and the book entry. **Open, to brainstorm:** the name of milestone 2 (the stamp "Release Ready" is a candidate, and a noun for the people who reach it: Refiner, Polisher; the ladder Finisher, ?, Author), and whether Hibernating or going back a stage cancels or only pauses a Finisher record. **Verify first:** the migration that created `writing_projects.phase` allows only planning, drafting, revising, on_hold, complete and abandoned, while the app shows the longer list above; read `src/lib/writing-projects.ts` and later migrations before relying on either.

### Writer profiles: show writing, goals, books and facts on the public page _(Needs Scheduling)_
The public page (`/w/<handle>`, `docs/product/WRITER_PROFILES.md`) shows only the name, photo and bio. **Why deferred:** what a writer shows needs the three-level visibility control (package 1) so an item can be "open to the internet" separately from "inside Quill & Cup", and the Bookshelf and the profile facts are their own packages. **Where to start:** add a method to `WriterProfileRepository` that returns the items at the globe level for the page (a field only enters `PublicWriterProfile` through `toPublic`, and `tests/helpers/writer-profile-contract.ts` pins the keys: extend it with the new field), render them in `src/components/writer/WriterProfileView.tsx`, and add them to the JSON-LD only if the page shows them. Books and awards are public by default but only once the page is on.

### Writer profiles: a photo for members who only have a Gravatar
**Resolved:** choosing the globe for a Gravatar photo fetches the member's own Gravatar once (their address on the one configured Gravatar host, `d=404`), re-encodes it, stores it in `member-photos` and publishes that copy, so the public page never carries the email hash; a member with no Gravatar is pointed to the upload in Settings, Profile, Photo (`docs/product/WRITER_PROFILES.md`, "The photo").

### Remove the old writer_pages flag rows _(Needs Scheduling)_
The flag key was renamed from `writer_pages` to `writer_profiles`; the migration copied the rows (one member preview existed in production) and left the old ones, because the code live until that deploy still reads the old key. **Why deferred:** a migration never removes what live code uses (expand, then contract). **Where to start:** after the rename has deployed, a migration that deletes the `writer_pages` rows from `user_feature_previews`, `feature_flag_segments` and `feature_flags`.

### Writer profiles: a profile whose display name is removed while it is on _(Needs Scheduling)_
A profile that shows the display name falls back to the name on the account if the member later clears their display name, which breaks the rule that the account name is only used when picked. **Why deferred:** found while building the explicit account-name choice; the profile is behind a flag and nobody is affected yet. **Where to start:** `toPublic` in `src/lib/data/writer-profiles.ts` (do not serve, or keep the last name) and the display-name action in `src/app/(member)/settings/identityActions.ts` (refuse clearing it while a profile is on without another choice).

### Writer profiles: the sitemap's page size is above the API's row limit
**Resolved:** `publicHandles` now reads a file in batches of 1000 rows by the unique `id` (`fetchRowWindow`, `src/lib/supabase/paginate.ts`), so a file holds up to `sitemapPageSize` (10,000; the protocol allows 50,000) addresses with none dropped or repeated. Tests: the repository contract (2000 pages; sizes 999, 1000, 1001, 2000 and exactly 1000 or 1001 pages, both implementations) and `tests/lib/writer-sitemap.test.ts`.

### Writer profiles: report a page, and staff takedown _(Needs Scheduling)_
A public page can hold anything a member types (a bio), and a visitor has no way to report one; staff have no screen to take one down. **Why deferred:** the foundation is off for everyone until announced, and a takedown policy needs the owner. **Where to start:** `writer_profiles.deleted_at` already hides a page and frees its handle, but only the service role can set it; add an admin action (`requireActionPermission(...)`, audited, a plain sentence in `describeRow`) and a "Report this page" link on the page that sends an alert (`docs/infrastructure/SLACK_OPERATIONS_ALERTS.md`) with the handle only, never the visitor's details. Decide what a removed member sees.

### Writer profiles: rate limiting the public pages _(Needs Scheduling)_
`/w/<handle>` does one service-role read per request, with nothing to slow a script that tries many handles. **Why deferred:** a page is cheap and cached per request, and there is no member data to guess; worth doing before the feature is announced widely. **Where to start:** `RateLimitRepository` (`src/lib/data/rate-limit.ts`) and how `/api/` routes use it; key on the visitor's address and answer the same 404 shape.

### Writer profiles: handle squatting and impersonation _(Needs Scheduling)_
Anyone with an account can claim any handle that is not reserved, and a given-up handle is held for 90 days. **Why deferred:** needs the owner's policy: who may take a name close to a real writer's or to the community's, and whether staff can reclaim one. **Where to start:** `writerProfiles` in `config/app.ts` (`reservedHandles`, `handleReservationDays`) and `set_writer_handle` in `supabase/migrations/20270828000000_writer_profiles.sql`; staff reclaiming a handle would be a second service-role path that also writes an Activity Log entry.

### Writer profiles: custom domains _(Idea)_
A writer's own domain pointing at their page (`ada-example.com`). **Why deferred:** not asked for; it changes the canonical URL, the CSP, the proxy and Vercel's domain setup, and needs the handle history to follow. **Where to start:** Vercel domains API and `writerProfileUrl` (`src/lib/writer-profile.ts`), the one place the canonical address is built.

### Show a member their own private photo _(Needs Scheduling)_
A photo set to private is left out of `member_directory`, which is what every avatar in the Hub reads, so the member's own avatar in Hub Chat and elsewhere shows their initials while it is private (Settings still shows it, from `members`). **Why deferred:** the directory is one row per member for every viewer, with no notion of who is reading; the member-facing rule was the point of the change, and showing the owner their own photo needs a viewer-aware read. **Where to start:** `src/lib/chat/load.ts` (`memberProfiles`, `loadMemberCard`) and `src/lib/chat/names.ts` build avatars from `repos.directory`; for the viewer's own id, read the photo from `repos.members.byId` instead. Do the same for the member profile page (`src/app/(member)/members/[id]/page.tsx`) when the viewer is the member, with a test for each.

### Drop the old Who's coming anchor from bookmarks _(Idea)_
"Who's coming" moved from Settings, Profile to Settings, Preferences (`profile-whos-coming` is now on the Preferences tab). A bookmark to `/settings/profile#profile-whos-coming` opens the Profile tab without scrolling anywhere. **Why deferred:** nothing in the app links to it (search points at the new tab) and a fragment is never seen by the server, so a redirect cannot fix it. **Where to start:** only if a member asks; a small client redirect in `src/app/(member)/settings/profile/page.tsx` that reads `location.hash`.
