## Analytics & Matching

### Conversion and retention cohorts, in aggregate (`analytics.cohorts.read`) _(Needs Scheduling)_
**What the specialist needs.** The conversion specialist (marketing) is to work out lifetime value and compare cohorts: **when members convert** (how long from first contact or first join to a paid membership, and which route: trial prickle, lead form, retreat), and **how long they stay** (months of membership, when they lapse, who comes back), grouped by cohort (the month or quarter they joined, and by how they arrived). Output is counts, rates, medians and curves (for example, of members who joined in a given month, the share still active after 3, 6 and 12 months), never a person.

**What must never show.** No names, emails, member ids, Kajabi or Stripe ids, and no row per member. The page, its API and the database function behind it return aggregates only. **Small-cohort suppression:** a cohort under a minimum size (pick one, for example 10, as a single named constant with a test) must not be shown with its numbers: either hide it or merge it into a neighbouring cohort (an "other" bucket, or a wider period), so a count of one or two can't identify someone. Apply the same rule to every breakdown a viewer can combine (a cohort split by route is a smaller cohort).

**The permission.** `analytics.cohorts.read` already exists (`src/lib/permissions.ts`, reserved: no surface yet), held by the admin, the assistant and the conversion specialist, in no other bundle. The specialist's own client cannot read `members` (only lead and cancelled rows) or the Kajabi and Stripe Bronze tables, so the data must come from a SECURITY DEFINER function with an `is_admin()` or `is_conversion_specialist()` check (pattern: `get_activity_feed`, `20270817000000`) that returns only the aggregates, with the suppression applied in it, not in the page.

**What exists to start from.** `src/lib/member-tenure.ts` and `src/lib/membership-timeline.ts` (how long a member has been a member, active periods and gaps), `src/lib/community-stats.ts` (aggregates over members), the Kajabi data (`src/lib/kajabi/`, `kajabi_customers`, `kajabi_purchases`, `kajabi_offers` for when someone bought), and `src/lib/resubscription-detection.ts` / `resubscription-data.ts` (who left and came back). Caveat: a Kajabi access date (`effective_start_at`) can lag the transaction date, so check conversion dates against purchases before trusting them (read prod first, `.claude/rules/verify-against-prod.md`).

**To build.** The database function and its pgTAP test (including the suppression and a denial for a plain member and a community manager), a page under `/admin/insights/` with `requirePermission("analytics.cohorts.read")` and a line in `ADMIN_PAGE_PERMISSIONS` (`src/lib/admin-paths.ts`), a sidebar entry, tests, and take the permission off `RESERVED_PERMISSIONS`. **Separate entries, not part of this one:** the email and announcements surfaces ("Email, scheduled email and announcements surfaces") and a data export (`data.export`, "Guard a data export"); this surface is read-only and shows no export or download.

### Slack matching: email aliases only count for the DM resolver _(Needs Scheduling)_
`matchSlackUsersToMembers` (`src/lib/slack-matching.ts`) takes the member's active email aliases as a last-resort tier, but only `src/lib/slack-member-ids.ts` (the notification and DM recipient lookup) passes them. The other callers still match on the member email, a Slack alias and the name alone: the Slack processing routes (`src/app/api/process/slack`, `src/app/api/process/chat`), the Slack reconciliation and unmatched-user reports, Wheel of Wonder, and the admin hygiene pages (unmatched Slack users, members without Slack). A member whose Slack email is one of their aliases is therefore reachable by DM but still listed as unmatched there.

**Why deferred:** the processing routes feed Bronze to Silver, so changing who a Slack user maps to changes stored data and needs a reprocess and a look at prod first (prod, 2026-11: 3 members would gain a match, 0 would collide). Start by loading `repos.aliases.emailAliases({ activeOnly: true })` in each caller and passing it as the fifth argument, then reprocess Slack and check the counts.

### Name matching: a first-name-only Zoom name can land on the wrong member _(Needs Scheduling)_
`matchAttendeeToMember` (`src/lib/member-matching.ts`, rule 4) matches a one-word name such as a Zoom display name by comparing it with members' *legal* first names, and takes the match when exactly one member has that first name. A member who goes by a different first name than their legal one (their Zoom name is the name they use, the legal name differs) and has no alias for it yet is attributed to whichever other member happens to have that legal first name. It happened in the live room ("who is in the prickle now"): a member who signs in under their chosen first name showed as another member until they added the alias themselves. Nothing marks the match as a guess, so attendance and the live room trust it.

**Why deferred:** the fix changes what existing names match (the rule is what makes plain first names work for everyone else), so it needs a look at prod first: which members' attendance rests on rule 4 alone, and how many would become unmatched. **Where to start:** have rule 4 also consider the display name (`display_name`) and prefer an alias or display-name match over a legal first name, or return `ambiguous` when the name is a first name of one member and a display first name of another; or report rule-4-only matches on a hygiene page (`/admin/hygiene/name-matching`) so a missing alias is found before it misattributes. Check `src/lib/live-room.ts` and `/api/process/attendance`, which both call the matcher. Test in `tests/lib/member-matching.test.ts` with two fake members, one whose legal first name equals the other's chosen one.

### Alias key: accented letters _(Needs Scheduling)_
The key aliases are compared by (`aliasKey` in `src/lib/member-matching.ts` and `public.name_alias_key`, kept in step) keeps only `a-z` and `0-9`, so an accented letter is dropped: `José` becomes `jos`, which also equals `Jos`. Unlikely to cause a wrong match today (prod had no such collisions when the key was last checked), but two different names can share a key, and a non-Latin name with some Latin letters is reduced to those letters.

**Why deferred:** fixing it means folding accents (`unaccent` in SQL and an equivalent in JS, so the two still agree) and re-checking prod for new collisions across members; not needed for the separator change. **Where to start:** change both functions together in one migration plus `aliasKey`, extend `supabase/tests/database/name_alias_conflict.test.sql` and `tests/lib/member-matching.test.ts`, and run the cross-member collision query against prod first.

### Best Feedback Friend (BFF) program: enrollment and matching in the Hub _(Needs Scheduling)_
Today a member joins the BFF program (quarterly enrollment, a quarter-long commitment: read each other's work, meet at least monthly, give reinforcing and constructive feedback) by filling out an external **BFF interest form**, and the pairing is done by hand from the answers. The Hub has no BFF screen, table or notification; the only trace is the BFF Program as a Kajabi product (`docs/TODO.md`, "Multi-product" notes) and the "Former BFF"-style labels below. The Kajabi lessons "How to get a BFF" and "What to expect in the BFF Program?" (a draft that only points to an attached document) stay in Kajabi until this exists, so there is no help article.

**Why deferred:** it's a program with a matching decision (compatibility, availability), not a form, and the shape isn't settled. **Where to start:** an enrollment window (open and close dates, per quarter), an interest form in the Hub (replacing the external one), a pairing table (two members, quarter, status, `deleted_at`) written by an admin, a member page for "my BFF", and a notification kind (`src/lib/notifications/registry.ts`) when a pairing is made. Once it exists, write the help article from the real screens (a demo scene, `src/content/help/`, `HELP_ORDER` in `src/lib/help.ts`). Related: "Analytics & Matching" below (matching signals), `docs/product/BIG_SISTERS.md` for a pairing program that already exists.

### Member Interactions: Who Replied to Whom _(Parked, Refinement)_
`member_interactions` (Silver, `20261212000000`, `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md` phase 3 part 12) is a derived table of who talked with whom in chat (thread replies, DMs, reactions, mentions; who, toward whom, where, when, public or private; never content). It feeds the admin member network graph (`/admin/members/network`, chat off by default for prickles-only, `?chat=public|all` to add it). It is built and rebuilt by `/api/process/member-interactions` from the chat tables.

**Parked: refining who a thread reply is *for*.** Today every reply points at the thread's author. Slack doesn't record who a reply is meant for, and that rule has two known biases: it makes the thread's author the hub of every conversation, and it misses the back-and-forth between everyone else (A answering B's comment). Mentions (`<@U..>`) say who a message names, but not why:
- `@sarah the answer is…` is addressed to Sarah;
- `like @sarah mentioned`, `I agree with @sarah` refer to something Sarah said, so the reply is to an earlier comment;
- `you should ask @sarah` is a referral, not a reply to her;
- a bare `@sarah` is an invitation to a conversation she hasn't joined.

**Tried and set aside:** a layered rule (a reply that tags someone is for them; else if the replier was tagged and hasn't posted since, for the tagger; else for whoever spoke last; else nobody), written, tested (27 pgTAP assertions) and closed unmerged as PR #330, branch `worktree-chat-thread-rule`. It rests on the first step, which the examples above show is wrong about half the time.

**If it's picked up:** classify *how* a mention is used (position and wording of the tag, e.g. at the start of the message or after a referral phrase; or a small model over the message text, run offline), weight an interaction by that confidence instead of counting it as 1, and use "whoever spoke last" only for a reply with no tag at all. Possibly cap one pair's contribution within one thread (a long ping-pong) if a few chatty pairs dominate. `channel_comember` (sharing a conversation) was left unbuilt on purpose: pairing every member of a big channel is quadratic and says little.

**Why parking costs nothing:** the table is entirely derived from `chat_messages`, `chat_message_contents` (kept, including for deleted messages), `chat_reactions` and `chat_channel_members`, all of which keep their full history. Any change to the rule can be rebuilt over any date range afterwards (`POST /api/process/member-interactions` with `fromDate`/`toDate`), so nothing is lost by waiting.

**When to revisit:** when staff use the graph with chat turned on and find its chat edges misleading (a few members looking like hubs). Until then the simple rule is enough, and the default view doesn't include chat.

### Member Matching Logic (In Progress)
Build fuzzy matching to connect Zoom attendance to members when emails aren't available:
- Name normalization (nicknames, variations)
- Manual mapping table for common aliases
- Confidence scoring

### Zoom Name Matching: Options Beyond Aliases _(Needs Scoping)_
Checked against prod on 2026-10-24 (counts only): 7.1k attendee rows since January, about 94% matched by alias, email or full name (the first-name rules in `src/lib/member-matching.ts` match more), 88 active Zoom aliases covering 70 of 82 active members, 20 distinct ambiguous names.

**What doesn't work as an identity key.** Zoom's participant report gives a stable id only for people in our own Zoom account. Everything else was unusable: `participant_id` is empty, `registrant_id` and `customer_key` are never set, the payload's `participant_user_id`/`id` are filled on about 2% of rows with 2-3 distinct values, and `user_id` is a recycled per-session slot (131 distinct values; 52 of 90 matched members appear under 10 or more of them). Emails come back on 3% of rows even with "Only authenticated participants can join" on, because Zoom hides external users' emails from reports (`/docs/api/using-zoom-apis/#email-address-display-rules`). So connecting a member's Zoom account (OAuth) gives us their Zoom user id, but nothing in the attendee data to join it to.

**What OAuth could still do** (not built): with a user-level app, `GET /users/me` (`user:read`) returns the account's `display_name`, so Settings > Identity could show "your Zoom name is X, your Hub name is Y" and create the alias on confirm; `PATCH /users/me` (`user:write`) can set `display_name` to the Hub name. Both only help someone who joins signed in to that Zoom account (a guest or a different device still shows whatever name they typed). Our members are not on our Zoom account, so a *private* or *internal* app won't do (those are limited to users on the developer's own account); it has to be *published*, listed or unlisted, and Zoom reviews both (Zoom's docs: "Before you build > App types", and the app review process); a *beta* app reaches a limited number of outside users for a limited time. Unlisted still needs the listing metadata (images, description, categories). So it's an app-review project, not a quick integration. **Done:** the cheaper first step: the Getting started tour's Zoom name stop now says to set your Zoom name to your Real Name or add the name you use in Zoom, and a name can't be added if it's already yours or belongs to another member (`name_alias_conflict`, `20261223000000`).

**Other routes that put identity into the attendee record:** registration on the recurring meetings (gives a registrant id and email), or per-member join links. Both add friction at join time.

**Nudge by elimination (not built):** a member completed a prickle check-in but has no matched attendance for it, and that prickle has an unmatched or ambiguous Zoom name: ask the member "were you 'X'?" and add the alias on yes. Weak when a prickle has several unmatched names or several missing members, and blind to drop-ins (no check-in is sent to them), so it must stay a suggestion the member confirms. Revisit once launched, if `member_name_aliases.source = 'member'` shows members aren't setting names themselves.

**Still open:** merge, split and move-aliases move existing alias rows between members without the check (deliberate reassignments); an admin who really means to give a name that is another member's real name to someone else has no override (move or rename that member first); the member-facing check can't tell a first name shared by several members (a single-word alias like a first name is allowed, and then claims every Zoom join under that name).


### Slack Name → Member Lookup Tool (Needs Scoping)
Staff currently resolve an unknown Slack display name/nickname to a member by hand: search Slack, guess at a real name, then search Kajabi/Hedgie Hub by email. Came up while bootstrapping member birthdays from a hand-maintained sheet of Slack names — the fuzzy matcher (`src/lib/member-matching.ts`, `suggestMemberMatches`) plus the alias tables (`member_name_aliases`, `member_email_aliases`) already have most of what's needed to do this automatically; there's just no UI for an ad-hoc "who is this Slack name" lookup outside the existing unmatched-Slack/ambiguous-names reconciliation flows (which only cover names Slack/Zoom processing has already flagged).

**Possible shape:** a small admin search box — paste a name, get back ranked candidate members (name/email/status) using the same matching logic, with a one-click "add as alias" action. Could live alongside `/admin/hygiene/unmatched-slack` or as its own lightweight tool.

**Priority:** Not scoped yet — came from a one-off bootstrap task, not a recurring pain point (yet).

### Marketing site → Hub attribution via GA browser ID _(Needs Scoping)_
Hub and the marketing site (www.quillandcup.com, on Kajabi) are separate GA4 properties, so GA's own reports can't show "visited the sales page, then joined Hub". GA's browser ID (the `_ga` cookie) belongs to the website domain, not to a property, so two properties on quillandcup.com normally share one ID, and PR #100 already stores it on every `access_events` row (`ga_client_id`, the value GA4's BigQuery export calls `user_pseudo_id`). That makes cross-site attribution a join in our own data, not a GA report.

**Shape:** turn on GA4's free BigQuery export for the marketing site's property and join its `user_pseudo_id` to `access_events.ga_client_id` to attribute each member's marketing-site visits (first touch, pages seen, campaign) to the member they became. Needs the next item first.

**When to switch approach:** once the sales funnel moves into Hub, Hub *is* the marketing site; at that point add Hub as a second web stream in one property (Google's standard multi-site setup) so GA shows the whole journey, and this join goes away.

**Needs:** the marketing site's GA cookie working (below), a BigQuery project, and a decision on retention/privacy for the joined data (it ties anonymous marketing visits to a named member).

### Why the marketing site sets no GA cookie _(Investigate)_
On 2026-10-17, signed out in Chrome, `www.quillandcup.com` loaded Google's tag scripts (`gtag/js` and `gtm.js`, so a Tag Manager container is involved) but had **no `_ga` cookies**, while `hub.quillandcup.com/login` had the cookie plus four per-property session cookies. So the shared browser ID that cross-site attribution relies on isn't being set there, at least in that browser.

**Check, in Kajabi and the container:**
- Whether Kajabi's GA setting (Settings → Marketing/Analytics) or the Tag Manager container actually fires the GA4 tag for the "Quill & Cup Website" property, and on which pages.
- Whether a consent setting (Kajabi's cookie banner, or Consent Mode in the container) is holding analytics storage at "denied" until the visitor accepts.
- Whether the tag is configured with a cookie domain that isn't quillandcup.com (GA defaults to the whole domain, which is what lets properties share the ID).
- Repeat the check in a fresh profile after accepting any banner, to separate "never sets it" from "waiting for consent".

**Done when:** a visitor to the marketing site gets a `_ga` cookie scoped to `.quillandcup.com` that Hub then reuses (compare `ga_client_id` on their first Hub visit).

---

## Live Page (Who's in the Prickle Right Now)

Show a `/live` page displaying the currently active prickle and its attendees in real time.

**What was built and reverted:** A server-rendered `/live` page with 30-second auto-refresh querying `prickles WHERE start_time <= now AND end_time >= now`, plus attendee lists split into "currently present" vs "was here earlier". Reverted because attendance data isn't available during live sessions.

**The blocker:** `prickle_attendance` (silver layer) is populated from Zoom reports, which are only available *after* a meeting ends. The page would always show 0 attendees during an active session.

**The Zoom webhook gap (partly closed):** `/api/webhooks/zoom` now records every `meeting.participant_joined` / `meeting.participant_left` event in `bronze.zoom_participant_events` (migration `20261003150000`), and `src/lib/zoom-presence.ts` pairs them into per-member presence intervals, matched with the attendance import's rules. The prickle check-out DM already uses it. The live page can build on the same table and helpers instead of the `live_participants` table sketched below (steps 1, 2 and 5 are done; "currently present" = an interval with no leave).

**What's needed to build this properly:**

1. **Handle `meeting.participant_joined` in the Zoom webhook** — write to a lightweight `live_participants` table (not part of the silver pipeline). Schema: `(meeting_uuid, zoom_user_id, name, join_time, left_at TIMESTAMPTZ nullable)`.
2. **Handle `meeting.participant_left`** — set `left_at` on the matching row (already triggers the full import, which can remain).
3. **Handle `meeting.ended`** — clear all rows for that `meeting_uuid` from `live_participants`.
4. **Match live participants to prickles** — join `live_participants.meeting_uuid` to `prickles.zoom_meeting_uuid` to know which prickle is live.
5. **Match live participants to members** — reuse the existing alias/email matching logic to resolve `zoom_user_id` or name to `members.id`.
6. **Update the live page** — query `live_participants` instead of `prickle_attendance` for who is currently present, fall back to `prickle_attendance` for "was here earlier."

**Alternatively (simpler but less real-time):** On `meeting.participant_joined`, immediately trigger the Zoom import (Zoom's live participant list API is available mid-meeting). This would populate `zoom_attendees` and then reprocess attendance, showing joined participants with no leave_time set yet.

**Priority:** Low — the page itself is simple; the prerequisite is wiring up real-time Zoom participant data.

## UI Enhancements

### Tip of the Day _(Idea)_
The Hub gains features faster than members find them. Idea: a small "Tip of the Day" card (like the one iTerm2 shows) that nudges a member toward one thing to try, with "Dismiss" and "More tips / turn these off". **Open decisions:** where it shows (a dismissible card on the member home, or a modal on sign-in; the card is the gentler default); where tips live (a typed list in `src/content/`, one tip per recent changelog entry or help article so it can point at `/help` and the feature, with an optional feature flag so a tip never advertises something the member can't use); how one is picked (rotate through those the member has not seen, skip features they already use, one per day in the member's zone, `.claude/rules/dates-and-timezones.md`); and the opt-out (a per-member setting under their preferences, off by default only if the owner wants it quiet). **Why deferred:** needs the owner's call on tone and placement, and it should reuse the changelog and help content rather than a new authoring surface. **Where to start:** `src/content/changelog.ts` and `src/lib/help.ts` for the source of tips, the member preferences page for the opt-out, `docs/architecture/` usage analytics tracking plan (`usage-analytics.md`) to measure dismiss and click-through.

### Date range on the central Activity Log _(Needs Scheduling)_
A member's Activity tab has a custom From/To range and an "All time" preset (`docs/architecture/ACTIVITY_AND_AUDIT_LOG.md`, "The date range on a member's Activity tab"). The central log (`/admin/activity`) still has only the look-back presets (24h to 1y), because the owner asked for the member tab and the central page was left alone. To adopt it: set `rangeFilter: true` on `CENTRAL_DEFAULTS` in `src/lib/activity-feed.ts`, render `ActivityRangeForm` and the "All time" pill in `ActivityFilters` for the central variant (the member variant already does, behind `defaults.rangeFilter`), and show `ActivityRangeSummary` above the table. Then drop the "longest window" fallback on the member tab's "Open in the central log" link (`MemberDetails.tsx`) and the "central log ignores from and to" test in `tests/lib/activity-feed.test.ts`. The parsing and formatting are in `src/lib/activity-range.ts` and need no change.

### Cmd/Ctrl+Enter to submit forms, with a hint _(Resolved for modals; one thing open)_
**Resolved:** `Modal` submits the form a Ctrl/Cmd+Enter was pressed in (`submitFormOnModEnter`, `src/lib/submit-form-on-mod-enter.ts`: `form.requestSubmit()`, ignoring IME composition, a key already handled, and a form whose Save button is disabled), and the search inputs (`EntitySearch`, `MultiEntitySearch`) use the same helper so an open list never swallows it. The primary button of the book, award, log-progress, new-project, project-details and re-sign-in modals shows `<Kbd keys={["mod", "enter"]} onAccent />` with `aria-keyshortcuts="Control+Enter Meta+Enter"`.

The Kajabi import dialog, the media editor, the Trackbear import and the prickle check show the same hint on their Save or Import button (the second step of the Kajabi import, "Replace the draft", is not a form, so it has none). The panel lists the key under "Forms in dialogs" only while a `Modal` with a form is open, and the picker keys under "Pickers" only while an `EntitySearch` or `MultiEntitySearch` is on the page (both groups are page-scoped through `useRegisterShortcuts`).

**Still open:** forms outside a modal (for example "Start plan" on the Hedgie Care page) have no handler at all. Add the hint per form, and attach `submitFormOnModEnter` to a form's `onKeyDown` for the ones outside a modal. A form where Ctrl/Cmd+Enter means something else opts out with `data-no-mod-enter-submit`.

### Keyboard row navigation on the remaining tables _(Needs Scheduling)_

**Resolved:** `TableKeyboardNav` (`src/components/TableKeyboardNav.tsx`, hook `src/lib/hooks/useTableKeyboardNav.ts`) gives a table j/k and arrow movement, Home/End, Enter to open, Space and Shift+j/k to select, and `[` / `]` for pages. It is on the All Members, attendance (member page and list), All Prickles schedule, prickle insights, events, programs, pages, member overrides, hedgieversaries, badge recipients, aliases, missing Stripe, outreach, course roster and resubscribers tables.

**Resolved (second round):** the tables whose first link is not the row's main one now carry `data-row-primary` on the right link and are wrapped: `ActivityFeedTable` (the row's source, else the member), `OffersTable` (the Hub resource, else its Kajabi page), `UsersClient`, `HostsClient`, `MatchesPanel`, `WorkQueueSections` (one table per section), `PrickleTypesTable`. Also wrapped: the reconciliation member, granted-membership and inactive-attendee tables, the course comment tables (reported, held, unanswered), `NotificationInbox`, the attendees table in `PrickleDetails`, `GroupedTable` (Enter expands the group), `ZoomMeetingsReview` (movement and Space to select) and `MemberSlackActivityPanel` (Enter opens the message in Slack). Pages with several tables work as designed: j/k only start from nothing when exactly one table has rows, Tab reaches each table's region, and the keys act on the focused one (tests in `tests/components/TableKeyboardNavAdoption.test.tsx` and `TableKeyboardNav.test.tsx`).

**Still open:** tables left out on purpose. `ProjectDetailClient` (entries): the only link is the optional "During a prickle" one and the row's real action (edit) opens a modal, so Enter would be inconsistent; wrap it once the row has one action. `ReconciliationClient`'s Stripe subscribers with no member record: no link or action on the row. `UnmatchedEventsTable` and `PreviewsClient`: the row actions open modals, which belong with the modal flow work. `ProgramDetailClient` (expired members) and `HostingScheduleManager` (slots): no row action yet, so only movement would work; add one first. `CareLists` shows cards in a list, not a table, so it needs its own list navigation. Wrap each in `<TableKeyboardNav table={table} label="...">` around the table and its pager. **Resolved:** the sortable column headers are keyboard operable: `SortableTh` holds a real button (Tab, Enter or Space), `aria-sort` stays on the `th`, and no other mouse-only sort header exists in the repo.

### Registry-driven keyboard handlers _(Needs Scheduling)_
The shortcuts panel registry (`src/lib/shortcuts.ts`) only describes the keys; each handler (search palette, `Modal`, `Composer`, the pickers, `CalendarKeyboardNav`, `useTableKeyboardNav`) still reads its own keys. Drift is caught by tests (`tests/components/ShortcutDrivers.test.tsx`, `tests/lib/shortcut-listeners.test.ts`, `.claude/rules/keyboard-shortcuts.md`), not prevented. The fuller design: the registry holds the bindings and components call `useShortcut(id, fn)`, which removes the drift and lets the provider detect a runtime conflict between two mounted handlers. Deferred because it means migrating every existing handler, with regression risk in the most-used keys. Worth doing when a real key conflict appears or when shortcuts become user-configurable. Start at `src/lib/shortcuts.ts`, `src/lib/keyboard.ts` and `src/components/shortcuts/ShortcutsProvider.tsx`. **Resolved:** the three page-wide handlers that were unlisted are registered and driven by tests: Ctrl/Cmd+S in the landing page editor (group "Page editor"), and Left/Right in both lightboxes through `src/lib/hooks/usePhotoViewerKeys.ts` (group "Photo viewer"). **Still open:** the registry-driven design above.


### Typing `?` in the shortcuts panel's own search box types a character _(Needs Scoping)_
`?` is ignored while typing (`isTypingTarget`), so inside the panel's search box it types a question mark instead of closing the panel; Slack does the same. Decide whether that is right or whether Esc alone should close it (it does today). Start in `src/components/shortcuts/ShortcutsProvider.tsx` (the `?` branch) and `ShortcutsPanel.tsx`.

### Move the app's colours onto the semantic tokens _(Needs Scheduling)_

The landing pages take their colours from semantic tokens (`bg-page`, `bg-card`, `bg-tile`, `text-ink`, `text-ink-muted`, `border-accent`, `text-accent-ink`, `text-warm`): light and dark values are defined once as `--lp-*` in `src/app/globals.css` and named in `config/tailwind.ts`, so one edit retunes every page. The rest of the app does not do this, light or dark: surfaces and text are hard-coded Tailwind classes, each with its own `dark:` twin (about 1,300 `text-slate-600`, 600 `bg-white`, 420 `border-slate-200`, 360 `text-slate-900`, 80 `bg-slate-50`, plus the `dark:bg-slate-900/800/700/950` counterparts), and `--background` / `--foreground` are barely used. Deferred because a codemod over thousands of class uses is a risky single PR. Start by deciding whether the app shares the landing tokens (renaming them off the `lp` prefix) or gets its own set, then convert one shared component or page area at a time (cards, tables, form fields first), keeping both modes visually identical to today.

### Keep new code on the tokens _(Needs Scheduling)_

Once the app is moving onto the semantic tokens (see the entry above), add a guard so new code does not write another raw `bg-white dark:bg-slate-900` pair: an ESLint rule or a test like `tests/lib/landing-theme-tokens.test.ts` (which already does this for the landing blocks), with an allowlist of files not yet converted that shrinks as they move. Deferred until the tokens cover enough of the app that the rule would not flag most of it.

### Standardize action icons across admin lists _(Needs Scheduling)_

**Resolved:** `src/components/Icon.tsx` is the one icon set (one name per meaning, drawn by `lucide-react`; rule in `.claude/rules/icons.md`, guard in `tests/lib/icons.test.ts`). The course builder, the Pages list, the writing lists and the "reset to published" button use it. **Still open:** other admin tables and lists spell actions as text links or draw inline icons; convert them one table at a time, keep an accessible name on every icon-only control, and add any missing meaning to `ICONS` instead of drawing it in the page. The remaining files are listed in the guard test's baselines (see "Move Inline SVGs and Direct Lucide Imports to the Icon Set").

### Portal the remaining pickers' lists _(Needs Scheduling)_

`EntitySearch` now draws its result list on `<body>` so a modal's `overflow-hidden` can't clip it (`docs/architecture/Z_LAYERS.md`, "Pickers inside a modal"). The other lists that open under a field are still positioned in place and clip the same way when they sit in a modal or a scrolling panel with `overflow-hidden`: the hand-written dropdown in `UnmatchedEventsTable`, `MemberSlackSearchForm`, `BuilderOutline`'s Add content menu, `VideoActions`, `AddToCalendar`. Deferred because each needs its own outside-click and keyboard handling checked. **Resolved:** `MultiEntitySearch` is portaled too, and the placing logic is the shared `useBelow` hook (`src/lib/hooks/useBelow.ts`). **Still open:** the rest above; reuse `useBelow` and check each one inside a modal.

### Lesson editor: image in the text, and the thumbnail's "Image actions" menu _(Needs Scheduling)_

The Hub lesson editor (`src/app/(admin)/admin/courses/[id]/lessons/[lessonId]/`) now has a fuller text toolbar (text style, bold, italic, underline, strike, lists, quote, link, undo and redo), and its text saves with the page's Save. Still open: (1) inserting an image from the media library into the lesson text (the editor keeps images a lesson already has, but the toolbar can't add one; start from `src/components/media/MediaPicker.tsx` and the `Image` extension in `LessonTextEditor.tsx`, and check the lesson allowlist in `src/lib/courses/sanitize.ts` and how the image would be served to members); (2) the Lesson thumbnail card still shows `MediaPicker`'s own Change / Adjust crop / Remove buttons instead of one "Image actions" `ActionMenu` like Kajabi, because `MediaPicker` is shared by many screens and needs a variant prop first; (3) the lesson text editor is its own TipTap setup, not the Hub Chat message box (`src/components/chat/MessageBox.tsx`), which is built for short messages with mentions and emoji; unify them if a shared rich-text core is wanted.

### Move the hand-written table search boxes onto `DataTableSearch`, and add a server-mode search _(Needs Scheduling)_

`useDataTable` takes `search: (row) => text` and `DataTableSearch` is the input (first user: `/admin/offers`; rule in `.claude/rules/reuse-ui-components.md`). Still on their own input and filter loop: the events list (`src/app/(admin)/admin/events/EventsClient.tsx`, which also searches on the server with dates), the Hedgie Care DM list (`CareDmList.tsx`) and the course outline (`BuilderOutline.tsx`). `useServerDataTable` has no search: a server table keeps the query in the URL (`?q=`) and the server filters, so it needs the same input wired to `push` and the page's query to read `q`. Worth doing when one of those pages is touched next; start from `src/lib/hooks/useDataTable.ts` and `src/components/DataTableSearch.tsx`.

### Event places left: count extra tickets, pick the right offer, show "you're in" _(Needs Scheduling)_

Places left on an event (`src/lib/event-signup.ts`, `docs/product/CALENDAR_EVENTS.md`, "Places left and sign-up from the linked offers") counts each person once, so a buyer of two tickets (the second may be a gift for someone who is not a member) takes one place, and the Sign up button uses the first linked offer's checkout when an event has several (deposit, payment plan, full price). Worth doing when a retreat sells out with extra tickets, or offers differ by price: count active purchases of the full-price offer per ticket, or let staff mark which linked offer is the one to send members to. The page also does not yet tell a member who already has a place that they are in; start from `eventSignups` and the member's own attendee or purchase row.

### Move the last hand-built tooltips onto a shared `Tooltip` _(Needs Scheduling)_

`ReasonBadges` and the chat `ReactionHoverCard` are the two remaining hand-built tooltips (allowlisted in `tests/lib/tooltips.test.ts`; both are display:none or unmounted at rest, so they can't widen a page). Move them onto a shared `Tooltip` primitive that `InfoTip` builds on, then empty the allowlist. The phone-width overflow check is built: `tests/demo/scenes/phone-overflow.spec.ts` (`pnpm demo:phone-overflow`, runs with the other scenes in the help-screenshots workflow); extend its page list as pages are added.

### Clean up stale media renditions when a focal point or fit changes _(Needs Scheduling)_

A rendition is stored at `renditions/<asset id>/<preset>-<hash of fit and focal point>.<ext>` (`renditionPath`, `src/lib/media/render.ts`). Changing the focal point or fit makes a new object and nothing removes the old one: they are only deleted with the whole image, 30 days after it is deleted (`src/lib/media/sweep.ts`). Renditions are invisible derived copies (no table row, not listed in the library), so the cost is storage only, a few tens of KB per preset per edit. Deferred because it is small and bounded. To do it: after a successful PATCH that changed focal or fit (`src/app/api/media/[id]/route.ts`), or in the nightly sweep, list `renditions/<id>/` and remove every object whose name is not one of the current paths for the presets (`renditionPath` for each preset); keep a short grace period so a page still holding the old URL isn't broken.

### Feedback: a readable id, then link the Activity Log straight to the item _(Needs Scheduling)_

A feedback entry in the Activity Log (and anywhere else) can only link to the member's filtered list (`/admin/feedback?member=<mbr_ id>`), not to the one feedback item, because `feedback.id` is a uuid: a Tier 2 id, and an admin page URL counts as exposed (`.claude/rules/resource-ids.md`, "Resource ID Tiers"). To link to the item itself: (1) promote `feedback` to Tier 1 with the prefix `docs/decisions/SHORT_IDS_PLAN.md` already reserves (`fdbk_`): one migration converting the table the way phase 1 did, `fdbk` added to `ID_PREFIXES` in `src/lib/ids.ts`, and `src/app/api/feedback/route.ts` minting its id with `new_id`-style code instead of `randomUUID()` (the same id is in the screenshot's storage path, `<auth user id>/<id>.png`, which has to move with it; see the plan's "own server code" note); (2) give an item an address (`/admin/feedback/<fdbk_ id>`, or `?id=` on the list, opening that item), reachable only by admins; (3) point `activityLink` in `src/lib/activity-links.ts` at it: the activity row already carries the feedback id as its `entity_id` (migration `20270503000000`), so only the link changes, and the "See their feedback" fallback stays for rows older than the conversion if their ids are not carried over. Deferred because it is a table conversion, worth doing together with the rest of Tier 2 or when reading feedback from the Activity Log becomes common. Related: "Activity Log: link more of the sources".

### Activity Log: link more of the sources _(Needs Scheduling)_

The Activity Log links a check-in, check-out or attendance to its prickle, an audited change to a member to that member, and a feedback entry to that member's feedback (`src/lib/activity-links.ts`; the feed function gives activity rows an `entity_type`/`entity_id`, migration `20270503000000`). Still without a link: Slack messages and reactions (needs the Slack permalink: the workspace address is config, and restricted channels should not link), lesson completions (the admin lesson page needs the course id as well as the lesson id), writing progress, commitments and course sources. Feedback goes to the member's filtered list and not to the one item on purpose: a feedback id is a uuid (Tier 2, `.claude/rules/resource-ids.md`, "Resource ID Tiers") and an admin page URL counts as exposed; elevating `feedback` to a readable prefix would allow a direct link. Where to start: add the case to `activityLink`, and the source to the `CASE` in `get_activity_feed`'s activity rows.

### Retire the per-user feature checkboxes on Users, and the `/admin/segments` redirect _(Needs Scheduling)_

Segments became the Previews page (`/admin/previews`), which also turns a flag on or off per person (`PATCH /api/admin/users/[id]` with `features`). The Users page still edits the same previews in its row editor, on purpose, for a transition period. Once Previews is the habit, drop the Feature Flags column and checkboxes from `src/app/(admin)/admin/users/UsersClient.tsx`, and delete the redirect at `src/app/(admin)/admin/segments/page.tsx` (plus its `EXCLUDED` line in `tests/lib/site-pages.test.ts`) when no old links remain. Also open: the Previews page lists people from `/api/admin/users`, which loads every account; if that gets slow, give Previews its own search endpoint.

### Sync a segment from one program cohort, not only the whole program _(Not Needed Yet)_

The segment sync on Previews (`src/lib/segment-sources.ts`, `POST /api/admin/segments/[id]/sync`) offers a program as one choice that adds everyone ever enrolled in any of its cohorts, including cohorts that ended long ago. For a flag preview you may want just one cohort, such as the group in the program now. Deferred because it may not be needed: nobody has asked for it, and the whole-program choice covers the cases so far. If it is, add a `cohort` source type next to course, program and event: list each cohort under its program labeled with its name and dates (`program_cohorts`: `name`, `starts_at`, `expires_at`, as events carry their date so repeats can be told apart), and read its members with `ProgramRepository.memberIdsInCohort`. Leave "all cohorts" as it is. A "current cohorts only" shortcut would need a rule about expiry dates, so skip it unless asked.

### Pasted mentions: open a profile on click _(Needs Scheduling)_

Pasting `@Full Name` into the chat box now becomes a real mention (`src/lib/chat/paste-mentions.ts`, `MessageBox`). Slack also opens a small profile card/modal when you click such a mention in the box; skipped for now since the box shows plain text. Start in `src/components/chat/MessageBox.tsx` (a mention could now be an editor node, like the custom emoji one); mentions in sent messages already have `MemberHoverCard`.

### Full "Browse channels" page for chat _(Needs Scheduling)_

The chat sidebar lists only the conversations a member is in. Public channels they could join are in a small panel behind the icon in the Channels heading (`BrowseChannels` in `src/components/chat/ChatSidebar.tsx`), and archived channels are in neither (the sidebar's Archived section was removed). A full page, like Slack's channel directory, would hold what the panel can't: archived channels, filters (channel type, joined or not), member counts and a longer search. Deferred because the panel covers finding a channel to join for now; start from `loadChatChannels` (`src/lib/chat/load.ts`), which already returns archived channels and a `joined` flag. Slack's "+" (create a channel) and the heading's ellipsis menu are also not built.


### Shared `SubmitButton` that disables itself while its action is pending _(Needs Scheduling)_

There is no shared way to stop a double submit. Found while reviewing the event-log design (`docs/architecture/EVENT_LOG.md`, "Correlation"): a very fast double click can send an action twice and, for analytics, overwrite the correlation cookie before the first request reads it. Counts when checked: 58 files render a `<form>`, 19 use `useTransition`, none use `useFormStatus`, and `src/components/` has no submit-button component, so each form handles pending state (or doesn't) on its own.

Deferred because it touches many forms. Worth doing for its own sake (duplicate saves, duplicate notifications), not only for correlation. Start with a `SubmitButton` in `src/components/` built on `useFormStatus` (disabled and a pending label while the form action runs), convert the forms that create or send something first, and add a component test per the rules in `reuse-ui-components.md`. Enter-key submits and two different controls clicked quickly are not covered by disabling one button, so correlation stays best effort.

### Move display preferences from the device to the database _(Needs Scheduling)_
The Animations choice (`src/lib/animations.ts`, `AnimationsSwitcher`) and the theme (`ThemeSwitcher`) are saved in `localStorage`, so a member must set them again on every browser and phone. For animations this is an accessibility need, not a taste: a member who relies on it should not meet a moving GIF on a new device. Deferred to ship the setting quickly without a migration. Start with a column on `user_profiles` (like `timezone_preference`, `TimezoneSwitcher`) for animations, read it in the root layout so the `data-animations` attribute is correct on first paint (no flash), keep following the OS reduce-motion setting for the "system" choice, and decide whether the theme moves too (it is deliberately per device today, see the note in `ThemeSwitcher`).

### Convert Page Layouts to Container Queries _(Needs Scheduling)_
Pages choose their layout with viewport breakpoints (`lg:`, `md:`) but really depend on the content area's width, which is the window minus the sidebar (see "Responsive Layout" in .claude/rules/ui-layout.md). Moving the sidebar to `lg` fixed the worst case, but each page still hard-codes a guess about the shell. Mark each page's wrapper `@container` and switch to `@md:`/`@lg:`/`@max-md:` so it adapts to the room it has, with or without the sidebar. Check at widths just either side of each breakpoint.

### Event photo import: the Google Photos picker only lists the importer's own photos in a shared album _(Needs Scheduling)_
A member who found a shared album in Google's picker saw only the photos they uploaded themselves, though the same album opened by its link shows everyone's. The picker is Google's own screen (Photos Picker API, `src/lib/google-photos-picker/client.ts`); Google removed the shared-album read scopes in April 2025, so the app cannot list the album's items itself, and I found no Google documentation of this behaviour, so it is unconfirmed whether it is a limit or a bug. Deferred because nothing on our side controls it. Options, simplest first:
- **Each contributor imports their own photos** from the same album; a re-import skips photos already imported (`planImportBatch`, `src/lib/google-photos-picker/import-batch.ts`). No code.
- **Save the album's photos to the importer's library first** ("Save to library" in Google Photos) and pick them from the main Photos view. Not yet verified that the picker then lists them; try it on the next event.
- **An upload fallback:** an admin downloads the album as a zip from Google and uploads the files on the import page (`src/app/(admin)/admin/events/[slug]/import-photos/`), through the same storage, `event_photos` rows and sized copies. The reliable route, and the most work.
Update `ImportPhotosClient.tsx`'s shared-album note once one of these is confirmed.

### Event photos: store sized copies and serve them from a CDN _(Needs Scheduling)_
The event photo route (`src/app/api/events/[eventId]/photos/[photoId]/route.ts`) resizes with sharp on every request (`?w=480` for grids, `?w=1600` for the lightbox) and relies on the browser's one-day private cache, so the first view of each photo is still slow and every request still pays for auth, a database lookup and a full-original download. Two follow-ups, deferred until the page is still slow after the resize change:
- **Store the sized copies at import time** (the import commit route, `src/app/api/admin/events/[id]/photos/import/commit/route.ts`), the way `renditionOf` in `src/lib/media/library.ts` caches library renditions, and backfill the photos already imported. Removes the slow first view.
- **Serve them from a CDN.** The photos are private and checked per viewer, so this needs short-lived signed Storage URLs handed out by the page instead of the proxy route, and a decision on how long a hidden photo's old URL may keep working. A bigger change; only worth it if the stored copies are not enough.

### Sidebar auto-hide: pin and wider rollout _(Needs Scheduling)_
Chat now rests the member sidebar as an icon rail that opens over the page on hover or focus (`src/components/MemberNavigation.tsx`). Deferred until members have used it: a pin button with a per-member preference to keep it open, and whether other pages with a second pane should do the same. The admin sidebar (`AdminNavigation.tsx`) is unchanged; change all three navigation files together if the behavior spreads.

### Managing events in the Hub: the rest of the plan _(Needs Scheduling)_
**Still open**, from a conversation with the community lead, in the order to build it:
1. **Events model: what's left.** **Still open:** time of day (events are date-only; a webinar or book launch with a time is a prickle listed in Events for now), guest speakers as members rather than text, an image picked from a per-kind default set or uploaded (events have a photo gallery and no cover image), `kinds` as a table with default images and flags if the list keeps growing, and `announced_publicly` is stored but nothing public reads it.
2. **Registration and application.** A registrations table (member, event, status `registered`/`applied`/`accepted`/`waitlisted`/`declined`, application answers) for the workshop that needs an application (a query workshop with two sessions attendees must both attend) and sign-ups. Notifications go through `createNotifier`.
3. **Calendar-linked events and series.** **Still open:** matching a calendar item to a prickle type from the Events side (today only on `/admin/hygiene/unmatched-events`; the same resolve routes can serve both); calendar items that are no prickle can only be reached by tagging them `#event` in Google Calendar; a link from an event to its prickle page (and back) for events that are prickles; times of day on the member Events pages (`events.start_time`/`end_time` are stored, the cards and page show dates); per-prickle-type defaults for the Hub-only metadata (image, audience) that each automatic event would inherit, so a recurring session doesn't need it entered every time; writing title, description and location back to Google Calendar instead of overriding them (needs the write scope in `src/lib/google-calendar/client.ts`, now `calendar.readonly`, and the service account shared on the calendar with "Make changes to events"; the Hub-only metadata stays in the Hub either way); a nullable `parent_event_id` for a group of events (book fair slots, a writing marathon); letting an admin override more than title, description and location; past sessions of a listed prickle type are not made into events (only tagged ones are), to keep Past from filling with a recurring series.
4. **Programs.** Challenges (an invitation, opt-in, held accountable in a channel, a badge, possibly a word-count project) are closer to `programs` and cohorts than to events; an event could point at a program. A book launch is more an announcement, celebration or recognition than something to show up to, so it may belong with those rather than as an event; it shows under Special events for now. Book launches ("releasing in 10 days", pre-orders open) and a "where are you in the process" badge (drafting, editing, querying) are separate ideas to scope.
5. **Public listing.** Only members see Events today. A signed-out page for announced events with guest registration needs email capture, anti-spam and an unsubscribe path.

**Why deferred:** each step is its own PR (this one only generalized the educational-prickle list); the audience flags and the program/event boundary were agreed in principle, not yet confirmed with the community lead. **Where to start:** `supabase/migrations/20260905000000_create_events_and_event_photos.sql`, `src/app/(member)/events/`, `src/app/(admin)/admin/events/`, `src/lib/events-filter.ts`.

### Events and prickles: one schedule, later sub-events and write-back _(Needs Scheduling)_
**Still open:**
1. **Sub-events.** A book fair, a writing marathon or a retreat is an event with sessions (author spotlights, agenda items, each its own calendar item). The model for it is a Local `event_sessions` table (`event_id`, `prickle_id` unique) so Silver `prickles` never points at Local; an event's dates are the earliest start and latest end of its sessions, its agenda the list of them. Attach a calendar item by an `#event <slug>` tag in its description or from the prickle page. This replaces the single `prickle_id` and the `parent_event_id` idea in "Managing events in the Hub".
2. **Write back to Google Calendar.** An event made in the Hub gets a `prickles` row with `source = 'hub'` that the calendar sync leaves alone, because there is no calendar item to build it from. Writing events to Google (the write scope in `src/lib/google-calendar/client.ts`, the service account shared with "Make changes to events") lets the Hub create the calendar item and the normal sync make the prickle; then drop `source = 'hub'`.
3. **Announcements.** A book launch is mostly an announcement with its own dates (pre-orders announced, pre-orders open, release), a visibility window and no sessions; a live launch party is an event. Announcements are not events or prickles, so they get their own model when someone builds them.
4. **Admin links that still carry the event id.** The admin event page address is the slug, but the member badges panel, the media editor's usage list and the Google Photos sign-in return still link `/admin/events/<id>`; the page redirects an `evt_` id to its slug, so they work. Pass the slug where they have it (`MediaUsage` for events carries only the id) and drop the redirect once nothing sends ids.
5. **Event photo import and attendee API routes** keep taking the internal event id in the path (our own client only); fine until an external consumer appears.

6. **Calendar-backed edits are overrides until write-back lands.** Editing a calendar-backed event's title, description, host, guest speaker or location (on the event page or the prickle page) saves a decision in `unmatched_calendar_events` that the sync applies on top of the calendar; the Google item itself is not changed, and the times (start, end) cannot be edited at all. Write-back (item 2) turns these into real edits to the calendar item and makes the times editable.

**Why deferred:** the first PR only had to remove the duplicated fields and the two-sources-of-truth editing before launch. **Where to start:** `src/lib/calendar-events.ts`, `src/lib/data/events.ts`, `docs/product/CALENDAR_EVENTS.md`.

### Events for one cohort only (live course lessons) _(Needs Scheduling)_
The first group through a course (the Ready Writer) has its lessons run live, as calendar items titled "<course> | Lesson N: ...". They are events, but only for the people in the course. Every event today is visible to every member, and there is no audience narrower than "members" (`guests_allowed` and `announced_publicly` only widen it), so tagging such an item `#event` would show it to the whole community. Until this exists the items stay in the unmatched queue (`/admin/hygiene/unmatched-events`), left pending rather than ignored, and the cohort is told about the sessions where it already meets.

**Why deferred:** it is a feature, not a flag: who may see an event has to be enforced everywhere events are read, and launch was days away. **Where to start:** a nullable cohort on `community_events` (`program_cohorts` has the window and `program_id`; `member_program_enrollments` says who is in it), so visibility is "an event with no cohort is for everyone, one with a cohort is for its enrolled members and admins". Enforce it in the `community_events` RLS select policy first, then check every reader that bypasses RLS with the service role: the member Events pages and the past list (`src/lib/events-agenda.ts`, `src/app/api/events/past-prickles`), search (`src/lib/search/universal.ts`), the calendar feed (`src/lib/data/calendar-feed.ts`), notifications, and the admin forms (a cohort picker beside the audience fields, `src/components/EventAudienceFields.tsx`). Decide how a lesson item gets its cohort: by an `#event cohort=<slug>` style tag in the calendar description or a prickle type tied to a program (`src/lib/calendar-events.ts`, `hasEventTag`). The related idea of an event pointing at a program is item 4 under "Managing events in the Hub". The prickle on such an event is still a normal calendar prickle of the Event type, so it stays out of the session lists and prickle search (they skip the Event type) and shows in the admin calendar. Not checked: whether a member outside the cohort can still open it by its prickle id (`/prickles/<id>`, and the RLS select policy on `prickles`); verify that before relying on the event's own visibility.

### Check-in answer scale on Slack's phone app: does the layout bias answers? _(Needs Scheduling)_
The prickle check-in DM (`src/lib/prickle-checkin-dms.ts`) asks "how do you feel" and "how's your energy" as rows of five buttons (Very bad ... Very good, Very low ... Very high) in a Slack `actions` block. In the demo's phone mockup (`demo/mockups/frames.tsx`, an approximation, not Slack's own layout) the last button wraps onto a second line on its own. If Slack's iPhone app really does that, the scale is no longer one row of five equal options: the isolated option stands out, the order and spacing cues are lost, and rating scales are known to shift with layout (position, spacing and grouping change which options people pick). Check-in answers feed the member's own history and the admin views, so a skew from the layout, not the feeling, would be quiet and hard to spot.

**What's unknown:** how Slack's iPhone and Android apps lay out five buttons in one `actions` block (wrap, stack, scroll sideways). **Why deferred:** needs a real-device look first, then a decision on the message design. **Where to start:** (1) send the check-in to a phone from Admin → Test Notifications and screenshot it on iPhone and Android, replacing the approximate frame's guess; (2) if it wraps or stacks, try a form that stays one row of equal options, such as short labels or numbers (1 to 5) or one emoji face per option, keeping the same order and the endpoints' text in the message, or a single `static_select` for each scale; (3) compare answer distributions before and after (`prickle_checkins`, the feelings and energy keys in `src/lib/prickle-checkins.ts`) and watch `src/lib/analytics/events.ts` for how often each is answered. Related: the same wrapping applies to any future message that puts several buttons in one row.

It is not "only staff can see it". The Hub reads `locked` and `drip` alike as "Coming soon" (the title shows, it can't be opened: `visibilityFromPublishing` in `src/lib/courses/visibility.ts`) and has no prerequisite or unlock logic, and its status menu offers Lock on lessons as well as modules, so a Hub item set to Lock stays closed until someone changes it. **Decision for the registry:** the lock icon stays "only you or staff" (unpublish, draft), as on the writing toggles; the Locked, Drip and Scheduled statuses are the "coming soon" family and get their own picture (a clock or hourglass), never the lock. **Decided:** build prerequisite unlock ("Course editor: prerequisite unlock for locked modules"); the status choices join the registry once Locked means that, with the Schedule and Drip entry ("Course editor: Schedule and Drip statuses") in the same family. **Also:** the lesson delete confirm button lost its "Deleting…" busy text (`ConfirmDialog` disables the buttons while pending but doesn't relabel them); add a `pendingLabel` to `ConfirmDialog` if that is missed. **Where to start:** `src/app/(admin)/admin/courses/` and `src/lib/lifecycle-actions.ts`; give an action an entry only when something really has the behavior.
