### Site-wide "Return To" Handling, and the gates standard _(Started: the standard and the first gate are built)_

**Resolved:** the standard for system-mandated detours ("gates") is `.claude/rules/gates-and-return-to.md`: a registry (`src/lib/gates.ts`), the proxy forwarding the address being asked for to Server Components (`x-hub-path`), `redirectToGate`, `GateExit` ("Not now"), and one destination validator (`safeNextPath`). The connect-Slack gate (`requireChat`) is the first on it: it carries the page the visitor asked for through Settings and Slack's approval page and back, and always offers a way out. Modeled on Laravel's `intended()` (every interception records the request it stopped; whatever finishes sends them back), Devise's rules for what to remember (GET page loads only, never the sign-in pages), and Auth.js's same-origin fallback.

**Found in production (2026-11-10) and fixed:** a signed-out Slack reader whose browser still had the Hub open in another tab landed on the dashboard after the magic link, not the message, because that tab's background request for `/` overwrote the remembered page. The proxy now remembers only real page navigations and never the bare `/` (`isRememberableVisit`, tested in `tests/lib/proxy-remember-navigation.test.ts`). **Still to move onto the standard** (each should declare a gate, carry the destination and show `GateExit`): a signed-in visitor with no member record (`/admin`, then `/no-access`: nothing remembered and no way out but signing out); a course the member can't open, and Big Sisters (`redirect("/dashboard")` with no explanation); the sudo return (below); the `/login` fallback checks repeated in about forty pages (the proxy already handles them). **Also:** the remembered sign-in address is used up at sign-in and not restored if the visitor abandons a later gate at a third party (Slack's page) and never comes back (it stays for up to an hour and could take the next sign-in somewhere they stopped trying to go); consider a shorter life for the cookie once a gate hands off. A bar that can be dismissed (`SlackConnectPrompt`) is not a gate.

**Still open:**
- **Session expiry or idle timeout mid-form.** Whatever detects a 401 or expired session in the browser should send the member to `/login` with the current location stored (not wait for the next full page load to hit the proxy), and ideally keep their unsaved input.
- **"← Back to …" links** are hardcoded per page (e.g. `src/app/(member)/prickles/[id]/page.tsx` picks "Back to Calendar" vs "Back to My Prickles") and ignore where the visitor came from. Carry the origin (a `?from=` param or history state) and derive the label from a route registry ("Back to Alex's profile"); keep today's default as the fallback.
- **Sudo** keeps its own `sudo_return_to` (Referer, `src/lib/sudo-redirect.ts`). Fold it into one module (`src/lib/return-to.ts`: `storeReturnTo`, `takeReturnTo`, `peekReturnTo`, named slots, one same-origin-path validator, GET navigations only) with a shared open-redirect test suite.

### Member Location History, and Video Viewing Attribution _(follow-ups)_
**Why:** Kajabi's per-viewer video stats show where each view came from ("Muncie, Indiana, US") and the device. We want that, and more generally geographic insight into the membership. A member's location changes over time (prickles ask "Where are you writing from today?"), so it belongs to a *period*, not to the member row.

- **Vercel's geo headers** are added to every request on a deployed Vercel project (absent locally, so test with a stubbed header): `x-vercel-ip-country` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2), `x-vercel-ip-country-region` (ISO 3166-2 region code), `x-vercel-ip-city` (city name, **URL-encoded** per RFC 3986, so decode it), `x-vercel-ip-latitude`, `x-vercel-ip-longitude`, `x-vercel-ip-postal-code`, and `x-vercel-ip-timezone`. Vercel's `geolocation(request)` helper (`@vercel/functions`) returns them parsed. They are coarse (city level, sometimes wrong, VPNs and mobile carriers skew them) and describe where the *connection* is, not where someone writes from.
- **Why the access log, not a history table:** each row already has the auth session and user agent and is the "where was this member at time T" source, and the latest-event-before-T join is the date-range join this entry first proposed with periods. A compact per-place "periods" view or table (collapsing consecutive events at one IP) can be derived from it later if the log gets large.
- **Still open:** whether to also join the self-reported "where are you writing from today?" answer as a second, more honest source beside the IP-derived one; showing a member's place history on their admin page (the access history page doesn't show it yet); and retention for raw IPs (nothing prunes `access_events` today).
- **Decision recorded:** raw IP and the derived place are both kept for now (the owner's call), described to members on the flagged `/privacy` page ("Where you connect from"). It is first-party and not consent-gated; see `docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`, "Course viewing data", for the EU/UK note.
- **Notice:** the flagged `/privacy` page now says how course videos are watched and where visits come from. Still to consider: a Settings switch to opt out of course viewing statistics (the playhead for resume would stay).

### Back up the original course videos before leaving Kajabi _(Needs Scheduling)_
The Hub never stores the course videos itself. `src/lib/courses/media.ts` gives Mux a link to the file in Kajabi and Mux fetches it, so **once the Kajabi subscription ends, the original files may exist nowhere but Mux** (production, checked 2026-10-30: 10 `videos` rows, all with a Mux asset; our storage holds only lesson PDFs in `course-files` and images in the media library). I have not verified what Mux keeps of an uploaded original: by default it keeps the encoded renditions, and the original is only downloadable with "master access" (or MP4 renditions) turned on for the asset. Before cancelling Kajabi: (1) decide where the originals should live (a private bucket, or Descript's exports, which Ania already has); (2) for every video, either keep the Kajabi source file (Video actions, Download video in Kajabi's lesson editor; the link `src/lib/courses/media.ts` records expires after 7 days) or turn on Mux master access and download it; (3) check the count of originals against `videos`; (4) note it in `docs/product/COURSES.md`. Wistia-hosted copies behind Kajabi go away with the subscription too.

### Link previews: Slack shows course and lesson pictures as a small cropped square _(Needs Scheduling, cosmetic)_
A course or lesson link pasted into Slack unfurls with the right title and description, but the picture (1280x720) is a small thumbnail on the right, center-cropped to a square, so a thumbnail with text near its edges loses it. **Tried (2026-11-02):** the page asks for the large card (`twitter:card: summary_large_image`) and now declares the picture's size (`og:image:width`/`height`, read from our library copy); a fresh paste (`?x=3`) looked the same, so a missing size is not the cause. **Not tried:** a dedicated 1200x630 share image per course and lesson (a title over a branded background, or a crop of the thumbnail chosen for the card, via `src/lib/media/` presets or a Next `opengraph-image` route), and checking what Slack does with the same image on a page with `og:type: website`. **Why deferred:** cosmetic, the links work, and each try needs a deploy plus a manual paste in Slack (Slack caches a card per URL, so use a new `?x=` each time). **Where to start:** `src/app/course-preview/[slug]/page.tsx`, `src/app/lesson-preview/[slug]/[lessonId]/page.tsx`, `src/lib/courses/public-preview.ts`, and "Public course preview" in `docs/product/COURSES.md`.

### Launch day: check a shared lesson link as a signed-out visitor _(Low priority)_
**Update (2026-11-02):** the lesson link was checked in production (Slack unfurl works) and a course address gets the same public preview (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Public course preview"); check the course unfurl in Slack the same way once it is deployed. Original entry:
The public lesson preview (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Public lesson preview") is only covered by unit and database tests, and it can't show anything in production until at least one lesson is open (the `courses` flag it once needed has been removed) (checked 2026-10-31: 53 lessons in The Ready Writer, 8 coming soon, 45 hidden, none open). Once launched: (1) open an open lesson's address in a private window and confirm the card (course, title, excerpt, thumbnail, "Sign in to watch"), and that signing in lands on the lesson; (2) paste it into Slack and a message to see the real unfurl (Open Graph large image); (3) in the builder, "Copy link with thumbnail" on that lesson and paste into an email or doc; (4) open a coming-soon or draft lesson's address signed out and confirm it still goes to the login page with no title or image. If the unfurl shows no picture, check that the thumbnail's address (`/api/courses/image/lesson/<id>`) loads signed out.

### Taken-over courses: keep importing members' Kajabi progress _(Needs Scheduling)_
A course taken over from Kajabi (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Taking a course over from Kajabi") is skipped by every Kajabi import step, because each looks only at `source = 'kajabi'` rows. For structure, images, files and videos that is the point. For **members' lesson progress** it is a gap while Kajabi is still live: a member who keeps watching in Kajabi has their progress imported into the Hub only for courses not yet taken over. It matters only until Kajabi is switched off, and only for members still using Kajabi for that course. **Where to start:** `src/lib/courses/progress-import.ts` (lines that read `courses({ source: "kajabi" })` and `lessonsOf(..., { source: "kajabi" })`): also include Hub-owned courses and lessons that still carry a Kajabi `source_key`, matching by it. Decide first whether progress should keep flowing in at all for a course members now use in the Hub, since an import could overwrite progress made in the Hub.

### Course editor: the rest of a Hub-owned lesson's page _(Needs Scheduling)_
A Hub-owned lesson has its own page (`/admin/courses/<course>/lessons/<lesson>`, `loadAdminLesson` reads the Hub's rows for it) where the **title, the status and the text** can be changed (the text is a Tiptap editor, `LessonTextEditor`, saved with a Save button; `saveLessonText` cleans it with `sanitizeLessonHtml` and sets the teaser; there is no way to insert an image yet, an image already in the text is kept; each save is one audit entry holding the old and new text). Still read only there, as for an imported lesson: the order of the downloads (files can be added and removed: `docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Lesson downloads"; a new one goes last), the comment settings (lesson comments are not built: `docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Lesson comments"). The video (uploaded, replaced, removed, with captions) and the thumbnail (chosen from the media library or made from the video's current frame) can be changed: `docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Video" and "Lesson thumbnails". Deleting a lesson works from its page and from the outline, and removes its stored downloads (its Mux video stays, see below). **Where to start:** each is a repository method plus a server action wrapped in `withAction`, like `renameCourseItem`.

### Course editor: clean up Mux assets and abandoned uploads _(Needs Scheduling)_
Replacing or removing a Hub-owned lesson's video only changes which video the lesson points at: the old `videos` row and its Mux asset stay (Mux bills storage and delivery by minutes stored). An upload that was started and never finished leaves a `videos` row with no Mux asset (`status` `preparing`, Mux times the upload out). **To do:** find `videos` rows of `source_kind` `upload` that no lesson uses (and have no watch segments, which are members' history) and delete their Mux asset (`mux.video.assets.delete`) and the row, in a nightly job; start by counting them in production. Also: captions/transcripts for uploaded videos (`video_text_tracks` exists), and checking a very large upload against the browser's memory (UpChunk streams it, but it has not been tried above a few GB).

### Course editor: edit a lesson video in Descript, and pull the edit back; versions for all media _(Needs Scheduling, prove the API first)_
**The idea.** Ania edits course videos in Descript. Today that is: download the video from Kajabi, edit, export, upload to Kajabi or the Hub. Make it two buttons on a lesson's video in the course editor: **Edit in Descript** (opens the video's Descript project in a new tab) and **Pull the edit back** (renders it and makes the result the lesson's video). Descript has an API (`https://docs.descriptapi.com/`, the reference at `https://help.descript.com/developers/api-reference/overview`, CLI `@descript/platform-cli`) but **no embeddable editor**. Its "Edit in Descript" partner link is an invite-only integration for recording tools and makes a new project on every use, so it is not an option.

**Constraints from the owner (2026-11-12).** (1) Only videos in the Hub are handed to Descript: a Kajabi video is copied to the Hub first (the existing, billed "Videos" panel upload to Mux, `src/lib/courses/media.ts`), and the button stays disabled until it is there. (2) **One Descript project per video, ever**, so Descript storage doesn't grow with every edit. (3) Versions of a video are **linked as versions of one thing**, not separate unrelated assets.

**What exists today (checked 2026-11-12).** There is no versioning. `videos` is one row per Mux asset and a lesson points at one (`course_lessons.video_id`); replacing a video uploads a new asset and row and repoints the lesson, and nothing records that the two are related. `video_text_tracks` (captions) belong to one `videos` row and `video_watch_segments` reference `video_id`, so a replacement starts with no captions and its watch stats are split from the old one's. The old Mux asset stays (see "Course editor: clean up Mux assets and abandoned uploads"). `media_assets` (images) works the same way: one original per asset, deduplicated by SHA-256, a replacement is a new asset, no versions.

**Design.**
- **Versions, for every kind of media, not only videos (owner, 2026-11-15).** One model for all of it: a **work** is one logical thing (a video, an image, later audio or a file) and its **versions** are the stored files, linked to it. Prefer one generic `media_works` table (text id from `new_id`, new prefix, Tier 2: never in a URL) with `kind` (`video`, `image`, ...) and `current_version_id` over a table per kind, and give `videos` and `media_assets` each `work_id` and `version_number` (the migration gives every existing row a work of its own). Each kind keeps its own file row, so playback, renditions, access checks and the player don't change: a lesson still points at a `videos` row, an event or book cover at a `media_assets` row. **Replacing is always "add a version and make it current"**: the previous version stays with a **Restore** button, and every place that shows the work follows the current version (all lessons on a video; for an image, the cover or picture that uses it, instead of today's new asset plus a cleanup of the old one, `docs/product/MEDIA_LIBRARY.md`). Per-kind details: video captions stay per version and stats roll up by work; for images the focal point, alt text, title and tags belong to the work (they describe the picture, not one file), the SHA-256 dedupe still maps the same bytes to one stored original, and renditions are made per version. The Descript link (`descript_project_id`, `descript_composition_id`) lives on the work, so it is a column on a video-only side table or a nullable pair on `media_works` (decide when building). Build the generic table and the video side first, then move `media_assets` onto it in a following PR (the book cover replace, the media library's replace and `/admin/media`'s "where used" are the places to change), unless the first design review finds it cheaper to do both at once. Expand then contract (`.claude/rules/migrations.md`), through `MediaWorkRepository` (plus `CourseRepository` and `MediaRepository` methods) with both implementations and the contract suite.
- **Shared videos.** A duplicated lesson points at the same video (`duplicate_course_lesson`), so pull-back moves **every lesson on the work** to the new version, and the button says how many lessons it affects.
- **Edit in Descript.** First click: `POST /v1/jobs/import/project_media` with no `project_id` creates the project from a fetchable URL of the Hub video (poll `GET /v1/jobs/{id}` or pass a `callback_url`), then store `project_id` and the composition id (`GET /v1/projects/{id}` lists compositions and existing share pages). Every later click only opens `https://web.descript.com/<project id>`: **no new import**. A project Ania already made by hand is linked once by pasting its URL. Before creating, `GET /v1/projects?name=` can look for an existing one.
- **Pull back.** `POST /v1/jobs/publish` (project, composition, `media_type` Video, resolution, `callback_url`); the result carries `share_url` and a time-limited signed `download_url` with `download_url_expires_at`. Publishing the same `(project, composition, media type)` again reuses and overwrites the same share URL, so nothing piles up in Descript. A webhook route (idempotent, authenticated like the other callbacks; plus a pull-based way to check a job, per "Nothing through webhooks alone") fetches the file at once and hands it to the same Mux upload path as a Hub upload (`lesson-video-actions.ts`, `source_kind` `upload`), as a new version. New videos from Descript are exported without burned-in captions and with a separate subtitle file (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Captions and other per-video assets").
- **Source URL for Descript.** It must be reachable from outside, support HTTP Range and ideally be signed for 12 to 48 hours (the import reference). For a Hub video the candidate is a Mux master-access download of the original (a short-lived link; enable per asset): **unverified**, and it is the same question as the Download video action in "Course builder: the Video actions that are still disabled" (static renditions have a storage cost; check `docs/infrastructure/MUX_CONFIG.md`). Fallback: keep the original in Hub storage with a longer signed link, which also serves "Back up the original course videos before leaving Kajabi".
- **Housekeeping.** Keep the last two or three versions and prune older Mux assets (ties to the cleanup entry above); an audit entry for who pulled an edit back and which version it replaced (`docs/architecture/ACTIVITY_AND_AUDIT_LOG.md`, one entry for the intent); staff only (`requireActionPermission(...)`); `withAction` outcome tracking and tracking-plan events (`edit_in_descript_opened`, `descript_edit_pulled_back`); the API token as a new secret env var (`new-env-var.md`: declare, `.env.example`, sync to Vercel before the code deploys); a changelog entry (staff audience, `src/content/changelog.ts`) and `docs/product/COURSES.md` updated.

**Prove these first** (a throwaway script outside the repo, one real video, a few minutes with `curl` and a token; report pass or fail on each before building anything):
1. **Does a published `download_url` come back as a real file?** The help center says direct media export isn't supported yet while the publish reference returns a `download_url`. Check resolution, length and codec. The whole pull-back plan depends on it; if it fails, keep only the Edit button and let Ania export and upload through the existing replace flow (still with versions and the one-project link).
2. **Does publish work on a project made by hand in the Descript app**, with the composition id from `GET /projects/{id}`? All the docs examples are API-created projects.
3. **Can Descript fetch a Mux master-access link** (Range requests, link lifetime)? If not, use the storage fallback.
4. **Does the round trip look right in the Hub?** Edit in Descript, publish, play it in the lesson: length, audio sync, quality, and how long publish takes for a lesson-length video (tells whether the callback is required or optional).
5. **Token scope, rate limits and plan.** Which drive the token belongs to (hers or a shared one), whether her plan includes API access, whether the API counts against her transcription or storage limits, and that importing a video we already hold doesn't double her Descript storage in a way a manual upload wouldn't.

**What is needed to start:** a Descript API token from her account (a secret; never in the chat or the repo), one short, already-edited, Hub-hosted lesson video that is safe to experiment with, and one project she made by hand (test 2). The API is described by one third party as early access: re-read the reference before building, and note the version checked. **Suggested split:** PR 1, the generic work and version model with the video side, Restore and the repository; PR 2, the same for `media_assets`; PR 3, the Descript buttons, the webhook and the token.

### Course access: an admin screen to grant and remove access by hand _(Needs Scheduling)_
Course access comes from active Kajabi purchases of a course's offers plus `course_access_grants` (`src/lib/courses/access.ts`), but nothing in the app writes a grant: the table is only read by the processing step (`rebuildCourseAccess` in `src/lib/courses/process.ts`). A member who pays outside Kajabi's checkout (an invoice, a payment plan, a one-off) and is added by hand in Kajabi has no purchase of the offer, so the Hub shows them no course. At launch the first one was fixed with a SQL insert run by hand in production, which staff can't do. This is separate from member overrides (`member_status_overrides`), which grant membership status and are not read by course access. **Why deferred:** not a launch blocker, one case. **Where to start:** a "Grant access" section on the admin course page (`/admin/courses/<id>`) with a member picker (`EntitySearch`), start and end dates (default the course's access length from today), and a reason; list and remove existing grants. Write through `CourseProgressRepository` (add `addGrant`/`removeGrant` to the interface, both classes and the contract suite), as the admin's own client so the audit log names them, with `requireActionPermission(...)` and an audit trigger on the table, and RLS and grants in the migration. A grant takes effect on the next access rebuild, so also offer to run it, or write the access row at once. Check that a refreshed purchase doesn't remove a grant-only member (`rebuildCourseAccess` deletes only members no longer derived from purchases or grants).

### Course editor: captions, the rest _(Needs Scheduling)_
A Hub-owned lesson's video can have caption tracks added (a WebVTT or SubRip file, or Mux's automatic subtitles) and removed (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Captions"). Not built: **transcripts** (`kind = 'transcript'`: text for search and a follow-along panel, never attached to the player), a way to set which track is on by default (`video_text_tracks.is_default`), captions in a language outside the 22 offered (`CAPTION_LANGUAGES`, the ones Mux can write itself; an uploaded file could be any BCP 47 code), editing a track's name or replacing its file in place (today: remove and add again), and showing whether an automatic track has finished (Mux fills it in after a while; the row is recorded at once, and the player shows it when it is ready; the Mux webhooks `video.asset.track.ready` and `.errored` could update the row). Imported videos have their captions burned in (`videos.has_burned_in_captions`) and are not offered this. **Where to start:** `src/lib/courses/captions.ts`, `lesson-caption-actions.ts`.

### Course editor: lock a whole course _(Needs Scheduling)_
A module or lesson can be Locked (members see it as coming soon), but a whole course can only be made available or not (`courses.is_draft`, Publish / Unpublish). A course-wide Lock (everything visible but not open, for example before a cohort starts) was dropped when course availability stayed `is_draft` and the two were not combined. **Where to start:** decide whether it is a third course state or just "set the top-level modules to Locked", and where it shows (the course card, `src/lib/courses/member.ts`); a course-level setting would also sit above the item rule in `course_item_state` and `planFullTree`, which today treat the top as always open.

### Course editor: Schedule and Drip statuses _(Needs Scheduling)_
Kajabi's status menu has five choices; the Hub offers three (Draft, Publish, Lock). **Schedule** needs a date (and an unlock job or a read-time check, since members' access is by `state`, which row-level security reads), and **Drip** needs a delay from the purchase date (per member, so it can't be a single stored `state` at all: it is computed per member from `course_access.starts_at`). Today `scheduled` and `drip` rows are kept as imported: drip reads as coming soon (as in `visibilityFromPublishing`), scheduled as hidden (an unknown value), and `set_course_item_status` refuses both (`unsupported_status`). **Where to start:** a `scheduled_at` on `course_modules`/`course_lessons`, a rule for what a scheduled item is before its date (coming soon is the likely one, matching Kajabi), and either a nightly recompute or a view; for drip, a delay column and a per-member check where lessons are opened (`src/lib/courses/member.ts`). Check what the Kajabi import gives for scheduled and drip items first (`bronze.kajabi_course_*.publishing_option` is only ever draft, published or locked in production today, checked 2026-10-31).

### Course editor: prerequisite unlock for locked modules _(Needs Scheduling)_
Decided to build Kajabi's lock: a module or submodule held until the member completes a chosen lesson (Kajabi's article "Lock content in a course"; it also lets a quiz or assessment be the prerequisite, which the Hub doesn't have). Kajabi only lets modules and submodules be locked, never a single lesson; a submodule stays locked while its parent is; members who try a lesson inside it see a "locked" message instead of the lesson; the unlocking lesson must itself be published. **Missing today:** the Hub reads `locked` like `drip`, as "Coming soon" (`visibilityFromPublishing` in `src/lib/courses/visibility.ts`), has no prerequisite anywhere, and its status menu offers Lock on lessons too, so an item set to Lock never opens. **Why deferred:** it needs a place for the prerequisite and a per-member check, like drip, because whether a module is open depends on what that member has completed, so it can't be a single stored `state` (members' access reads `state` through row-level security). **Where to start:** (1) a nullable `locked_by_lesson_id` on `course_modules`, set by the editor (a "locked by lesson" picker that lists the published lessons before it) and cleared when the status leaves Lock; (2) the check where a member opens a lesson or sees the outline (`src/lib/courses/member.ts`, `src/lib/courses/outline.ts`): a locked module is open for a member whose own completion of the prerequisite lesson exists (what "complete" means in the Hub is `MarkCompleteButton` in `src/app/(member)/courses/`; find which table it writes), otherwise shown as locked with the lesson that opens it; nested modules inherit; (3) the Lock choice only on modules and submodules, with the lesson menu not offering it (`src/lib/courses/status-choices.ts`, `StatusPill` in `src/app/(admin)/admin/courses/BuilderOutline.tsx`); (4) imported Kajabi courses: the API doc names locked as "locked until certain conditions are met (e.g., completing previous lessons)" but I found no field for which lesson, so check what the import returns (`src/lib/kajabi/openapi.yaml`, `bronze.kajabi_course_*`) before promising anything for them; until then they stay "Coming soon". Also cover the Schedule and Drip entry above, which is the same per-member shape, and give Locked its own status icon (not the lock, which means only you or staff: `.claude/rules/icons.md`, "Bring the rest of the publish lifecycle onto the registry").

### Quizzes: results export and completion emails _(Needs Scheduling)_
Kajabi's quiz sends the member a completion email with a link to their results, sends the team a completion email, and lists submissions in a Results tab (which has an export). The Hub's quizzes (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Quizzes") have the results list in the admin and the member's own score page, and nothing else: **not built:** (1) **the two completion emails** (they would go through `createNotifier`, a new kind with a dry-run rollout, `.claude/rules/member-notifications.md`; the member one is a convenience since the score shows right after submitting, the team one is what Ania misses most if she relied on it); (2) **a results export** (a CSV of attempts per quiz: member, date, score, passed; member data, so a permission of its own, `export`, per `.claude/rules/permissions-for-resources.md`); (3) **a per-question view** (how many members got each question wrong, from `quiz_attempt_answers`). **Why deferred:** the user asked for the build, the score gate and the results list first; emails and exports need a decision on who receives them. **Where to start:** `src/lib/data/quizzes.ts` (`attemptsOfQuiz`, `answersOf`), the admin quiz Results tab, `src/lib/notifications/`.

### Quizzes: short answer and file upload questions _(Needs Scheduling)_
**Resolved** (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Questions that are not graded"): short answer and file upload questions, the "Graded question" toggle, members' own text and files (visible to them and to staff only), the staff view of an attempt, and the Kajabi import of these question types (read, not scored). **Still open:** (1) **Manual grading.** Kajabi grades a short answer or file upload "manually" (a Pass or Fail per submission, feedback per question, "Publish grade"); the Hub records these and never scores them, so there is no review queue, no feedback to the member and no pending state. Worth building only if the owner wants to grade in the Hub: it needs a grade and feedback on the attempt, a notification (`createNotifier`) and a rule for a pass while grades are outstanding. (2) **Per-question and per-option extras seen in Kajabi's editor:** an optional image on a question, an optional image per answer, and an optional explanation per answer (shown after submitting). **Where to start:** `src/lib/courses/quiz-scoring.ts`, `src/lib/courses/quiz-member.ts` (`submitQuizAttempt`), the `quiz_attempt_answers` table, `parseKajabiQuiz`.

### Verify the quiz responses capture against the live Kajabi admin _(Needs Scheduling)_
The responses capture (`courses:content --responses`, `src/lib/kajabi/quiz-responses.ts`, `docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Importing past quiz responses") was built and tested against invented fixtures: no agent can log in to Kajabi. **Resolved by the owner's probe** (field names and types, 2026-11-25): the Results list and a submission's view are JSON:API, the answers carry the question's title, type and position but no question id, the email is `contactEmail`, the date `dateSubmittedAt`, a file's link `fileUrl`; the reading follows that and joins an answer to the Hub's question by position with the title as a check (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Importing past quiz responses"). **Still unverified:** the values of `type` and `questionType` (the capture now prints the distinct ones it sees), whether `choices` carries ids or text, whether a file's link needs the login, how the list is paged (its `meta` is read; moving to the next page is a click on a Next control), and that the "Grade quiz" controls open every row. **What is needed:** the owner runs the probe on one quiz's results page and one submission (the run instructions are in the doc), then the dry run on Self-Editing Academy, and sends back the printed counts and field names; the fix is in `parseSubmission` / `submissionsListedIn` (a pure reading, so it re-runs on a new capture) and in `readQuizResponses` in `scripts/kajabi-course-content.ts`. Also check against Kajabi's own Results list that every submission came in (the quiz with 4 submissions, 489121, is the first check).

### Removal on request: quiz responses in Bronze _(what is left)_
**Resolved (2026-11-25, `docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Removal on request" under "Importing past quiz responses"):** removing a member now deletes their Hub quiz attempts, answers and files, and the Kajabi capture copies keyed by their email or any alias (past quiz responses and the import copies of their files, per-lesson and per-product progress, and the captured comments the import matched to them); the dry run lists each kind with its count before anything is removed; a fingerprint of each removed address and comment key (`removal_tombstones`) keeps a later capture from bringing it back. **Still open:** (1) **captured comments that only carry the member's name** are counted in the dry run and kept: the capture has no email or id, so they can't be shown to be theirs, and deleting by name would delete a namesake's words; if a member wants them gone, staff delete the rows by hand after checking in Kajabi (`bronze.kajabi_lesson_comments`, by `author_name`). A way to do it from the removal screen (staff pick which captured comments are the member's) would close it. (2) **The Hub's copy of a matched comment stays** under "Former member" with its text (the catalog's `repoint` for `lesson_comments`), and so does the lesson progress; decide whether a comment's text, which the member wrote, should be cleared or deleted on removal like their quiz answers. (3) **Rows under an address another member also holds** are kept (never guessed); the dry run counts them. **Where to start:** `member_data_handling` (`lesson_comments.member_id`), `src/lib/member-removal.ts`.

### Comments import: replies to a removed member's comment become top-level _(Needs Scheduling)_
Found while covering removal (2026-11-25). After a member is removed, the comments import no longer matches their captured name to anyone (their address and aliases are gone), so their comments are not "wanted"; `linkReplies` (`src/lib/courses/comments-import.ts`) then treats a reply to one of them as an orphan, and the next import rewrites that reply as a top-level comment (`orphanReplies`, `updated`). The removal itself keeps the thread (the Hub comment stays under the placeholder), so the damage is the import's. This was already true before removal covered the capture copies (an unmatched author has the same effect); deleting the matched capture rows does not change it. **Why deferred:** needs a rule for a reply whose parent is in the Hub but not in the current match. **Where to start:** in `importKajabiLessonComments`, when a reply's parent is not wanted but already exists in the Hub (`existing` by `sourceKey`), keep the reply's existing `parent_id` instead of null; add a case to `tests/lib/comments-import.test.ts`.

### Quizzes: sweep orphaned answer files _(Needs Scheduling)_
Files members upload as quiz answers go to the private `quiz-answer-files` bucket before the quiz is submitted. A member who uploads and never submits (or removes the file and picks another), and answers deleted by the Kajabi sync when Kajabi drops a question, leave objects with no `quiz_attempt_answers` row. Deleting a question or a quiz in the builder, and a member removal, delete their files; nothing else does. **Why deferred:** the first release is about taking and reading answers; the leftovers are the member's own files in a private bucket nobody else can read. **Where to start:** a nightly job like `purgeStagedChatFiles` (`src/lib/chat/staging.ts`): list the bucket, keep paths that a row names or that are younger than a day, delete the rest (`src/lib/courses/quiz-files-storage.ts` has the helpers). Also contract: the five-argument `save_quiz_question` is kept as a wrapper for deployed code; drop it in a later migration once nothing calls it.

### Quizzes: smaller follow-ups
Left out of the first quiz release (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Quizzes"): (1) **no cap on retakes**: a member can submit as often as they like and, for a quiz that shows answers, learn the right ones from the review; if that matters for a course, add a per-quiz "attempts allowed" setting (`quizzes` column, checked in `submitQuizAttempt`, shown in Settings) or a wait between attempts; (2) **the member course outline** draws a quiz lesson like any lesson (no quiz icon, no "Quiz" label): `OutlineLesson` in `src/lib/courses/outline.ts` would carry an `isQuiz` from `QuizRepository.quizzesOfLessons`; (3) **keeping a member's best score** is not shown (the lesson page lists the last five attempts); (4) **a member merge doesn't move quiz attempts** (listed under "Member merge drops most of a member's data": `quiz_attempts` is in `NOT_MOVED`, `src/lib/member-merge-coverage.ts`). **Where to start:** `src/lib/courses/quiz-member.ts` and `src/components/courses/QuizTaker.tsx`.

### Kajabi quiz capture: verify against the live quiz editor _(Needs Scheduling)_
**Resolved (2026-11-24) for The Ready Writer's sample quiz:** the owner ran the capture as a dry run, then with `--apply`, and the quiz arrived in production with its three questions, twelve answers and the right answer marked on each (checked read-only against Kajabi's editor). **Still open:** (1) open the imported quiz in the Hub as a member and compare it with Kajabi (order, the passing grade, "hide answers"); (2) the capture has not run on a course with non-graded questions, such as Self-Editing Academy's reflection quizzes (the question types and the Graded toggle below are still guessed); (3) whether Kajabi's multiple choice can have several right answers.

The original entry, kept for the steps: the quiz capture (`src/lib/kajabi/quiz-capture.ts`, `scripts/kajabi-course-content.ts`; `docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Importing quizzes from Kajabi") was built and tested only against fixtures: no agent can log in to Kajabi, and what was observed of the live admin was the screen (a quiz lesson has Questions, Settings and Results tabs; questions have options and an "Auto-graded" badge), not its JSON. The reading is tolerant by design, but **whether it finds the sample quiz (lesson 2200968704, quiz 1573355: three questions, no passing grade) is unverified**, and so are the exact paths the editor calls. **What is needed:** someone with the Kajabi login runs the capture on The Ready Writer as a dry run, then with `--apply`, then Sync from Kajabi, and opens the quiz in the Hub to compare it with Kajabi (questions, order, right answers, the passing grade and "hide answers"). If a quiz line says no questions were read, send the printed field names and types (or run `--probe <quiz editor address>` while opening the questions tab); the fix is in `parseKajabiQuiz` and re-runs on the stored Bronze documents with no new capture. Also check: whether Kajabi's multiple choice can have several right answers (the reading marks a question multiple-answer when more than one answer is marked right), and the **question types and the Graded toggle**: short answer and file upload are recognised by their type name and the toggle by a `graded`-like field, both guessed from what the editor shows (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Questions that are not graded"). **Where to start:** `tests/lib/kajabi-quiz-capture.test.ts` (add the real shape as a fixture first).

### "Move to position" doesn't say where a position is
The menu lists "Position 1 (current)", "Move to position 2", "Move to position 3" and so on (`positionItems` and `positionsHeading` in `src/lib/reorder.ts`, used by `SortableRow` in `src/components/writing/SortableList.tsx` and the course builder outline in `src/app/(admin)/admin/courses/BuilderOutline.tsx`). **What is wrong:** a number says nothing about what is at that place; someone choosing position 4 has to count rows, and on a phone the open menu covers the very list they are counting. It doesn't scale: a module with dozens of lessons gives dozens of menu items, and nested lists (modules, then submodules, then lessons) only move among the row's own siblings, so a lesson can't be sent to another module this way. **Why deferred:** the first version was the accessible alternative to dragging, and it works; a better one needs a design. **Where to start:** ideas to weigh, not decisions: name the place by its neighbors ("After “Welcome”", "To the top", "To the bottom") instead of a number; for a long list, a "Move after…" picker over the siblings' titles with search (`EntitySearch` is the base for pickers) rather than a list of positions; a "Move to…" dialog showing the outline with the landing spot marked, which also allows another parent (the reorder action `reorderCourseItems` in `src/app/(admin)/admin/courses/outline-actions.ts` takes a sibling order; moving between parents is a different change); show on screen where the row will land before it is confirmed. Dragging and the keyboard handle stay as they are. Settle the pattern for both the writing lists and the builder together, so they keep one look.

### Reorder icon on the goal rows, and on the grip handle
The "Move to position" menus use the `reorder` icon (`src/components/Icon.tsx`, an up-down arrow) as their trigger in the course builder outline and the projects list (`SortableRow` with no extra items). **Still open:** the goal rows on the project page keep the ⋯ on narrow screens, because that menu mixes Edit and Delete (its `extraItems`) with the positions. **Where to start:** split it into a reorder-icon menu with only the positions and a ⋯ with Edit and Delete in `src/components/writing/SortableList.tsx`. **Also:** the owner liked making the grip handle itself the way into the menu (a click on the handle opens "Move to position", a drag still drags); try it once the icon trigger has been used for a while, and check how the drag library tells a click from a drag start, keyboard use (Space and the arrow keys already work on the handle) and touch.

### Course editor: outline follow-ups _(Needs Scheduling)_
Left out of the first outline editor (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Editing model") on purpose; each is small on its own.
- **Move across lists.** Dragging works within one list (a module's lessons, a course's modules, a module's submodules). A **lesson** can be moved to another module (or out of every module) with the picker on its page (`move_course_lesson`; it goes to the end of the module, and the list it left keeps its numbers until the next reorder). Not built: dragging a lesson onto another module in the outline, and moving a **submodule** into another module, which needs a function like `move_course_lesson` for `parent_id` (keeping the one-level rule, which the database already enforces) and drop targets on module headers.
- **Duplicating a lesson doesn't copy its downloads.** The files live in the private `course-files` bucket under the lesson's id (`course_lesson_files`); a copy needs the storage objects copied too, so a lesson doesn't share a file with its copy (deleting one would remove the other's).
- **Lessons without a module.** The database allows a lesson in no module (imported courses have some), but the editor only adds lessons inside a module.
- **Deleting a lesson members have progress in is refused** ("hide it instead"), and the status menu on each row of a Hub-owned course's outline is that way out (Draft or Lock it).

### Course builder: the Video actions that are still disabled _(Needs Scheduling)_
The lesson builder's Video actions menu (`src/app/(admin)/admin/courses/[id]/lessons/[lessonId]/VideoActions.tsx`) copies Kajabi's. **Working now:** Video stats (links to the lesson's watch stats) and Copy timestamp URL (the lesson's member address with `?t=<seconds>`, which the member lesson page honours, `startSeconds` in `src/lib/courses/watch.ts`). **Disabled, and why:**
- **Closed captions** (Kajabi opens a "Transcription playground": Transcriptions, Translations and Dubbing tabs, upload or generate a transcript). It edits, and no captions manager exists yet. Where to start: the `video_text_tracks` table (`docs/product/COURSES.md`, "Captions and other per-video assets"), the planned small admin Videos screen for SRT/VTT uploads, and attaching tracks to the Mux asset. A read-only view of existing tracks could ship first.
- **Download video.** Mux serves no downloadable copy unless MP4 static renditions are turned on for the asset (a Mux setting with storage cost), and the Kajabi source link expires after 7 days. Decide whether the cost is acceptable; then an admin-only route that redirects to a signed `highest.mp4`. Check `docs/infrastructure/MUX_CONFIG.md` first.
- **Remove video** is an edit, so it waits for saving.

### Remove legacy `?tab=` redirects _(Cleanup, review on or after 2027-01-03)_
Tabs became URL paths on 2026-10-03 (`/settings/notifications`, `/my-prickles/all`; see `src/lib/tab-routes.ts` and .claude/rules/tabbed-pages.md "Tabbed Pages"). Old `?tab=` links still work because each base page calls `redirectLegacyTabParam`. Nothing is stored; it's four one-line calls plus the helper:
- `src/app/(member)/settings/page.tsx`, `src/app/(member)/my-prickles/page.tsx` (also rewrites `?tab=commitments&slot=X` to `/my-prickles/all?commit=X`), `src/app/(member)/projects/page.tsx`, `src/app/(admin)/admin/members/[id]/page.tsx`
- `redirectLegacyTabParam` in `src/lib/tab-routes.ts`, its tests in `tests/lib/tab-routes.test.ts`, and the legacy cases in the page tests (`settings-routes`, `my-prickles-tabs`, `my-writing-page`)

Old links come from Slack DMs (the check-in/check-out "Notification settings" link, sent from 2026-10-03 until this change deploys; Slack keeps them forever), calendar feed descriptions (refreshed by calendar apps within a day), and bookmarks or links pasted into Slack, emails and docs.

**When:** each redirect logs `[legacy-tab-redirect]` with the page and tab. After 2027-01-03, search the Vercel logs for it. If none (or only a stray few) in the last 30 days, delete the calls, the helper and their tests, and drop the "old `?tab=` links" bullet from `.claude/rules/tabbed-pages.md`. A `?tab=` URL then just opens the page's first tab, which is a fine fallback. If they're still being hit, look at which page and tab and check again in another quarter.

### Drag-and-Drop Table Column Reordering (Not Persisted)
Every admin table (Hedgieversaries, Members, etc.) has a fixed column order set in code — on mobile, whichever columns come first are the only ones visible without scrolling, so getting the "right" default order matters but can't cover every viewer's priorities. Letting users drag-and-drop reorder columns in the standard table widget (session-only, not persisted per-user) would let each viewer put what they care about first without a code change per table.

**Scope:** this is a shared table component enhancement (wherever `SortableTh`/the standard table widget lives), not a one-off per page — worth building once and getting for free everywhere.

### CRM & Outreach Automation Roadmap (Needs Scoping — Multi-Phase)
`/admin/outreach` (hot/warm/cold status, the daily 25-lead queue, `outreach_touches` log) is the first slice of a much bigger planned system: a built-in CRM with a unified inbox, response-driven outreach, lead scoring, and eventually an AI prospecting agent. Stated 2026-09-09 as the direction to build toward — not scoped or started beyond Outreach itself. Phases below are roughly the order they'd need to happen (later phases depend on earlier ones), not a commitment to build all of them.

**Phase 1 — Centralized activity log (CRM backbone). In progress.** `member_activities` already existed (written by Slack import + writing-progress) and is being extended to also mirror prickle attendance, outreach touches, and Hedgie Hub logins — see `docs/architecture/ACTIVITY_AND_AUDIT_LOG.md` for the schema (`actor_kind`/`actor_user_id` for who acted, `data` for the event's actual content) and the separate `audit_log` (field-level record-change history, distinct from this activity log — see that doc for why they're two tables). `audit_log`, its triggers and the admin `/admin/activity` page (Audit / Everything filter over both tables plus page-visit sessions) are built. The member page has an Activity Log tab over the same feed. The clock icon (`RecordHistoryButton`, top-right of each page) is on the member, event, program, badge type and prickle type pages and the Segments, Users, Overrides and Hosts lists. Every audited resource now has a history button. Remaining work is listed in the follow-ups below.

Phase 1 follow-ups (activity/audit log):
- [ ] Email sent/opened/clicked events in `member_activities`. Email is now a notification channel (`src/lib/channels/`, Resend), so it is a source; nothing is logged as activity yet.
- [ ] `p_changed_by` argument on the audit functions, for the first service-role write made on behalf of a user. None exists yet, so add it with that first write; see `docs/architecture/ACTIVITY_AND_AUDIT_LOG.md`.
- [ ] Privacy view: log break-glass grants and reads of restricted Slack content. Today it covers only a channel being restricted or the restriction lifted (`restricted_slack_channel`); add the new entity types to `PRIVACY_ENTITY_TYPES` in `src/lib/activity-feed.ts`.
- [ ] Slack channel names on old activity rows are a snapshot from insert time, so a later channel rename isn't applied to them. Decide whether to backfill on rename or resolve the name at read time.

**Phase 2 — Inbox.** A Beeper Desktop-inspired unified inbox inside Hedgie Hub showing messages across connected accounts, with filter views: All / Unread / Drafts / Unanswered / Archived / Requests / Low Priority. Requires real per-channel integrations to pull messages in — Instagram DMs (Graph API, needs a Business/Creator account linked to a Facebook Page, and Meta's app review for `instagram_manage_messages` has real lead time), email (provider-dependent — Gmail API vs. a shared inbox forwarding setup), Slack (already have API access, just needs message-fetch + a read UI). Likely built incrementally per channel rather than all at once.

**Phase 3 — Outreach ↔ Inbox integration.** Once Phase 2 exists, Outreach should surface a "needs response" view — leads who replied to a cold outreach touch, as a filtered subset of Inbox scoped to outreach recipients. This is where a reply stops being invisible in personal Instagram/email and becomes an actionable queue item, closing the loop that today's one-way `outreach_touches` log doesn't.

**Phase 4 — Lead scoring.** Augment Ania's manual hot/warm/cold with a computed score, using Phase 1's activity log as the input (engagement signals, response patterns, etc.) once there's enough activity data to make a score meaningful.

**Phase 5 — AI prospecting & messaging agent.** An agent that (a) finds people not yet on the lead list by searching online (Instagram and beyond), (b) reads their profile/page for talking points and fit signals, (c) drafts an initial outreach message, and (d) over time learns Ania's voice and optimizes messaging for response rate and conversion — likely needs its own feedback loop off Phase 1's activity log and Phase 4's scoring. Longer-term: SMS and voice-call outreach (Vapi mentioned as a candidate for voice).

**Open questions (apply across phases):**
- Single shared Instagram/email account for outreach, or per-staff-member accounts? Affects OAuth/connection architecture for Phase 2.
- Data model for Phase 1 — settled on extending `member_activities` (one row per event) rather than a new table; see `docs/architecture/ACTIVITY_AND_AUDIT_LOG.md`.
- How much of Phase 5's "AI agent" runs autonomously vs. drafts-for-review — cold outreach to strangers sourced by an AI carries real brand/trust risk if it goes out unreviewed.
- **ORM evaluation** (decoupled from the above — came up while designing `audit_log`'s trigger-based change tracking, which needs no ORM and works regardless of this decision): this codebase has no ORM/model layer, just direct `supabase-js` calls across routes. Worth evaluating on its own merits (type safety, query ergonomics) at some point, but not framed as something the audit log or any of the above phases are blocked on or temporary until.

**Still open:** (1) the rules in `src/lib/prospecting/rules.ts` are a first guess until Ania answers the questions in `docs/product/PROSPECTING.md`; (2) discovery: Bluesky runs from a button; still open: a nightly run (pg_cron + heartbeat), the Instagram source (needs Meta app review, see `docs/product/PROSPECTING.md`) and other platforms; logged-in Instagram scraping is deliberately not done; (3) prospect touches do not feed the daily 25 queue (needs a decision on whether prospects become `members` rows); (4) no found → joined funnel in `src/lib/analytics/funnels.ts`; (5) retention for rejected prospects and a privacy-policy line before any automation; (6) `pnpm types:gen` was not run for this migration (the local database was not available to this worktree), so `src/lib/data/database.types.ts` was edited by hand: run `pnpm types:check` and commit any diff.

**Staff notes and Instagram on the member profile (asked for by Ania).** **Still open:** (1) reading Instagram instead of logging "sent insta DM" by hand. Nothing is built, because the only sanctioned route is Meta's Instagram messaging API: it needs a Business or Creator account linked to a Facebook Page, a Meta app, and App Review for `instagram_manage_messages` (days to weeks), and it only covers conversations on that one account. Logged-in scraping stays off the table (`docs/product/PROSPECTING.md`, decision 2). Start by asking Ania which account she sends from and whether it can be a professional account, then follow Phase 2 (Inbox) above: a DM webhook writing Bronze rows matched to members by `members.instagram_url`, shown as items on this panel. A per-DM "sent" touch could also be one tap that logs an `outreach_touches` row instead of a free-text note. (2) Notes are written on the member's Activity Log tab only; the Overview shows the latest note with a link to all of them (the tab filtered to notes, 1y range), but has no add box. (3) The edit window's length is passed to the database function by the app (SQL can't read `config/app.ts`), so an admin calling the function directly could pass a longer one; sync it to Vault (`SHARED_CONFIG_IN_VAULT`) if that ever matters. (4) The migration and `supabase/tests/database/member_admin_notes.test.sql` were not run locally (the shared local database was on another branch's schema); CI runs both.

**Priority:** Phase 1 in progress; the rest is roadmap only.

### "helpme" Hidden Debug Shortcut
Add a hidden keyboard shortcut (typing "helpme", similar to Confluent Cloud's debug overlay) that pops up a small debug panel showing:
- Environment name, git branch, and short commit SHA (same data already surfaced by `EnvironmentIndicator`/`EnvironmentInfo` in `src/components/EnvironmentIndicator.tsx` and `src/lib/config.ts`)
- The full list of feature flags from `src/lib/config.ts`'s `features` object (`debugMode`, `analytics`, `showEnvironmentBadge`, `verboseErrors`, and any added later) with their current enabled/disabled state

**Why a keystroke shortcut instead of always-visible UI:** Unlike `EnvironmentIndicator` (which is env-gated and only ever shows in non-production), this should work in production too, so admins/devs can check build/flag state on prod without needing a preview deployment — hence hiding it behind a deliberate keystroke sequence rather than always rendering it.

**Implementation notes:**
- Needs a small client component listening for the "helpme" keystroke sequence (buffer recent keydown events, reset on non-matching key, trigger on match)
- Reuse `src/lib/config.ts`'s `deployment` (branch/commitSha) and `features` objects rather than duplicating that data
- Since this is meant to work in production, it should not be gated behind `features.showEnvironmentBadge` the way `EnvironmentIndicator` is — consider restricting it to admin users only (auth check) since it may reveal internal build/config info

### Fewer queries on My Prickles and the Dashboard: the medium-risk rest

Deliberately not done:

- **Hosting stats, schedules, eligibility and the member directory load on every tab.** `Tabs` renders only the active tab but gets every tab's content from the server and switches on the client with `history.replaceState`, so skipping a tab's data on the server would show it empty after a click. Needs the heavy tabs to load their data on demand (a client fetch or a server action when first opened), or each tab to be a real navigation. Start in `src/app/(member)/my-prickles/MyPricklesPage.tsx` (`getMyHostingStats`, `fetchOtherMembers`).
- **The ranking fetches the member's whole attendance history** (`getRankedUpcomingWithHistory`, `src/lib/upcoming-prickles.ts`) for streaks and sister streaks. Narrowing it changes what the ranking sees, so it needs a check that the ranked output is identical.
- **The layout blocks the page** on its own lookups. Un-blocking it is a shell change.

**Measured before launch (production, one signed-in admin, 10 / 25 / 50 parallel requests across `/dashboard`, `/courses`, `/my-prickles`):** throughput stops at about 6 pages a second and latency grows linearly with concurrency (p50 2.3s, 4.1s, 9.0s), before and after the first pass above. `/courses`, which makes few queries of its own, slows down with the others, so the cost shared by every member page (the layout's 12 to 14 mostly sequential queries, the proxy's Auth `getUser()` and its `access_events` insert) is a large part of it. Public pages (`/login`, `/privacy`) do about 300 requests a second at 150ms with 50 parallel, so the function layer is not the limit; the limit is behind it (PostgREST and the database, at up to 28 queries running at once and connections peaking at about 50 of 90). Functions run in `cle1` and the database in `us-east-2`, so region is not the cause.

**Layout lookups and page speed (2026-11-03):** the member layout no longer waits for the install-prompt flag, tour state, Slack need or in-app notifications (#902, #909). `pnpm page-speed` (`scripts/page-speed.ts`, read-only, from `vercel metrics`) records per-route function duration and real-user FCP/LCP/TTFB in `docs/decisions/PAGE_SPEED.md`. The day after both were live showed no clear change against the day before (`/dashboard` server p50/p75 21/92ms then, 34/175ms now; FCP p75 2954 then, 3040ms now; the windows covered different hours and many deploys, so cold starts muddy it): server time on the member pages was already tens of milliseconds, so there was little to win. Moving the profile, features and identity behind promises too is **not worth doing** on this evidence (it would need the redirect and navigation restructured).

**Still open: the gap between function time and the real-user TTFB.** `/dashboard` runs in about 34ms p50 (161ms p75 on a hot function) and the proxy's TTFB p75 is 118ms, yet Speed Insights shows a TTFB p75 around 1s on `/dashboard` and `/settings` (`/projects`, `/chat/[id]` are under 100ms). Cold starts are too rare to set it (39 of 2,582 `/dashboard` requests, 1.5%, at 1.95s p75). A guess, not checked: Speed Insights counts the redirect chain of a first load (sign-in, `/auth/callback`, then the page), and 2026-11-02 was launch day with many sign-ins. Rerun `pnpm page-speed --record` a few days after the sign-in rush, over a full day, and compare; if `/dashboard` TTFB is still high, trace one real navigation (redirects, the proxy's Auth `getUser()` and its `access_events` insert, see the measurement above).

Next steps, in order: (1) the TTFB gap above; (2) cache the global reads (feature flags and their segment links, prickle types) with `unstable_cache`; (3) narrow the ranking's attendance history; (4) rerun the same load test (script idea: mint a session with the admin API for a test member, three concurrency steps, sample `pg_stat_activity` every few seconds) and compare. Not worth doing: raising `default_pool_size` alone.

### Link the holder's name in the "already an alias" error
Adding an alias that another member holds says `"AJ" is already an alias for <name>. Move it from there first.` (`src/lib/name-alias-conflict.ts`) as plain text, so the admin has to search for the holder. Deferred as a nit: the message is a string the identity page shows as is. Return the holder's id with the conflict and render the name as a link to `/admin/members/<id>` where the message is shown.

### Calendar naming convention for virtual retreats

Existing virtual retreats are titled "<year> <season> Virtual Retreat: <topic>" (for example "2026 Fall Virtual Retreat: Plot"), but newer calendar items such as "Quill & Cup Virtual Retreat" do not follow it. Deferred on purpose: renaming automatically is too much magic, and the event type now comes from the words "virtual retreat" in the title either way (`prickleEventKind`). Follow the pattern when creating the Google Calendar item; if the mismatch keeps happening, show a hint on the event admin page when a Virtual Retreat title lacks the pattern (`src/lib/events-filter.ts`, `src/components/EventDetailsCard.tsx`).

## Internationalization

### Secondary Time Zone: Remaining Displays
Members talk about prickles in Eastern whatever their zone, so a member can opt in (Settings → Preferences, `user_profiles.secondary_timezone`, off by default, like Google Calendar's additional time zone) to see a second zone in parentheses beside theirs: "4:00 AM (7:00 AM ET)" (`src/lib/secondary-time.ts`, `src/components/SecondaryTimezone.tsx`). Usage is visible as the `secondary_timezone_changed` action outcome and the `settings-secondary-timezone*` click tracking. **Still open:** the hosting schedule and its calendar picker, `src/lib/prickle-writing.ts`, Slack/email notification text, the calendar's hour column (Google Calendar shows a second column there), and a label next to the zone.

### Basic Date Format Localization (Needs Scoping)
Dates are currently always displayed US-style (`MM/DD/YYYY`), but many/most other countries prefer `DD/MM/YYYY`. Language should stay English and money stay USD (that's all Quill & Cup charges in and speaks) — this is scoped to date display only, not full i18n.

**Needs scoping before implementation:**
- Where dates are formatted today (likely scattered `toLocaleDateString()`/manual formatting calls across admin and member pages) vs. a shared date-formatting utility that could centralize this
- Source of the preferred format: browser locale (`navigator.language`) vs. an explicit per-member/user setting (ties into "User settings - additional preferences" under UI Enhancements, which already floats a "working location for global time analysis" preference)
- Whether this only affects display formatting, or also needs to affect date *input* parsing (e.g. any place a date is typed rather than picked via `<input type="date">`, which is locale-formatted by the browser natively already)

## Data Audits

### Access-date vs transaction-date audit (`kajabi_purchases`)
Find members/purchases where `effective_start_at` is more than ~7 days after the actual transaction date (`created_at_kajabi`). These purchases look like overlapping or out-of-order subscriptions in `detectResubscriptions`, which uses `effective_start_at` as the start of a membership period. A large lag between transaction date and access date can cause legitimate resubscriptions to be missed — e.g. a purchase made in January but access granted in February will appear to overlap with the prior subscription that ends in February, so no gap is detected.

Query: `SELECT * FROM bronze.kajabi_purchases WHERE effective_start_at - created_at_kajabi > interval '7 days'`

---

## Secrets at Rest

Where a secret lives depends on what the app does with it:
- **A credential we must hand to someone else** (a member's Slack token, a future provider key per member) goes in **Supabase Vault** behind service-role-only functions, with only a pointer in our table. `member_slack_connections` is the pattern (`20261021000000`).
- **A credential we only need to recognise** (single-use sign-in tokens, anything looked up by value) is stored as a **hash**, never the value. `slack_sign_in_tokens` already does (SHA-256).
- **App-wide credentials** (bot token, API keys, signing secrets) are env vars declared in `config/env-vars.ts`.

Audit (2026-10-10): nothing else needs moving to Vault, but two things are worth a look:
- **`calendar_feed_tokens.token`** is a plaintext bearer credential: anyone with the URL reads that member's hosted and committed prickles. It is read-only, scoped to one member, revocable ("Generate new link"), and the table is RLS-protected (owner and admins can read it), so this is low risk. It is a table value rather than a hash only because members can see their link again in Settings. Options if it matters: store an HMAC for the lookup and derive the displayed URL from a per-member secret, or hash it and make members regenerate to see it. Admins can currently read every member's feed token through RLS; at minimum drop that.
- **`push_subscriptions` `p256dh` / `auth`** are a browser's push encryption keys. Sending a push also needs our VAPID private key (an env var), so a leaked row alone can't be used to send. The table is service role only. Fine as is; revisit if it ever becomes readable by members or admins.

When Slack token rotation is adopted (see `docs/product/SLACK_BRIDGED_CHAT.md`, "Slack app configuration"), the bot token moves out of the `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` env var into Vault too, since it then changes every 12 hours.

## Bug Fixes

### A guard test for the Activity Log _(Idea)_

`.claude/rules/audit-and-activity-log.md` ("Every Action a Person Takes Is in the Activity Log") says every action that changes data leaves a plain-sentence entry, but nothing checks it, which is how the page builder shipped with generic wording. **Idea:** a test (like `tests/lib/tooltips.test.ts`) that reads the migrations for every `audit_row_change('<entity>', ...)` and fails when `<entity>` has neither a label in `ENTITY_LABELS` nor a sentence in `describeRow` (`src/lib/activity-feed.ts`), with a short shrink-only allowlist for entities that are intentionally terse; and a second check that every `src/app/**/actions.ts` file calling `requireActionPermission(...)` and writing through a repository either goes through an audited table or names its event (a comment or a constant the test can read). **Why deferred:** the first check is cheap and could land alone; the second needs a convention for naming events that actions write outside a trigger. **Where to start:** the migrations' trigger lines, `src/lib/activity-feed.ts`, and the guard-test style in `tests/lib/`.

### Flaky unit run: an unhandled "event must be an instance of Event" from a real WebSocket _(Needs Scheduling)_

One local `pnpm test:unit` run (2026-11-20) finished with all tests passing but one "Unhandled Error": `TypeError: The "event" argument must be an instance of Event` (`ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE`), thrown inside undici's WebSocket code while `tests/components/pages/admin-calendar-data.test.tsx` was running. The next full run and two runs of that file alone were clean. **Likely cause (not confirmed):** a component under test opens a real WebSocket in the jsdom environment, and the connection finishes after the test, dispatching jsdom's `Event` into Node's undici `WebSocket`, which wants Node's own `Event`; whether it fires depends on timing. **Why deferred:** rare, and an unhandled error fails the run only when it lands. **Where to start:** find what opens the socket (probably a Supabase realtime client created by the page under test) and stub it in the test, or close it on unmount; then run the file in a loop.

### Flaky db test: the seed loader collides on prickle ids _(Needs Scheduling)_

`tests/api/data/seed-loader.test.ts` ("loadProfile": "loads badges, and a re-run replaces the earlier load..." and "clears only its own profile") failed once in CI with `seed prickles: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "prickles_pkey"` (PR #1536, the db suite of `Test (db) & build`), and passed on a re-run of the same commit; the same suite had passed on `main` minutes earlier and the PR did not touch this area. **Likely cause (not confirmed):** `loadProfile` (`src/lib/data/seed-loader.ts`) clears a profile's prickles by the `seed:<profile>` marker and then inserts the dataset's prickles with ids it derives itself, while the tests use a per-run suffix only for emails (`RUN`); two tests (or two workers) loading the same profile at the same moment against the one shared database can have one test's rows still present when the other inserts, so the ids collide. **Why deferred:** it is rare and a re-run clears it. **Where to start:** make the prickle ids in the test's datasets unique per run (as the emails are), or serialize the tests that load the same profile; then run the file in a loop against a local stack (`pnpm test:db`) to check. Do not re-run CI over it again without noting it here.

**Second occurrence (2026-11-20, PR #1574, a change to one CSP string, its test and docs):** the same two tests failed with the same error on the first run of the db suite and passed when only that job was re-run; the unit suite and migrations passed on the same commit, and the db suite had passed on the `main` runs just before. **What that run and a local check say about the guess above:** (1) the guess that only the emails carry the run suffix looks wrong: `mine()` in the test puts `RUN` in the profile's *name*, and `buildDataset` derives each prickle id from `seedId("prkl", "<name>|<date>|<start>|<summary>")` (`src/lib/data/seed-profile.ts`), so ids already differ per run; (2) the dataset builder gave no id shared between the two profiles at any of the 300 clock offsets the test can use, so it is not a weekday coincidence; (3) the file passed 6 of 6 times in a row alone against the local stack, so it needs something else running, and no other test loads these profiles into the database (the page tests only build them in memory). That leaves a rarer cause: two loads of the *same* name overlapping (a test that timed out or was retried while its inserts were still running, with the database slow at that moment: the failing job also logged `connection lost` from other tests a minute earlier), or a real id collision inside one dataset. **Where to start now:** log the colliding id and the existing row's `type` in the error from `check()` in `src/lib/data/seed-loader.ts` (`seed prickles: duplicate key ...` says neither), so the next failure names its cause instead of guessing; then decide between a per-run guard in the test and a unique-by-construction id.

### Member merge drops most of a member's data _(High Priority: data loss)_
A member merge (`src/app/api/admin/members/merge/route.ts`) deletes the duplicate, and about 45 tables that point at `members` cascade on delete, so what the merge does not move is gone. It moves prickle attendance, activities, hiatuses and status overrides, hosting, name and email aliases, program enrollments, and (since the retreat fix) event attendance, badges, Big Sister matches and the volunteer record. A merge in November 2026 lost a retreat attendance with its badge and three mentorships, found through the audit log (`audit_log` rows for the member's delete and the cascaded deletes in the same transaction, by `txid`).

**Still open:** every table in `NOT_MOVED` in `src/lib/member-merge-coverage.ts`: the members' own writing, books, awards, course progress and access, notes, onboarding, notification settings and push subscriptions, chat drafts and memberships, calendar feed, check-ins and commitments, outreach, segments, and the join-date and profile overrides. Each needs a rule for what to do when both members have a row (keep the kept member's, merge, or move both). The test fails on a new table that references `members` until it is classified; remove a table's line when the merge moves it.

**Until then:** before merging two members, check the "What <name> has" panel in the merge dialog (it counts what the duplicate owns and marks what would be lost; the Merge & Fix page's "Merge All" button has no dialog and shows nothing, so open its groups one by one); a merge cannot be undone, and the audit log (`entity_type = 'member'`, `action = 'delete'`, same `txid` for the cascaded rows) is the only record of what went. **Why deferred:** the retreat and Big Sister tables were the ones with a known loss; the rest need a per-table decision, and most are owned data (a book, a project) where the right answer is not obvious. **Where to start:** the audit log shows what past merges dropped (`entity_type = 'member'`, `action = 'delete'`), so check it before deciding what to restore; then move the member-owned tables with real content first (projects, books, awards, notes), through their repositories with a `move...(from, to)` method like `moveAttendances`.
