### DB tests that load every member in one query fail when the table is busy _(seen once, 2026-11-08)_
`tests/member-matching.test.ts` (14 tests) and `tests/api/reprocessability/orphaned-meetings-idempotency.test.ts` (2 tests) failed together in one `Test (db) & build` run on a PR that touched neither members nor Zoom data (the same files passed on every other recent run). Both read the whole `members` table with a single `select('id, name, email')` and no pagination, so once the table holds more than 1000 rows (other DB test files run in parallel and each leave members in flight) the PostgREST limit cuts off the test's own member and the assertion sees `null`/0. That reading fits the failures but was not confirmed from the log (the member count at the time isn't recorded). **Fix:** have the helpers use `fetchAllRows` (`src/lib/supabase/paginate.ts`) with a stable `.order("id")`, per the pagination rule in `.claude/rules/api-routes-and-queries.md`, or look the test's member up by email instead of scanning; check for other tests with the same unpaginated `members` read (`grep -rn "from('members').select" tests`).

### Seed-loader DB test failed once with a duplicate prickle id _(seen once, 2026-11-24)_
`tests/api/data/seed-loader.test.ts` failed two tests in one `Test (db) & build` run on a PR that touched neither the seed loader nor the database ("loads badges, and a re-run replaces the earlier load instead of piling up", and "clears only its own profile"): `seed prickles: duplicate key value violates unique constraint "prickles_pkey"`, thrown from `insertChunked` in `src/lib/data/seed-loader.ts` (called from `loadProfile` at lines 63 and 81 of the test). The other 228 test files passed, and re-running only the failed job passed, with the same code; `main` had passed the same job minutes earlier. **Not explained:** a prickle id is `seedId("prkl", profile.name|date|startLocal|summary)` (`src/lib/data/seed-profile.ts`) and the test's profile name carries the run's timestamp, so two runs should not collide, and the day loop steps in UTC so a daylight saving change cannot repeat a date. **Where to look:** whether `clearProfile` (deletes prickles by the `seed:<profile>` type marker, then members) can leave a row behind when a delete races another test file; whether the same-named `HOSTING_HEAVY` profile loaded by two tests in the file (`mine()` gives both the same `RUN` suffix) collides when the first test's rows are still being cleared; and the `RUN = Date.now()` suffix being equal across files started in the same millisecond. Start by running `pnpm test:db tests/api/data/seed-loader.test.ts` in a loop against a busy local stack. A real fix is likely to give each test its own suffix.

### Member merge modal: say when the two Kajabi contacts still need merging
- **Missing:** merging two members here does not tell the admin that their Kajabi contacts are still separate. Kajabi's public API has no merge call (only get, update and delete), so the contacts are merged by hand in Kajabi first, and when that step is skipped the leftover duplicate keeps its email in Kajabi and blocks later email changes ("Email is already taken"). The primary-email change already names the contact that holds the address and offers to delete one that is empty (`describeKajabiConflict` in `src/lib/member-email.ts`, `src/app/api/admin/members/[id]/delete-empty-kajabi-contact`).
- **Why deferred:** the email change was the pain point; the merge modal needs its own design (when to check, what to show for Stripe, which contact is kept).
- **Where to start:** `src/app/(admin)/admin/members/[id]/MergeMemberModal.tsx` and `src/app/api/admin/members/merge`. Look both members' `kajabi_id` up in Kajabi (`fetchContactWithAttachments`) and, when both exist and differ, show what is attached to each and a reminder to merge them in Kajabi first. Re-check Kajabi's API reference for a merge call before building anything that deletes.

### Chat: browser-level checks for layout the unit tests cannot see
- **Still open:** these have unit tests only for the inputs (style attributes, classes, placement math), because jsdom does no layout: that the message box really grows to fit a long paste up to its cap (`MessageBox` sets `min-height`, `max-height`, `overflow-y` and no height; `tests/components/MessageBox.test.tsx`), that nothing in `/chat` can scroll past the composer (`min-h-0`/`overflow-hidden` on the member shell and chat layout), and that the picker heading stays above scrolling emoji. A Playwright scene in `tests/demo/scenes/` (see `chat-formatting.spec.ts`) could measure `offsetHeight` before and after a paste, `document.scrollingElement.scrollHeight` against the viewport, and the heading's stacking.
- **Where to start:** `tests/demo/scenes/chat-formatting.spec.ts`, `src/components/chat/MessageBox.tsx`, `src/app/(member)/chat/layout.tsx`.

### MemberCalendarClient: week and month navigation still use the host's zone
- **Missing:** `src/components/MemberCalendarClient.tsx` computes the current week start (`setDate`/`setHours(0,0,0,0)`) and the initial/previous/next month from the browser's zone, while the data and the grid cells are keyed in the member's `timezone` preference. When the two differ, the "current week" and "current month" can be the wrong one around midnight. `AttendanceMonthGrid` cell keys and "today" were fixed (they shifted a whole day); the navigation state was not.
- **Why deferred:** found while making unit tests zone-independent; a fuller fix means holding the week/month as zone-free calendar dates (see `startOfWeek` in `src/app/(member)/my-prickles/AllPricklesCalendar.tsx` for the pattern) and needs boundary tests.
- **Start:** `MemberCalendarClient.tsx` (`currentMonthDate`, `currentWeekStart`, `handleToday`), `AttendanceMonthGrid.tsx`, `src/lib/org-week.ts`.

### Reconciliation: Split monolithic endpoint and unify discrepancy logic _(refactor + code smell)_
- **Code smell:** `has_discrepancy` is currently a hybrid — computed server-side for Kajabi/Stripe in `/api/analyze/subscription-reconciliation`, but Slack absence is ORed in client-side in `src/app/(admin)/admin/reconciliation/page.tsx`
- **Root cause:** Slack, Stripe orphans, and Zoom access were already extracted into their own endpoints (`/api/analyze/slack-reconciliation`, `/api/analyze/stripe-orphans`, `/api/analyze/zoom-access`), but the subscription endpoint is still an all-in-one monolith that owns the `has_discrepancy` flag
- **Fix:**
  1. Split `/api/analyze/subscription-reconciliation` into separate endpoints (Stripe, Kajabi, etc.) following the same pattern
  2. Move all `has_discrepancy` logic client-side so the page computes it by combining all sources uniformly
  3. Remove `has_discrepancy` from the API response (it becomes a pure UI concern)

### Let a prickle's host report unmatched Zoom attendees _(feature idea)_
- **Idea:** Both prickle detail pages (`/admin/prickles/[id]` and `/(member)/prickles/[id]`) already compute `findUnmatchedZoomAttendees` and render `AliasSearchForm` — but the member-facing page gates it to `isActingAsAdmin` only, so a regular host never sees "these Zoom names showed up but didn't match anyone" for a prickle they hosted, even though the host is the one person who actually knows who that was.
- **Blockers found:**
  1. `src/app/(member)/prickles/[id]/page.tsx` — the `AliasSearchForm` render and its data fetch are conditioned on `isActingAsAdmin`; would need to change to "viewer is this prickle's host" (or host OR admin).
  2. `/api/aliases` and `/api/aliases/slack` are both `requireApiPermission`-guarded. A host-facing version needs a scoped write path — only allow aliasing a Zoom name that actually appeared in a prickle that member hosted, not arbitrary alias creation — which is its own design/security decision, not a drop-in relaxation of the existing admin check.
- **Why not built yet:** Raised during the data-hygiene dashboard cleanup (see "Data hygiene / reconciliation dashboard cleanup" work); deliberately scoped out since the permission model needs its own design pass.
- **Planned shape: ask in real time.** While a prickle is running, automatically ask its host (a notification through `createNotifier`, with a link to the exact prompt) to say who an unmatched Zoom name is, using the join events already in `bronze.zoom_participant_events` / `src/lib/zoom-presence.ts`. For a Pop-Up Prickle (no host) ask the most senior member present instead, or a similar rule; "senior" needs a definition (tenure, hosting history, staff). The host's answers need the same choices an admin has: match to a member (alias), "ignore here" (this prickle only) or "ignore always".
- **Guests and speakers aren't just "ignored".** An unmatched Zoom name on one prickle is sometimes a known person who isn't a member (a guest, a speaker). "Ignore here" now records a free-text reason, but that only hides the name. Decide whether to record who they were, or tag the attendee as a guest or speaker, so it counts for the prickle (and for `names_guest_speaker` prickle types) instead of vanishing. Start at `ignored_zoom_appearances` and `src/lib/prickle-unmatched.ts`.

### Remaining places that format dates with the host's locale and zone _(hydration mismatch risk)_
- **What:** A bare `toLocaleDateString()` / `toLocaleTimeString()` / `toLocaleString()` formats in the host's zone, so the server render (UTC on Vercel) and the browser can print different text and React reports a hydration mismatch. It also breaks the `ORG_TIMEZONE` rule (`.claude/rules/dates-and-timezones.md`).
- **Still open:** Bare `toLocaleString()` / `toLocaleDateString(undefined, ...)` / `toLocaleTimeString()` calls remain in several admin pages and panels (hygiene, wheel-of-wonder, gap-fills, feedback, reconciliation, insights tables, `SessionsPanel`, `NotificationInbox` tooltip, `MemberSlackActivityPanel`, `EventsBrowser`) and `en-US` ones without a `timeZone` (member profile, hosting, streaks, bookshelf, projects panels). Date-only values built with `new Date(`${d}T00:00:00`)` should use `formatDateOnly`; instants should use the viewer's zone.
- **Why deferred:** None is a known cause of an error; the shared helpers were the part that touched every prickle page.
- **Where to start:** `grep -rn "toLocale" app components lib`, skipping number `toLocaleString()` calls.

### Admin member activity tab hits the statement timeout _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is wrong:** `GET /admin/members/[id]/activity` died once with Postgres `57014` (`canceling statement due to statement timeout`, the API role's 8s limit; Sentry issue HUB-8, 2026-10-15, one event, so far). The page loads the feed through `fetchActivityFeed` in `src/lib/activity-feed.ts`, which calls the SQL functions `count_activity_feed` and `get_activity_feed` over `member_activities` (58,593 rows in prod on 2026-10-23). The Sentry event doesn't say which statement timed out, and nothing else has reproduced it.
- **Why deferred:** One event, no repro, and the cause may be a cold cache or a member with an unusually long history rather than a missing index. Not worth guessing at.
- **Where to start:** Run `explain (analyze)` for `get_activity_feed` and `count_activity_feed` against prod (read-only, `verify-against-prod.md`) for the members with the most `member_activities` rows, with and without the default filters. If the count is the slow one, a capped or estimated count may do. Keep HUB-8 open in Sentry until fixed. The same `57014` also showed in the project's events on 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-15 under other issues, so check whether it is recurring.

### `members.joined_at` throws away the time it was given _(Needs Scheduling)_
- **What is wrong:** The column is `timestamptz`, and its source, `created_at_kajabi`, is a real timestamp, but `src/app/api/process/members/route.ts` cuts it to a date (`created_at_kajabi.split('T')[0]`) before writing, so every value is midnight UTC (prod, 2026-10-23: 944 of 944 members, none with a time of day). Date-only values then get formatted as if they were instants: shown in Eastern a member can appear to have joined the day before, and the admin at-risk page needed `formatDateOnly` as a workaround.
- **What it should be:** the instant the member joined, kept as is and shown in the viewer's zone (`formatDateInZone`). Two things mix into it today and need a decision: a staff `hire_date` (a true date) replaces it when earlier, so convert that to the start of that day in `ORG_TIMEZONE` when it is used; and `first_joined_at` / `most_recent_joined_at` (and the `member_join_date_overrides` that correct the first one) are genuine dates, `DATE` columns, and should stay that way.
- **Why deferred:** Found while fixing date formatting (PR #353). Changing it needs a members reprocess and a look at the 27 files that read `joined_at`, to see which expect a date.
- **Where to start:** Stop truncating in `src/app/api/process/members/route.ts` and in `reprocess_members_atomic` (its latest definition is in `supabase/migrations/20261215000000_short_ids_tier1.sql`: read the newest `CREATE OR REPLACE` before editing). Then switch the readers to `formatDateInZone` and drop the `formatDateOnly(member.joined_at)` workaround in `src/app/(admin)/admin/at-risk/page.tsx`. Add a reprocessability test that a Kajabi time of day survives.

### `/members` hydration mismatch in the page check
**Resolved:** it was a false alarm caused by the harness, not an app bug. `shot()` (`tests/demo/fixtures.ts`) scrolled and ran its checks right after navigation; before React had hydrated, that changed attributes the server rendered, and the console logged "a tree hydrated but some attributes of the server rendered HTML didn't match". It reproduced on `/members`, `/changelog` and others whenever a scene called `shot()` straight after `goto`, and not for anyone who waited first (which is why four attempts to find an app cause failed). `shot()` now waits for the page to settle (capped, since member pages poll) before it screenshots or checks; a scene that failed 3 of 3 times before passes 3 of 3 now. **Still open:** none for this entry.

### The auto-hide sidebar covers the footer links while it is open
**Resolved:** by design. The auto-hide sidebar opens over whatever is on the screen and covers the footer's Privacy and Contact links while it is open; the owner decided that is fine. **Still open:** the demo harness's `control-covered` check flags it when a scene opens the rail on a page with the footer visible; a scene that does so should not treat that as a failure (start from `tests/demo/fixtures.ts` `shot()`).

### Hedgieversary dates drift by a few days after a cancel and resubscribe gap
**Resolved:** milestone dates no longer come from a subtraction of rounded months. `members.inactive_periods` holds the dated cancel/resubscribe gaps (written by the members reprocess from the same stints as the Membership History), hiatus windows are read live, and `src/lib/member-tenure.ts` merges them, rounds each merged period to calendar months once and shifts only the milestones that fall after a period began.
**Still open:** members get their gaps on the next members reprocess after the deploy (until then a member's `inactive_periods` is null and only hiatus shifts their dates). Before relying on the new dates, count in production how many members' next Hedgieversary moved and by how much (`verify-against-prod.md`). **Resolved (later):** the gaps now treat program-cohort enrollments and gift / direct Stripe overrides as active (`computeInactiveGaps`, same notion as the admin membership timeline), so a stretch with no coverage before a program or gift is found even when no later Kajabi stint closes it; coverage is merged, so overlapping purchases no longer yield a gap longer than the Membership History implies. **Still open:** stored `inactive_periods` only change on the next members reprocess after this deploys, so run one (staff-triggered, not from a session) and then recount in production how many members' next Hedgieversary moved.

## CRM Features

### Outreach for the conversion specialist: prospect de-duplication _(Needs Scheduling)_
The conversion specialist runs outreach (`outreach.manage`) and reads only the `members` rows with status `lead` or `cancelled` (RLS, `20270930000000`). One thing that reads more than that doesn't work for them: Pasted prospects are kept free of people we already have with `MemberRepository.instagramUrls()` (every status, `src/lib/prospecting/import.ts`), which the specialist's client can't read for current members, so a current member can show up as a prospect. **Where to start:** a small database function that answers "is this Instagram URL a member's" (or returns the list of URLs, not the rows) for `is_conversion_specialist()`, called from `src/lib/prospecting/import.ts` and `src/app/(admin)/admin/outreach/prospects/actions.ts`.

### Email, scheduled email and announcements surfaces (for the conversion specialist and the admin) _(Idea)_
`email.send`, `email.schedule` and `announcements.manage` exist in the permission registry (`src/lib/permissions.ts`, reserved; the conversion specialist and the admin hold them) but nothing in the Hub sends a campaign or edits an announcement from the staff area: member email goes through `createNotifier` and the shell bars are code (`src/components/announcements/`). **Why deferred:** the role was set up first; the product (provider, audiences, unsubscribe, a sending rule per `.claude/rules/member-notifications.md`) is not designed. **Where to start:** design the surface, then guard it with those permissions and take them off `RESERVED_PERMISSIONS`.


### Slack Engagement Signals
Still open:
- Data hygiene UI for unmatched Slack users.
- Use Slack activity in at-risk detection (members who stopped posting, lurkers who attend but never post) and fold it into one engagement/risk score; refine `engagement_score` weights by activity type and recency.
- Staff alerts: "hasn't posted in 14 days (usually 3x/week)", "very active in Slack but no prickle in 30 days".
- Channel health metrics (messages per day, participation, dead channels).
- A wider activity feed (Slack messages, reactions, file shares, whitepaper downloads, email opens/clicks, retreat registrations) combined with attendance; same idea as the Dashboard's "Recent Activity Feed".

### Lesson Activity: Reconciliation, Churn Recency (follow-ups to the lesson activity rows)
Lesson viewing now writes `member_activities` rows (`lesson_watched`, `lesson_completed`, source `courses`; `src/lib/courses/activity.ts`), so it shows in the Activity Log and adds to the engagement score. Deferred:
- **Reconciliation.** The rows are a best-effort copy written next to the primary write, so a failed insert is logged and never retried, and a changed threshold or weight doesn't rewrite past rows. If drift shows up, add a pull-based pass (nightly) that re-derives missing rows from the source tables.
- **Churn recency ignores lessons.** `riskLevel` (`src/lib/member-engagement.ts`, `admin_members_page`, `/admin/at-risk`) is days since the last *prickle*, so a member who only watches lessons still rates "high" risk. Decide whether recency should count any activity type, or fold risk into the engagement score as a trend (recent points against the member's own earlier baseline); the three places apply the rule separately today.

### Email timeline: what is left
Deferred (Phase 1, Resend events on the member timeline, is in `docs/architecture/EMAIL_TIMELINE.md`):
- **Replies (phase 2).** Resend can receive mail (`email.received`; the webhook carries metadata only, the body comes from `resend.emails.receiving.get`). It needs an inbound address on a name that can carry an MX record (`hub.` is a CNAME to Vercel, so a subdomain such as `reply.`), `Reply-To` set on Hub email, the event stored (the route ignores `email.received` today), the body kept in a service-role-only table like Slack content, and an admin read through a server action. Start: `src/lib/email-events.ts` (`STORED_EVENT_TYPES`), and "Email Routing on Separate Subdomains" under Infrastructure.
- **Read an email's body inside the Hub.** Today a timeline row links to the email at Resend. An admin server action (`requireActionPermission`) could fetch `GET /emails/{id}` and show the text in a dialog; bodies would not be stored.
- **Monitor the nightly import.** The Checkly heartbeat for `/api/reconcile/email` is switched off (`unmonitored` in `src/lib/cron-heartbeats.ts`) because the Hobby plan's 10 uptime monitors are all used. Free a monitor or upgrade, then remove that line (see `docs/architecture/EMAIL_TIMELINE.md`, setup step 4). The Mux webhook API check does not count against the 10, so removing it frees nothing.
- **Retention and removal.** `bronze.resend_events` (addresses, subjects) and the `resend` timeline rows have no retention rule, and `remove_member_data` does not clear the Bronze copy (it is keyed by address, like the other Bronze copies listed under "Still open" there). Decide how long to keep them and extend the routine.
- **Auth and launch email tags.** Supabase Auth's emails go over SMTP and match by address only. Sending them through the Send Email hook would let them carry tags and a real `Reply-To`. Sign-in link scanners click every link, so once click tracking is on, clicks on those emails are noise; consider hiding `email_clicked` for `kind` null, or fix it at the source with the `auth.` domain above.
- **Click tracking and Supabase sign-in emails.** Click tracking is not on yet (2026-11-06; see "Where this stands" under "Email Routing on Separate Subdomains", which has the steps to enable it). Once it is, check that a real sign-in email's link still works: Resend's guide for Supabase Auth emails says link tracking can corrupt verification links, and the switch is per domain. The lasting fix is the `auth.` domain without tracking. Nothing in the Hub reads or depends on the answer.
- **A separate Resend key for the import.** `RESEND_API_KEY` is Full access since 2026-11-04 because the nightly pull lists emails, and Resend has no read-only permission. The same key is Supabase Auth's SMTP password and lives in Vercel and GitHub, so a leak could delete domains and keys. Least privilege would be a second Full-access key used only by `/api/reconcile/email` (a new env var such as `RESEND_IMPORT_API_KEY`, Vercel production only, read in `src/app/api/reconcile/email/route.ts`) and the sending key back to Sending access. Not built; decide whether the risk is worth the extra key.
- **Members with several addresses.** Matching uses the member's own address, not their aliases.
- **Opens (decided 2026-11-03: left out for now).** Open tracking stays off; clicks, deliveries, bounces and replies are the data the CRM starts with. The reason to revisit is a real CRM signal: a member who keeps opening reminders and never acts is different from one who never opens them. If it is turned on, do it as an experiment on the notifications domain (after the `auth.` split, since the switch is per domain): let opens accumulate in Bronze for a couple of weeks, watch bounce and complaint rates in Resend, then tune. Opens are noisy (Apple Mail Privacy Protection and corporate scanners open mail before the person does, some clients block the pixel, plain-text mail has none), so classify, don't score: add `data.open_signal` ("likely person" or "likely automated") in `emailActivities` (`src/lib/email-events.ts`) from, in order of strength, the time between `email.delivered` and the open (seconds after delivery is a scanner or a prefetch), the opener's user agent and IP (image proxies identify themselves; the click payload has them, so check the open payload; the raw payload is already stored), and corroboration (a later click, a reply, opens on different days). Don't weight by recipient domain: the mail app decides, not the address (Apple's prefetch applies to any address in Apple Mail). Keep `engagement_value` at 0 and keep it out of at-risk detection until real data has been looked at. Changing a rule reprocesses from Bronze, nothing to migrate. The route already handles `email.opened` events.
- **Dashboard.** Delivery health (bounce and complaint rates) from `bronze.resend_events`, and an engagement signal if opens or clicks prove meaningful (their `engagement_value` is 0 on purpose).

### Host Confirmation Flow (not built)
After each prickle ends, message the host to confirm participants and resolve unmatched Zoom attendees. For unhosted prickles: TBD, options include assigning a random active member or the most senior hedgie (by join date or total duration excluding hiatus). Build it as a notification kind sent with `createNotifier`, not wired directly to Slack. Related: "Let a prickle's host report unmatched Zoom attendees".

### Payment Failure Notifications (not built)
On payment failure, notify the member (a `payment_failed` notification kind via `createNotifier`, Slack by default) to prompt them to update their payment method.

## Writing Projects Tracking

Full roadmap: `docs/superpowers/specs/writing-projects-tracking.md`. Core single-author flow (log progress against a project/goal, prickle-linked nudges, streak/chart stats) is scoped there phase-by-phase.


### Journey tab: use the full page width _(Needs Scheduling)_

The Journey tab (My Writing, behind the `writing_journey` flag) shows its diagram inside the tab container, which is `max-w-3xl` for every My Writing tab, so on a laptop it opens fitted at about 0.57x. Deferred: an "Open larger" overlay (`PanZoomCanvas` `expandTitle`, `Modal` `fill`) covers it for now. Worth doing if members rarely open the overlay: let only this tab break out of the container (`src/app/(member)/projects/ProjectsPage.tsx`, the `Tabs` `className`), keeping the tab bar aligned with the other tabs.

### Soft-delete the rest of the member-written tables _(Decided 2026-10-31: independent records only)_

Members' deletes of writing goals, progress entries, books and awards soft delete (`deleted_at`; see "Members' own business resources soft delete too" in `.claude/rules/data-pipeline-reprocessability.md`). **Decided: the rule is independent records only**, with settings and in-place edits overwriting. So three member deletes stay row `DELETE`s on purpose:

- **Private member notes** (`member_notes`): clearing a note deletes it. A note is private to its author (no admin access, not audited, hidden in sudo) and saving overwrites the old text, so keeping a copy of a cleared note would work against why it is private.
- **Calendar-feed items added by hand** (`calendar_feed_items`, `removeMyCalendarItem`): a selection of what appears in the member's feed; the change is already in `audit_log`.
- **Starting balances** (`replaceStartingBalances` in `src/lib/data/writing.ts`): clearing a measure removes a baseline number that an edit also overwrites.

"Ask me about" topics were never a delete: the form saves the topics array (empty when cleared). Still open and small: if a restore path is ever built for progress entries, decide whether the entry's `member_activities` row should come back (the delete removes it so a bogus entry doesn't inflate engagement).

### Prickle Check-ins: Visibility Wording + Host Aggregates (Needs Scoping)
`prickle_checkins` (mood before/after, named feeling, intention, got what they came for) is readable by the member and admins, never hosts or other members. The check-in card deliberately says nothing about who can see responses yet. Open questions:
- Whether the card should say something (e.g. "Hosts can't see your individual responses").
- Whether hosts get aggregate trends for their prickles. Small prickles make aggregates identifying, so only show a prickle's (or a host series') aggregate once at least 5 distinct members checked in, and serve it through a SECURITY DEFINER function, never row access.

### Prickle Check-ins Phase 3: Admin Check-in Patterns (Needs Scoping)
Check-ins (`prickle_checkins`) and progress entries linked to prickles (`writing_progress_entries.prickle_id`) are live, and the Prickle Picker already uses a member's own checked-out check-ins (#54). Next is an admin view of each member's patterns, as a reach-out prompt:
- Per member: recent mood before/after (and the change), intentions, average "got what I came for" against their own baseline, and trend over time.
- Flag members whose pleasantness coming in has stayed low or fallen over a recent window (e.g. 3+ of their last 5 check-ins at 1-2), whose "got what I came for" has dropped well below their usual, or who stopped answering (people skip when they feel worst). See the measurement plan in `docs/product/CHECKINS.md`. Feeds the existing outreach touches (`/api/admin/outreach-touches`).
- Admins already have row read access to `prickle_checkins` (RLS), so this is an admin page plus queries, no schema change. Named feelings roll up to their Feeling Wheel core with `EMOTION_CORE` in `src/lib/prickle-checkins.ts`.
- Open: where it lives (member admin page tab vs a dedicated "check-ins" admin page) and the exact flag thresholds.

### Prickle Check-ins: Save Find a Prickle's Answers as a Check-in (Idea)
The Slack check-in DM now asks the check-in questions ~20 minutes before a prickle, for active members (a writing goal is not needed) who have the prickle on their calendar feed. The "How are you feeling?" answers in Find a Prickle (My Prickles → Find a Prickle, `src/lib/prickle-picker.ts`) still only pre-fill the check-in on the prickle the member clicks through to; nothing is saved unless they press Save there. Idea: save them as that prickle's check-in when the member picks it (and skip the check-in DM, which `checkinAnswered` already does once both answers exist).

### Prickle Check-ins Phase 3: Member Insights + Normalized Prickle Ratings (Wait for Data)
Only meaningful once a few weeks of check-ins exist.
- **Member insights:** a "What works for you" view on the member's own pages, e.g. "When you came in tense, focused prickles with Host X got you what you came for", and which prickles lift their mood most (pleasantness and energy after minus before). Reuse `similarCheckins` from `src/lib/prickle-picker.ts`.
- **Normalized prickle ratings:** score a prickle series by each member's "got what I came for" and mood change minus that member's own average, so a member who always answers low doesn't drag a series down (and one who answers everything "Completely" doesn't inflate it). Use it wherever series ratings surface (Prickle Picker ranking, host stats).
- Same privacy rules as check-ins: members see only their own; any host-facing aggregate needs at least 5 distinct members (see the check-in visibility entry above).

### Prickle Check-ins: Learn Feeling Suggestions from Searches (Needs Scoping)
The "Name the feeling" search suggests Feeling Wheel words for common words that aren't on the wheel ("stuck" → Frustrated, Discouraged, Helpless). That table, `EMOTION_SUGGESTIONS` in `src/lib/prickle-checkins.ts`, was written by hand from the old feelings list, so it only knows the words we guessed. Deferred from the Feeling Wheel PR (#361) to ship the redesign first.
- Record, anonymously, what members type into the feeling search and what they pick next (or that they picked nothing): the term and the chosen wheel word, no member id, so typed text can't be traced to a person. It is typed text, so it can't be a `track()` property (`docs/product/ANALYTICS.md`); it needs its own small table (service role writes, admin reads, with explicit grants per `supabase-table-grants.md`) or an aggregate counter per (term, pick).
- An admin view listing frequent terms with no exact match and what people chose for them, as input for new `EMOTION_SUGGESTIONS` entries (still added by hand and reviewed).
- Covers the in-app picker (`EmotionPicker` in `src/components/writing/CheckinFields.tsx`, which matches with `searchEmotions`). Slack filters its own select client-side, so those searches can't be seen.
- Add a line to the privacy page saying feeling searches are logged without a name.

### In-App Pipeline Visualization + Next-Stage Nudges (Needs Scoping)
Show each member, on their own writing project, a visual of where they sit in the overall Quill & Cup pipeline (idea → draft → self-edit → feedback → publish → launch/sell) — both to make the system visible ("we have a system, and you're on it") and to nudge them toward the next stage as they approach it, which doubles as adoption/revenue for the programs at each stage as they get built.

**Reference:** the pipeline is drawn in the app as the Journey map (`src/components/writing/JourneyMap.tsx`, stages in `src/lib/writing-journey.ts`); the old static `docs/pipeline-map.html` was removed.

**This is a product/business decision as much as a build, not just a tech task:** most of the pipeline stages aren't Quill & Cup programs yet (Beta Readers, Self-Publishing Course, Trad Publishing/Query Course, and arguably Line & Copy Edit) — building the in-app visualization is a relatively contained UI feature, but *nudging members toward a stage* only pays off once that stage is a real, staffed program. Sequencing (visualize the roadmap now vs. wait until each program exists) is a call for Cody/Ania, not something to default on.

**How `writing_projects.phase` maps to the pipeline (resolved; see `src/lib/writing-journey.ts`):** each status puts a project at one stage, and a stage can have no status (the publishing fork).
- `planning`/`outlining`/`drafting` → Draft (The Ready Writer)
- `first_read_through`, `revising`, `developmental_edit`, `beta_readers`, `line_copy_edit`, `final_read_through`, `final_beta_read` → the stages of the hire-a-pro swimlane (First read-through, Revision, Developmental edit, Revision, Beta readers, Revision, Line & copy edit, Final read-through, Final beta read). The Self-Editing Academy is a bar over all of them and lists all those statuses. `revising` is shown at the first Revision only, since the three share the status
- `publishing` and `complete` → the Publishing box (Self-Publish, ARC readers, Query & Submit). `complete` stays there until what it means next to `publishing` and `published` is settled
- `published` → Launch & Sell
- `hibernating` → not a pipeline position; a pause/exit flag that applies at any stage (it also covers what used to be `on_hold`)
- **Why a status can't say which path a project is on** (the Self-Editing Academy or a hired professional; see "Journey: tell the two edit paths apart" below): `phase` answers "how far along is the manuscript" (member-set from a simple dropdown); the pipeline answers "which program or vendor are you using," which has forks (DIY vs. hired, self-publish vs. query) a linear enum can't represent without conflating progress with vendor choice. Disambiguating which node a `revising` project is actually on belongs to program-enrollment data — the `member_products` table already planned above under Kajabi Data Expansion — once it exists, not a bigger phase list.

**Still needs scoping before implementation:**
- What a "nudge" actually is — a banner on `/writing`, a Slack DM (ties into the Messaging Abstraction Layer under CRM Features), an email, or just a visual marker with no push?
- Whether the visualization is linear per project or needs to show the same fork/parallel structure as the Journey map (e.g. a member who hired a Developmental Editor shouldn't be nudged toward the Self-Editing Academy) — likely blocked on `member_products` existing, per above
- How stages that don't exist yet as programs should render — greyed out/"coming soon," or hidden until built?

### Journey: tell the two edit paths apart _(Needs Scheduling)_
The Journey map (`src/components/writing/JourneyMap.tsx`, stages in `src/lib/writing-journey.ts`) draws two ways through the edit stages: the Self-Editing Academy (one bar over Developmental edit, both rounds of beta readers and Line & copy edit) and the do-it-yourself hired-professional path (each stage on its own). A project's status (`writing_projects.phase`) says how far along the manuscript is, not which path the writer is on, so two members at "Developmental edit" or "Beta readers" can be on different paths, and the map can't show it. Today only the Revising status maps to the Academy bar; every other edit status shows under the hired-pro stage of the same name.

**Why deferred:** the fix is enrollment data, not more statuses. Splitting `phase` per path would mix progress with which vendor or program the member chose. The data this needs is a record of who is in which program (the planned `member_products` table, see "Kajabi Data Expansion"), and the Academy does not exist in the Hub as a course yet (see "Catch-up from imported Kajabi progress").

**Where to start:** build `member_products` from `kajabi_purchases` and `kajabi_offers` (the Kajabi Data Expansion entry). Then, per project, take the path from the member's active Self-Editing Academy access (never typed, derived like trial conversion in `src/lib/mentorship.ts`), highlight the Academy bar or the hired-pro stages on the map from that, and let it drive next-stage nudges (a member who hired a developmental editor shouldn't be nudged toward the Academy; see "In-App Pipeline Visualization + Next-Stage Nudges"). A member with no enrollment data keeps today's behavior.

### Collaborative / Multi-Author Projects (Deferred — needs scoping)
Some members write as part of a group — e.g. Ania is one of ~7 authors writing separate novellas set in the same shared town, with intertwining characters (a shared-universe anthology). The core writing-projects data model is single-owner only and doesn't account for this.

Two genuinely different problems, not yet scoped:
- **Shared universe / collection** — each author owns their own separate project; projects group into a collection with mutual visibility across co-authors. Likely fit for Ania's case (separate books, shared setting) — lower complexity, no changes to the single-owner entry/RLS model, just a grouping layer.
- **True co-authored project** — multiple people log progress against one shared manuscript/project with a combined total. Needed only if some future project is genuinely one shared document rather than separate books. Bigger change: `writing_projects` needs multiple owners (collaborator join table), and the "member can only read/write their own rows" RLS assumption needs rework.

**Why deferred:** get the single-author loop (log → nudge → streak/chart) shipped and proven first; scope collaboration once that foundation exists rather than designing it speculatively now.

### Point-in-Time Goal Versioning (Deferred)
`writing_goals` rows are edited in place — `computeHabitGoalProgress`/`computeGoalProgress` always evaluate a goal's *current* definition (period, threshold, measure) against its *full* entry history. Editing period or threshold on an existing goal retroactively reshapes how every past period scores, with no record of what the goal used to say.

There used to be one narrow fix: changing a `measure='prickles'` goal's anchor archived the old row and forked a new one. Anchors were removed in October 2026 (no production goal used one; see Part 3 of `docs/product/COMMITMENTS.md`), and that fork went with them. When goals link to commitments instead, switching a goal to a different commitment should archive-and-fork the same way, so an earned streak isn't recomputed against the new slots.

**Still open:** editing threshold/period on any goal, or changing its measure, silently rewrites history. Real point-in-time versioning (a goal "changed as of" record, with past periods evaluated against whatever was true then) would fix this properly.

**Why deferred:** it's a known gap but hasn't caused a reported problem yet, and versioning every field is real design work (schema for change history, UI for "goal changed on this date," how charts/exports treat a versioned goal) beyond what's warranted to build speculatively.
